# PART I: LAZARUS SATURDAY, PALM SUNDAY, AND GREAT AND HOLY PASSION WEEK

## Lazarus Saturday: Vespers on Friday Evening

*At Vespers, Kathisma: "Unto the Lord in my affliction" (Kathisma 18).[^3]*
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 stichoi, and chant the idiomelon stichera. Tone 8. On: "Lead forth from prison:"*[^4]

*Tone 8. Idiomelon:*
Having completed the forty days that bring profit to our souls,[^5] we entreat Thee, O Lover of mankind, to let us behold the Holy Week of Thy Passion, that in it we may glorify Thy mighty acts and Thine ineffable dispensation toward us, singing with one mind: O Lord, glory to Thee. *(Twice)*

*Then, the Martyrikon:*
O Martyrs of the Lord, pray unto our God, and entreat for our souls a multitude of compassions and cleansing from our many transgressions, we beseech you.

*Then, to Saint Lazarus. 5 stichera, repeated to make 6, idiomela. The composition of the Pious Emperor Kyr Leo the Wise.[^6] Tone 6:*

*Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
Wishing to behold the tomb of Lazarus, though by Thine own will Thou wouldest be seen in the tomb, Thou didst inquire: [continued on next leaf] "Where have ye laid him?" And having learned that which Thou knewest full well, Thou didst call unto him whom Thou lovedst: "Lazarus, come forth!" And the lifeless corpse obeyed Him Who gives breath unto him, even Thee, the Savior of our souls. *(Twice)*

*Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
O Lord, Thou didst come unto the tomb of Lazarus who was four days dead; and shedding tears over Thy friend, Thou didst raise up the four-day-dead corpse, O Ear of Life! Therefore death was bound by Thy voice, and the grave-bands were loosed by human hands; then the multitude of the disciples was filled with joy, and from all there arose one concordant cry: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, have mercy on us! *(Twice)*

*Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
O Lord, Thy voice destroyed the kingdom of Hades, and the word of Thy sovereign power raised up from the tomb him that was four days dead; and Lazarus became a saving prefiguration of the rebirth of being. All things are possible unto Thee, as King of all: grant unto Thy servants cleansing and great mercy. *(Twice)*

*Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
O Lord, wishing to confirm Thy disciples in Thine own resurrection from the dead, Thou didst come unto the tomb of Lazarus; and when Thou didst call him forth, Hades was despoiled and released the four-day-dead man, who cried aloud unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, glory to Thee! *(Twice)*

*Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
O Lord, taking Thy disciples with Thee, Thou didst come unto Bethany to raise Lazarus; and having wept over him according to the law of human nature, yet as God Thou didst raise him up who was four days dead; and they cried unto Thee, O Savior: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory:*
Standing before the tomb of Lazarus, O our Savior, and calling out to the dead man, Thou didst raise him up as if awakening another, by the nod of Thy secret incorruptibility; and bound in grave-bands, he came forth at Thy word. All things Thou art able to do; all things serve Thee, O Lover of mankind; all things submit unto Thee: O our Savior, glory to Thee!

*Now and ever: Tone 6:*
Having completed the forty days that bring profit to our souls, let us cry aloud: Rejoice, city of Bethany, homeland of Lazarus! Rejoice, Martha and Mary, his sisters! For tomorrow Christ comes to quicken with a word your dead brother; hearing His voice, bitter and insatiable Hades shall tremble with fear and groan exceedingly, and shall release Lazarus bound in grave-bands. Marveling at His wonder, the assembly of the Hebrews shall meet Him with boughs and branches; and the children praising Him shall appear, at whom the fathers shall look with envy: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord, the King of Israel!

*Entrance with the censer. "O Joyful Light."*
*Prokeimenon. Psalm 123 (LXX).[^7] Tone 6:*
Our help is in the name of the Lord, Who made heaven and earth.
*Stichos: If the Lord had not been among us.*

*Reading from the Book of Genesis (49:29–50:26 [LXX]):*[^8]
Jacob gave commandment unto his sons; and having gathered up his feet upon his bed, he expired, and was gathered unto his people. And Joseph fell upon his father's face, and wept bitterly over him, and kissed him. And Joseph commanded his servants the undertakers to bury his father; and the undertakers buried Israel. And forty days were fulfilled for him, for so are the days of burying fulfilled; and Egypt wept for him seventy days. [continued on next leaf] And when the days of mourning were past, Joseph spoke unto the potentates of Pharaoh, saying: If I have found grace in your sight, speak into the ears of Pharaoh, saying: My father bound me by oath before his decease, saying: In the tomb which I dug for myself in the land of Canaan, there shalt thou bury me. Now therefore let me go up and bury my father, and I will return. And Pharaoh said according to the word of Joseph. And Pharaoh said unto Joseph: Go up, bury thy father, as he bound thee by oath. And Joseph went up to bury his father; and with him went up all the servants of Pharaoh, and the elders of his house, and all the nobles of the land of Egypt, and the whole house of Joseph, and his brethren, and all his father's household, and his kinsmen; only their sheep and oxen did they leave in the land of Goshen. And chariots and horsemen went up with him, and it was a very great host. And they came unto the threshing-floor of Atad, which is beyond Jordan; and there they lamented him with a great and very sore lamentation; and he made mourning for his father seven days. And when the inhabitants of the land of Canaan saw the mourning at the threshing-floor of Atad, they said: This is a great mourning unto the Egyptians; therefore the name of that place was called "Mourning of Egypt," which is beyond Jordan. And his sons did unto him even as he commanded them; for his sons carried him into the land of Canaan, and buried him in the double cave which Abraham had acquired for the possession of a burial place from Ephron the Hittite, over against Mamre.

And Joseph returned into Egypt, he and his brethren, and all that had gone up with him to bury his father. And when Joseph's brethren saw that their father was dead, they said: Lest Joseph remember the evil we did against him, and requite us with retribution for all the evils which we showed unto him! And they drew near unto Joseph, saying: Thy father bound us by oath before his decease, saying: Thus shall ye say unto Joseph: Forgive them their injustice and their sin, for they showed evil unto thee; and now receive the injustice of the servants of the God of thy father. And Joseph wept while they spoke unto him. And they came unto him and said: Behold, we are thy servants. And Joseph said unto them: Fear not, for I am God's. Ye devised evil against me, but God devised good concerning me, that it might be brought to pass as it is this day, that many people should be nourished. And he said unto them: Fear not; I will nourish you and your households. And he comforted them, and spoke unto their heart.

And Joseph dwelt in Egypt, he and his brethren, and the whole household of his father; and Joseph lived one hundred and ten years. And Joseph saw the children of Ephraim unto the third generation; and the sons of Machir the son of Manasseh were born upon Joseph's knees. And Joseph spoke unto his brethren, saying: I die; but God will surely visit you with a visitation, and bring you out of this land unto the land which He swore unto our fathers, Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob. And Joseph bound the sons of Israel by oath, saying: In the visitation [continued on next leaf] wherewith God shall visit you, ye shall carry up my bones hence with you. And Joseph died, being an hundred and ten years old; and they buried him in a coffin in Egypt.

*Prokeimenon. Psalm 124 (LXX).[^9] Tone 4:*
They that trust in the Lord shall be as Mount Sion: he that dwells in Jerusalem shall never be shaken.
*Stichos: For the Lord will not leave the rod of sinners upon the lot of the righteous.*

*Reading from the Proverbs (31:8–31 [LXX]):*[^10]
My son, open thy mouth with the word of God, and judge all things soundly. Open thy mouth, and judge righteously, and plead the cause of the poor and weak. Who can find a valiant woman? For such an one is more precious than costly stones. The heart of her husband trusts boldly in her; such an one shall not lack good spoils. For she works good for her husband all her life, and not evil. Getting wool and flax throughout the year, she has wrought what is useful with her hands. She has become like a merchant ship trading from afar, gathering her wealth. She rises while it is yet night, and gives food to her household, and appointed tasks to her maidservants. Having surveyed a field, she bought it; and from the fruit of her hands she planted a possession. Having girded her loins with strength, she strengthened her arms for work. She tasted that working is good, and her lamp goes not out the whole night. She stretches forth her arms unto profitable things, and her hands she applies unto the spindle. Again she opens her hands to the needy, and reaches forth fruit to the poor. Her husband is not anxious concerning those in his house when he lingers elsewhere, for all that are with her are clothed. Double garments has she made for her husband, and of fine purple and scarlet are her own vesture. Her husband is renowned in the gates, whenever he sits in council with the elders, the inhabitants of the land. Linen sheets has she made, and sold them to the Phoenicians, and girdles unto the Canaanites. Strength and comeliness has she put on, and rejoices in the days to come. She opens her mouth with discretion and lawfully, and order has she appointed for her tongue. Strict are the ways of her household, and the bread of idleness she has not eaten. Her children have risen up and enriched her, and her husband has praised her: "Many daughters have wrought power, many have acquired wealth; but thou hast excelled and surpassed them all!" False charms and vain beauty of women are not in thee; for a prudent woman is blessed, and let her praise the fear of the Lord. Give her of the fruit of her lips, and let her husband be praised in the gates.

*And the remainder of the Presanctified Liturgy.*

*Rubric: But where the Presanctified Liturgy is not celebrated, we conclude the Hours in this manner:*
*After the Prayer, "Forgive, remit:" and after the concluding metanias, we recite the Trisagion; after "Our Father:" "Lord, have mercy" (12 times). Then the Prayer, "All-Holy Trinity:" and we chant: "Blessed be the name of the Lord," (3 times), and Psalm 33 (LXX):[^11] "I will bless the Lord at all times:" then "It is truly meet:" and the Dismissal.*
*And thereafter we begin Vespers without metanias: "Come, let us worship" (3 times). And Psalm 103 (LXX):[^12] "Bless the Lord, O my soul:" then the appointed Kathisma. And at "Lord, I have cried," we chant the Stichera to the Righteous Lazarus on 6, Tone 6: "Lord, wishing to behold the tomb of Lazarus:" Glory: Now and ever: Tone 8: "Having completed the forty days that bring profit to our souls:" Then: "O Joyful Light" [continued on next leaf]* "O Joyful Light," without an entrance; the Prokeimenon and the readings. At the Aposticha, the Idiomela: "Having completed the forty days that bring profit to our souls" (twice), and the Martyrikon (once). And we recite the Stichoi: "Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes:"[^13] Glory: Now and ever: "Standing before the tomb of Lazarus:" written as a Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried." Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart." The Trisagion; after "Our Father:" Troparion: "Giving us before Thy Passion an assurance of the general resurrection:"[^14] Then the Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy:" Thereafter, the Reader's prayer, and the Dismissal.

*Markian Rubric: Be it known, that from this day forward, we chant neither Martyrika, nor Theotokia, nor from the Octoechos, until the Saturday of Saint Thomas.*[^15]

*Typikon Chapter 3: It is necessary to know, that if the Feast of the Annunciation occur on Lazarus Saturday:*[^16]
*On Friday Evening: We do not chant the Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," the Idiomelon Stichera of the day, 6: the lesser "Having completed the forty days that bring profit to our souls;" the Martyrikon is not chanted. Then the Feast, 3: "The Premundane Counsel;" and to Saint Lazarus, 5, Tone 8: "Lord, wishing to behold the tomb of Lazarus:" Glory: Tone 8: the great "Having completed the forty days that bring profit to our souls." Now and ever: of the Feast, Tone 6: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day, and the readings of the day; then the 3 readings of the Feast; and the remainder of the Presanctified Liturgy. After "Let my prayer be set forth," 3 metanias; and after "Now the heavenly powers," 3 metanias; and at "Blessed be the name of the Lord," 3 metanias.*
*At the Trapeza: We eat cooked food with oil, and drink wine.*

*At the sixth hour of the night (around midnight), they strike the great bell (or sufficiently upon the wooden klepalo). And we assemble in church and chant Great Compline. And after the Doxology ("Glory to God in the highest"), the Procession into the Narthex, and we chant the Idiomela of the Feast, 3, Tone 1. Glory: Now and ever: Tone 2: "Gabriel proclaims the good tidings." And at the conclusion, we re-enter the church. At the Aposticha: We chant the Idiomela, Tone 1. And we say the Stichoi: "Proclaim the good tidings from day to day,"[^17] Stichos 2: "Sing unto the Lord a new song."[^18] Tone 4: "Today is joy." Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart." After "Our Father:" Troparion of the Feast (3 times), and the Blessing of Loaves (Artoklasia); and a reading for the Feast.*

*At Matins: "God is the Lord;" Troparion of the Annunciation (twice); Glory: "Giving us an assurance of the general resurrection;" Now and ever: of the Annunciation. After the Kathismata: Sessional Hymns of the Feast, and a reading together with the righteous Lazarus. Polyeleos, and the Stichera of the Feast, and a reading for the Feast. Anabathmoi: the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon and Gospel of the Feast; "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ;" Psalm 50 (LXX);[^19] Sticheron of the Feast. Canons: of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6, and to Saint Lazarus both Canons on 8; likewise also the Four-Ode Canon (Tetraodion). Katavasia of the Canon of Lazarus: the Heirmos: "Having traversed the water:" both choirs together. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Sessional Hymn to Lazarus; Glory: Now and ever: of the Feast; reading for the Feast: "Again the joy of the Annunciation." After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast. At Ode 9: we do not chant "More honorable than the cherubim;" weekday Heirmoi are not chanted until Thomas Week. Then: "Holy is the Lord our God," in Tone 1. Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory: Now and ever: to Lazarus. At the Praises: Stichera of the Feast, 3, and to Saint Lazarus, 3: both on 6, with 8 stichoi. Stichos 1: "Arise, O Lord my God;"[^20] Stichos 2: "I will confess Thee, O Lord."[^21] Glory: to Lazarus, Tone 2: "A great and marvelous wonder;" Now and ever: of the Feast, the same Tone: "The mystery hidden from all ages." Great Doxology. Troparion of the Feast; Glory: Now and ever: "Giving us an assurance of the general resurrection." Litany, catechism, and Dismissal. First Hour in the narthex: Troparion of the Feast; Glory: "Giving us an assurance of the general resurrection;" Now and ever: Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion and after "Our Father:" Kontakion of the Feast. Thus we say the Troparia and at the other Hours; but we alternate the Kontakia. At the second hour of the day (8:00 AM), make the procession outside the Monastery; and having returned, chant the Hours. At the Divine Liturgy: the Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 3 on 4, and of Lazarus, Ode 6 on 4. After the Little Entrance: Troparion of the Feast, and to Lazarus; Glory: Kontakion to Lazarus; Now and ever: Kontakion of the Feast. In place of the Trisagion: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ."[^22] Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse: first of the Feast, then to Saint Lazarus. In place of "It is truly meet," we chant the Heirmos of the Annunciation: "As the living Ark of God."*

*On the Same Friday, at Small Compline:*
*We chant the Canon. The composition of the holy Father Kyr Andrew of Crete, the Hierosolymite.[^23] Tone 1:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us all sing a song of victory unto God, Who hath wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm, and saved Israel, for He hath been glorified!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!*

*Troparion:*
Having raised Lazarus who was four days dead, O my Savior, Thou didst transform corruption by Thy high arm, and didst show forth Thy power as the Mighty One.

*Troparion:*
Having called unto Lazarus, Thou didst straightway raise him from the tomb; but Hades below lamented bitterly, and groaned and [continued on next leaf] trembled before Thy power, O Savior!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst weep over Lazarus, O Lord, manifesting the incarnation of Thy dispensation; and being God by nature, Thou didst become man by nature for our sake.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst assuage the tears of Martha and Mary, O Lord, by raising Lazarus from the dead, O Savior, and by displaying Thy power over him that was dead as if newly slain.

*Troparion:*
According to the law of human nature Thou didst inquire, O Master: "Where hath Lazarus been laid?" displaying unto all, O Savior, the reality of Thy dispensation toward us.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst then shatter the bars of Hades, calling aloud unto Lazarus, and didst shake the enemy's dominion, compelling them even before the Cross to tremble before Thee, O only Savior!

*Troparion:*
Lazarus being bound and held fast by Hades, O Master, as God Thou didst anticipate and loose him that was bound from his bonds, for all things powerfully yield unto Thy command.

*Glory:*
Glorify the Father, the Son, and the Spirit, the undivided Trinity in unity of nature; and with the angels let us praise Her as the one uncreated God!

*Now and ever: Theotokion:*
Without mutation hast thou given birth, O Virgin Mother, unto the Creator of nature, Who through the good pleasure of the Father became what we are through the Holy Spirit, without alteration and without defilement.

*Ode 2. Heirmos:*
Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak, and will sing in praise of Christ, the Savior of the world and the only Lover of mankind![^24]

*Troparion:*
Glory to Thee Who didst but call aloud, and didst raise up from the tomb Thy friend Lazarus, who was dead for four days!

*Troparion:*
The lifeless corpse heard Thy voice, and endowed with soul, straightway arose from the dead: glory to Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Receiving the command of Thy life-bearing voice, my Savior, Lazarus arose from the tomb though he was stinking.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst weep over Thy friend, O my Savior, giving us assurance that Thou didst bear our nature, even while raising him up.

*Troparion:*
Hades trembled when it saw him that was bound in grave-bands running forth straightway at Thy voice unto life here on earth.

*Troparion:*
The Hebrew peoples were dismayed, O Savior, when Thou didst call aloud and raise by Thy word the stinking Lazarus.

*Troparion:*
The treasuries of Hades were shaken when Lazarus was quickened below at the voice of Him Who gives life.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The stone which the builders rejected hath become the head of the corner; this is the rock upon which Christ hath established His Church, which He redeemed from the nations.[^25]

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!*

*Troparion:*
A fearful and most glorious wonder: how the Creator of all, Who was not ignorant, inquired as one who knew not: "Where lies he over whom ye weep? Where hath Lazarus been buried?"—whom in a little while I will raise up alive unto you from the dead!

*Troparion:*
The stone which they who buried thee had rolled against thee, Jesus commanded to be taken away; and straightway He raised thee, crying aloud unto thee: "Lazarus, arise, come forth unto Me, that Hades may be terrified at thy voice!"

*Troparion:*
Martha and Mary cried aloud with weeping, O Lord: "Behold, he that was loved by Thee, four days dead, now stinks; if Thou hadst been here, Lazarus had not died!" But as Thou art present everywhere without separation, Thou didst straightway raise him up by calling unto him.

*Troparion:*
Having shed tears over Thy friend for the sake of the dispensation, Thou didst reveal the flesh which Thou tookest from us, having united Thyself unto our essence in reality and not in semblance, O Savior; and as God, the Lover of mankind, Thou didst straightway raise him up by calling unto him.

*Troparion:*
"Woe is me truly! Now am I undone!" cried Hades, and thus he shouted unto Death, saying: "Behold, the Nazarene hath shaken the nether regions, and cutting through my belly, He hath called aloud and raised up the breathless corpse!"

*Troparion:*
Where is the Hebrew madness? Where is their unbelief? How long will ye be strangers? How long will ye be deceivers? Behold the dead man coming forth at His voice, yet ye believe not in Christ's resurrection, having become sons of darkness!

*Glory...*
I know Thee as One of the Trinity, even though Thou hast taken flesh; as One I praise Thee, the incarnate Son Who sprang forth from God without seed, and Who with the Father and the Spirit art glorified as the only Son!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
A strange and dread vision of the dispensation, which the unerring Prophets foresaw, O Virgin who didst contain God: that thou shouldest conceive and in incorruption give birth unto God, yet remain pure after childbirth!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
The sun was lifted up, and the moon stood still in its order; Thou wast lifted up, O Long-suffering One, upon the Tree, and upon it Thou hast established Thy Church.[^26]

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shed tears over Lazarus, O Lord, showing that Thou art man; and Thou didst raise up the dead man, O Master, and didst show unto the peoples that Thou art the Son of God!

*Troparion:*
The breathless corpse heard Thy command: "Lazarus, come forth!" And he that was bound with grave-clothes arose and ran, leaping forth, showing forth Thy dominion, O Good One!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst comfort the tears of Martha and Mary, O Christ; having called Lazarus aloud with sovereign authority, Thou didst raise him up by Thy voice, and he bowed down in worship before Thee!

*Troparion:*
Having wept as man over Lazarus, Thou didst raise him up as God; Thou didst inquire: "Where hath the four-day dead man been buried?"—giving assurance, O Good One, of Thine incarnation!

*Troparion:*
Desiring to give assurance of the signs of Thy Passion and of Thy Cross, O Good One, Thou didst tear asunder the insatiable belly of Hades, and didst raise up the four-day dead man as God!

*Troparion:*
Who hath seen and who hath heard that a dead man already stinking hath arisen? Elijah indeed raised up, and Elisha, but not from a tomb, nor one that was four days dead![^27]

*Troparion:*
We sing of Thy might, O Christ, and we praise Thy Passion, O Christ; for by the one, as tender-hearted, Thou didst work marvels, while by the other, through the dispensation, Thou didst suffer as man!

*Troparion:*
Thou art God and man, truly in reality and in name; Thou didst come unto the tomb in the flesh, O Word, and didst raise up the four-day dead man as God!

*Troparion:*
The Hebrew peoples were dismayed, O Master, when they saw the dead Lazarus arise from the tomb at Thy voice, yet they remained without attaining unto faith through Thy marvels!

*Glory...*
Without beginning hast Thou shone forth from Thy Father, as One of the Trinity, O Savior; and in the fullness of time, from a Virgin's maidenly blood Thou didst come forth, having taken upon Thyself human flesh, O Thou Who art beyond all communion!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Seedless was the conception, O Virgin Mother, and birth without the passion of corruption; for God, having wrought marvels in both, emptied Himself that He might unite Himself unto us!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Grant us Thy peace, O Son of God, for besides Thee we know no other God; we name Thy Name, for Thou art the God of the living and of the dead.[^28]

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!*

*Troparion:*
Being the Life, O Lord, and the true Light, Thou didst call aloud and raise up Lazarus, for being mighty in all things Thou hast shown that Thou art God over the living and the dead!

*Troparion:*
Hades, which had received many souls, could not endure Thy steadfast command, O Jesus, but was terrified; and at Thy voice it gave up Lazarus, four days dead, alive and not dead!

*Troparion:*
Thou Who in ancient times didst unite dust unto spirit, breathing life into the clay by Thy Spirit, O Word: now also by Thy word hast Thou raised up Thy friend from corruption and the nethermost depths!

*Troparion:*
None could withstand Thy beckoning nod, O Lord; when Thou didst cry aloud unto the dead Lazarus, the breathless man straightway arose, walking with his feet bound in bands!

*Troparion:*
O the Jewish madness! O the darkening of the adversaries! Who hath ever seen a dead corpse arise from the tomb? Elijah raised the dead in ancient times, yet not from a tomb, nor one four days dead!

*Troparion:*
O invincible and long-suffering One, Who as God dost all things for our sake, and as man dost endure: make us all partakers of Thy Kingdom, by the prayers of Lazarus!

*Glory...*
O Trinity transcendent in origin, co-unoriginate, equal in honor: O Father Almighty, Son, and Holy Spirit; O Holy Unity in three Hypostases: save those descended from Adam who faithfully sing Thy praise!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thy most pure womb did He sanctify, O pure Maiden; He Who took flesh from thee, transcendent in divinity, adored in the Trinity: the Word from the Father, and God with the Spirit!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Thou hast cast me into the depths of the heart of the sea, and hast saved me, O Savior, from the bondage of death, and hast loosed the bonds of mine iniquities.[^29]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst inquire: "Where is he?"—knowing all things; yet Thou didst shed tears, O Savior, as man by nature, and by Thy command Thou didst raise me up who was dead!

*Troparion:*
"Thou didst call me aloud, O Savior, from nethermost Hades," Lazarus cries out unto Thee, the Destroyer of Hades, "and by Thy command Thou hast raised me up who was dead!"

*Troparion:*
Having torn asunder the insatiable belly of Hades, Thou hast snatched me away, O Savior, by Thy sovereign power, and by Thy command Thou hast raised me up who was dead!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast given soul unto the breathless form of my flesh, O Savior; Thou hast knit me together with bones and sinews,[^30] and by Thy command Thou hast raised me up who was dead!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast clothed me, O Savior, in an earthly body, and breathed life into me, and I beheld Thy light; and by Thy command Thou hast raised me up who was dead!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst bear our compound nature entirely, O Savior, yet Thou didst preserve pure and blameless the Mother from whom Thou didst come forth incarnate, being One of the Trinity!

*Glory...*
O Holy Trinity, I glorify Thy compassion, and with the Angels I sing the thrice-holy hymn: have mercy on the souls of us who praise Thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Into thy most pure womb entered the Word, and after childbirth He preserved it pure again, O Mother of God: a marvel truly most glorious!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Unto Thy youths in the furnace, O Savior, the fire neither touched nor harmed them; then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: "Blessed is the God of our fathers!"[^31]

*Troparion:*
Over the dead man Thou didst shed tears, O Savior, Lover of mankind, that Thou mightest show unto all peoples that being God, Thou didst appear as man for our sake; and willingly didst Thou shed tears, setting before us patterns of heartfelt love!

*Troparion:*
When Lazarus, four days dead, heard Thy voice below, O Savior, he arose and praised Thee, and rejoicing he cried aloud: "Thou art my God and my Creator; I worship Thee and glorify Him Who raised me up!"

*Troparion:*
"Even though I am encompassed with grave-bands," Lazarus cried from below unto the Deliverer, "yet in no wise shall I remain in the belly of Hades, if only Thou callest aloud unto me: 'Lazarus, come forth!'—for Thou art my Light and Life!"

*Troparion:*
"I implore thee, Lazarus," said Hades, "arise and go forth quickly from my bars; depart indeed, for it is better for me to lament bitterly over one man being snatched away, than over all whom I greedily swallowed in ancient times!"[^32]

*Troparion:*
"Why dost thou tarry, Lazarus? Come forth! Thy Friend stands calling thee; depart therefore, that I also may find relief; for since I devoured thee, my food hath become unto me an emetic!"

*Troparion:*
"Why dost thou not arise quickly, Lazarus?" cried Hades lamentably from below; "Why dost thou not straightway arise and hasten hence? Lest Christ, having raised thee, plunder my other captives as well!"

*Troparion:*
Thou wast marvelous, O Master Christ, having wrought many wonders at that time; for Thou didst open the eyes of the blind and the ears of the deaf by Thy word, and Thy friend Lazarus from among the dead Thou didst call aloud and raise up as God!

*Glory...*
Let us sing a hymn unto the Trinity, glorifying the unoriginate Father, the Son, and the simple Spirit: one single Essence which we praise in Trinity: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O Trinity!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
As One of the Trinity we praise Thee, O Christ, for having been incarnate of the Virgin without change, Thou didst contain all things in Thine humanity, without departing from the Father's essence, O Jesus, even though Thou hast been united unto us!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
All ye heavens, and ye waters that are above the heavens: bless, sing, and exalt the Lord unto ages of ages![^33]

*Troparion:*
The Creator and Sustainer of all came unto Bethany for Mary's sake, to raise up Lazarus!

*Troparion:*
The four-day dead, stinking, and bound with burial bands, was changed by the breath of life from breathlessness, when Thou didst call aloud, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
When the Jewish people beheld the dead man arisen at Thy voice, O Christ, they were filled with rage!

*Troparion:*
O ye Jews darkened concerning the Light, why do ye not believe in the resurrection of Lazarus, which is the work of God?

*Troparion:*
Let Zion rejoice, and let it sing unto the Giver of life, Who by His word hath raised up Lazarus from the tomb!

*Troparion:*
The heavenly hosts and the race of mortals sang Thy praise, my Savior, when Thou didst raise up Lazarus!

*Glory...*
With the Father and the Son I glorify the Spirit, and singing I ceaselessly cry aloud: O Thrice-radiant Trinity, glory to Thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
We bless and worship Thee Who wast born of the Virgin, without separating from the Throne of Thy holy glory!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
He hath shown strength with His arm; He hath put down the mighty from their seats, and exalted the humble, the God of Israel; wherein the Dayspring from on high hath visited us, and guided our feet into the way of peace.[^34]

*Troparion:*
Let Zion sing of the marvel of the vision; for in her the Creator wept, according to the law of the nature of the flesh when raising Lazarus; and turning Martha's tears and Mary's weeping into joy, He raised up the dead man!

*Troparion:*
Giving assurance of Thy resurrection, O Word, Thou didst call Lazarus from the tomb, and didst raise him up as God, that Thou mightest show unto the peoples that Thou art truly both God and man in one, and that Thou hast raised up the temple of Thy Body!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shake the gates and iron bars, Thou didst terrify Hades with Thy voice, and Death likewise was dismayed with him, when they beheld Lazarus, their single captive, given soul by Thy voice and risen, O Savior!

*Troparion:*
All were dismayed when they saw Thee shedding tears, O Savior, over the dead Lazarus, and moved unto utterance of how Thou didst love him; straightway therefore Thou didst call him aloud, and the breathless man arose, casting off corruption at Thy command!

*Troparion:*
The doors were shaken, the bars were shattered, the bonds of the dead man were loosed at the voice of Christ's power; and Hades groaned bitterly, weeping and crying aloud unto his minions: "Woe is me! What and whence is this voice that gives life unto the dead?"

*Troparion:*
"Arise thence, give ear unto the voice! For thy Friend calls thee forth; He is the One Who previously raised the dead. Elijah raised the dead, and Elisha together with him; but they acted through prayers and supplications!"

*Troparion:*
"And whence comes this Man unto me Who commands the dead? For by a command He said: 'Lazarus, come forth!'—and all my strength is confounded as by God; Lazarus leaves me, and comes forth loosed from the tomb; the belly of the Nazarene's foes cannot hold him!"

*Troparion:*
We sing unto Thee, O Word, Thine invincible power; for with bones and sinews Thou didst raise up the dead man by Thy word, as Creator of all; and Thou didst raise him up, O Savior, from the nethermost depths, even as Thou didst raise the widow's son from the bier before his mother![^35]

*Glory...*
All-Holy Trinity, unoriginate Father and God, co-unoriginate Son and Divine Word, Good Comforter and Holy Spirit of God: O single and thrice-radiant Light, essential Essence, One God and Lord: have mercy on the world!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Jesus Who hast made all things in wisdom, and didst clothe Thyself completely with me from the Virgin, yet ever abidest wholly in the bosom of the Father: having sent down Thy Holy Spirit upon Thy flock as God, visit and preserve us!

*On Saturday of the Holy and Righteous Lazarus.*
*At the Midnight Office:*
After *Blessed is our God:* and the Creed (*I believe in one God:*), and after the first Trisagion (*Our Father:*), we chant the Troparion: *By raising Lazarus from the dead...* (*Ѡ́бщее въскресе́нїе...*). After the final Trisagion and *Our Father:*, the Kontakion: *Christ the Joy of all...* (*Всѣ́хъ ра́дость Хрїсто́съ...*). *Lord, have mercy* (12 times), and the Dismissal. But the prayer *Remember, O Lord...* (*Помянѝ Го́споди...*) we do not say.[^36]

## Lazarus Saturday: Matins

*At Matins:*
*At "God is the Lord," the Troparion in Tone 1 (three times):*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
By raising Lazarus from the dead before Thy Passion, Thou didst confirm the universal resurrection, O Christ God! Therefore, like the children, we also carry the symbols of victory and cry aloud unto Thee, the Vanquisher of Death: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He Who comes in the Name of the Lord![^37]

*Then the Psalter reading (Kathisma 16): "The Lord said unto my Lord..." (Psalm 109 LXX).*[^38]

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the stone was sealed" (Ка́мени зна́менану):*[^39]
Having pitied the tears of Martha and Mary, Thou didst command the stone to be rolled away from the tomb, O Christ God; and calling aloud, Thou didst raise up the dead man, confirming the resurrection of the world in Thyself, O Giver of life: Glory to Thy power, O Savior; glory to Thine authority; glory unto Him Who by His word established all things!
*Glory... Both now... The same.*

*Then we chant the "Blameless" [Psalm 118], and we sing the Resurrectional Evlogitaria: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord... The angelic assembly was astonished..." and the rest as is customary on Sunday. And at their completion:*[^40]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginning with the Father" (Събезнача́лное сло́во):*[^41]
O Fountain of wisdom and foreknowledge, having arrived in Bethany, Thou didst inquire of those with Martha, saying: "Where have ye laid My friend Lazarus?"—for whose sake, having shed tears in Thy tender compassion, Thou didst raise up the four-day dead man by Thy voice, O Lover of mankind and Merciful One, as Giver of life and Lord!
*Glory... Both now... The same.*

*Then we say: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50.*
*The Gospel is not read, except in a temple dedicated to Saint Lazarus. We read the homiletic explanations from the Gospel of John for this day: Homilies 63 and 64.*[^42] *Then the Canons, up to Ode 6; for from that point the Four-Ode Canon begins.*

*The First Canon with its Heirmos on 8; and the Second on 6. A composition of Lord Theophanes.*[^43]
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, a song of victory, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparion:*
By Thy Divine nod Thou hast raised up the dead Lazarus, as Thou art the Creator and the Treasury of life, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
By Thy word, O Immortal One, Thou didst call aloud Lazarus, four days dead, destroying with might the dark kingdom of Hades!

*Troparion:*
Unto all hast Thou shown the knowledge of Thy transcendent Godhead, O Master, by raising from the dead the four-day Lazarus!

*Troparion:*
Today Bethany heralds beforehand the Resurrection of Christ the Giver of life, dancing in triumph at the raising of Lazarus!

*The Second Canon. A composition of John the Monk.*[^44]
*Tone 8, Heirmos: "Crossing the water as on dry land" (Во́дꙋ проше́дъ):*
*Troparion:*
Having originally brought forth all creation from non-existence, and knowing the secrets of hearts, Thou didst foretell unto the Disciples, as Master, the falling asleep of Lazarus!

*Troparion:*
Having shared in the nature of man through the Virgin, O Christ, Thou didst inquire as man to learn where Lazarus was buried, though as God Thou wast not ignorant of where he lay!

*Glory...*
Giving assurance of Thy resurrection in truth, O Word, Thou didst raise up Thy friend from the tomb as from sleep, even though he was already stinking, being four days dead!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The ranks of Angels and mortals ceaselessly bless thee, O unwedded Mother; for Thou didst bear in thine arms thine own Creator as an infant!

*Katavasia: Crossing the water as on dry land, and fleeing from the evils of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those that run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the Light of those in darkness, and my spirit sings praise unto Thee!

*Troparion:*
Displaying Thy two operations, Thou hast shown the duality of Thy natures, O Savior; for Thou art both God and man!

*Troparion:*
Being an abyss of understanding, Thou dost inquire: "Where hath the dead man been laid?"—desiring to raise him that lay there, O Giver of life!

*Glory...*
Inquiring concerning places as one Who had become man, Thou wast circumscribed; yet Thou fillest all things as uncircumscribed God!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou hast raised up Lazarus by Thy Divine voice; raise up me also, I pray Thee, who am dead through many transgressions!

*Another Heirmos: "O Lord, the Architect of the vault of heaven" (Нб҃сно́мꙋ крꙋ́гꙋ):*
*Troparion:*
Standing at the tomb, O Wonderworking Lord, in Bethany Thou didst shed tears over Lazarus, confirming Thy flesh which Thou didst assume according to the law of nature, O Jesus my God!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst straightway soothe Mary's weeping, O Savior, and Thy sovereign authority was shown unto Martha; for Thou art the Resurrection, Thou art the Life, as Thou in truth didst say, for Thou art Lord of all!

*Glory...*
Thy friend Lazarus, wrapped in grave-bands, O Lord, Thou didst snatch away from dark Hades by Thy word, shattering the keys and the kingdoms of all-powerful Death!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou didst dwell bodily in the Virgin, O Lord, appearing unto mortals as it was fitting for Thee to be seen; and Thou hast shown her to be truly the Mother of God and the helper of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Katavasia:*
*O Lord, the Architect of the vault of heaven and Builder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O Summit of desires and Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!*[^45]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^46]
The sisters of Lazarus stood together before Christ, and weeping bitterly and lamenting for him, they said unto Him: "Lazarus is dead!" But He, as God not being ignorant of his burial, inquired as man: "Where have ye laid him?" And approaching the tomb, He called aloud the four-day Lazarus; and they lovingly bowed down in worship before Him Who called him forth!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^47]
Foreknowing all things as Creator, in Bethany Thou didst foretell unto Thy Disciples: "Our friend Lazarus hath fallen asleep today!" And knowing, Thou didst inquire, saying: "Where have ye laid him?" And Thou didst pray unto the Father, having wept as man: therefore, having called aloud him whom Thou lovedst, Thou didst raise up from Hades the four-day Lazarus, O Lord! Therefore with faith we cry unto Thee: Receive, O Christ God, those who dare to offer praise, and make all worthy of Thy glory!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.[^48]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst not require any helper, O Christ; but fulfilling Thine ineffable dispensation, Thou didst readily raise up the dead man, four days in the tomb, O Almighty One!

*Troparion:*
The Word, co-eternal with the Father, and God previously unseen, now makes supplication as man, He Who receives the supplications of all!

*Glory...*
Thy voice destroyed all the power of death, O Savior, and the foundations of Hades bowed low before Thy Divine power!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Let us sing praise unto the Virgin, who remained a Virgin after childbirth, and gave birth unto Christ God, Who delivered the world from error!

*Another Heirmos: "Thou art my strength, O Lord" (Ты моѧ̀ крѣ́пость Гдⷭ҇и):*[^49]
*Troparion:*
As a Shepherd, O Christ, Thou didst snatch away man, who had already decomposed four days in the grave, O Creator of all, from the cruel and all-devouring wolf in truth; for as Almighty and Lord, Thou didst thereby reveal the universal glory of Thy three-day resurrection!

*Troparion:*
Beholding Thee, the Life, those with Martha cried aloud unto Thee, O Christ: "If Thou hadst been here, O Lord, the Enlightenment of all and Life, that man had not died, Lazarus had not been shown as dead!" But being the Life of the dead, O Lover of mankind, Thou dost turn their weeping into joy!

*Glory...*
The abysses fear Thee as the Fountain, O Lord; the springs and the seas serve Thee; the bars of Hades tremble before Thee, O Christ, and the keys are broken by Thy dominion, when Lazarus was raised from the dead by Thy voice, O Almighty Savior and Lover of mankind!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou art the boast of the faithful, O Unwedded One; thou art the protectress, thou art the haven and the wall of Christians: for unto thy Son thou dost offer supplications, O all-blameless one, and dost deliver from perils those who with faith and love honor thee as Mother of God!

*Katavasia:*
*Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing; Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, yet didst visit our poverty: therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!*

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "Why hast Thou cast me off" (Вскꙋ́ю мя̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ):*[^50]
*Troparion:*
Standing at Lazarus's tomb, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst summon him and grant him life; for being immortal Life unto all mortals, as God Thou didst thus prefigure the future resurrection!

*Troparion:*
Bound hand and foot, Lazarus walked—a wonder among wonders! For Christ was shown strengthening him who was restrained, unto Whose word all things serve as bondslaves, doing homage unto Him as God and Master!

*Troparion:*
O Christ Who didst raise up the dead and stinking Lazarus, four days in the tomb: raise up me also, who am now dead through my sins and laid in the grave and shadow of death, and as Thou art compassionate, deliver and save me!

*Heirmos: The same.*
*Troparion:*
Offering glory unto the Father, praying that Thou mightest not be thought an adversary of God, giving assurance unto the standing crowd, O Long-suffering One, and rendering thanksgiving unto Thy Father, by a command Thou dost raise up Lazarus!

*Troparion:*
O voice promised by God! O Divine power of Thy dominion, O Savior! Whereby Thou didst shatter the gates of Hades and all-devouring Death; snatch me also away from my passions, as Thou didst formerly snatch away Thy four-day friend Lazarus!

*Glory...*
By the prayers of Lazarus, of Martha, and of Mary, make us worthy to behold Thy Cross and Thy Passion, O Lord, and the radiant queen of days, Thy Resurrection, O Lover of mankind!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Possessing maternal boldness toward thy Son, O all-pure one, despise not Thy kindred care for us, we pray thee; for thee alone do we Christians present unto the Master as an acceptable reconciliation!

*Katavasia: Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O never-setting Light; and a strange darkness hath enveloped me, wretch that I am? But turn me back, and direct my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!*

*From here begin the Four-Ode Canons [Tetraodia]. The Heirmoi are sung twice, and the Troparia on four. By Lord Cosmas.*[^51]
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Thou didst cause Jonah alone to dwell in the sea-monster, O Lord; save me also, bound in the nets of the enemy, as Thou didst save him from corruption![^52]

*Troparion:*
Love led Thee unto Bethany, O Lord, unto Lazarus;

*Troparion:*
and him who was already stinking Thou didst raise up as God, and deliveredst from the bonds of Hades!

*Troparion:*
When Martha despairing saw Lazarus four days dead, Christ as God raised up him who was uncorrupted, and by His voice restored him unto life!

*Another Heirmos. By John the Monk. The same tone. "Cleanse me, O Savior" (Ѡ҆чи́сти мя̀ Сп҃се):*
*Troparion:*
Being true God, Thou knewest the falling asleep of Lazarus, and this Thou didst proclaim beforehand unto Thy Disciples, confirming, O Master, the boundless power of Thy Godhead!

*Troparion:*
Being circumscribed in the flesh, though uncircumscribed, arriving in Bethany as man, O Master, Thou weepest over Lazarus; yet as God, willing it, Thou dost raise the four-day dead!

*Katavasia:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and Thou hast heard me, O God of my salvation!*

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Въ вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^53]
Christ, the Joy, the Truth, the Light, the Life of all, and the Resurrection of the world, hath appeared unto those on earth in His goodness; and He hath become the prototype of the resurrection, granting Divine forgiveness unto all!

*Ikos:*[^54]
The Creator of all foretold unto His Disciples, saying: "Brethren and acquaintances, our friend hath fallen asleep!"—by these words foretelling and teaching that Thou knowest all things as Creator of all. "Let us go therefore, and behold a strange burial, and the weeping of Mary, and let us see the tomb of Lazarus; for there I intend to work a marvel, accomplishing the divine predetermination, and granting Divine forgiveness unto all!"

*Synaxarion on the Saturday of Righteous Lazarus.*
*Bless, Father, to read.*
*Verses:*
*Thou weepest, O Jesus: this is of human nature;*
*Thou revivest Thy friend: this is of Divine power.*[^55]

On this same day we celebrate the resurrection of the holy and righteous friend of Christ, Lazarus of the four days. He was a Hebrew by race, of the sect of the Pharisees, and the son, as is found recorded in some accounts, of Simon the Pharisee, originating from the village of Bethany.[^56] When our Lord Jesus Christ was walking upon the earth for the salvation of our race, Lazarus was thus joined unto Him in friendship. For because Christ often conversed with Simon—who also inquired earnestly of Him concerning the resurrection of the dead—and frequently visited his house, Lazarus came to be dearly loved by Him, and not he alone, but also his two sisters, Martha and Mary. Now as the saving Passion was drawing near, wherein it was needful that the mystery of the resurrection be more clearly assured, Jesus was staying beyond the Jordan, having previously raised the daughter of Jairus and the son of the widow from the dead. But His friend Lazarus, being seized by a grievous illness, died. Jesus therefore, though not being there, said unto the Disciples: "Lazarus our friend hath fallen asleep"; and pausing a little, He said: "Lazarus is dead." Leaving the Jordan, He came unto Bethany, having been sent for by his sisters. Now Bethany is distant from Jerusalem about fifteen stadia or furlongs. And the sisters of Lazarus met Him beforehand, saying: "If Thou hadst been here, our brother had not died; but even now, if Thou willest, Thou canst raise him up, for Thou art able." Jesus then inquired of the people: "Where have ye laid him?" And straightway all went before Him unto the tomb. And when the stone was taken away, Martha said: "Lord, by this time he stinks, for he hath been dead four days." Jesus therefore, having prayed and shed tears over him that lay there, cried out with a loud voice: "Lazarus, come forth!" And straightway he that was dead came forth; and being unbound, he went away unto his house.

This strange marvel stirred up the Jewish people unto envy, and they raged against Christ. Jesus therefore again withdrew and departed. And the chief priests took counsel to put Lazarus also to death, because many seeing him were believing in Christ. But he, perceiving their thoughts, fled unto the island of Cyprus. And dwelling there, he was later consecrated by the Apostles as Bishop of the City of Kition.[^57] Having lived worthily and in a manner pleasing unto God for thirty years after his revival, he died again, and was buried there, having wrought many wonders.

It is said that after his revival he would eat nothing except what was sweet; and that the All-Pure Mother of God, having prepared an omophorion with her own hands, bestowed it upon him.[^58] His precious and holy body was subsequently translated thence by the most wise Emperor Leo, in consequence of a certain divine vision, and reverently and sumptuously deposited in Constantinople, in the church built by him in honor of this saint, on the right hand as one enters the temple, in the wall opposite the holy altar; and even unto this day his precious body remains there, his relics working a certain ineffable sweet fragrance.[^59]

Now the celebration of his resurrection was appointed for the present day because our holy and God-bearing Fathers, and more especially the holy Apostles, desiring that after the Forty-Day Fast, through purification, we should behold the holy Passion of our Lord Jesus Christ—and seeing that they found this wonder to be the primary beginning and cause of the Jewish fury against Christ—therefore placed this supernatural marvel here. This wonder the Evangelist John alone records, the other Evangelists having omitted it because Lazarus was still alive and visible.[^60] It is also said that for this very reason the rest of the Gospel was written, and because the others had mentioned nothing concerning the unoriginate generation of Christ. For this was what was sought to be assured: that Christ was the Son of God and God, that He arose, and that there shall be a resurrection of the dead; which is assured above all through Lazarus. Lazarus related nothing concerning what was in Hades, either because it was not permitted him to see what was there, or because, having seen it, he was commanded to keep silence concerning it. Nevertheless, even now every man who dies is called a "Lazarus," and the burial garment is called a *lazaroma*, signifying the word concerning Lazarus, so that it may come in remembrance of the first Lazarus.[^61] For if he by the word of Christ arose and lived again, so also this man, even though he has died, shall arise at the last trumpet and live unto eternity.

By the prayers of Thy friend Lazarus, O Christ God, have mercy on us, always, now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!"[^62]

*Troparion:*
Having shed tears as man, O Merciful One, Thou didst raise up as God him that sat in the tomb, who celebrated deliverance from Hades; and Lazarus cried aloud: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!"

*Troparion:*
Bound with burial bands, having fled from the chasm of Hades and of darkness at the Master's word, Lazarus cried aloud: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!"

*Another Heirmos: "The Youths that came from Judea" (Ѿ І҆оуде́а доше́дшїи):*[^63]
*Troparion:*
Having wept over Thy friend, O Merciful One, Thou didst comfort the tears of Martha; and by Thy voluntary Passion Thou hast wiped away every tear from the face of Thy people: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
O Treasury of life, Thou didst raise up the dead man as from sleep, O Savior; and having torn asunder the belly of Hades by Thy word, Thou didst raise him up singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
The dead man, stinking and bound with burial bands, Thou didst raise up, O Master; raise up me also, who am bound with the cords of transgressions, as I sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Katavasia:*
*The Youths that came from Judea in Babylon of old, trampled upon the flame of the furnace by their faith in the Trinity, saying: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
When the musical instruments sounded together, and countless peoples bowed down before the image on Dura: the three youths, not yielding, sang and glorified the Lord unto all ages![^64]

*Troparion:*
As a Shepherd Thou didst seek out the lamb, and didst snatch him from the cruel and destructive wolf, renewing him that was decomposed, as he sings unto Thee: Praise and exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
As man Thou didst seek out the tomb; as Creator Thou didst raise up the dead man by Thy sovereign command, at which Hades was terrified, as he cries unto Thee: Praise and exalt Him unto ages!

*Another Heirmos: "The King of Heaven, Whom the hosts of angels praise" (Цр҃ѧ нбⷵнаго є҆го́же):*[^65]
*Troparion:*
Thou inquirest indeed as mortal, yet as God Thou hast raised up by Thy word the four-day dead: therefore we sing Thy praise and exalt Thee unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Unto Thee with pious mind, O Master, Mary brings myrrh as a rightful offering, singing praise unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou callest upon the Father indeed as man, yet as God Thou didst raise up Lazarus: therefore we sing Thy praise, O Christ, unto ages!

*Refrain:*
*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord; singing and exalting Him unto all ages!*

*Katavasia:*
*The King of Heaven, Whom the hosts of angels praise: hymn and exalt Him unto all ages!*

*"More honorable than the Cherubim" is not sung.*[^66]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Let us, O peoples, honor the pure and glorious Mother of God, who received the fire of the Godhead in her womb without being consumed; and with unceasing hymns let us magnify her.[^67]

*Troparion:*
Beholding the four-day dead man walking, the people marveled at the wonder, and cried aloud unto the Deliverer: Thee, O God, in hymns do we magnify!

*Troparion:*
Pre-assuring Thy glorious resurrection, O my Savior, Thou didst deliver from Hades Lazarus, four days dead, who magnifies Thee in hymns.

*Another Heirmos: "Thee, the true Mother of God" (Въи́стиннꙋ Бцⷣꙋ тя̀):*
*Troparion:*
Honoring Thine own Father and showing that Thou art not opposed to God, Thou makest prayer, O Christ, having raised the four-day dead by Thine own sovereign authority.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst raise Lazarus from the tomb of the four days, O Christ, showing unto all a trustworthy witness of Thy third-day resurrection.

*Troparion:*
Thou walkest and weepest, and Thou speakest, O my Savior, showing Thy human operation, and manifesting Thy divine power, Thou raisest Lazarus.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast ineffably wrought my salvation, O Master, my Savior, according to both of Thy two natures, by Thy sovereign will.

*Katavasia:*
*Confessing thee to be truly the Mother of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, with the heavenly hosts we magnify thee.*

*Then, in Tone 1: Holy is the Lord our God (thrice).*
*Exapostilarion, Tone 3, Podoben: "He hath visited us" (Посѣти́лъ ны є҆́стъ):*[^68]
By Thy word, O Word of God, Lazarus is now transformed, having returned again unto life; and with branches the people honor Thee, O Sovereign Lord, for by Thy death Thou shalt utterly destroy Hades.

*Glory... Both now...*
*Theotokion of the Exapostilarion:*
By Lazarus, O Christ, death is already destroyed; the weeping of Bethany is now turned unto Thee: let us all bring branches of victory unto Him!

*At the Praises:*
*Let every breath praise the Lord. We set four verses, taking two of the first and two of the other. Idiomela. Tone 1:*[^69]
*Verse: This glory shall be to all His saints.*

*Sticheron 1:*
Being the resurrection and the life for mankind, O Christ, Thou didst stand before the tomb of Lazarus, assuring us of Thy two natures, O Long-suffering One: that Thou camest as God and man from the pure Virgin. For as man Thou didst inquire where he was buried, and as God Thou didst raise the four-day dead by Thy life-bearing nod.

*Verse: Praise God in His saints; praise Him in the firmament of His power.*[^70]

*Sticheron 2:*
Having shaken the dominion of death, O Christ, Thou didst raise Lazarus, four days dead, from Hades before Thy death; and by one beloved friend Thou didst foretell unto all mankind deliverance from corruption. Therefore, worshipping Thine almighty power, we cry: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, have mercy on us!

*Verse: Praise Him for His mighty acts; praise Him according to the multitude of His greatness.*[^71]

*Sticheron 3:*
Mary and Martha said unto the Savior: "Lord, if Thou hadst been here, Lazarus had not died!" But Christ, the resurrection of the departed, raised him, already four days dead, from the deceased. Come, all ye faithful, let us worship Him Who comes in glory to save our souls!

*Verse: Praise Him with the sound of the trumpet; praise Him with the psaltery and harp.*[^72]

*Sticheron 4:*
Granting Thine own disciples an image of Thy Godhead, O Christ, Thou didst humble Thyself among the multitudes, desiring to remain hidden. Therefore, as God Who foresees all things, Thou didst foretell the death of Lazarus unto the Apostles; and appearing unto the people in Bethany, as one not knowing the tomb of Thy friend, Thou didst inquire to know it as man. But he who was raised by Thee after four days revealed Thy divine dominion: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee!

*Tone 4. Verse: Praise Him with timbrel and choir; praise Him with strings and the organ.*[^73]

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst raise Thy friend after four days, O Christ, and didst assuage the weeping of Martha and Mary, showing unto all that Thou art He that accomplishes all things by divine power, through Thy sovereign will; unto Whom the Cherubim unceasingly cry: "Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou, O God over all, glory to Thee!"

*Verse: Praise Him with high-sounding cymbals; praise Him with cymbals of jubilation. Let every breath praise the Lord.*[^74]

*Sticheron:*
Martha cried unto Mary: "The Teacher is come and calls for thee; approach!" And she ran and came to where the Lord was standing, and beholding Him, she cried out, falling down and worshipping, kissing Thy most pure feet and saying: "Lord, if Thou hadst been here, our brother had not died!"

*Tone 8. Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end.*[^75]

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst raise Lazarus, dead in Bethany, four days in the tomb: for Thou didst but appear at the sepulcher, and Thy voice became life unto the dead man; and Hades, groaning, released him in fear. A great marvel, O Most Merciful One: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart; I will speak of all Thy marvelous works.*[^76]

*Sticheron:*
Even as Thou didst say unto Martha, O Lord: "I am the resurrection," by deed Thou didst fulfill Thy word, calling Lazarus forth from Hades. And me also, who am dead through passions, as Thou art compassionate, raise up, I pray Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
*Doxastikon of the Praises:*
A great and most glorious wonder was accomplished today: for Christ, having called aloud, raised the four-day dead man from the tomb, and summoned His friend. Let us glorify Him as most glorious, that by the prayers of righteous Lazarus He may save our souls!

*Both now... In the same tone:*
*Theotokion of the Praises:*
Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos, for through Him Who became incarnate of thee, Hades is taken captive, Adam is recalled, the curse is annulled, and Eve is set free: death is put to death, and we are brought to life. Therefore singing hymns we cry aloud: Blessed is Christ our God, Who was thus well-pleased, glory to Thee!

*Then, the Great Doxology, the First Hour, and the usual dismissal.*

## Lazarus Saturday: Divine Liturgy

*At the Liturgy:*
*The Beatitudes: Odes 3 and 6, on 8. After the Little Entrance, the Troparion of the Feast. Glory... Both now... Kontakion. But instead of the Trisagion, we sing: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia."*[^77]
*Prokeimenon, Tone 3: The Lord is my light and my savior; whom shall I fear? Verse: The Lord is the protector of my life; of whom shall I be afraid?*
*The Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 333 (from the middle): Brethren, receiving a kingdom which cannot be moved...*[^78]

*Jesus Christ the same yesterday, and today, and unto ages of ages.*
*Alleluia, Tone 5: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty. Verse: For He established the universe which shall not be moved.*
*The Gospel according to Saint John, Pericope 39: At that time, a certain man was sick, named Lazarus...*[^79]
*Communion Hymn: Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise. Alleluia.*[^80]

*At the Meal: We refresh ourselves with cooked food with oil; and if we have caviar, three ounces are given to each brother, and we drink wine.*[^81]

*Notice: It should be known that if the temple is dedicated to Righteous Lazarus: after the customary kathisma, the Polyeleos; the Megalynarion for a Hierarch, and the Psalm: Hear this, all ye nations; then the Sessional Hymn. After Ode 3, also the Life of Saint Lazarus, written by Nikephoros Xanthopoulos. The first Antiphon of the Songs of Ascents of Tone 4. Prokeimenon: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints. Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord? Let every breath praise the Lord. The Gospel according to Saint John, Pericope 36: I am the door... Psalm 50. Sticheron, Tone 6: O Lord, Thy voice destroyed Hades... Two canons on 16, and the Katavasia of the sixth canon. After Ode 3, the Sessional Hymn of the Feast; after Ode 6, the Kontakion: Joy of all... and the Ikos; and the rest of the order as indicated.*[^82]

## Palm Sunday: Small Vespers

*Palm Sunday (Flower-Bearing Sunday).*
*On Saturday evening, the memory of Saint and Righteous Lazarus.*
*At Small Vespers: Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тре́тїй дне́сь въскр҃лъ є҆сѝ Хрⷭ҇тѐ):*[^83]

*Sticheron 1:*
Sitting upon a foal, O Christ, Thou didst accept a triumphal hymn from innocent babes, coming unto Thy Passion: Thou Who art praised by angels with thrice-holy hymns!

*Sticheron 2:*
Behold, thy King, O Zion, comes unto thee in meekness and bringing salvation, sitting upon a foal, to strike down the enemies among the nations in His strength: rejoice and be radiant, celebrating with palm branches![^84]

*Sticheron 3:*
Let us also, the faithful, clap our hands in unison, bringing branches of virtues unto Christ as children now, and let us spread out garments of divine deeds before Him, and honorably receive Him!

*Sticheron 4:*
Let us bring palm branches of virtues, O brethren, unto Christ God Who comes, willing for our sake to suffer in the flesh as man, that by the power of His Godhead He may grant dispassion unto all!

*Glory... Both now... In the same podoben:*
Borne upon the shoulders of the Cherubim as God, sitting upon a colt, coming willingly upon an ass for our sake: come, let us earnestly sing unto Him with palm branches!

*At the Aposticha, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^85]
*Sticheron 1:*
Be radiant, O new Zion, with palm branches; sing with the children: behold, thy King, bringing salvation, comes unto His Passion!

*Verse: Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise.*

*Sticheron 2:*
Rejoice, O Adam and Eve, with the Prophets: behold, the Lord comes to call you through His Passion!

*Verse: O Lord our Lord, how marvelous is Thy name in all the earth!*

*Sticheron 3:*
He Who on high with the Father and the Spirit accepts angelic singing, on earth becomes strangely poor, and accepts the children's hymn!

*Glory... Both now... In the same tone:*
I sing in fear of Thy fearful dispensation, crying "Hosanna": for Thou comest to save me, O blessed Lord!

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart... The Trisagion, and the rest according to custom. And the Dismissal Troparion, Tone 1: By raising Lazarus from the dead... (see fol. 10);*[^86][^87]
*and the other, Tone 4:*
*Troparion 2:*
Buried with Thee through baptism, O Christ our God, we have been made worthy of immortal life by Thy Resurrection; and singing praises we cry: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord![^88]

*And the dismissal takes place; and we enter into the Refectory.*

## Palm Sunday: Great Vespers

*At Great Vespers:*
*Blessed is the man: the entire first Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and sing the Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 6, each twice:*

*Sticheron 1:*
Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together, and taking up Thy Cross, we all say: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord! Hosanna in the highest!

*Sticheron 2:*
Having heaven for Thy throne and the earth for Thy footstool, the Word and co-eternal Son of God the Father humbled Himself today upon a brute colt, coming unto Bethany. Therefore the Hebrew children, holding branches in their hands, praised Him with their voice: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is the King of Israel that comes!

*Sticheron 3:*
Come also, all we of the new Israel, the Church drawn from among the nations, and let us cry aloud with the Prophet Zechariah: Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; proclaim aloud, O daughter of Jerusalem: for behold, thy King comes unto thee, meek and bringing salvation, sitting upon the foal of an ass, the offspring of a beast of burden! Keep festival, holding branches in children's hands, and give praise: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is the King of Israel that comes!

*Sticheron 4:*
Prefiguring for us Thy holy Resurrection, Thou didst raise the breathless dead man, Lazarus Thy friend, O Good One, by Thy command, from the four-day tomb while he was already decomposing. Therefore, riding upon a foal symbolically as upon a chariot, Thou didst tame the Gentiles, O Savior. For this cause beloved Israel offers praise from the mouths of sucklings and innocent infants, beholding Thee, O Christ, entering into the Holy City six days before Pascha!

*Sticheron 5:*
Six days before Pascha, Jesus came unto Bethany; and His disciples came unto Him, saying unto Him: "Lord, where wilt Thou that we prepare for Thee to eat the Passover?" But He sent them, saying: "Go into the village over against you, and ye shall find a man bearing an earthen vessel of water; follow him, and say unto the master of the house: The Teacher says, with thee I will keep the Passover with My disciples."

*Glory... Both now... The Sticheron: "Today the grace of the Holy Spirit..."*
*The Entrance. O Gladsome Light. Prokeimenon: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty. Verse: The Lord is robed in strength and hath girded Himself. Verse: For He hath established the world, which shall not be shaken. Verse 3: Holiness becomes Thine house, O Lord, unto length of days.*[^89]

*The Reading from Genesis (Chapter 49:1–2, 8–12):*[^90]
Jacob called his sons, and said: Gather yourselves together, that I may tell you what shall befall you in the last days. Gather yourselves together, and hear, ye sons of Jacob; give ear unto Israel your father. Judah, thee shall thy brethren praise; thy hands shall be upon the neck of thine enemies; thy father's children shall bow down before thee. Judah is a lion's whelp; from the tender branch, my son, thou art gone up. He stooped down, he couched as a lion, and as an old lion; who shall rouse him up? The scepter shall not depart from Judah, nor a lawgiver from between his feet, until Shiloh come, and unto Him shall the gathering of the people be: binding his foal unto the vine, and his ass's colt unto the choice vine; he washed his garments in wine, and his clothes in the blood of grapes. His eyes shall be red with wine, and his teeth white with milk.

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Zephaniah (Chapter 3:14–19):*[^91]
Thus says the Lord: Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; proclaim aloud, O daughter of Jerusalem; be glad and rejoice with all thy heart, O daughter of Jerusalem! The Lord hath taken away thy judgments, He hath delivered thee out of the hand of thine enemies. The King of Israel, even the Lord, is in the midst of thee; thou shalt not see evil any more. In that day it shall be said to Jerusalem: Fear thou not, O Zion; let not thine hands be slack. The Lord thy God in the midst of thee is mighty; He will save thee, He will rejoice over thee with joy; He will rest in His love, He will joy over thee with singing, as in the day of a solemn feast. And I will gather them that are sorrowful. Woe unto him that took reproach upon himself! Behold, at that time I will work in thee for My sake, says the Lord; and I will save her that is cast off, and gather her that was driven out; and I will get them praise and renown in every land.

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Zechariah (Chapter 9:9–15):*[^92]
Thus says the Lord: Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; shout, O daughter of Jerusalem: behold, thy King comes unto thee; He is just, and having salvation; lowly, and riding upon an ass, and upon a colt the foal of an ass. And He shall cut off the chariot from Ephraim, and the horse from Jerusalem, and the battle bow shall be cut off; and He shall speak peace unto the heathen; and His dominion shall be from sea even to sea, and from the river even to the ends of the earth. As for thee also, by the blood of thy covenant Thou hast sent forth thy prisoners out of the pit wherein is no water. Turn you to the stronghold, ye prisoners of hope: even today do I declare that I will render double unto thee. For I have bent Judah for Me, filled the bow with Ephraim, and raised up thy sons, O Zion, against thy sons, O Greece, and made thee as the sword of a mighty man. And the Lord shall be seen over them, and His arrow shall go forth as the lightning; and the Lord God Almighty shall blow the trumpet, and shall go with whirlwinds of the south. The Lord Almighty shall defend them!

*At the Litiya:*
*Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 1:*
*Sticheron 1:*
The All-Holy Spirit, Who taught the Apostles to speak with other strange tongues, commands also the innocent Hebrew children to cry aloud: "Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes, the King of Israel!"

*Sticheron 2:*
Co-unoriginate and co-eternal, the Son and Word of the Father, sitting upon an irrational colt, came today into the city of Jerusalem: He Whom the Cherubim cannot gaze upon with fear was praised by children with palm branches and boughs, mystically singing praise: "Hosanna in the highest! Hosanna to the Son of David, Who hast come to save all our race from deception!"

*Sticheron 3:*
Six days before Pascha, Thy voice was heard by Tartarus in the depths of Hades: therefore Thou didst raise Lazarus of the four days; and the Hebrew children cried aloud: "Hosanna unto our God, glory to Thee!"

*Tone 2:*
*Sticheron 4:*
As Thou wast entering into the Holy City, O Lord, sitting upon a foal, Thou didst hasten to come unto Thy Passion, that Thou mightest fulfill the Law and the Prophets. And the Hebrew children, foretelling the victory of the Resurrection, met Thee with branches and palms, saying: "Blessed art Thou, O Savior, have mercy on us!"

*Sticheron 5:*
Glory to Thee, O Christ, Who sittest on high upon the throne, and art now expected with Thy precious Cross! Therefore the daughter of Zion rejoices, the nations of the earth are glad, the children hold branches, the Disciples spread garments, and the whole universe has learned to cry unto Thee: "Blessed art Thou, O Savior, have mercy on us!"

*Glory... Both now... Tone 3:*
*Doxastikon of the Litiya:*
Six days before the occurrence of Pascha, Jesus came unto Bethany to find Lazarus, dead four days, and to proclaim the resurrection. And the women, Martha and Mary, sisters of Lazarus, met Him, crying aloud unto Him: "Lord, if Thou hadst been here, our brother had not died!" Then He said unto them: "Did I not tell you: he that believes in Me, though he were dead, yet shall he live? Show Me where ye have laid him." And the Creator of all cried unto him: "Lazarus, come forth!"

*And the usual Litiya prayers in the narthex.*
*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 8:*
*Sticheron 1:*
Rejoice and be glad, O city of Zion; adorn thyself and keep festival, and rejoice, O Church of God: for behold, thy King is come in righteousness, sitting upon a foal, praised by the children: "Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou Who hast a multitude of compassions, have mercy on us!"

*Verse: Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise.*[^93]

*Sticheron 2:*
The Savior came today into the city of Jerusalem to fulfill the Scripture; and all took palm branches in their hands, and spread garments before Him, knowing that He is our God, unto Whom the Cherubim unceasingly cry: "Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou Who hast a multitude of compassions, have mercy on us!"

*Verse: O Lord our Lord, how marvelous is Thy name in all the earth!*

*Sticheron 3:*
Borne upon the Cherubim and hymned by the Seraphim, Thou didst mount upon a foal in the manner of David, O Good One; and the children praised Thee in a manner worthy of God, while the Jews blasphemed lawlessly: the untamed seat of the colt prefigured the Gentiles, transformed from unbelief unto faith. Glory to Thee, O Christ, Who alone art merciful and the Lover of mankind!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
*Doxastikon of the Aposticha:*
Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together; and taking up Thy Cross, we all say: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord! Hosanna in the highest!

*Both now... In the same tone.*
*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart... The Trisagion, and the rest according to custom.*
*Then, before the blessing of the loaves, the Troparion, Tone 1:*
*By raising Lazarus from the dead... (twice); (see fol. 10).*[^95][^96]
*Glory... Both now... Troparion, Tone 4:*
*Buried with Thee through baptism... (see fol. 20).*[^97]
*And: Blessed be the name of the Lord (thrice), and Psalm 33: I will bless the Lord at all times.*
*And the Patristic Reading: the festal panegyric homily for the feast.*

*It should be known that if the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God coincides with Palm Sunday:*[^98]
*On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of Palm Sunday, 3, repeated to make 4, Tone 1, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Свы́шше зва́);*[^99] *Glory... Both now... for the Feast of the Annunciation, Tone 1: In the sixth month the Angel was sent. At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1, with the verses of Great Vespers; Glory... Both now... of Palm Sunday, Tone 6: Unto the Master, the true Son... Troparion of the Feast; Glory... Both now... By raising Lazarus from the dead... Little Litany, and Dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers: Kathisma: Blessed is the man. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: of the Feast of the Annunciation, 6, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Все оу҆пова́нїе):*[^100] *The eternal counsel... and of Palm Sunday on 4, in the same tone; Glory... of Palm Sunday, in the same tone: Today the grace... Both now... of the Feast, in the same tone: Gabriel was sent... Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and Readings of the Feast, 5, and of Palm Sunday, 3. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1: In the sixth month... and Idiomelon Stichera of Palm Sunday, Tone 1, in order; Glory... of Palm Sunday, Tone 3: Six days before the occurrence of Pascha;*[^94] *Both now... of the Feast, Tone 6: Gabriel proclaims the good tidings. At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Stichera of Palm Sunday, Tone 8, with their verses; Glory... of Palm Sunday, Tone 6: Today the grace... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 6: The mystery hidden from the ages. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion of the Feast, twice, and of Palm Sunday, once: By raising Lazarus from the dead... and the Reading of the Feast.*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory... By raising Lazarus from the dead... Both now... Buried with Thee through baptism... After the first Kathisma: Sessional Hymns of Palm Sunday; Glory... Both now... of the Feast; likewise the Reading. After the second Kathisma: Sessional Hymns of Palm Sunday; Glory... Both now... of the Feast, and the Reading. The 17th Kathisma (Amomos / The Blameless) is not sung. Then the Polyeleos; and after this, the Right Choir begins the Megalynarion of Palm Sunday, while the Left Choir begins the Megalynarion of the Annunciation. When the first choir sings, the other keeps silence. The Canonarch announces the psalm verse for Palm Sunday to the first choir, and the psalm verse for the Annunciation to the second choir. Sessional Hymn of Palm Sunday; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Songs of Ascents: the first Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon of Palm Sunday: Out of the mouth of babes... Let every breath praise the Lord. Gospel of Palm Sunday. Psalm 50. Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ is not said.*

*And the brethren kiss the Holy Gospel, and then approach and make reverence to the Superior, who distributes palm branches; and the Ecclesiarch distributes candles unto all. Sticheron of Palm Sunday, Tone 6: Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together...*
*Then the Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8, and of Palm Sunday with the Heirmos on 8. Katavasia: the Heirmoi of both Canons, each choir chanting its own Heirmos. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Sessional Hymn of Palm Sunday; Glory... Both now... Sessional Hymn of the Feast, and the Reading of the Feast: "Again joy..." After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast.
