At Ode 9, "More honorable than the Cherubim" is not sung, but only the Refrain of the Feast: "Proclaim, O earth, great gladness..." Holy is the Lord our God, in Tone 1. Exapostilarion of the Feast, in Tone 6. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, 3, and of Palm Sunday, 3; Glory... of the Feast, Tone 8: Let the heavens be glad... Both now... Tone 6: Six days before Pascha... The Great Doxology. Troparion: By raising Lazarus from the dead... Glory... Both now... of the Feast. We sing the Prokeimenon of the Feast: Proclaim the good tidings from day to day... and the Gospel of the Feast. Then the Litany, the Catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite; after which we chant the First Hour in the narthex, and the Dismissal.*
*At the appointed hour, we make the Procession outside the Monastery, and sing the idiomela of the Feast and of Palm Sunday. And returning, we chant the Hours. At the Hours, Troparion of the Feast; Glory... By raising Lazarus from the dead... Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. Trisagion; and after the Our Father, we alternate the Kontakia.*
*At the Divine Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 3 on 4, and of Palm Sunday, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, the Entrance Verse: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord! Troparion of the Feast, and of Palm Sunday: By raising Lazarus from the dead... Glory... Kontakion: Sitting on high upon the throne... Both now... Kontakion of the Feast. Then the Trisagion. Prokeimenon of Palm Sunday, and of the Feast. Epistle of the Feast, and of Palm Sunday. Alleluia, and Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Feast, and of Palm Sunday.*

## Palm Sunday: Matins

*On Palm Sunday, at Matins:*
*At "God is the Lord," both Troparia: By raising Lazarus from the dead... (twice); (see fol. 10).*
*Glory... Both now... Buried with Thee through baptism... (see fol. 21).*
*After the first Kathisma: Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^101]
With branches and with souls purified in mind, like the children let us faithfully praise Christ, crying aloud with a loud voice unto the Master: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, Who camest into the world to save Adam from the ancient curse; spiritually becoming the new Adam, O Lover of mankind, according to Thy good pleasure: Thou Who hast ordered all things for our benefit, O Word, glory to Thee!

*Glory... Both now... Another Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^102]
Having raised four-day Lazarus from the tomb, O Lord, Thou hast taught all to cry aloud unto Thee with palms and branches: Blessed art Thou that comest!

*After the second Kathisma: Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́ся):*
Over Thy friend Lazarus lying dead, O Christ, Thou sheddest secret tears, and raisest him up from among the deceased, wherein Thou didst humanely show compassion: and the multitude of babes, having learned of Thy coming, O Jesus, went forth today holding palms in their hands, crying "Hosanna" unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, for Thou hast come to save the world!

*Glory... Both now... Another Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени знамена́нꙋ):*[^103]
Praise in unison, ye peoples and nations: for the King of angels mounts now upon a foal, and goes forth, willing to strike down His enemies upon the Cross as one mighty! For this cause the children also, with palm branches, cry aloud the hymn: Glory to Thee, O Conqueror Who hast come; glory to Thee, O Savior Christ; glory to Thee, O blessed One, our only God!

*Then, the Polyeleos; and the Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повеле́нное):*[^104]

Borne upon the Cherubic throne, and sitting upon a foal for our sake, arriving unto His voluntary Passion, today He hears the children crying "Hosanna," and the multitudes shouting: "O Son of David, hasten to save those whom Thou hast fashioned, O blessed Jesus! For unto this art Thou come, that we might perceive Thy glory!"
*Glory... Both now... In the same tone.*

*And we read the Homily for the Feast: of Kyr Andrew of Jerusalem, or from John Chrysostom, Homilies 65 and 66.*[^105] *Then the Songs of Ascents: the first Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise. Verse: O Lord our Lord, how marvelous is Thy name in all the earth! Let every breath praise the Lord. The Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 83: At that time, when Jesus drew near unto Jerusalem...*[^106] *Psalm 50. Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ is not said.*

*Then the Priest, taking the censer, censes in the form of a cross, saying the Prayer for the Blessing of the Palms:*[^107]
**Deacon:** Let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.

**Priest:**
O Lord our God, Who sittest upon the Cherubim, Who didst stir up the power of Thine own Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, that He might save the world through His Cross, Burial, and Resurrection; Whom, when He came now into Jerusalem unto His voluntary Passion, the people that sat in darkness and the shadow of death met, bearing symbols of the resurrection—branches of trees and boughs of palms—prefiguring the resurrection: Do Thou Thyself, O Master, keep and preserve us also who, in imitation of them on this eve of the feast, carry palm boughs and branches of trees in our hands; that even as those multitudes and children offered "Hosanna" unto Thee, so we also in hymns and spiritual songs may attain unto the life-creating and third-day Resurrection, in the same Christ Jesus our Lord, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thy most holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*And while the brethren kiss the Holy Gospel, the Superior distributes the palm branches.*[^108]
*We chant Psalm 50. Glory... Tone 8:*
Today Christ enters into the town of Bethany, sitting upon a foal, destroying the most evil brute ignorance of the Gentiles, which formerly raged fiercely.
*Both now... the same.*
*Then: Have mercy on me, O God; and the Sticheron, Tone 6: "Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together..." (see fol. 23).*
*The Priest says the prayer: O God, save Thy people... Lord, have mercy (12 times); and after the exultation:*

*The Canon of Palm Sunday*
*Heirmos chanted twice, Troparia on 12. Katavasia: both choirs chant the Heirmos together. The composition of Lord Cosmas, bearing the acrostic: "Hosanna unto Christ the Blessed God."*[^109]
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The springs of the deep were seen, lacking moisture, and the uncovered foundations of the billowing sea were revealed: for by Thy nod Thou didst rebuke its tempest, and didst save Thy chosen people, as they sang a song of victory unto Thee, O Lord![^110]

*Troparion 1:*
Out of the mouth of innocent babes and sucklings Thou didst perfect praise for Thy servants, to destroy the adversary and avenge through the Passion of the Cross the fall of ancient Adam, raising him up through the Tree, as he sings a song of victory unto Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion 2:*
The Church of the righteous offers praise unto Thee, O Christ, Who dwellest in Zion; and in Thee, their Creator, Israel rejoices: and the mountains, the stone-hearted Gentiles who were contrary, are made glad before Thy face, singing a song of victory unto Thee, O Lord!
*Katavasia: The springs of the deep... both choirs together.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The hard rock, flowing when cleft at Thy command, poured forth drink for the people of Israel; but Thou, O Christ, art the Rock and Life, wherein the Church is established, crying aloud: "Hosanna! Blessed art Thou that comest!"[^111]

*Troparion 1:*
At Thy command, Hades released trembling Lazarus from among the dead, dead four days: for Thou art the Resurrection and Life, O Christ, wherein the Church is established, crying aloud: "Hosanna! Blessed art Thou that comest!"

*Troparion 2:*
Sing in a manner worthy of God in Zion, O ye people, and offer prayer unto Christ in Jerusalem! He Himself comes in glory with authority, wherein the Church is established, crying aloud: "Hosanna! Blessed art Thou that comest!"

*Hypakoe, Tone 8:*
Singing first with branches, the unbelieving Jews afterward seized Christ God with staves: but we, honoring Him unceasingly with unwavering faith as our Benefactor, ever cry aloud unto Him: Blessed art Thou that comest to recall Adam!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ our God Who comes openly shall come and shall not delay, from the shaded and dense mountain, the Virgin who bore Him without knowing man, as the Prophet said of old: therefore we all cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^112]

*Troparion 1:*
Let the mountains and all the hills drop down strong gladness upon Olivet, and let the trees of the forest leap for joy: praise Christ, ye nations, and all ye peoples singing praise unto Him, cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion 2:*
Reigning unto the ages in strength, the Lord comes clothed with majesty; the beauty and glory of His loveliness in Zion is beyond compare: therefore we all cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion 3:*
He Who measured heaven with a span and the earth with the palm of His hand, the Lord is come! For He hath chosen Zion, wherein He was well pleased to dwell and reign, and He loved the people who faithfully sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Get thee up into the mountain, O thou that bringest good tidings unto Zion, and lift up thy voice with strength, thou that preachest unto Jerusalem: glorious things are spoken of thee, O city of God: peace upon Israel, and salvation unto the nations![^113]

*Troparion 1:*
God Who sittest on high upon the Cherubim and beholdest the humble: He comes in glory with authority, and all things shall be filled with His divine praise: peace upon Israel, and salvation unto the nations!

*Troparion 2:*
O Zion, holy mountain of God, and Jerusalem, lift up thine eyes round about, and behold thy children gathered unto thee! For behold, they are come from afar to worship thy King: peace upon Israel, and salvation unto the nations!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The spirits of the righteous cried aloud in gladness: now is the New Covenant established for the world, and let the people be renewed by the sprinkling of divine blood![^114]

*Troparion 1:*
Receive the Kingdom of God, O Israel! And let him that abides in darkness behold a great light, and by the sprinkling of divine blood let the people be renewed![^115]

*Troparion 2:*
Release thy captives, O Zion, and bring them forth from the waterless pit of ignorance; and by the sprinkling of divine blood let the people be renewed![^116]

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
Borne upon a throne in heaven, and on earth upon a colt, O Christ God, Thou didst accept the praise of angels and the hymn of the children who cried out unto Thee: Blessed art Thou that comest to recall Adam![^117]

*Ikos:*
Since Thou didst bind Hades, O Immortal One, and put death to death, and raise up the world, the children with branches praised Thee, O Christ, as the Victor, crying unto Thee today: "Hosanna to the Son of David!" For no longer, He says, shall infants be slain for the sake of Mary's Infant; but for all, infants and elders, Thou alone art crucified. No longer shall the sword be wielded against us, for Thy side is pierced with a spear. Therefore rejoicing we say: Blessed art Thou that comest to recall Adam![^118]

*Synaxarion of Palm Sunday, the Master's Feast*[^119]
*Verses:*
*Seated upon a colt, He Who by His word stretched out the heavens,*
*Seeking to deliver mortals from their irrationality.*

On this manifest and most radiant day we celebrate the Feast of Palms, for the following reason: After the raising of Lazarus from the dead, many, seeing what had come to pass, believed in Christ. But the council of the Jewish Sanhedrin resolved to kill Lazarus himself also. Jesus therefore withdrew, giving opportunity for their malice to abate; but the Jews made a decree to kill Him at the feast of the Passover. A considerable time of withdrawal having elapsed, six days before the Passover, as it is written, Jesus came to Bethany, where Lazarus was who had died; and when a supper was prepared there, Lazarus sat at meat with Him, and his sister Mary poured myrrh upon the feet of Christ.[^120] On the morrow He sends His Disciples to bring an ass and a colt. He Who has heaven for His throne, seated upon the colt, enters into Jerusalem. And the Hebrew children themselves spread their garments before Him, and branches of palms, some cutting them down, others carrying them in their hands, crying aloud and sending forth unto Him: "Hosanna to the Son of David! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord, the King of Israel!"[^121]

This came to pass as the Holy Spirit moved their tongues unto the glory and high praise of Christ. By palms—that is, branches—they manifested the victory of Christ over death; for in Hebrew *vaïon* signifies a tender branch.[^122] For it was customary that victors in athletic contests or in wars were honored and escorted in triumphal processions with branches of evergreen trees. Mystically, the colt signified us, the people from among the Gentiles, upon whom Christ sat and rested, and was proclaimed Conqueror, Victor, and King of all the earth. Concerning this feast the Prophet Zechariah also spoke: "Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; behold, thy King comes unto thee, meek, and sitting upon an ass, and a colt the foal of an ass."[^123] And David again spoke concerning the children: "Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings Thou didst perfect praise."[^124] But as Christ entered, all Jerusalem was moved, and the crowds, incited by the chief priests into turmoil, sought to put Him to death.

He Himself, hiding, concealed Himself, and at other times spoke unto them in parables. Through Thine ineffable compassion, O Christ our God, make us victors over irrational passions, and vouchsafe us to behold Thy radiant victory over death and Thy joyful and life-bearing Resurrection, and have mercy on us, now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire, and didst slay the Chaldeans who unjustly ensnared the righteous: O Lord God of the fathers, praised above all, blessed art Thou![^125]

*Troparion 1:*
Bowing down in worship, the people, rejoicing with the Disciples, cried aloud with branches: "Hosanna to the Son of David!" O Lord God of the fathers, praised above all, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion 2:*
The innocent throng, an age yet in infancy, in a manner worthy of God praised Thee, O King of Israel and of Angels: O Lord God of the fathers, praised above all, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion 3:*
With branches the multitudes lauded Thee, O Christ, crying: "Blessed is the King of the ages Who comes!" O Lord God of the fathers, praised above all, blessed art Thou!
*Katavasia: Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire...*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Be glad, O Jerusalem; keep festival, ye that love Zion! For the Lord of hosts, reigning unto the ages, is come: let all the earth stand in awe before His face, and let it cry aloud: All ye works, praise the Lord![^126]

*Troparion 1:*
Seated upon a young colt, Christ thy King stands before thee, O Zion: for He is come to destroy the irrational deceit of idols, and to check the unrestrained impulse of all the nations, that they may sing: All ye works, praise the Lord!

*Troparion 2:*
Rejoice greatly, O Zion, for thy God Christ reigns unto the ages! He, as it is written, is meek and saving: our righteous Redeemer is come upon a colt, to destroy the equine fury of the enemies who cry not: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^127]

*Troparion 3:*
The transgressing assembly of the disobedient is deprived of divine gifts, for they have made the house of prayer of God a den of thieves, casting out from their heart the Redeemer, unto Whom we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^128]
*Katavasia: Be glad, O Jerusalem...*
*Magnify, O Jerusalem... The "More honorable" is not sung.*[^129]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us: make ready the feast, and rejoicing come, let us magnify Christ with palms and branches, crying in hymns: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord our Savior![^130]

*Troparion 1:*
Why rage ye, O nations? Scribes and priests, why do ye meditate on vain things, saying: "Who is this?" unto Whom children with palms and branches sing in hymns: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord our Savior![^131]

*Troparion 2:*
This is our God, unto Whom none is like: He has found out every way of righteousness and given it unto beloved Israel; and after these things He appeared and dwelt among men: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord our Savior![^132]

*Troparion 3:*
O ye disobedient, why have ye laid stumbling paths before your feet? Your feet are swift to shed the Master's blood: yet He shall surely rise to save those that cry: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord our Savior![^133]

*The Exapostilarion is not said; but immediately: Holy is the Lord our God (3 times), in Tone 4.*[^134]

*At the Praises: Let every breath praise the Lord (Psalm 150:6).*
*We set 6 verses, and chant these four idiomela Stichera, repeating them. Tone 4:*
*Verse: Praise God in His saints (Psalm 150:1 LXX).*
A multitude of people, O Lord, spread their garments upon the way; and others cut down branches from the trees and carried them. And they that went before and they that followed after cried out, saying: "Hosanna to the Son of David! Blessed art Thou that art come, and art coming again in the name of the Lord!" (Twice)

When Thou wast about to enter into the holy city, O Lord, the people carried branches from the gardens, praising Thee, the Master of all; and beholding Thee seated upon a colt, they beheld Thee as though upon the Cherubim: therefore they cried aloud thus: "Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou that art come, and art coming again in the name of the Lord!" (Twice)

Go forth, ye Gentiles, go forth also, ye peoples, and behold today the King of heaven, entering into Jerusalem upon a lowly colt as upon a high throne! O faithless and adulterous Jewish race, come and behold Him Whom Isaiah saw coming in the flesh for our sake![^135] How He betroths unto Himself as a chaste bride the new Zion, and casts off the condemned synagogue! As at an incorruptible and undefiled wedding, the undefiled and innocent children ran together, chanting praises; and singing with them, let us also cry aloud the angelic song: Hosanna in the highest, unto Him that has great mercy!

Fore-showing the common resurrection of all before Thy voluntary Passion, O Christ God, Thou didst raise dead Lazarus four days in the grave in Bethany by Thy sovereign power; and unto the blind Thou didst grant sight as the Giver of Light, O Savior; and Thou didst enter into the holy city with Thy Disciples, seated upon the colt of an ass, fulfilling the preaching of the Prophets as One borne upon the Cherubim: and the Hebrew children met Thee with branches and palms. Therefore we also, carrying olive branches and palms, cry unto Thee with thanksgiving: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*
Six days before the Passover, Jesus came to Bethany. And His Disciples came unto Him, saying unto Him: "Lord, where wilt Thou that we prepare for Thee to eat the Passover?" But He sent them, saying: "Go into the village over against you, and ye shall find a man bearing a pitcher of water: follow him, and say to the master of the house: The Teacher says: With thee will I keep the Passover with My Disciples."[^136]

*The Great Doxology. And the Troparion: "Confirming the common resurrection..." (see fol. 10).[^137] Then the Litanies. The First Hour. And the Catechesis of Saint Theodore the Studite. And the usual Dismissal. And there is a Litiya outside the monastery.*[^138]

## Palm Sunday: Divine Liturgy[^139]

*Antiphon 1. Psalm 114 (LXX):*
*Verse 1:* I am well pleased that the Lord will give ear to the voice of my prayer (Psalm 114:1 LXX).
*Refrain:* Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us!
*The other choir the same. Verse 2:*
The pangs of death surrounded me, the dangers of Hades found me (Psalm 114:3 LXX).
*Refrain:* Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us!
*Verse 3:*
I found affliction and sorrow, and I called upon the name of the Lord (Psalm 114:3b–4 LXX).
*Refrain:* Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us!
*Glory... Both now...*
Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us!

*Antiphon 2. Psalm 115 (LXX):*
*Verse 1:* I believed, therefore have I spoken; but I was humbled exceedingly (Psalm 115:1 LXX).
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst sit upon a colt, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia! (3 times)
*Verse 2:* What shall I render unto the Lord for all the things that He has rendered unto me? (Psalm 115:3 LXX).
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst sit upon a colt, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia! (3 times)
*Verse 3:* I will take the cup of salvation, and I will call upon the name of the Lord (Psalm 115:4 LXX).
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst sit upon a colt, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia! (3 times)
*Verse 4:* I will pay my vows unto the Lord in the presence of all His people (Psalm 115:5 LXX).
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst sit upon a colt, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!
*Glory... Both now...*
*Only-Begotten Son and Word of God...*

*Antiphon 3. Psalm 117 (LXX):*
*Verse 1:* Give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages! (Psalm 117:1 LXX).
*Troparion: Confirming the common resurrection...*
*Verse 2:* Let the house of Israel now say that He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages! (Psalm 117:2 LXX).
*Troparion: Confirming the common resurrection...*
*Verse 3:* Let the house of Aaron now say that He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages! (Psalm 117:3 LXX).
*Troparion: Confirming the common resurrection...*
*Verse 4:* Let all that fear the Lord now say that He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages! (Psalm 117:4 LXX).
*Troparion: Confirming the common resurrection...*

*The Little Entrance. Entrance Hymn:*
Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord: God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us (Psalm 117:26–27 LXX).
*Troparion: Confirming the common resurrection...*
*Glory... Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having been buried with Thee in baptism, O Christ our God, we were made worthy of immortal life through Thy Resurrection; and chanting in praise we cry: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord![^140]
*Both now... Kontakion, Tone 6:*
Borne upon a throne in heaven, and on earth upon a colt, O Christ God, Thou didst accept the praise of angels and the hymn of the children who cried out unto Thee: Blessed art Thou that comest to recall Adam!
*Then the Trisagion: Holy God...*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord (Psalm 117:26 LXX).
*Verse:* Give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages! (Psalm 117:1 LXX).
*Epistle to the Philippians, Pericope 247 [Philippians 4:4–9]:* Brethren, rejoice in the Lord always...
*Alleluia, Tone 1:* Sing unto the Lord a new song (Psalm 97:1 LXX).
*Verse:* All the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God (Psalm 97:3 LXX).
*Gospel according to Saint John, Pericope 41 [John 12:1–18]:* Six days before the Passover, Jesus came to Bethany...
*The Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom.*
*Communion Hymn:* Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord: God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us. Alleluia (Psalm 117:26–27 LXX).

*At the Trapeza: consolation for the brethren. For we eat fish and drink wine, giving thanks unto God.*[^141]

## Palm Sunday: Vespers[^142]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and chant these three idiomela Stichera, repeating them. Tone 8:*
Rejoice and be glad, O city of Zion! Be beautiful and celebrate, O Church of God! For behold, thy King is come in righteousness, seated upon a colt, praised by children: "Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou that hast a multitude of compassions, have mercy on us!" (Twice)

The Savior is come today into the city of Jerusalem, to fulfill the Scripture...

...to fulfill the Scripture: and all took palms into their hands, and spread garments before Him, knowing that He is our God, unto Whom the Cherubim cry unceasingly: "Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou that hast a multitude of compassions, have mercy on us!" (Twice)

Borne upon the Cherubim and hymned by the Seraphim, Thou didst ascend upon a colt in Davidic fashion, O Good One, and the children praised Thee in a manner worthy of God; but the Jews blasphemed unlawfully: the untamed seat of the colt prefigured the Gentiles, transformed from unbelief unto faith. Glory to Thee, O Christ, Who alone art merciful and the Lover of mankind! (Twice)
*Glory... the Sticheron: Rejoice and be glad... Both now... Borne upon the Cherubim...*

*Entrance. The usual Prokeimenon:*
*Behold now, bless ye the Lord, all ye servants of the Lord (Psalm 133:1 LXX).*
*Verse: Ye that stand in the house of the Lord, in the courts of the house of our God (Psalm 133:2 LXX).*

*At the Aposticha, idiomela Stichera. Tone 2:*
Passing from branches and palms as from a divine feast unto a divine feast, let us run, O ye faithful, unto the honored, saving celebration of the Passions of Christ; and let us behold Him enduring voluntary suffering for our sake: and giving thanks, let us sing unto Him a fitting hymn, crying tenderly: O Fount of tender-heartedness and Haven of salvation, O Lord, glory to Thee![^143]

*Tone 3. Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song (Psalm 95:1 LXX).*
It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God: He is the Judge of thoughts and intents of hearts.[^144] Let no one enter tempting the undefiled faith; but in meekness and fear let us draw near unto Christ, that we may receive mercy and find grace to help in time of need![^145]

*Tone 7. Verse: All the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God (Psalm 97:3 LXX).*
O deceitful and adulterous assembly, which kept not faith with its husband, why dost thou hold fast the Covenant of which thou wast not the heir? Why dost thou boast in the Father, having rejected the Son? Thou didst not receive the Prophets who preached the Son: be at least ashamed before thine own children, who cry thus: "Hosanna to the Son of David! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord!"[^146]

*Glory... the first Sticheron, Tone 2: Passing from branches and palms... Both now... Tone 3: It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God...*
*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart... And after Our Father... Troparion: Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos... And the rest as usual with Prostrations.*

*Be it known that "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos" and the rest are chanted with vocal inflection on all Sundays of the Holy Forty Days, as also on this present Sunday. On the remaining days following of Great Week they are not said.* [Mark this well][^147]

*It is necessary to know: If the Annunciation falls on Great Monday.*[^148]
*On Palm Sunday Evening, at Little Vespers: Sticheron of the feast, and Troparion. At Great Vespers: we sing "Blessed is the man..." the first Glory. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the feast on 6, and of Palm Sunday on 4. Glory... of Palm Sunday, Tone 6: "He Who has heaven for His throne..." Both now... of the feast, in the same Tone: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent..." Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Three Readings of the feast. At the Litiya: Stichera of the feast. Glory... Both now... of the feast, Tone 6: "Gabriel brings the good tidings..." At the Aposticha: the idiomela Stichera of the day in the Triodion with their verses. Glory... of Palm Sunday, Tone 2: "Passing from branches and palms..." Both now... of the feast, in the same Tone: "The mystery hidden from the ages..." At the Blessing of the Loaves: Troparion of the feast (3 times), and the reading of the feast.*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the feast (3 times). After the 1st Kathisma and after the 2nd: Sessional Hymns and Reading of the day. After the 3rd Kathisma: Sessional Hymn of the feast and Reading of the feast. Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymn of the feast and Reading. Songs of Ascents: 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon: "Proclaim from day to day the salvation of our God" (Psalm 95:2 LXX). "Let every breath..." Gospel of the feast. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the feast. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos on 6, and the Triode on 8. Katavasia: the Heirmos in the Triodion, both choirs together. And where there is no Triode, we chant the Canon of the feast with the Heirmos on 12. Katavasia of the feast. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the day, and Reading of the feast: "Joy again..." After Ode 6: Kontakion of the feast and Ikos, and Reading in the Prologue. At Ode 9: the "More honorable" is not sung. Exapostilarion of the feast. Glory... of the day. Both now... of the feast. At the Praises: Stichera of the feast on 4. Glory... of the feast, Tone 6: "The mystery hidden from the ages..." Both now... of the day, Tone 8: "Unto the withered fig tree..." At the Aposticha: idiomela of the day with their usual verses: "We were filled in the morning..." Verse 2: "And let the brightness..." Glory... of the day, Tone 8: "A second Eve..." Both now... of the feast, in the same Tone: "Let the heavens rejoice..." Troparion of the feast. Litany, and 3 great Prostrations. "Come, let us worship..." (3 times), and the First Hour. And at its conclusion, 3 great prostrations with the prayer; and the Catechesis of Saint Theodore the Studite, and the Dismissal. And the usual exit into the Narthex.*

*At the third hour of the day, the klepalo is sounded, and there is a Litiya outside the monastery. And having returned, we chant the Third Hour, and the Sixth with Kathisma and with Reading; and we read the Tetra-Evangelion. And in them are said the Troparion and Kontakion of the feast. Likewise also the Ninth Hour with all its order. And we chant the Beatitudes simply without singing, and the rest. After Our Father: Kontakion of the feast; "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), and 3 great prostrations, and the prayer: "O All-Holy Trinity..." and the Dismissal. And immediately: "Come, let us worship..." (3 times). And we begin Vespers from prostrations, and without Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," idiomela Stichera of the day, 4; and of the feast, 3; and to the Archangel, Prosomoia, 3. Glory... of the day, Tone 8: "Unto the withered fig tree..." Both now... of the feast, Tone 6: "The Archangel was sent..." Entrance with the Gospel. "O Gladsome Light..." Prokeimenon and Reading of the day, and of the feast (4 times). Then: "Let my prayer be set forth..." and 3 prostrations. Then the Little Litany, and the Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia of the feast. Gospel of the feast and of the day, and the rest. The Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom. Communion Hymn of the feast.*

*At the Trapeza we eat with oil and drink wine: but fish we do not eat at all, on whatever day the Annunciation happens to fall in Great Week.* [Mark this well][^149]

*At Compline we make no prostrations, save after "Glory to God in the highest," 3 prostrations; and at the Trisagion, 3 prostrations; and at the conclusion, 3 great prostrations and 12 small ones; and at the final Trisagion, 3 prostrations. And we chant the Triode of Lord Andrew of Crete.*[^150]

*It is necessary to know this also: that throughout all Passion Week there is no Apodosis of the Annunciation of the Theotokos, and we do not chant her Canon at Compline. Likewise also to the Archangel: we chant his Canon at Compline, except on Great Friday.* [Mark this well][^151]

*If the Annunciation of the All-Holy Theotokos falls on Great Monday, and if it is her temple or the temple of the Archangel Gabriel,*
*then we celebrate with an Apodosis. On Sunday evening, we celebrate according to the Typikon: likewise also on Monday. On Great Tuesday at Matins: Troparion: "Behold, the Bridegroom comes..." (3 times). After the 2nd Kathisma: Sessional Hymn in the Triodion. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos on 8; Heirmos twice, Troparia on 6, and to the Archangel on 6. Katavasia of the Theotokos. Where there is a Triode in the Triodion, we chant the Canon of the Theotokos with the Heirmos on 4, and to the Archangel on 2 (two for one), and in the Triodion on 8. Katavasia: in the Triodion the Heirmos, both choirs together. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the Theotokos and her Sessional Hymn. After Ode 6: Kontakion in the Triodion. If it is the temple of the Archangel Gabriel, then after Ode 3, Kontakion in the Triodion; after Ode 6, Kontakion to the Archangel. At Ode 9: the "More honorable" is not sung. Exapostilarion of the day. Glory... Both now... of the Theotokos. At the Praises: Stichera in the Triodion, 3; and to the Archangel, 3. Glory... in the Triodion. Both now... of the Theotokos. At the Aposticha: Stichera in the Triodion. Glory... in the Triodion. Both now... of the Theotokos. And the rest of the service of the day and of the Theotokos. Or if the Superior decides, let it be sung on the remaining days of this week.* [Mark this well]

*Be it clearly known that in this holy and great week we complete the Psalter once:*[^152]
*On Saturday at Vespers: 1 Kathisma. On Palm Sunday at Matins: 2 Kathismata. On Great Monday at Matins: 3 Kathismata. And at the Third Hour: 2 Kathismata; and 2 Kathismata [at the Sixth Hour]. At the Ninth Hour there is no Kathisma; and at "In my distress I cried..." (Psalm 119 LXX) at Vespers. On Great Tuesday at Matins: 3 Kathismata; and at the Hours: 2 Kathismata. On Great Wednesday: at Matins: 3 Kathismata; and at the Hours: 2 Kathismata. And we finish here. For at Compline and the Great [Services] the Psalter is not read. On Great Saturday: "Blessed are the undefiled..." (Psalm 118 LXX) at the Tomb.* [Mark this well] *Compline on the Same Palm Sunday*[^153]
*At Compline we chant the Triode of Great Monday, the composition of Lord Andrew of Crete. We do likewise on the remaining evenings of Great Week at Compline.*
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who broke wars with His arm, and led Israel through the Red Sea: let us sing unto Him as unto our Redeemer, for He is glorified![^154]

*Troparion 1:*
Let us, O ye faithful, imitate the chastity of Joseph, who honored human rational nature with understanding, having lived in clean preservation of active virtue![^155]

*Troparion 2:*
The failure to work good is likened unto the fig tree. Let us therefore turn away from this, that we wither not away as it did then—the proud assembly covering itself with leaves![^156]

*Troparion 3:*
Prefiguring goodness, Joseph is cast into a pit and sold by his kinsmen; the glorious one endures all things, truly in the likeness of Christ![^157]

*Troparion 4:*
Having fled the barrenness of the fig tree, brethren—the spiritual type—let us not wither away as it did then, when the Lover of mankind, returning, came unto it hungering!

*Troparion 5:*
Jesus, hastening to suffer voluntarily for the world, goes with His Disciples into the city of Jerusalem, unto the voluntary Passion which He came to suffer.

*Troparion 6:*
Cleaving unto the Lord Who hastens to suffer all things, let us be ready for spitting, for reproaches, and for humiliations, that through His most honored Passions we may be glorified together with Him, O ye faithful!

*Troparion 7:*
By His Passions He heals passions, He Who suffered for our sake; for voluntarily He brings about upon our human nature His life-bearing Passions, that we may be saved!

*Glory...*
I glorify three unoriginate Ones, I hymn three holy Ones, I preach three co-beginningless Ones in one essence: for one God is glorified in Father, Son, and Holy Spirit!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The rod of Moses and the rod of Aaron had a strange transformation and a dispensation surpassing the mind: but thy womb, O Mother of God, renews a new birth![^158]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Яви́лся есѝ днесь):*
Let not the condemnation of the fig tree overtake thee; but hasten to render good fruits of the heart, like clusters of grapes, unto thy Creator Christ, bringing them unto Him in repentance![^159]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who was manifested on the holy mountain, and in the fiery bush revealed the mystery of the Virgin unto Moses: praise ye the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages![^160]

*Troparion 1:*
Having adorned our life with chastity and preserved the faith in simplicity, let us seek righteous ways, that following with courage we may be crucified with Christ!

*Troparion 2:*
Having encountered the Egyptian woman as a second Eve, the Patriarch Joseph was not overcome unto an unfitting deed: but he stood like an adamant, unfallen from sinful passions![^161]

*Troparion 3:*
Walking through earthly paths, O my Savior, Thou didst hunger voluntarily, desiring the salvation of all: for this Thou didst hunger for—the conversion of those who were far from Thee!

The forefather, seeing from the tree that he was known to be naked and put to shame, took fig leaves and girded himself:[^162] for the assembly stripped of Christ was prefigured.

Prepare thyself, O my soul, before the departure; order thyself for that life, and unto Christ Who hastens to suffer for thy sake, that He may glorify thee: hasten to suffer with Him, and to be crucified with Him, and to die!

How shall death not be dismayed, O my Savior? How shall Hades not fear, seeing Thee, Who of Thy good pleasure dost hasten unto the Passion, and seeing the Righteous One come to suffer for the unrighteous?[^163]

The priests and Levites, having prepared an accursed agreement through envy, through deceit of betrayal handed over Christ unto death to Pilate.[^164]

Thine ewe-lamb and handmaid and Virgin, seeing Thee hastening unto the Passion and laying down Thy life for us, the Good Shepherd, wept for Thee with maternal grief![^165]

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord:*
As a Unity in essence I hymn Thee, as a Trinity in Persons I honor Thee: O Father, Son, and good Spirit: the unoriginate dominion of Thy Kingdom I glorify unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The Theotokos entreats Thee, O Christ; the choir of Thine Apostles entreats Thee: to grant Thy peace unto Thy world, and to bestow Thy compassions richly upon us unto the ages!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and supremely exalting Him unto the ages.*
*Heirmos: Unto Him Who was manifested on the mountain...*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Virginity is alien unto mothers, and childbearing is strange unto virgins: yet in thee, O Theotokos, both were accomplished. Therefore all the generations of the earth unceasingly magnify thee![^166]

*Troparion 1:*
Chastity is alien unto the ungodly, and transgression of the Law is strange unto the righteous. But great Joseph turned away from sin and embraced chastity, truly an icon and likeness of Christ!

*Troparion 2:*
Good order is alien unto lawless ones, and the knowledge of God is strange unto the faithless. But the Jews rejected this through lawlessness: therefore they alone brought upon themselves a curse, like the fig tree!

*Troparion 3:*
Hungering for the salvation of mankind, Christ my God, Who is the Bread of Life: when He beheld the fig tree of the deceitful one—the barren assembly overgrown with legalistic leaves—He cursed it![^167]

*Troparion 4:*
The rod of Moses of old was changed into a serpent, and the rod of Aaron turned into greenery and blossomed with leaves;[^168] but the barren and withered assembly of transgressors was transformed into a barren fig tree!

*Troparion 5:*
Prepare thy priests, O Judea; make ready thy hands for deicide! For behold, meek and silent He is come unto the Passion, our Lamb and Shepherd, Christ the King of Israel![^169]

*Troparion 6:*
Receive thy King, O Judea! For behold, He comes voluntarily unto the Passion, that He may suffer and save those who cry unceasingly: Blessed is He that comes to save all things by the Cross!

*Troparion 7:*
Thou hast turned the feasts of the Jews into mourning, as according to the prophecy;[^170] for thou hast shown thyself a deicidal city against Him Who turned the rock of old, even the flinty rock, into pools of water and fountains, as David sings![^171]

*Troparion 8:*
Thou didst curse the barrenness of the Law, as leaves burgeoning with the letter of knowledge but bearing no fruit of good works because of lawlessness: but make us all sons of grace of Thy good pleasure![^172]

*Glory...*
It is alien unto lawless ones to glorify Thee, the unoriginate Essence, Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, the uncreated Almighty Dominion, by Which the whole world was made through the nod of Thy divine power!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
We bring unto Thee the Theotokos in intercession: through her prayers and those of Thine Apostles, make us partakers of Thy good things, O Good One, and vouchsafe us the radiance of Thy Resurrection, O Savior!

*And again the Heirmos: Virginity is alien unto mothers... Then the rest as usual, and the Dismissal.*

## Great and Holy Monday: Matins[^173]

*At Matins, the Priest says:* Blessed is our God... *And we say:* Amen. *The Trisagion, and 3 Prostrations. After "Our Father," "Lord, have mercy" (12 times). "Come, let us worship..." (3 times). Then the Six Psalms, and the Litany. After this, the Alleluia in Tone 8, in a loud voice and with sweet melody.*
*Verse 1:* From night my spirit wakes at dawn unto Thee, O God, for Thy commandments are a light upon the earth (Isaiah 26:9 LXX).
*Verse 2:* Learn righteousness, ye that dwell upon the earth (Isaiah 26:9b LXX).
*Verse 3:* Bring more evils upon them, O Lord, bring more evils on the glorious of the earth (Isaiah 26:15 LXX).
*Verse 4:* Thus were we to Thy beloved (Isaiah 26:16–17 LXX).

*Then this Troparion (3 times), Tone 8:*
Behold, the Bridegroom comes at midnight, and blessed is the servant whom He shall find watching; but unworthy again is the servant whom He shall find careless. Beware therefore, O my soul, lest thou be weighed down with sleep, lest thou be given over unto death, and be shut out from the Kingdom; but awake, crying aloud: Holy, holy, holy art Thou, O God: through the Theotokos, have mercy on us![^174] *(Chanted 3 times with a loud voice.)*

*Then the Psalter, with Prostrations.*

*After the 1st Kathisma: Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*
The present day causes the honored Passions to shine forth upon the world as perfect luminaries; for Christ comes to suffer through His goodness: He Who holds all things in His palm is held, that He may be stretched out upon the Tree to save mankind!
*Glory... Both now... the same.*[^175]

*After the 2nd Kathisma: Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*
O invisible Judge, how wast Thou seen in the flesh, and how dost Thou go to be slain by lawless men, condemning our condemnation by Thy Passion? Therefore sending up praise, majesty, and glory unto Thy goodness, O Word, we offer it with one accord!
*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 3rd Kathisma: Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
The present day brings brightly the beginnings of the Lord's Passions. Come therefore, ye lovers of the feast, let us meet Him with hymns! For the Creator comes to accept the Cross, interrogations and scourging, judged by Pilate: therefore, struck on the head by a servant, He endures all things to save man. Therefore let us cry aloud unto Him: O Lover of mankind, Christ God, grant remission of sins unto those who worship Thy most pure Passions in faith!
*Glory... Both now... the same.*[^176]

*We read the Patristic Homilies from the Commentary on the Gospel of Saint Matthew: the Homily of Saint John Chrysostom on the day concerning the withered fig tree, whose incipit is: "Seeing then..." or two: and the Homily of Saint John Damascene on the withered fig tree, and on the Parable of the Vineyard.*[^177]
*After this, Psalm 50. Then: "O God, save Thy people..." "Lord, have mercy" (12 times).*

*The Triode: The composition of Lord Cosmas.*[^178]
*Bearing the acrostic: ΤΗ ΔΕΥΤΕΡΑ ("On the second day", that is, Monday: for Sunday is called the first day among Greeks and Latins). Heirmos chanted twice; Troparia on 12; afterward both choirs sing the Heirmos together. Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who by His divine command dried up the untraversed, billowing sea, and guided the people of Israel to pass through: let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified![^179]

*Troparion 1 (Acrostic: T):*
The ineffable condescension of the Word of God—wherein Christ Himself is both God and Man—not deeming equality with God a thing to be grasped,[^180] when taking the form of a servant reveals it unto the Disciples: for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion 2 (Acrostic: H):*
"I Myself am come to serve the impoverished Adam, in whose form I the Creator was willingly clothed, though rich in Godhead; to lay down My life as a ransom for him, though impassible in Godhead!"[^181]
*Then the Little Litany.*

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Я́кѡ нача́тки е҆стества̀):*
Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph, while the noble youth sat upon a chariot, honored as a king; for having not enslaved himself unto the pleasures of the Egyptian woman, he was glorified by Him Who knows the hearts of men and bestows an incorruptible crown![^182]

*Ikos:*
Let us now add lamentation unto lamentation, and pour forth tears, weeping with Jacob for the ever-memorable and chaste Joseph, who was enslaved in body, but preserved his soul unenslaved, and reigned over all Egypt: for God bestows upon His servants an incorruptible crown!

*[Woodcut: Four scenes from the Life of Patriarch Joseph the All-Comely: (1) Joseph cast into the pit and sold to the Midianites; (2) Joseph fleeing Potiphar's wife, leaving his garment; (3) Joseph interpreting the dreams of the cupbearer and baker in prison; (4) Joseph ruling in Egypt, his brothers bowing before him.][^183]*

*On Holy and Great Monday: The Synaxarion*[^184]
*Bless, Father.*
*Verses on Joseph the All-Comely:*
*Chaste Joseph, fleeing, holds fast to righteousness,*
*And as the giver of grain, offers sheaves of blessings.*
*Verses on the Withered Fig Tree:*
*Christ, likening the Jewish synagogue unto a fig tree*
*Destitute of spiritual fruits,*
*Withers it with a curse: let us flee a like fate!*

On Holy and Great Monday, we commemorate the blessed Joseph the All-Comely, and the withered fig tree: because from this point the holy Passions of our Lord Jesus Christ take their beginning, and Joseph is first received as a type of Him. For he was the younger son of the Patriarch Jacob, born unto him of Rachel; and he was envied by his brothers on account of certain dreams. First...

hidden in the digging of a pit; and his father, deceived by the children with the blood-stained garment, grieved, thinking that he had been devoured by a beast. Then he was sold for thirty pieces of silver unto the Ishmaelites, who in turn sold him unto Potiphar, the chief of the eunuchs of Pharaoh, King of Egypt. And when his mistress raged against the chastity of the youth—because he refused to commit iniquity—he left his garment and fled; and she slandered him unto his master, and bonds and a bitter prison received him. Then, on account of the interpretation of dreams, he was brought forth, and shown unto the King, and appointed lord of all the land of Egypt. And again, on account of the distribution of grain, he was revealed unto his brethren. And having well spent the whole course of his life, he died in Egypt, renowned for his great chastity alongside his other virtues. This is a type of Christ: for Christ also was envied by His fellow Jews, and sold by His Disciple for thirty pieces of silver, and shut up in the dark and gloomy pit of the Tomb; and bursting forth thence by His own sovereign power, He reigns over Egypt—that is, over every sin—and utterly overcomes its dominion. And He rules over all the world, and in His love for mankind redeems us, the mystical Giver of grain, since He gave Himself for us, and nourishes us with heavenly Bread, His own Life-bearing Flesh. For this reason, then, the all-comely Joseph is commemorated today.[^185]

On this day also we commemorate the withered fig tree: because the divine Evangelists, namely Matthew and Mark, record after the account of the Palms: on the morrow, as they went out from Bethany, He hungered. And the other says: returning in the morning into the city, He hungered; and seeing a fig tree having leaves only—for it was not the season of figs—He came to it, and when He found no fruit upon it, He said: Let no fruit grow on thee henceforward unto the age! And immediately the fig tree withered away.[^186] Now the fig tree signifies the Jewish synagogue, upon which the Savior found not the fitting fruit, but only the shadow of the Law; and this He took away from them, leaving it entirely destitute. And if anyone should say: Why did the soulless tree, which had not sinned, become dry after receiving a curse? Let him learn that the Jews, seeing Christ always doing good unto all, and having never brought anything painful or grievous upon anyone, supposed that He had power only to do good, but not to work harm. But the Master and Son, the Lover of mankind, would not show this upon a man, that the ungrateful people might indeed be convinced that He possesses sufficient power unto punishment also; yet since as God He desires not to punish man, He worked this chastisement upon a soulless and insensible nature. Together with this, there is also a certain ineffable teaching handed down to us from the ancient wise elders, as Saint Isidore of Pelusium says: that this was the tree of transgression, whose leaves the transgressors needed for a covering; therefore it was cursed by Christ in His love for mankind, since it had not suffered this then, that it might no longer bear fruit that was the cause of sin. And that sin is likened unto a fig is manifest: for it possesses the sweetness of sensual pleasure, the stickiness of guilt, and afterwards the hardening and prickling of the conscience. Nevertheless, by the Fathers, the narrative of the fig tree is placed here for the sake of compunction, just as that of Joseph is placed here because he bears the type of Christ. For the fig tree is every soul destitute of all spiritual fruit; wherein in the morning, after this present life, the Lord finding no rest in her, withers her with a curse and sends her into the eternal fire. And she stands as a withered pillar, striking fear into those who do not bring forth the fitting fruit of virtues. Through the prayers of the all-comely Joseph, O Christ our God, have mercy on us, now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.[^187]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The single-substance soul and undefiled body of the pious youths struck awe into the unwearied fire, nourished in abundant matter; and as the flame died away, an unceasing hymn of praise was sung: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all the ages![^188]

*Troparion 1 (Acrostic: T):*
"Ye shall then be known as My Disciples if ye keep My commandments," said the Savior unto His friends as He went to His Passion: "have peace among yourselves and with all men; and thinking humble thoughts, be ye exalted, and knowing Me as Lord, praise and exalt Me above all unto the ages!"[^189]

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*

*Troparion 2 (Acrostic: E):*
"Let the dominion over brethren be of an order opposite unto the Gentile rule; for tyranny, a self-chosen mind, is not My portion. Let him therefore who desires to be first among you be the last of all; and knowing Me as Lord, praise and exalt Me above all unto the ages!"

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him above all unto the ages.*
*And again the Heirmos: The single-substance soul... The "More honorable" is not sung.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thou hast magnified, O Christ, the Theotokos who bore Thee, from whom, O our Creator, Thou wast clothed in a body of like passions with us, for the remission of our transgressions: blessing her, all generations magnify Thee![^190]

*Troparion 1 (Acrostic: R):*
"Casting aside all passionate defilement, let us receive a well-ordered mind worthy of the divine Kingdom," Thou didst say beforehand unto Thine Apostles, O Wisdom of all, "wherein ye shall be glorified, shining more radiantly than the sun!"

*Troparion 2 (Acrostic: A):*
"Looking upon Me," Thou didst say, O Lord, unto Thy Disciples, "think not haughty thoughts, but condescend unto the humble; drink the cup that I drink, that in the Kingdom of the Father ye may be glorified with Me!"

*And again the Heirmos: Thou hast magnified, O Christ...*
*In place of "It is truly meet," a prostration to the earth.*

*Exapostilarion (Chanted slowly and with melody, 3 times; by the Vanarkhist alone in the midst of the church; Glory... on the second; Both now... on the other; and thus we chant through the whole week):*
I behold Thy bridal chamber adorned, O my Savior, and I have no wedding garment that I may enter therein; enlighten the vesture of my soul, O Giver of light, and save me![^191]

*At the Praises, Stichera Idiomela on 4, Tone 1:*
As Thou wentest, O Lord, to Thy voluntary Passion, Thou didst say unto the Apostles on the way: "Behold, we go up to Jerusalem, and the Son of Man shall be delivered up, as it is written of Him." Come therefore, we also, with cleansed minds, let us journey with Him and be crucified with Him, and let us mortify ourselves for His sake unto the pleasures of life, that we may also live with Him, and hear Him crying aloud: "No longer do I go unto the earthly Jerusalem to suffer, but I ascend unto My Father and your Father, and unto My God and your God, and I shall exalt you into the Jerusalem on high, into the Heavenly Kingdom!" *(Twice.)*[^192]

*Tone 5:*
Having attained, O ye faithful, unto the saving Passion of Christ God, let us glorify His ineffable longsuffering, that by His tender compassion He may also raise up us who were slain by sin, as He is good and the Lover of mankind! *(Twice.)*

*Glory... Both now... Tone 5:*
O Lord, going to Thy Passion, confirming Thine own Disciples, Thou didst say, having taken them aside: "How is it that ye remember not My words which I spoke unto you before? For it is not written that any Prophet should be slain except in Jerusalem. Now therefore the time is at hand of which I told you: for behold, I am delivered into the hands of sinners to be mocked, who also, nailing Me upon the Cross and delivering Me to burial, shall account Me loathsome as a dead man. Yet take courage: for on the third day I shall rise again, unto the joy of the faithful and life eternal!"

*At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela, Tone 5:*
O Lord, unto the ineffable mystery of Thy dispensation, the mother of the sons of Zebedee, not comprehending, asked of Thee to grant unto her children the honor of a temporal kingdom; but instead of this, Thou didst promise unto Thy friends to drink the cup of death, which cup Thou didst say that Thou Thyself wouldst drink before this, for the cleansing of sins. Therefore we cry aloud unto Thee: O Salvation of our souls, glory be to Thee![^193]
*Verse:* We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord (Psalm 89:14 LXX).

O Lord, teaching Thine own Disciples to meditate on more perfect things, Thou didst tell them not to be like the Gentiles in exercising dominion over the lesser. "For it shall not be so with you, My Disciples, for of My own will I am poor. Let the first among you therefore be the servant of all; and he that rules, as he that is ruled; and the foremost, as the last. For I Myself am come to serve the impoverished Adam, and to give My life as a ransom for many, who cry unto Me: Glory be to Thee!"
*Verse:* And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us, and direct the works of our hands (Psalm 89:17 LXX).

*Tone 8:*
Fearing the rebuke of the withered fig tree for its barrenness, O brethren, let us bring forth worthy fruits of repentance unto Christ, Who grants unto us great mercy![^194]

*Glory... Both now... Tone 8:*
The serpent, having found the Egyptian woman as a second Eve, sought through deceitful words to ensnare Joseph; but he, leaving his garment, fled from sin, and being naked was not ashamed, was not ashamed, even as the first-formed man before the disobedience. Through his prayers, O Christ, have mercy on us!

*Note: These verses are said on Tuesday and on Wednesday.*
*Then: "It is good to give praise..." and the rest, with the customary Prostrations.*

## Great and Holy Monday: First Hour

*The First Hour is read without a Kathisma, only the three customary Psalms; and the rest with Prostrations. Then the Dismissal:*
May Christ our true God, Who came to His voluntary Passion, through the prayers of His most pure Mother and of all the saints... *This Dismissal is said on Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, and on Great Friday.*

*At the third hour of the day, the Paraecclesiarch strikes the klepalo, as is the custom; and having assembled in church, we sing the Third Hour with its Psalms and customary prostrations. Then the Troparion: "O Lord, Who didst send down Thy Most Holy Spirit..." and the Theotokion: "Thou art the vine..." and 3 Prostrations. Upon a richly adorned analogion they place the Holy Tetraevangelion, and a lighted lamp. The Deacon goes forth and censes around the whole church, and the brethren according to order. At the Sixth Hour he strikes the analogion and the Gospel only. At the Ninth Hour he censes the whole church and the brethren. And thus the Priest reads the Tetraevangelion, vested in his phelonion, all standing with uncovered heads and listening. At the conclusion of the Gospel we read the Homily of Saint Ephrem on the All-Comely Joseph. And after this we say: "Blessed is the Lord God..." and the rest. The Kontakion of the day is said: "Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph" (fol. 36). And the 15 customary prostrations.*[^195]

*It is necessary to know how the Tetraevangelion is read: The Gospel according to Matthew is read in its entirety in two readings. Likewise that according to Mark is read in its entirety in two readings. That according to Luke is read in three readings. That according to John is read up to the beginning of the Holy Passions, in two readings: up to "Now is glorified" (John 13:31). The Gospels are read across three days: on Monday, Tuesday, and concluding at the Hours on Wednesday.*

## Great and Holy Monday: Sixth Hour

*And immediately we begin the Sixth Hour. "Come, let us worship..." (3 times), and the customary Psalms. The kathisma of the day. Then the Troparion of the Sixth Hour of the day, and the Theotokion: "Seeing that we have no boldness..." and 3 Prostrations. Then the Troparion, Tone 2:*
With a contrite soul we fall down before Thee, and we pray Thee, O Savior of the world: for Thou art the God of the penitent!
*Glory: the conclusion. Both now: the whole.*
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 125, Tone 4:* When the Lord turned back the captivity of Zion (Psalm 125:1 LXX).
*Verse:* Then were our mouths filled with joy (Psalm 125:2 LXX).

*Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel (Chapter 1:1–14 LXX):*[^196]
Now it came to pass in the thirtieth year, in the fourth month, on the fifth day of the month, as I was in the midst of the captivity by the river Chebar, that the heavens were opened, and I saw visions of God. On the fifth day of the month, which was the fifth year of the captivity of King Jehoiachin, the word of the Lord came expressly unto Ezekiel the priest, the son of Buzi, in the land of the Chaldeans, by the river Chebar; and the hand of the Lord was upon me. And I looked, and, behold, a whirlwind came out of the north, and a great cloud, and a light around about it, and flashing fire. And from the midst thereof as the appearance of electrum in the midst of the fire, and light in it; and in the midst thereof as the likeness of four living creatures. And this was their appearance: they had the likeness of a man. And every one had four faces, and every one had four wings. And their legs were straight, and their feet winged, and sparks flashing like burnished bronze, and their wings were light. And the hand of a man was under their wings on their four sides; and their faces and their wings were four, each close to the other. And their faces turned not when they went; they went every one straight forward.

And as for the likeness of their faces, they four had the face of a man, and the face of a lion on the right side; and they four had the face of an ox on the left side; and they four had the face of an eagle. And their wings were stretched upward; two wings of every one were joined one to another, and two covered the upper part of their bodies. And they went every one straight forward: whither the spirit was to go, they went; and they turned not as they went. And in the midst of the living creatures was an appearance like burning coals of fire, and like the appearance of lamps turning among the living creatures; and the fire was bright, and out of the fire went forth lightning. And the living creatures ran and returned, as the appearance of a flash of lightning. And I looked, and, behold, one wheel upon the earth beside the four living creatures. And the appearance of the wheels and their workmanship was as the appearance of chrysolite; and they four had one likeness: and their appearance and their work was as it were a wheel in the middle of a wheel. When they went, they went upon their four sides: and they turned not when they went, nor their backs; and they were of lofty height; and I beheld them, and their backs were full of eyes round about them four. And when the living creatures went, the wheels went beside them: and when the living creatures were lifted up from the earth, the wheels were lifted up. Whithersoever the cloud was, thither was the spirit to go: the living creatures went, and the wheels were lifted up with them; for the spirit of life was in the wheels.[^197]
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 126, Tone 4:* Except the Lord build the house, in vain do the builders labor (Psalm 126:1 LXX).
*Verse:* Except the Lord keep the city, in vain doth the watchman watch (Psalm 126:1b LXX).

*And the Tetraevangelion is read. And immediately: "Let Thy tender mercies speedily prevent us..." The Trisagion. After "Our Father," the Kontakion from the Triodion of the day. "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), and the Prayer: "Thou Who at all times and at every hour..." and the rest according to custom with Prostrations, all as before.*

## Great and Holy Monday: Ninth Hour

*Note that we also sing the Ninth Hour with its Kathisma. After the three Psalms, we say the Troparion: "Thou Who at the ninth hour for our sake..." with its verses according to custom. Glory... Both now... "Thou Who for our sake wast born..." And the Tetraevangelion is read. After this, a reading from Ephrem. Then: "Deliver us not up utterly..." The Trisagion. After "Our Father," the Kontakion from the Triodion of the day. "Lord, have mercy" (40 times). "Thou Who at all times..." "Lord, have mercy" (3 times). Glory... Both now... "More honorable than the Cherubim..." and 3 great Prostrations. And the Prayer: "O Master, Lord Jesus Christ..." We sing the Beatitudes: "Remember us, O Lord..." with prostrations. Then: "The heavenly choir..." and the rest. Glory: "The choir..." Both now: "I believe..." "Pardon, remit..." "Our Father." The Kontakion in the Triodion of the day: "Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph" (fol. 36). "Lord, have mercy" (40 times). Glory... Both now... "More honorable than the Cherubim..." and 3 great prostrations, with the Prayer: "O Lord and Master of my life..." and 12 other reverences. After this, the Trisagion, and 3 Prostrations. After "Our Father," "Lord, have mercy" (12 times), and the Prayer: "All-Holy Trinity..." and the Dismissal.*

## Great and Holy Monday: Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts

*The Priest having given the blessing, we say: Amen. "Come, let us worship..." (3 times). Then Psalm 103: "Bless the Lord, O my soul..." Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3 times). Then the Litany. After this, the Kathisma: "Unto the Lord in my affliction I cried..." (18th Kathisma) with Prostrations.*[^198]
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses.*
*And we sing Stichera Idiomela, Tone 1:*
As Thou wentest, O Lord, to Thy voluntary Passion, Thou didst say unto the Apostles on the way: "Behold, we go up to Jerusalem, and the Son of Man shall be delivered up, as it is written of Him." Come therefore, we also, with cleansed minds, let us journey with Him and be crucified with Him, and let us mortify ourselves for His sake unto the pleasures of life, that we may also live with Him, and hear Him crying aloud: "No longer do I go unto the earthly Jerusalem to suffer, but I ascend unto My Father and your Father, and unto My God and your God, and I shall exalt you into the Jerusalem on high, into the Heavenly Kingdom!" *(Twice.)*

*Tone 5:*
Having attained, O ye faithful, unto the saving Passion of Christ God, let us glorify His ineffable longsuffering, that by His tender compassion He may also raise up us who were slain by sin, as He is good and the Lover of mankind! *(Twice.)*

O Lord, unto the ineffable mystery of Thy dispensation, the mother of the sons of Zebedee, not comprehending, asked of Thee to grant unto her children the honor of a temporal kingdom; but instead of this, Thou didst promise unto Thy friends to drink the cup of death, which cup Thou didst say that Thou Thyself wouldst drink before this, for the cleansing of sins. Therefore we cry aloud unto Thee: O Salvation of our souls, glory be to Thee! *(Twice.)*

O Lord, teaching Thine own Disciples to meditate on more perfect things, Thou didst tell them not to be like the Gentiles in exercising dominion over the lesser. "For it shall not be so with you, My Disciples, for of My own will I am poor. Let the first among you therefore be the servant of all; and he that rules, as he that is ruled; and the foremost, as the last. For I Myself am come to serve the impoverished Adam, and to give My life as a ransom for many, who cry unto Me: Glory be to Thee!" *(Twice.)*

*Tone 8:*
Fearing the rebuke of the withered fig tree for its barrenness, O brethren, let us bring forth worthy fruits of repentance unto Christ, Who grants unto us great mercy! *(Twice.)*

*Glory... Tone 8:*
The serpent, having found the Egyptian woman as a second Eve, sought through deceitful words to ensnare Joseph; but he, leaving his garment, fled from sin; and being naked was not ashamed, even as the first-formed man before the disobedience. Through his prayers, O Christ, have mercy on us!

*Both now... Tone 5:*
O Lord, going to Thy Passion, confirming Thine own Disciples, Thou didst say, having taken them aside: "How is it that ye remember not My words which I spoke unto you before? For it is not written that any Prophet should be slain except in Jerusalem. Now therefore the time is at hand of which I told you: for behold, I am delivered into the hands of sinners to be mocked, who also, nailing Me upon the Cross and delivering Me to burial, shall account Me loathsome as a dead man. Yet take courage: for on the third day I shall rise again, unto the joy of the faithful and life eternal!"

*Then the Entrance with the Gospel. "O Gladsome Light..."*
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 127, Tone 6:* The Lord bless thee out of Zion, and thou shalt see the good of Jerusalem (Psalm 127:5 LXX).
*Verse:* Blessed are all they that fear the Lord, that walk in His ways (Psalm 127:1 LXX).

*Reading from Exodus (Chapter 1:1–14 LXX):*[^199]
These are the names of the sons of Israel who went down into Egypt together with Jacob their father; each entered with his whole household: Reuben, Simeon, Levi, Judah; Issachar, Zebulun, Benjamin; Dan, Naphtali, Gad, and Asher. Now Joseph was already in Egypt. And all the souls that came out of Jacob were seventy-five. And Joseph died, and all his brethren, and all that generation. And the children of Israel grew and multiplied, and abounded and waxed exceedingly strong; and the land multiplied them. And there arose another king over Egypt, who knew not Joseph. And he said unto his people: "Behold, the race of the children of Israel is a great multitude, and is stronger than we. Come therefore, let us deal craftily with them, lest they multiply; and if war happen to befall us, they also join themselves unto our enemies, and having fought against us, depart out of our land." And he set over them taskmasters over their works, to afflict them in their works; and they built three strong cities for Pharaoh: Pithom, and Rameses, and On, which is Heliopolis. But the more they afflicted them, so much the more they multiplied, and waxed exceedingly strong. And the Egyptians abhorred the children of Israel, and violently oppressed the sons of Israel with force. And they made their life bitter with hard works, in clay and in brick-making, and in all manner of service in the fields, according to all their works wherein they made them serve with violence. And the King of Egypt spoke unto the Hebrew midwives, of whom the name of the one was Shiphrah, and the name of the second Puah; and he said unto them: "When ye do the office of a midwife to the Hebrew women, and they are about to be delivered: if it be a male child, kill him; but if it be a female, preserve her."

But the midwives feared God, and did not as the King of Egypt commanded them, but preserved the male children alive. And the King of Egypt called for the midwives, and said unto them: "Why have ye done this thing, and preserved the male children alive?" And the midwives said unto Pharaoh: "The Hebrew women are not as the Egyptian women; for they give birth before the midwives can come in unto them, and they were already delivered." Therefore God dealt well with the midwives; and the people multiplied, and waxed exceedingly strong.
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 128, Tone 6:* We have blessed you in the name of the Lord (Psalm 128:8 LXX).
*Verse:* Many a time have they fought against me from my youth (Psalm 128:1 LXX).

*Reading from Job (Chapter 1:1–12 LXX):*
There was a certain man in the land of Uz, whose name was Job; and that man was blameless, upright, true, godly, and abstaining from every evil deed. And there were born unto him seven sons and three daughters. And his cattle were seven thousand sheep, three thousand camels, five hundred yoke of oxen, and five hundred she-asses feeding; and he had a very great household, and great works upon the earth; and that man was noble from the rising of the sun. And his sons went and feasted one with another, making a banquet every day, sending also for their three sisters to eat and drink with them. And when the days of their feasting were completed, Job sent and purified them, rising up early in the morning, and offered sacrifices for them according to their number, and one calf for sin for their souls. For Job said: "Lest perhaps my sons have thought evil in their hearts toward God." Thus did Job all their days. And the day came, and, behold, the Angels of God came to present themselves before the Lord, and the devil came also with them. And the Lord said unto the devil: "Whence comest thou?" And the devil answered the Lord and said: "Having gone round about the earth, and walked under heaven, I am come." And the Lord said unto him: "Hast thou considered in thy mind My servant Job? For there is none like him upon the earth, a man blameless, upright, true, godly, abstaining from every evil deed." And the devil answered and said before the Lord: "Doth Job revere God for nought? Hast Thou not made a hedge about him, and about his house within and without, and about all that he hath round about? And Thou hast blessed the works of his hands, and multiplied his cattle upon the earth. But put forth Thine hand and touch all that he hath, and see if he will not bless Thee to Thy face!" Then the Lord said unto the devil: "Behold, all that he hath I give into thine hand; only touch not himself." And the devil went out from the presence of the Lord.

*If the Presanctified Liturgy is celebrated, after "Let my prayer be set forth...": The Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 98 (Matthew 24:3–35). Then the Litany: "Let us all say..." and the rest of the Presanctified, and the Dismissal.*[^200]
*If the Presanctified is not celebrated, we sing at "Lord, I have cried" half of the Stichera, on 6. Sticheron, Tone 1: "As Thou wentest, O Lord, to Thy voluntary Passion" (twice); "Having attained, O ye faithful" (twice); "O Lord, unto the ineffable mystery" (twice). Glory... Both now... "O Lord, going to Thy Passion."*
*After these, the Entrance with the Gospel, and "O Gladsome Light." Then the Prokeimena, the Readings, and the Gospel; then "Vouchsafe, O Lord..." and the Litany: "Let us complete our evening prayer..." At the Aposticha, from the same Stichera, Sticheron, Tone 5: "O Lord, teaching Thine own Disciples..." Verse: "Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes..." and again the same Sticheron. Verse: "Have mercy on us, O Lord..." Tone 8: "Fearing the rebuke of the withered fig tree..." Glory... Both now... Tone 8: "The serpent, having found the Egyptian woman..." Then "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." The Trisagion, and after "Our Father," the Troparion: "Behold, the Bridegroom comes..." (fol. 34). And the rest as usual, with Prostrations. And after the final Trisagion, and after "Our Father," "Lord, have mercy" (12 times). Then the Prayer: "All-Holy Trinity..." and "Blessed be the name of the Lord..." and the Dismissal. Thus we do on Tuesday and on Wednesday.*

*Note: At table we eat dry food (xerophagy), and with this we are satisfied. And as in the First Week of this Holy Fast, so all these days: Great Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday, it is fitting to fast.*[^201]
*It is necessary to know that the Sixth Ecumenical Council commanded through the holy fathers that during the Fast, prayers and compunction be doubly offered by the faithful, but at midnight of Great Saturday to cease fasting: since the divine Evangelists Matthew and Luke, the one saying "late on the Sabbath" and the other "very early in the morning," describe unto us the lingering hours of that night.*

## Great and Holy Monday: Great Compline

*The Priest having given the blessing, we say: Amen. The customary prayers. The Trisagion. After "Our Father," "Lord, have mercy" (12 times). "Come, let us worship..." (3 times), and the rest according to custom with Prostrations.*

*The Triode: The composition of Lord Andrew of Crete.*[^202]
*Tone 8. Ode 2. Heirmos:*
Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak, and I will praise Christ Who came in the flesh from the Virgin! Let us sing unto God Who willingly goes to suffer, and let us glorify His compassion!

*Troparion 1:*
Let us hear the blessed voice which He spoke unto us: that the time of your end draws near![^203]

*Troparion 2:*
Let us go with Christ unto the Mount of Olives in secret, and with the Apostles let us abide with Him!

*Troparion 3:*
Understand, O my humble heart, what the Parable of the Millstones is, of which Christ spoke before; and be sober henceforth!

*Troparion 4:*
Prepare thyself, O my soul, for thy departure: the coming of the inexorable Judge draws near!

*Troparion 5:*
Hide not in the earth the talent given unto thee, O my soul, that thou mayest repay the debt unto Christ with interest!

*Troparion 6:*
Honor the prudent servants today; work unceasingly the secret labor of incorruptible life!

*Troparion 7:*
Arise, the Bridegroom draws near! Let us make ourselves ready henceforth, for the bridal chamber is opened: let him therefore that is worthy enter!

*Troparion 8:*
The heavenly chamber is adorned and made ready now for those who desire to enter into the incorruptible bridal chamber!

*Troparion 9:*
Remember the wise virgins, O my soul, remember, that with Christ thou mayest enter radiantly into the bridal chamber!

*Another Heirmos, the same Tone:*
See, see that I am God, Who of old led Israel through the Red Sea, and saved him, and nourished him, and delivered him from the servitude of Pharaoh!

*Troparion 10:*
See, see that I am God, Who was before the existence of all things, and before the creation of earth and heaven, all things unto all, Who am in the Father and bear all things in Myself![^204]

*Troparion 11:*
By My Word I framed the heaven together with the earth, together with the Father, and by My Word I uphold all things: as Word, Wisdom, Power, Image, co-working and equal in operation!

*Troparion 12:*
Who appointed the seasons? Who rules the ages? Who determines and binds all things? The Co-beginningless, Who is ever with the Father, as the ray in the light!

*Troparion 13:*
O Thine immeasurable love for mankind, O Jesus! For Thou hast declared unto us the time of the end that shall come to pass, concealing the hour, yet radiantly revealing its signs unto the Disciples!

*Troparion 14:*
Thou knowest all things, Thou understandest all things, O Jesus, as having in Thyself the whole Fatherly divine possession, and bearing in Thyself the whole blessed Spirit, consubstantial with the Father!

*Troparion 15:*
Yea, O Lord, Creator of the ages, vouchsafe that we also may hear that voice calling the chosen of the Father into the Heavenly Kingdom!

*Glory:*
O unoriginate, uncreated Trinity, undivided Unity, Three being One, Father, Son, and Spirit, One God: receive a hymn from earthen tongues, as from flaming lips!

*Both now:*
Thou hast been revealed as a holy tabernacle, O Virgin Mother of God; for into thee the Heavenly King, having entered in the flesh, came forth radiant, fashioning man in Himself unto God!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Compassion" (Милосе́рдїя):*
Moved with compassion, O Christ our Benefactor, Thou comest willingly to Thy Passion, desiring by Thy suffering to deliver us from passions and from the condemnation in Hades: therefore we praise Thy venerable Passions, and we glorify Thy supreme condescension, O Savior![^205]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who is borne upon the throne of glory by Angels and the heavens, and is unceasingly glorified as God: bless, praise, and exalt Him above all unto all the ages!

*Troparion 1:*
In every way thou hast heard, O my soul, how Christ proclaimed the end unto His divine Disciples: understanding therefore thine own end, make ready henceforth, for the time of departure is come![^206]

*Troparion 2:*
Thou hast known, O barren soul, the parable of the wicked servant: fear, and neglect not the gift which thou hast received, lest thou hide it in the earth, but trade therewith!

*Troparion 3:*
Human life is likened unto a way and a fair: thou therefore, O my soul, walking in these, acquire something, and gain a treasure that cannot be stolen, the wealth of virtues!

*Troparion 4:*
Let the lamp be made bright, and let oil be added thereto—even compassion accompanying works—that thou, O my soul, mayest then find the bridal chamber of Christ opened!

*Troparion 5:*
"Flight on the Sabbath and in winter," said the Teacher, predicting the speedy end of this present seventh age, wherein the end shall come like winter!

*Troparion 6:*
