Like the speed of passing lightning: so shall that fearful coming of thy Master be, O my soul! Having heard this, therefore, make haste to be ready henceforth!

*Troparion 7:*
Desiring to show the multitude of Thy longsuffering, O Jesus, Thou wast pleased to be sold and taken, and kissed, and Thou didst endure all passions of Thine own will!

*Troparion 8:*
When the Judge shall come with myriads and thousands of angelic ranks and powers: what fear shall be thine, O my soul? What trembling shall lay hold of all as they stand naked?

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Trinity Troparion:*
One God is the Trinity: the Father hath not departed into sonship, nor hath the Son changed into procession, but each has what is proper: the Three, One Light, praise God unto the ages!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Through the prayers of the Mother of God, O God, receive our supplications; send down Thy mercies upon all richly, and grant Thy peace unto Thy people!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him above all unto the ages.*
*Then the Heirmos: Him Who is borne upon the throne of glory...*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Him Who was revealed on the Mountain unto the Lawgiver in the fire and the bush, for the salvation of us believers through the childbearing of the Ever-Virgin: with unceasing hymns we magnify![^207]

*Troparion 1:*
Thou hast heard, O my soul, the Judge foretelling and teaching thee the burdens of the end: prepare the works for thy departure, lest being found guilty, thou be cast out from God!

*Troparion 2:*
Learn of the end from the fig tree, O my soul: when its branch becomes tender and puts forth leaves, the season of harvest is at hand; and when thou seest these things, know that it is near, even at the doors!

*Troparion 3:*
Who else besides Thee knows Thy Father? Or who except Thee knows the hour or the day? For in Thee are all the treasures of wisdom hidden within, O Christ God!

*Troparion 4:*
The books shall then be opened, the thrones being set, and deeds shall be made manifest, as all stand naked: for all things are naked unto God!

*Troparion 5:*
The Judge of all comes to be led unto judgment—He that sits upon the Cherubic throne—to be delivered up to Pilate as one condemned, and to suffer all things, that He may save Adam!

*Troparion 6:*
Our great and divine Pascha is drawn near: for after two days Christ foretells, foreshadowing His Passion...

*Troparion 6 (conclusion):*
...foreshadowing the day on which the sacrifice shall be brought unto the Father.

*Troparion 7:*
Standing by Thy Cross, O Savior, the Mother beholding Thine unjust slaughter cried aloud: "Woe is me, my Child! Woe is me, Light unwaning! Shine forth, O Sun of Glory, Light unto all!"

*Glory:*
*Trinity Troparion:*
Holy Unity, Trinity, one Godhead, and Trinity, Unity, O God, Tri-hypostatic Nature, one honorable and undivided Glory: deliver our souls from afflictions!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Receive Thy Mother, O Christ, in prayer, that by her intercessions Thou mayest grant peace unto the world, establish the scepters of kingdoms, and unite Thy Churches into one!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Him Who was revealed on the Mountain... Sing in full.*

*It is truly meet. And a prostration to the ground. Trisagion. And after Our Father: Kontakion of the day: Lord, have mercy (40). Then: Lord of hosts, be with us. And the rest according to custom. And the Dismissal.*

## Typikon for the Feast of the Annunciation on Great and Holy Tuesday[^208]

*It is necessary to know: if the Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos happens to fall on Great Tuesday.*

*On Monday evening, at Vespers, the censing is not performed. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera Idiomela of the day (5), and of the feast (5). Glory, Tone 8: The Second Gospel. Both now, of the feast, Tone 6: "He was sent." Entrance with the Gospel. "O Joyful Light." Prokeimenon and reading of the day, and of the feast (2). "Let my prayer arise," and 3 prostrations. Gospel of the day, and the rest of the Presanctified. But prostrations cease in the evening service itself, except only 3 great prostrations: after "Let my prayer arise," after "Now the powers of heaven," and at "Blessed be the name of the Lord." Likewise at the end of Matins, and at the end of the Hours, only three great prostrations.*

*At the refectory, we eat cooked food with olive oil, and we drink wine.*

*At the sixth hour of the night, the great semantron is struck.[^209] And having assembled, we chant Great Compline. The priest censes according to custom, and after the "Glory to God in the highest," we go forth into the narthex, and we chant the Stichera Idiomela of the feast, Tone 1. Glory, another: "Gabriel proclaimed." At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela of the feast (3), Tone 1, with their verses. Glory, another, the same tone: "Today is joy." At the blessing of the loaves: Troparion of the feast (3), and the reading of the feast.*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the feast (3). And after the customary Kathismata, Sessional Hymns in the Triodion, and the reading of the day. After the 3rd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the feast, and the reading of the feast. Polyeleos and Sessional Hymn of the feast, and the reading of the feast. Songs of Ascent: 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon: "Let every breath." Gospel of the feast. Psalm 50 with the Stichera of the feast. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos to 6; Katavasia of the feast, the Heirmos by both choirs together. Where there is the Two-Ode Canon in the Triodion, we chant the Canon of the feast with the Heirmos to 6, and the Two-Ode Canon to 8; and afterward the Heirmos in the Triodion, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the day and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the feast, and the reading of the feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the feast. At Ode 9, we do not chant "More honorable." Exapostilarion of the feast. Glory, of the day. Both now, of the feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the feast, Tone 1, to 4. Glory, Tone 2, of the day: "Slumbering in spiritual sloth." Both now, of the feast: "The mystery hidden from the ages." At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela of the day, with their verses. Glory, of the feast, Tone 8: "Let the heavens rejoice." Both now, of the day, Tone 6: "When Thou shalt come." Troparion of the feast. Litany, and 3 great prostrations. "Come, let us worship" (3). And the First Hour. At the First Hour, Troparion, and after "Our Father," Kontakion of the feast. At the end, 3 great prostrations with the prayer, and Dismissal, and the customary procession into the narthex.*

*At the third hour of the day, the semantron is struck, and we make a procession outside the monastery. And having returned, we chant the Hours with the censer and the reading of the Four Gospels; and we say at the Hours the Troparion and Kontakion of the feast. At the Ninth Hour, without "It is meet" and without prostrations. The Beatitudes rapidly, without singing, and "Remember us," and the rest. Kontakion of the feast. "Lord, have mercy" (40), and 3 great prostrations. Then the prayer, and Dismissal.*

*Then we begin Vespers, with Kathismata and without prostrations. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera Idiomela of the day to 5, and of the feast to 5. Glory, of the day, Tone 6: "When Thou shalt come." Both now, of the feast, the same Tone: "He was sent."*

*"He was sent." Entrance with the Gospel. Prokeimenon and reading of the day, and of the feast (2). "Let my prayer arise," and 3 prostrations. Then the Little Litany. And the exclamation afterward. And we chant the Trisagion: "Holy God." Prokeimenon, Epistle, Gospel of the feast and of the day. And the rest of the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom.[^210] Communion hymn of the feast. At the refectory, we partake of cooked food with olive oil, and we drink wine.*

*And we chant Small Compline without prostrations. And we chant upon it the Triode by Sir Andrew of Crete appointed.*

## Great and Holy Tuesday: Matins[^211]

*At Matins: Alleluia, in Tone 8. Troparion, Tone 8 (thrice): "Behold, the Bridegroom comes at midnight..." as on Great Monday (see fol. 35)[^211].*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Възнесны́йся на крⷵтъ):*[^212]
Let us love the Bridegroom, O brethren! Let us adorn our lamps, shining with virtues and orthodox faith, that like the wise virgins of the Lord we may be ready to enter with Him unto the marriage banquet: for the Bridegroom, as God, bestows upon all an incorruptible crown!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*After the 2nd Psalter reading, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^213]
The lawless council against Thee, O Savior, being grievously gathered together by the priests and scribes in malice, stirred up Judas unto betrayal; therefore brazenly he went forth, saying unto the lawless people: "What will ye give me, and I will deliver Him into your hands?" From this condemnation deliver our souls, O Lord!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*After the 3rd Psalter reading, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^214]
Judas in his mind is overcome by the love of money; raging, he is moved against the Master; he deliberates and studies betrayal; he falls from the light, receiving darkness; he bargains the price, he sells the One Who is beyond price; therefore the wretch finds hanging as the reward for what he has done, and a bitter death, having betrayed Thee, the Lord! Deliver us from his portion, O Christ God, granting forgiveness of transgressions unto those who celebrate Thy most pure Passions with love!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*Then Psalm 50. Then: "Save, O God, Thy people..." Lord, have mercy (12). We chant the Little Litany of the Deacon.*

*Tone 2. Kontakion, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^215]
If thou ponderest the hour of the end, O wretched soul, and fearest the cutting down of the fig tree: labor industriously with the talent given unto thee, watching and crying aloud: "May we not remain outside the bridal chamber of Christ!"

*Ikos:*
Why art thou despondent, O my wretched soul? Why dost thou imagine unprofitable anxieties out of season? Why art thou occupied with passing things? The last hour is at hand, and we must depart from things present: while thou hast time, arise and cry aloud: "I have sinned against Thee, my Savior! Cut me not down like the barren fig tree; but as Thou art compassionate, O Lord, have mercy upon those who cry out with fear: May we not remain outside the bridal chamber of Christ!"

*Synaxarion for Great and Holy Tuesday. Bless, Father.*

*Verses:*  
Great Tuesday brings forth the Ten Virgins,  
Bearing the victory of the blameless Master.[^216]

*On Great and Holy Tuesday, we commemorate the Parable of the Ten Virgins:[^217] for as the Lord was ascending to Jerusalem and going forth unto His Passion, He spoke such parables unto His disciples, which parables He also directed toward the Jews. The Parable of the Ten Virgins He delivered turning it unto almsgiving, teaching together therewith that everyone must be ready before the end. For He had instructed them much concerning virginity and concerning eunuchs; and though virginity possesses great glory—for it is great in truth—yet lest anyone, fulfilling this one work alone, should neglect the others, and especially almsgiving, by which the lamp of virginity is illuminated, the Holy Gospel introduces this parable. And He names five Wise, because together with virginity they provided abundant and rich oil of almsgiving; and five Foolish, because they also possessed virginity, yet not equal almsgiving.[^218] On this account they are called foolish: for having accomplished the greater, they neglected the lesser, and differed in nothing from harlots; for while harlots were overcome in their bodies, these were overcome by their possessions. But as the night of this present life passed away, all the virgins slumbered—that is, they died; for death is called sleep. And while they were sleeping, a cry arose at midnight; and those who had abundantly supplied oil, the doors being opened, entered in together with the Bridegroom. But the foolish, having insufficient oil, sought oil after their sleep. And the wise, though desiring to give unto them, could not do so before entering, but answered, saying: "Lest there be not enough for us and you, go rather to them that sell—that is, unto the poor—and buy." But it was not easy, for after death this is impossible; as Abraham also showed concerning the Rich Man and Lazarus, saying: "There is a great gulf fixed, so that they who would pass from hence to you cannot." But the foolish, having arrived unenlightened, cry aloud thus, knocking at the gate: "Lord, Lord, open unto us!" But the Lord Himself through Himself gives that terrible answer, saying: "Depart, I know you not!" For how indeed shall ye behold the Bridegroom, not possessing the very mercy that is eternal? On account of this type, therefore, the Parable of the Ten Virgins was appointed by the God-bearing Fathers to be set here, instructing us ever to watch and to be ready for the meeting of the true Bridegroom through good deeds, and above all through almsgiving; for unknown is the day...*

*...unknown is the day and the hour of the end: even as through Joseph chastity is commended, and through the fig tree spiritual fruit is ever brought forth. For he that visibly performs one single and greater work, yet neglects the others, and especially almsgiving, enters not with Christ into eternal rest, but turns back put to shame. For nothing is more sorrowful and full of shame than virginity overcome by possessions! But O Bridegroom Christ, unite us with the wise virgins, and number us among Thine elect flock, and have mercy upon us, now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Then the Two-Ode Canon, whose acrostic is: TRITITE (that is: On the Third Day). The composition of the Lord Cosmas.[^219] The Heirmos twice, and the troparia to 6. The final Heirmos, both choirs together. Tone 2.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The three venerable children, not submitting unto the tyrant's decree, when cast into the furnace confessed God, singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"[^220]

*Troparion 1:*
Let us cast slothfulness far from us, and with glowing lamps let us meet the immortal Bridegroom, Christ, with singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, crying aloud!"

*Troparion 2:*
May the communicative oil of our soul suffice in our vessels, that we may not expend the season of honoring in buying, singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion 3:*
As many as have received the talent from God, an equally powerful grace: by the help of Christ Who gave it, increase it, singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him above all unto the ages.*

*And again both choirs sing the Heirmos: The three venerable children...*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thou who hast contained in thy womb the uncontainable God, and hast brought forth joy unto the world: we sing of thee, O All-Holy Virgin![^221]

*Troparion 1:*
Unto the disciples the Good One said: "Watch! For in what hour the Lord shall come, ye know not, to render unto each!"

*Troparion 2:*
At Thy second and dreadful coming, O Master, number me with the sheep on Thy right hand, overlooking the multitude of my transgressions!

*And again instead of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos: Thou who hast contained in thy womb...*

*Exapostilarion, Tone 3 (thrice):*
Thy bridal chamber do I see adorned, O my Savior, and I have no wedding garment that I may enter therein; enlighten the vesture of my soul, O Giver of light, and save me![^222]

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and we chant Stichera Idiomela, Tone 1:*
Into the splendors of Thy saints, how shall I enter being unworthy? For if I dare to enter into the bridal chamber, my garment reproaches me, that it is not a wedding garment, and being bound I shall be cast out by the Angels! Cleanse, O Lord, the defilement of my soul, and save me, as Thou art the Lover of mankind! *(Twice)*

*Tone 2:*
Slumbering in spiritual sloth, I have not acquired a burning lamp from virtues, O Bridegroom Christ, and I am likened unto the foolish virgins, wandering about in the time of labor: shut not against me the loving-kindness of Thy compassions, O Master; but having shaken off my darkened sleep, raise me up, and lead me with the wise virgins into Thy bridal chamber, where the voice of those who feast is pure...

...where the voice of those who feast is pure, and who cry unceasingly: "Lord, glory to Thee!" *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Tone 4:*
Hearing the condemnation of him that hid the talent, O soul, hide not the word of God! Proclaim His marvelous works, that multiplying the gift, thou mayest enter into the joy of thy Lord!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela, Tone 6:*
Come, ye faithful, let us labor eagerly for the Master: for He distributs riches unto His servants, and according to the measure let each of us multiply the talent of grace![^223] Let one bring forth wisdom through good works; let another accomplish a radiant service; let another share the word with the untaught in the mystery; and let another distribute his wealth unto the poor: for thus we shall multiply the great reward, and as faithful stewards of grace we shall be accounted worthy of the Master's joy! Of this make us worthy, O Christ God, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy...*

*Tone the same:*
When Thou shalt come in glory with the Angelic Hosts, and shalt sit upon the throne of judgment, O Jesus: do not separate me, O Good Shepherd! For Thou knowest the paths on the right, but crooked are those on the left: destroy me not therefore with the goats abominable in sins; but having numbered me with the sheep on the right hand, save me, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us...*
O Bridegroom, fairer in beauty than all the sons of men, Who hast called us unto the spiritual banquet of Thy bridal chamber: strip off the unsightly form of my sins through the communion of Thy Passions, and having adorned me with the robe of glory of Thy beauty, show me to be a radiant guest of Thy kingdom, as Thou art compassionate!

*Glory, both now, Tone 7:*
Behold, the Master entrusts thee with the talent, O my soul: receive the gift with fear; lend unto Him that gave it; distribute unto the poor, and acquire the Lord as a friend, that thou mayest stand on His right hand when He shall come in glory, and mayest hear the blessed voice: "Enter, good servant, into the joy of thy Lord!" Of this make me worthy, O Savior, who have gone astray, for the sake of Thy great mercy!

*And the rest of the sequence, according to custom. The Hours with the customary Psalter readings and the reading of the Tetraevangelion.*

## Great and Holy Tuesday: Sixth Hour[^224]

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 4:*
Unto those who sin beyond measure, abundantly grant forgiveness, O Savior, and account us worthy to worship Thy Holy Resurrection without condemnation, through the prayers of Thy Most Pure Mother, O Thou Who alone art abundant in mercy!

*Glory, at the end; Both now, without.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 129 LXX, Tone 6: For with the Lord there is mercy, and with Him is plenteous redemption.*  
*Verse: Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord; Lord, hear my voice.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel (Ezekiel 1:21–28 LXX):*[^225]
When the living creatures went, the wheels went; and when they stood, they stood; and when they were lifted up from the earth, they were lifted up with them: for the spirit of life was...

...was in the wheels. And the likeness above the head of the living creatures was as a firmament, as the appearance of crystal, stretched out over their wings above. And under the firmament their wings were straight, trembling one toward another: each one had two joined covering their bodies. And I heard the voice of their wings when they flew, as the voice of abundant waters, as the voice of God Shaddai; when they went, the voice of speech was as the voice of a host; and when they stood, their wings ceased. And behold, there was a voice from above the firmament that was over their heads: when they stood, their wings were let down. And above the firmament that was over their heads was the likeness of a throne, as the appearance of a sapphire stone; and upon the likeness of the throne was the likeness as the appearance of a man above upon it. And I saw as the appearance of amber, as the appearance of fire within it round about, from the appearance of the loins and upward; and from the appearance of the loins even downward, I saw as the appearance of fire, and the radiance round about. As the appearance of the bow that is in the cloud in the day of rain, so was the appearance of the radiance round about: this was the appearance of the likeness of the Glory of the Lord.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 130 LXX, Tone 4: Let Israel hope in the Lord, from henceforth and unto the ages.*  
*Verse: Lord, my heart was not exalted, neither were mine eyes lifted up.*

*And the rest. Together we chant also the Ninth Hour, with Kathismata; and we read the Four Gospels. And we chant the Beatitudes, and "Remember us, O Lord," with prostrations, and all the rest, even as was indicated on Great Monday (see fol. 41)[^224].*

## Great and Holy Tuesday: Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts[^226]

*On the same Great and Holy Tuesday evening.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and we chant Stichera Idiomela, Tone 1:*
Into the splendors of Thy saints, how shall I enter being unworthy? For if I dare to enter into the bridal chamber, my garment reproaches me, that it is not a wedding garment, and being bound I shall be cast out by the Angels! Cleanse, O Lord, the defilement of my soul, and save me, as Thou art the Lover of mankind! *(Twice)*

*Tone 2:*
Slumbering in spiritual sloth, I have not acquired a burning lamp from virtues, O Bridegroom Christ, and I am likened unto the foolish virgins, wandering about in the time of labor: shut not against me the loving-kindness of Thy compassions, O Master; but having shaken off my darkened sleep, raise me up, and lead me with the wise virgins into Thy bridal chamber, where the voice of those who feast is pure, and who cry unceasingly: "Lord, glory to Thee!"

*Tone 4:*
Hearing the condemnation of him that hid the talent, O soul, hide not the word of God! Proclaim His marvelous works, that multiplying the gift, thou mayest enter into the joy of thy Lord! *(Twice)*

*Tone 6:*
O Bridegroom, fairer in beauty than all the sons of men, Who hast called us unto the spiritual banquet of Thy bridal chamber: strip off the unsightly form of my sins, and through the communion of Thy Passions, having clothed me in the robe of glory of Thy beauty, show me to be a radiant guest of Thy kingdom, as Thou art compassionate!

*Tone 6:*
Come, ye faithful, let us labor eagerly for the Master...

...for He distributs riches unto His servants, and according to the measure let each of us multiply the talent of grace! Let one bring forth wisdom through good works; let another accomplish a radiant service; let another share the word with the untaught in the mystery; and let another distribute his wealth unto the poor: for thus we shall multiply what was lent, and as faithful stewards of grace we shall be accounted worthy of the Master's joy! Of this make us worthy, O Christ God, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Tone 7:*
Behold, the Master entrusts thee with the talent, O my soul: receive the gift with fear; lend unto Him that gave it; distribute unto the poor, and acquire the Lord as a friend, that thou mayest stand on His right hand when He shall come in glory, and mayest hear the blessed voice: "Enter, good servant, into the joy of thy Lord!" Of this make me worthy, O Savior, who have gone astray, for the sake of Thy great mercy!

*Glory, both now, Tone 6:*
When Thou shalt come in glory with the Angelic Hosts, and shalt sit upon the throne of judgment, O Jesus: do not separate me, O Good Shepherd! For Thou knowest the paths on the right, but crooked are those on the left: destroy me not therefore with the goats abominable in sins; but having numbered me with the sheep on the right hand, save me, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Entrance with the Holy Gospel.*

*O Joyful Light:*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 131 LXX, Tone 6: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the Ark of Thy sanctification.*  
*Verse: Lord, remember David, and all his meekness.*

*Reading from Exodus (Exodus 2:5–10 LXX):*[^227]
The daughter of Pharaoh came down to wash herself at the river; and her maidens walked along by the river's side; and when she saw the ark in the flags, she sent her maid to fetch it. And when she had opened it, she saw the child weeping in the ark; and the daughter of Pharaoh had compassion on him, and said: "This is one of the Hebrews' children." Then said his sister to Pharaoh's daughter: "Wilt thou that I go and call to thee a nurse of the Hebrew women, that she may nurse the child for thee?" And Pharaoh's daughter said to her: "Go." And the maid went and called the child's mother. And Pharaoh's daughter said unto her: "Take this child away, and nurse it for me, and I will give thee thy wages." And the woman took the child, and nursed it. And the child grew, and she brought him unto Pharaoh's daughter, and he became her son; and she called his name Moses, saying: "Because I drew him out of the water."

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 132 LXX, Tone 4: Behold now, what is good or what is pleasant, but for brethren to dwell together in unity!*  
*Verse: Like the precious ointment upon the head, that ran down upon the beard, even Aaron's beard.*

*Reading from Job (Job 1:13–22 LXX):*[^228]
It came to pass on that day, when Job's sons and his daughters were eating and drinking wine in their eldest brother's house, that there came a messenger unto Job, and said: "The oxen were plowing, and the she-asses feeding beside them; and the Sabeans fell upon them, and took them away, and have slain the servants with the edge of the sword; and I only am escaped...

...and I only am escaped alone to tell thee." While he was yet speaking, there came also another messenger unto Job, and said: "The fire of God is fallen from heaven, and hath burned up the sheep, and consumed the shepherds likewise; and I only am escaped alone to tell thee." While he was yet speaking, there came also another messenger unto Job, and said: "The Chaldeans made out three bands, and surrounded the camels, and have carried them away, and have slain the servants with the edge of the sword; and I only am escaped alone to tell thee." While he was yet speaking, there came also another messenger, and said unto Job: "Thy sons and thy daughters were eating and drinking wine in their eldest brother's house; and behold, there came a great wind from the wilderness, and smote the four corners of the house, and it fell upon the young men, and they are dead; and I only am escaped alone to tell thee." Then Job arose, and rent his mantle, and shaved his head, and sprinkled dust upon his head, and fell down upon the ground, and worshipped the Lord, and said: "Naked came I out of my mother's womb, and naked shall I return thither: the Lord gave, and the Lord hath taken away; as it pleased the Lord, so is it done: blessed be the name of the Lord unto the ages!" In all these things that befell him, Job sinned not, nor with his lips, nor did he charge God with folly.

*And after "Let my prayer arise": Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 102 (Matthew 24:36–26:2), and the rest of the Presanctified Liturgy.*

*If the Presanctified Liturgy is not celebrated, see the rubric for Great Monday (see fols. 43 and 44)[^226].*

## Great and Holy Tuesday: Great Compline[^229]

*At Compline we chant the Triode, the composition of Sir Andrew of Crete, Tone 2.*

*Ode 2. Heirmos:*
Make my barren mind fruitful, O God, as a cultivator of good things and a planter of blessings, through Thy tender mercy![^230]

*Troparion 1:*
The time of the end is at hand, let us turn back therefore, Christ teaches: for He shall come in a moment, He shall come and shall not tarry, to judge the whole world!

*Troparion 2:*
Revealing the suddenness of His coming, Christ spoke of the unexpected corruption in the days of Noah of old, about to be brought upon the earth!

*Troparion 3:*
The bridal chamber is opened, the divine wedding banquet is adorned with Him; the Bridegroom is near, calling us: let us prepare ourselves therefore!

*Troparion 4:*
The house of Simon contained Thee, O Jesus the King, Who art uncontainable everywhere; and the sinful woman anointed Thee with myrrh!

*Troparion 5:*
Filled with mystical fragrance, the woman was delivered from the former stench of many sins, O Savior; for Thou dost nourish the ointment of life!

*Troparion 6:*
Being Thyself the heavenly Food, the Life of those that hunger: Thou didst eat with men, O Christ, manifesting Thy condescension!

*Troparion 7:*
The senseless disciple, having renounced Thee, O Christ, stirred up the assembly of lawless people against Thee, turning toward betrayal!

*Glory:*
Together with the Father I worship Thee, O Son, and the Right Spirit, one Nature, and I sing praise, dividing the Persons and uniting the Essence!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
The Ewe that bare Thee, the Shepherd and the Lamb, ever...

...ever prays, O Jesus God, for all the people that believe in Thee!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The Angelic Choir" (Ли́къ А҆́гглскїй):*[^231]
Behold, the deceitful council is truly gathered together, raging to condemn as a criminal the Lord Who sits on high as the Judge of all! Now Herod convens with Pilate; Annas together with Caiaphas question the One Longsuffering!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who prefigured thy virginity of old in the Bush unto Moses upon Mount Sinai: sing, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages![^232]

*Troparion 1:*
He that holds the ages, not being ignorant of the time of our end, foretold that that day was unknown; but He set boundaries for all unto humility of mind!

*Troparion 2:*
I have become likened unto a tree, a field, and a dwelling, in living for myself, O my soul: therefore acquire a heart ready toward God, that thou mayest leave behind nothing for the corruption of the flesh!

*Troparion 3:*
When Thou shalt sit as the Judge, separating the goats from the sheep as Thou hast said, O Savior Shepherd: deliver us from that standing, and deprive us not of Thy divine right hand!

*Troparion 4:*
Thou art our Pascha, sacrificed for sin as a Lamb and an offering, and the cleansing of transgressions: and Thy divine Passions we exalt, O Christ, unto all ages!

*Troparion 5:*
Not unto the Pharisees, O Savior, nor unto Simon alone wast Thou pleased to enter into the house for food: but already both publicans and the harlot draw forth Thy tender mercy!

*Troparion 6:*
O blessed hands! O blessed tears and lips of the chaste harlot, with which she poured out ointment for the Savior upon Thy feet, wiping them and kissing them fervently!

*Troparion 7:*
As Thou didst recline at meat, O Word, the woman approached and, weeping at Thy feet, O Savior, poured out an alabaster box of myrrh upon the head of Thee, the immortal Myrrh!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*

*Trinity Troparion:*
With the Father we glorify the Son and the Holy Spirit, the Holy Trinity in one Godhead, crying aloud: "Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou unto the ages!"

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Through the prayers of Thy Most Pure Mother, O Christ, and of Thine Apostles, send down Thy mercies upon us richly, and grant Thy peace unto Thy people!

*Then: We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord... Heirmos: Unto Him Who prefigured thy virginity...*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Her who conceived in her womb according to the flesh beyond the laws of nature, the Word Who shone forth agelessly from the Father, the one Mother of God we faithfully magnify![^233]

*Troparion 1:*
It is the time for labor, the intention for salvation: having taken the talent, the ancient image, O my soul, purchase eternal life!

*Troparion 2:*
Having adorned our souls as radiant lamps: as the Bridegroom approaches, before the shut doors, let us enter with Him unto the incorruptible wedding banquet!

*Troparion 3:*
Being the Living Bread, O Jesus, Thou eatest with Simon the Pharisee, that the harlot may acquire Thine unbought grace through the outpouring of myrrh!

*Troparion 4:*
Desiring to show unto all the transcendent humility of Thine essence, O Jesus, Thou didst eat, supping in the house of Simon, O Food of the hungry!

*Troparion 5:*
Approaching...

...Approaching, the woman poured out myrrh upon Thy feet, O Savior, filling them with sweet fragrance, and being herself filled with the ointment of the cleansing of deeds![^234]

*Troparion 6:*
"Defiled are my hands, harlot-like are the lips within me; unclean is my life, corrupted are my members: but loose me and forgive me!" cries the harlot unto Christ.

*Troparion 7:*
"With spices I am rich, but in virtues I am destitute: what I have I bring unto Thee; give Thyself what Thou hast, and loose me and forgive me!" cries the harlot unto Christ.

*Troparion 8:*
"Myrrh from me is corruptible, myrrh from Thee is of life: for Myrrh is Thy Name poured forth upon the unworthy; but loose me and forgive me!" cries the harlot unto Christ.

*Glory:*
Unoriginate art Thou, O Father; uncreated, O Son; co-enthroned, O Spirit: one Trinity in Essence, and three Persons, one true God!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, hope of those that ever honor thee: cease not praying Him that was born of thee, to deliver me from afflictions and all temptations!

*Then the Heirmos: Her who conceived in her womb... And the rest as was indicated on folio 46 (see leaf p50).*

## Typikon for the Feast of the Annunciation on Great and Holy Wednesday[^235]

*It is necessary to know: if the Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos happens to fall on Great Wednesday.*

*On Tuesday evening: At Vespers, we do not chant the Kathisma, and we make no prostrations. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera Idiomela of the day (5), and of the feast (5). Glory, of the day, Tone 6: "Slumbering in spiritual sloth." Both now, of the feast, Tone 6: "He was sent." Entrance with the Gospel. Prokeimenon, and reading of the day, and of the feast (3). "Let my prayer arise," and 3 prostrations. Gospel of the day, and the rest of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts. And after "Now the powers of heaven," 3 prostrations; and at "Blessed be the name," 3 prostrations. At the refectory, wine and cooked food with olive oil.*

*At the sixth hour of the night, the bell is struck heavily, and the great iron semantron.[^236] And we chant Great Compline. The priest censes according to custom, and after "Glory to God in the highest," procession into the narthex, and we chant the Stichera of the feast. Glory, both now, of the feast, Tone 6: "Gabriel proclaimed." At the Aposticha, of the feast, Tone 1, with their verses. Glory, both now, of the feast, the same Tone: "Today is joy." At the blessing of the loaves: Troparion of the feast (3), and the reading of the feast.*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the feast (3). And after the customary Kathismata, Sessional Hymns in the Triodion, and the reading of the day. After the 3rd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the feast, and the reading of the feast. Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, and the reading of the feast. Songs of Ascent: 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon: "Proclaim from day to day the salvation of our God." "Let every breath." Gospel of the feast. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the feast. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos to 14; Katavasia: the Heirmos by both choirs together. Where there is the Three-Ode Triode, we chant the Canon of the feast with the Heirmos to 6, and the Triode to 8; Katavasia: the Heirmos of the Triode by both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of the day, Sessional Hymn and the reading of the feast: "Again joy." After Ode 6, Kontakion of the feast and Ikos. At Ode 9, we do not chant "More honorable." Exapostilarion of the feast. Glory, of the day. Both now, of the feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the feast to 4. Glory, of the day, Tone 6: "Today Christ comes." Both now, of the feast, Tone 6: "The mystery hidden from the ages." At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela of the day, with their customary verses: "We were filled," verse 6; and "Let the brightness": Glory, of the feast, Tone 8: "Let the heavens rejoice." Both now, of the day, the same Tone: "Lord, the woman fallen into many sins." And the rest as written before (see fols. 31 and 47)[^236].*

*At the third hour of the day, the semantron is struck, and we make a procession outside the monastery, and having returned we chant the Hours with their Kathismata, and the reading of the Four Gospels. At the Hours, Troparion and Kontakion, and the reading of the feast. Likewise also the Ninth Hour, at which we say the Beatitudes, and "Remember us," with "Lord, have mercy," rapidly without singing; after "Our Father," Kontakion of the feast, "Lord, have mercy" (40), and 3 great prostrations, and the Prayer: "O Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who art longsuffering..." Then the Priest: "Wisdom." Little "It is truly meet," Glory, both now, and Dismissal. And the mutual forgiveness to the ground (see below, fol. 93)[^236]. And resting, the Priest recites the Commemoration Book (*Pomiannyk*); then each one receives forgiveness from the other.*

*Then we begin Vespers, with Kathismata, and without prostrations. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera Idiomela of the day to 5, and of the feast to 6. Glory, of the feast, Tone 6: "He was sent." Both now, of the day, Tone 8: "Lord, the woman fallen into many sins." Entrance with the Gospel. "O Joyful Light." Prokeimenon and reading...*

...and the readings of the day, and of the feast (the 4th). "Let my prayer arise," and 3 prostrations. Little Litany, and the Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia of the feast, Gospel of the feast and of the day. And the rest of the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom. Communion hymn of the feast. At the refectory, we eat cooked food with olive oil, and we drink wine; but we do not eat fish under any circumstance.[^237] And we chant Small Compline as written before (see fol. 35)[^237]. We also chant the Triode by Lord Andrew of Crete appointed.

## Great and Holy Wednesday: Matins

*At Matins: Alleluia, in Tone 8. Troparion, Tone 8 (thrice): "Behold, the Bridegroom comes at midnight..." as in the Great Fast book (see fol. 34)[^238].*

*After the 1st Psalter reading, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "For the beauty of virginity" (Красо́тѣ дѣ́вства):*[^239]
The harlot approached Thee, pouring out myrrh with tears upon Thy feet, O Lover of mankind; and she is delivered from the stench of evil deeds at Thy command: but Thy ungrateful disciple, breathing Thy grace, casts it away and cloths himself in stench, selling Thee through love of money! Glory to Thy compassions, O Christ!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*After the 2nd Psalter reading, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^240]
Judas the deceiver, being lustful for love of money, deceitfully meditated betrayal against Thee, the Lord, the Treasure of life; therefore, being intoxicated with greed, he runs unto the Jews, saying unto the lawless ones: "What will ye give me, and I will deliver Him unto you, that ye may crucify Him?" From this condemnation deliver our souls, O Lord!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*After the 3rd Psalter reading, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The Soldiers standing guard at Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^241]
The harlot in weeping cried aloud, O Merciful One, warmly wiping Thy most pure feet with the hair of her head, and sighing from the depths of her soul: "Cast me not away, neither abhor me, O my God, but receive me repenting, and save me, as the only Lover of mankind!"

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*Then the Reading, and Psalm 50. Then the Two-Ode Canon.*

*Two-Ode Canon. The Composition of Lord Cosmas. Its acrostic: ("ΤΕΤРАΔΙ ΨАЛЛΩ"), that is: "On Wednesday I chant." The Heirmos twice, Troparia to 12. Afterward, the Heirmos by both choirs together.*[^242]

*Tone 2. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies: for my spirit hath rejoiced when singing unto Thee: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous besides Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion 1:*
In vain councils the assembly of the lawless is gathered together with evil intent, to deliver Christ the Deliverer as one condemned: unto Whom we chant: Thou art our God, and none is righteous besides Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion 2:*
The fierce assembly of the lawless is seen to possess a God-fighting soul, seeking to slay Christ the righteous as one unprofitable: unto Whom we cry: Thou art our God, and none is holy besides Thee, O Lord!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Възнесны́йся на крⷵтъ):*[^243]
Having lawlessly transgressed more than the harlot, O Good One, I have in no wise brought unto Thee streams of tears; but praying in silence I fall down before Thee, with love kissing Thy most pure feet, that as Master Thou mayest grant me forgiveness of sins, as I cry unto Thee, O Savior: From my defiled deeds deliver me!

*Ikos:*
The woman who before was a harlot suddenly appeared chaste, having come to hate the deeds of shameful sin and bodily pleasures, considering the great shame and judgment of torment which harlots and fornicators shall receive; of whom I am the first, and I fear; yet I abide in evil habit, mindless as I am. But the harlot, having taken fear and hastened, came crying unto the Deliverer: O Lover of mankind and Merciful One, deliver me from my defiled deeds!

*Synaxarion of the Flowery Triodion, on Great and Holy Wednesday, on the Sinful Woman who Anointed the Lord with Myrrh. Bless, Father.*[^244]

*Verses:*
The woman who laid myrrh upon the Body of Christ,
Prevented Nicodemus's myrrh and aloes.

On Great and Holy Wednesday, the most divine Fathers commanded that remembrance be made of the woman who was a harlot, who anointed the Lord with myrrh, because this took place shortly before the saving Passion.[^245] For when Jesus had entered into Jerusalem and was in the house of Simon the leper, the woman approached Him and poured out that precious myrrh upon His head. It was appointed to be commemorated here in order that, according to the Savior's word, that warmest deed of hers might be proclaimed without omission throughout the world.

Whence was she moved to come? Because she beheld the mercifulness of Christ, and His fellowship with all, and now especially when she saw Him enter into the house of the leper, whom the law commanded to be unclean and excluded from communion. The woman therefore thought: Even as He heals his leprosy, so also will He heal my spiritual infirmity!

And while He was reclining at supper, she poured myrrh upon the top of His head, which was worth three hundred denarii, that is, sixty denarii, nine peniazi, and three silver pieces.[^246] And the disciples rebuked her, especially Judas Iscariot; but Christ defended her, lest they should thwart her good intention; and He also made mention of His burial, turning Judas away from betrayal and deeming the woman worthy of honor, commanding that her good work should be proclaimed everywhere.

It is necessary to know that this woman is thought by some to be one and the same in all four Evangelists; but it is not so.[^247] For concerning three of them, as the divine Chrysostom saith, it is indeed one and the same, who was also named a harlot; but in John it is no longer so, but a certain good and marvelous woman of honorable life, Mary the sister of Lazarus, who was not a harlot, and was beloved by Christ.

Now this Mary, six days before Pascha, in her own house in Bethany, while He reclined at supper, performed the anointing with myrrh, pouring it upon the extremities of His feet, wiping them with the hair of her head, expending an abundance of price upon Him, and bringing myrrh unto Him as unto God. For she knew assuredly that oil was offered in sacrifices unto God, and priests were anointed with myrrh; and Jacob of old anointed a pillar unto God. And she brought it unto Him, offering a gift clearly unto the Master as unto God, and again on account of the life of her brother. Therefore no reward was promised unto her...

...no reward was promised unto her, even though Judas himself murmured, being covetous. But the other woman, that is, the harlot, two days before Pascha, while Christ was still in that same Bethany, though in the house of Simon the leper, reclining at supper then also, poured that precious myrrh upon His head, as the sacred Matthew and Mark narrated.[^248] Concerning this harlot the disciples also were indignant, foreseeing clearly that Christ was prepared for mercy. And unto her the reward was given, that her good work should be praised everywhere throughout the whole world.

Common opinion saith that there was one woman, but he of the Golden Tongue saith that there were two women. Yet there are some who say that there were three women: two being the aforesaid, when the Passion of Christ was drawing near to the doors; and the third a different one before them, or rather in the midst of the Gospel preaching, who did this deed, and she was both a harlot and a sinner. And this was inside the house, not of the leper, but of Simon the Pharisee, pouring out myrrh upon the feet of Christ alone, and in private; when one Pharisee alone was offended, and unto her the Savior granted the reward of the forgiveness of sins.

Concerning this woman alone the divine Luke narrats in his Gospel in the midst, as was said, in the Seventh Chapter.[^249] For after the account of this harlot, he immediately adds, saying: "And it came to pass afterward, that He went throughout every city and village, preaching and showing the glad tidings of the kingdom of God." From which it is evident that this took place not at the time of the Passion.

It is considered therefore from the time, and from those who received Him, and from the places, persons, and houses, and moreover from the manner of the anointing with myrrh, that these women were three: two indeed harlots, and the third Mary the sister of Lazarus, shining with an honorable life. And one house was that of Simon the Pharisee, another that of Simon the leper in Bethany, and yet another house that of Mary and Martha, the sisters of Lazarus, in that same Bethany.

From these things it is to be understood that there were also two suppers for Christ, both in Bethany: one six days before Pascha, in the house of Lazarus, when Lazarus also ate with Him; as also the Son of Thunder according to John testifies, saying thus: "Six days before Pascha, Jesus came to Bethany, where Lazarus was which had been dead, whom He raised from the dead. There they made Him a supper, and Martha served; but Lazarus was one of them that sat at the table with Him. Then took Mary a pound of ointment of spikenard, very costly, and anointed the feet of Jesus, and wiped His feet with her hair." And the other supper was for Him two days before Pascha, while Christ was still in Bethany, in the house of Simon the leper, when the harlot also came unto Him, pouring out precious myrrh, as the divine Matthew narrats, saying that Christ said unto the disciples: "Ye know that after two days is the feast of the Passover"; and shortly after he adds: "Now when Jesus was in Bethany, in the house of Simon...

...the leper, there came unto Him a woman having an alabaster box of very precious ointment, and poured it on His head, as He sat at meat." With which Mark also agrees, saying: "After two days was the feast of the Passover, and of unleavened bread... and being in Bethany in the house of Simon the leper, as He sat at meat, there came a woman," and what follows.

Those who contend and say that it was one and the same woman who anointed the Lord with myrrh in the four Evangelists, think that Simon the Pharisee and the leper were one and the same, whom some also fancied to be the father of Lazarus and of his sisters Mary and Martha; and that it was one and the same supper, and that same single house of his in Bethany, in which the furnished Upper Room was prepared; and they do not think well that it was the Mystical Supper.[^250] For these two suppers were outside Jerusalem in Bethany, six days and two days (as was said) before the legal Passover, when the women brought myrrhs variously unto Christ.

But the Mystical Supper, and the furnished Upper Room within the city of Jerusalem, was beautifully adorned one day before the legal Passover and the Passion of Christ: some say in the house of an unknown man, but others say in the house (as they say) of the Beloved Disciple John on Holy Zion; where the disciples also were hidden for fear of the Jews, and where the touching of Thomas took place after the Resurrection, and the coming of the Holy Spirit at Pentecost, and certain other ineffable and mystical things were accomplished.

Therefore this opinion of Chrysostom of the Golden Head seems to me to be truly right rather than otherwise: that there were indeed two women: one, as was said, in the three Evangelists, being a harlot and sinner, who poured myrrh upon the head of Christ; and the other in John, Mary the sister of Lazarus, who brought this and poured it upon the very divine feet of Christ. And that the suppers in Bethany were different, and different was the Mystical Supper.

And evidently from these things, after the narrative concerning the harlot, the Savior sends the disciples unto the city, to prepare the Passover: "Go ye," He saith, "into the city to such a man, and say unto him: The Master saith, I will keep the Passover at thy house with My disciples. And behold, there shall meet you a man bearing a pitcher of water; and he will show you a large upper room furnished; there make ready for us." And they having gone, he saith, found as He had commanded them, and they made ready the Passover, that is, the legal Passover which was at the doors. Which also having come, He accomplished with the disciples, as the divine Chrysostom saith. Afterward, when the supper was taking place, that is, the Mystical Supper, and the divine Washing having been accomplished in the midst, He immediately sat down, and delivered our Passover upon that very table, as the golden John also recounts. And these things are so.

Now the God-inspired John, and with him also the divine Evangelist Mark, added to the myrrh the word *Pistike*, naming it also very costly; and they thought this *Pistike* was so called as being genuine, unadulterated, unmixed, and refined in purity.[^251] So also this was a certain name of the most excellent and primary myrrh. Mark also adds that the woman brake the vessel out of haste, as being narrow-mouthed, which he also calls an alabaster box. Now this vessel was of glass, as the sacred Epiphanius saith, made without any handles, which is also called a *bykion*.[^252] And that myrrh was compounded of many kinds, but especially of these: flower of myrrh, fragrant cinnamon, that is, aromatic spices, and olive oil.

But Thou Who wast anointed with spiritual myrrh, O Christ God, deliver us from approaching passions and have mercy upon us, as the only Holy One and Lover of mankind! Amen.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^253]
The tyrant's decree having prevailed, the furnace was heated sevenfold of old, in which the Children were not burned, trampling down the king's command; but they cried aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages!

*Troparion 1:*
The woman, pouring out the precious myrrh upon Thy divine and awesome head, O Christ, touched Thy most pure footsteps with defiled hands, and cried aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages!

*Troparion 2:*
She that was guilty of sin washes with tears the feet of Him Who created her, and wips them with her hair; therefore she failed not of redemption for the deeds of her life, but cried aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages!

*Troparion 3:*
Redemption is sacredly wrought for the woman of good disposition, from the saving bowels of compassion and the fountain of tears, wherein having washed herself through confession, she was not ashamed; but cried aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages!

*Then: We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him above all unto the ages. And again the Heirmos: The tyrant's decree having prevailed...*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^254]
With pure souls and undefiled lips, come, let us magnify the undefiled and most pure Mother of Emmanuel, through her bringing prayer unto Him that was born of her: Spare our souls, O Christ God, and save us!

*Troparion 1:*
Ungrateful for the manifestation, and envious with malice, the betrayer sells by word the worthy and divine gift, whereby the debt of sins was loosed: the fierce Judas, stealing divine grace! Spare our souls, O Christ God, and save us!

*Troparion 2:*
Having gone unto the lawless rulers, Judas saith: "What will ye give me, and I will deliver Christ unto you, though none of you seek Him?"—casting away the touch of Christ for gold! Spare our souls, O Christ God, and save us!

*Troparion 3:*
O ungodly one, blinded by the love of money, whence hast thou forgotten that the world is in no wise equal in value to a soul, as thou wast taught? For by despair thou hast brought judgment upon thyself, O running betrayer! Spare our souls, O Christ God, and save us!

*Then the Heirmos: With pure souls and undefiled lips...*

*Exapostilarion (thrice):*[^255]
I see Thy bridal chamber adorned, O my Savior, and I have no garment that I may enter therein; enlighten the vesture of my soul, O Giver of light, and save me!

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and we chant Stichera Idiomela, Tone 1:*[^256]

The harlot, having recognized Thee as God, the Son of the Virgin, cried weeping and praying, as one who had done deeds worthy of tears: "Loose my debt, as I have loosed my hair; love her that loves Thee, who is justly hated; and close unto the publicans will I proclaim Thee, O Benefactor and Lover of mankind!" *(Twice)*

The harlot mixed precious myrrh with tears, and poured it out upon Thy most pure feet, kissing them; straightway Thou didst justify her: grant forgiveness unto us also, O Thou Who didst suffer for our sake, and save us!

When the sinful woman was offering Thee myrrh, then the disciple was agreeing with the lawless. The one rejoiced, pouring out very precious myrrh; the other hastened to sell the One Who is beyond price. She recognized the Master; he separated himself from the Master. She was set free, but Judas became a slave to the enemy. Grievous is slothfulness, and great is repentance! Which grant unto me, O Savior Who didst suffer for our sake, and save us!

O the wretchedness of Judas! He beheld the harlot kissing the footsteps, and he deceitfully meditated the kiss of betrayal. She loosed her hair, but he was bound with fury, bearing foul-smelling malice instead of myrrh: for envy knows not how to choose what is profitable. O the wretchedness of Judas! From which deliver our souls, O God!

*Glory, Tone 2:*
The sinful woman ran unto the myrrh to buy precious ointment to anoint the Benefactor, and unto the seller of myrrh she cried aloud: "Give me myrrh, that I also may anoint Him Who hath cleansed all my sins!"

*Both now, Tone 6:*
She that was submerged in sin found Thee a haven of salvation; pouring out myrrh mingled with tears, she cried unto Thee: "Behold, Thou Who waitest for the repentance of sinners! O Master, save me from the tempest of sin, for Thy great mercy's sake!"

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6:*[^257]
Today Christ comes into the house of the Pharisee, and the sinful woman, approaching His feet, fell prostrate, crying aloud: "Behold her that is submerged in sin, despaired of on account of her deeds, yet not rejected by Thy goodness; and grant me, O Lord, remission of evil deeds, and save me!"

*Verse 1: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced and were glad:*
The harlot spread out her hair unto Thee, the Master; Judas stretched out his hands to the lawless: the one to receive forgiveness, the other to receive silver pieces. Therefore we cry unto Thee, Who wast sold and hast set us free: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse 2: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us, and establish Thou the works of our hands upon us:*
The woman evil-smelling and defiled approached, pouring forth tears, loving Thee, O Savior, and confessing Thy Passion: "How shall I look upon Thee, O Master? For Thou Thyself hast come to save the harlot; from the depths of death raise me up, O Thou Who didst raise Lazarus from the tomb on the fourth day! Receive me, wretch that I am, O Lord, and save me!"

*Verse 3: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord my God, with all my heart, and I will glorify Thy name unto the ages:*
Despaired of for her life...

...Despaired of for her life, and well known for her character, bearing myrrh she approached Thee, crying aloud: "Cast me not away, harlot that I am, O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin; despise not my tears, O Joy of the Angels! But receive me repenting, whom Thou hast not cast off when sinning, O Lord, for Thy great mercy's sake!"

*Glory, both now, Tone 8. The Composition of Kassiani the Nun:*[^258]
Lord, the woman fallen into many sins, having perceived Thy Divinity, taking up the rank of myrrhbearer, with lamentation brings myrrh unto Thee before Thy burial, crying: "Woe is me! for night is unto me the frenzy of unrestrained lust, the dark and moonless love of sin! Receive the fountains of my tears, O Thou Who leadest forth the water of the sea by clouds! Incline Thyself unto the sighing of my heart, O Thou Who didst bow down the heavens by Thine ineffable self-emptying! I will kiss Thy most pure feet, and wipe them again with the hair of my head, whose sound Eve heard with her ears in Paradise at eventide, and hid herself in fear! The multitude of my sins, and the abyss of Thy judgments, who shall search out, O Savior of souls, my Savior? Despise me not, Thy handmaid, O Thou Whose mercy is boundless!"

*Then the First Hour, and Dismissal, and the rest as we indicated before, in the Great Philip Fast book (see fol. 34)[^238].*

## Great and Holy Wednesday: Sixth Hour

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion, Tone 8:*
Today the evil assembly is gathered together, and hath meditated vain things against Thee: today Judas through conspiracy prepares the strangling halter, and Caiaphas unwillingly confesses that Thou dost willingly accept death for all: O our Deliverer, Christ God, glory to Thee!

*Glory, conclusion of the Troparion. Both now, without.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 133, Tone 4: The Lord bless thee out of Zion, Who made heaven and earth. Verse: Behold now, bless ye the Lord, all ye servants of the Lord.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel (Ezekiel 2:3 - 3:3):*[^259]
The Lord said unto me: Son of man, I send thee to the house of Israel, to them that provoke Me, who have provoked Me, they and their fathers, having rejected Me even unto this day. And they are impudent children and stiff-hearted: I send thee unto them, and thou shalt say unto them: Thus saith the Lord God: whether they will hear, or whether they will fear, for they are a provoking house, yet shall they know that thou art a prophet in the midst of them. And thou, son of man, be not afraid of them, neither be dismayed at their face, because they will rage and surround thee on every side, and thou dwellest in the midst of scorpions: be not afraid of their words, and be not dismayed at their face, for they are a provoking house. And thou shalt speak My words unto them, whether they will hear, or whether they will fear...

...or whether they will fear, for they are a house that provokes to anger. And thou, son of man, hear Him that speaks unto thee, and be not thou provoking, like the house that provokes: open thy mouth, and eat that which I give thee. And I looked, and behold, a hand was stretched out unto me, and therein was a roll of a book. And He spread it before me, and it was written within and without; and there was written therein lamentations, and mourning, and woe. And He said unto me: Son of man, eat this roll, and go, speak unto the children of Israel. And I opened my mouth, and He fed me with this roll. And He said unto me: Son of man, thy mouth shall eat, and thy belly shall be filled with this roll that is given thee. And I ate it, and it was in my mouth sweet as honey.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 134, Tone 2: Ye that fear the Lord, bless ye the Lord. Verse: Praise ye the name of the Lord, praise Him, O ye servants of the Lord.*

*Rubric: It is necessary to know: at the last Trisagion of the Hours, the Paraklesiarch strikes the iron semantron, if any brethren have become slack; and when all are gathered into the church, the Abbot saith (or if in a city church, the Priest) the Dismissal Prayer in the hearing of all:*[^260]

"O Master, Great in mercy, Lord Jesus Christ our God..."[^261]—all of us lying upon our knees until the end, and praying likewise within ourselves. Then the Abbot makes a prostration unto the brethren (or the Priest unto the people), saying thus:

"Bless me, holy fathers, and forgive me, a sinner, wherein I have sinned in all my life, and throughout this whole Forty Days, by word, deed, thought, and all my senses!"

And the brethren answer all together:

"May God forgive thee, holy father!"

After this, the Abbot saith the Litany: "Let us pray for our sovereign Ruler..." and the rest. And at the end, the Abbot saith:

"Through the prayers of Thy most pure Mother and of all the saints, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us!"

And all the brethren begin two by two to make metanias unto the Abbot, saying thus: "Forgive me, holy father!" as above, until all have done this. And chanting, we go each unto his own cell.

## Great and Holy Wednesday: Vespers with the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts

*In the evening: We recite the Kathisma: "Unto the Lord in my affliction" (Psalms 119–133), without prostrations.[^262] At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and we chant Stichera Idiomela (or repeatedly at the Aposticha, if there be no Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts), Tone 1:*

The harlot, having recognized Thee as God, the Son of the Virgin, cried weeping and praying, as one who had done deeds worthy of tears: "Loose my debt, as I have loosed my hair; love her that loves Thee, who is justly hated; and close unto the publicans will I proclaim Thee, O Benefactor and Lover of mankind!"

The harlot mixed precious myrrh with tears, and poured it out upon Thy most pure feet, kissing them; straightway Thou didst justify her: grant forgiveness unto us also, O Thou Who didst suffer for our sake, and save us!

When the sinful woman was offering Thee myrrh, then the disciple was agreeing with the lawless. The one rejoiced, pouring out very precious myrrh; the other hastened to sell the One Who is beyond price. She recognized the Master; he separated himself from the Master. She was set free, but Judas became a slave to the enemy. Grievous is slothfulness, and great is repentance! Which grant unto me, O Savior Who didst suffer for our sake, and save us!

O the wretchedness of Judas! He beheld the harlot kissing...

...kissing the footsteps, and he deceitfully meditated the kiss of betrayal. She loosed her hair, but he was bound with fury, bearing foul-smelling malice instead of myrrh: for envy knows not how to choose what is profitable. O the wretchedness of Judas! From which deliver our souls, O God!

*Tone 2:*
The sinful woman ran unto the myrrh to buy precious ointment to anoint the Benefactor, and unto the seller of myrrh she cried aloud: "Give me myrrh, that I also may anoint Him Who hath cleansed all my sins!"

*Tone 6:*
She that was submerged in sin found Thee a haven of salvation; pouring out myrrh mingled with tears, she cried unto Thee: "Behold, Thou Who waitest for the repentance of sinners! O Master, save me from the tempest of sin, for Thy great mercy's sake!"

Today Christ comes into the house of the Pharisee, and the sinful woman, approaching His feet, fell prostrate, crying aloud: "Behold her that is submerged in sin, despaired of on account of her deeds, yet not rejected by Thy goodness; and grant me, O Lord, remission of evil deeds, and save me!"

*If there be no Liturgy, we chant these Stichera at the Aposticha. Tone 6:*[^263]
The harlot spread out her hair unto Thee, the Master; Judas stretched out his hands to the lawless: the one to receive forgiveness, the other to receive silver pieces. Therefore we cry unto Thee, Who wast sold and hast set us free: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Tone 6:*
The woman evil-smelling and defiled approached, pouring forth tears, loving Thee, O Savior, and confessing Thy Passion: "How shall I look upon Thee, O Master? For Thou Thyself hast come to save the harlot; from the depths of death raise me up, O Thou Who didst raise Lazarus from the tomb on the fourth day! Receive me, wretch that I am, O Lord, and save me!"

Despaired of for her life, and well known for her character, bearing myrrh she approached Thee, crying aloud: "Cast me not away, harlot that I am, O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin; despise not my tears, O Joy of the Angels! But receive me repenting, whom Thou hast not cast off when sinning, O Lord, for Thy great mercy's sake!"

*Glory, both now, Tone 8. The Composition of Kassiani the Nun:*
Lord, the woman fallen into many sins, having perceived Thy Divinity, taking up the rank of myrrhbearer, with lamentation brings myrrh unto Thee before Thy burial, crying: "Woe is me! for night is unto me the frenzy of unrestrained lust, the dark and moonless love of sin! Receive the fountains of my tears, O Thou Who leadest forth the water of the sea by clouds! Incline Thyself unto the sighing of my heart, O Thou Who didst bow down the heavens by Thine ineffable self-emptying! I will kiss Thy most pure feet, and wipe them again with the hair of my head, whose sound Eve heard with her ears in Paradise at eventide, and hid herself in fear! The multitude of my sins, and the abyss of Thy judgments, who shall search out, O Savior of souls, my Savior? Despise me not, Thy handmaid, O Thou Whose mercy is boundless!"

*Then the Entrance with the Gospel. "O Joyful Light."*

*Entrance with the Gospel. "O Joyful Light."*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 135, Tone 4: O give thanks unto the God of heaven, for He is good, for His mercy endures unto all ages. Verse: O give thanks unto the God of gods, for His mercy endures unto all ages.*[^264]

*Reading from Exodus (Exodus 2:11–22):*
In those days, when Moses was grown, he went out unto his brethren the children of Israel; and having looked upon their affliction, he spied an Egyptian smiting a certain Hebrew, one of his brethren the children of Israel. And he looked this way and that way, and when he saw no man, he slew the Egyptian, and hid him in the sand. And when he went out the second day, behold, two men of the Hebrews strove together; and he said unto him that did the wrong: Why dost thou smite thy neighbor? And he said: Who made thee a prince and a judge over us? Intendest thou to kill me, as thou killedst the Egyptian yesterday? And Moses feared, and said: Surely this thing is known! Now when Pharaoh heard this thing, he sought to slay Moses. But Moses fled from the face of Pharaoh, and dwelt in the land of Midian; and having come into the land of Midian, he sat down by a well. Now the priest of Midian had seven daughters, who kept the sheep of their father Jethro; and they came and drew water, until they had filled the troughs to water their father Jethro's sheep. And the shepherds came and drove them away; but Moses stood up and helped them, and drew water for them, and watered their sheep. And when they came to Reuel their father, he said unto them: How is it that ye are come so soon today? And they said: An Egyptian delivered us out of the hand of the shepherds, and also drew water enough for us, and watered our sheep. And he said unto his daughters: And where is he? Why is it that ye have left the man? Call him, that he may eat bread. And Moses was content to dwell with the man. And he gave Zipporah his daughter to Moses to wife. And the woman conceived, and bare a son, and Moses called his name Gershom, saying: For I have been a stranger in a strange land. And she conceived again, and bare a second son, and called his name Eliezer, saying: For the God of my father was mine help, and delivered me from the hand of Pharaoh.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 137, Tone 4: O Lord, Thy mercy endures unto all ages; despise not the works of Thine own hands. Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with all my heart, and before the Angels will I sing praise unto Thee.*

*Reading from Job (Job 2:1–10):*
Now there was a day when this came to pass, and the Angels of God came to present themselves before the Lord, and the Devil came also in the midst of them to present himself before the Lord. And the Lord said unto the Devil: Whence comest thou? And the Devil said before the Lord: From going to and fro in the earth, and from walking up and down in it, am I come. And the Lord said unto the Devil: Hast thou considered My servant Job, that there is none like him among those that are upon the earth, a man blameless, true, upright, God-fearing, eschewing every evil? And still he holds fast his integrity. But thou spokest of his possessions...

"...to destroy his substance in vain. And the devil answering said unto the Lord: 'Skin for skin, and all that a man hath will he give for his life. Not so only, but put forth Thine hand, and touch his flesh and his bones: surely he will bless Thee to Thy face?' And the Lord said unto the devil: 'Behold, I deliver him unto thee, only keep his life.' And the devil went out from the presence of the Lord, and smote Job with sore boils from his feet unto his head. And Job took himself a potsherd to scrape away his sores, and sat upon a dunghill outside the city. (And when much time had passed,) his wife said unto him: 'How long wilt thou endure, saying: "Behold, I wait a little longer, looking for the hope of my salvation"? For behold, thy memorial is perished from the earth, the sons and daughters, the pangs and labors of my womb, for whom in vain I labored with sorrows! Thou Thyself sittest in the rot of worms, passing the night under the open sky, while I wander about and am a servant-maid, going from place to place and from house to house, waiting for the sun to go down, that I may rest from my labors and the griefs that now compass me about! But say some word against the Lord, and die!' But Job looked upon her and said: 'Why hast thou spoken as one of the foolish women? If we have received good things from the hand of the Lord, shall we not endure evil things?' In all these things that befell him, Job sinned not, neither with his lips, and he gave no folly unto God." (Job 2:3–10)[^265]

*Then the Deacon: "Let my prayer be set forth..." The Gospel according to Matthew, pericopa 108 (Matt. 26:6–16). And the rest of the Presanctified Gifts; and after the Transfer of the Holy Gifts, three prostrations. And at "Blessed be the name of the Lord," three prostrations. And when we rise at the refectory, in place of "It is truly meet," we chant the Ninth Ode Heirmos of that day. After the Trisagion, the Kontakion in the Triodion. And straightway prostrations made in church cease: both great prostrations, until the bells ring for Matins of the radiant Resurrection of Christ.*[^266]

*At Compline, which we chant in the Narthex:*[^267]
*Three-Ode Canon. The Composition of the Lord Andrew. Tone 6.*
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^268]
The Prophet, having heard of Thy coming, O Lord, was afraid, that Thou wast about to be born of a Virgin and appear unto men, and he said: "I heard the report of Thee and was afraid: glory to Thy power!"

*Troparion 1:*
The furnished Upper Room received Thee, the Creator, and Thy fellow-initiates, and there Thou didst accomplish the Passover, and there Thou didst perform the Mysteries: for there, two of Thy disciples having now been sent, the Passover was prepared for Thee.

*Troparion 2:*
"Go unto such a one," He Who knows all things foretells unto the Apostles; and blessed is he who can faithfully receive the Lord! For he hath pre-prepared the upper room of his heart, and the piety of the supper.

*Troparion 3:*
Unto the Upper Room of the supper Thou didst send Thy disciples to make preparation, O Thou Who art uncontainable by creation and incomprehensible to men; and Thou didst say: "Go and prepare for Me to eat the Passover, which is among the Judeans."

*Troparion 4:*
Thy character was full of the love of money, and thy likeness full of folly, O senseless Judas! For having become a buyer tossed by the wind, thou didst not incline at all unto compassion, but didst shut up the inward parts of thy hard heart, having betrayed the only Compassionate One.

*Troparion 5:*
Thou didst rise up from supper, and didst gird Thy loins with a towel, portraying boundless humility, O all-good Lord, and Thou didst say unto Thy disciples: "As ye see, so do ye in turn, even as ye have seen today."

*Troparion 6:*
Knowing all things present, future, and passing away with time, Thou didst also foreknow the one who was about to betray Thee, and Thou didst say: "One of the disciples will betray Me unto the lawless, the only Sinless One."

*Troparion 7:*
A kiss full of deceit, that "Hail!" accompanied by a weapon, O cruel Judas, thou utterest indeed with the tongue unto union, yet by thy disposition thou givest a sign unto scattering: for deceitfully didst thou take counsel to betray the Goodness unto the transgressors of the Law!

*Troparion 8:*
Thou embracest and dost betray, Judas; thou kissest and art not ashamed, having run forth in deceit! Who that hates kisses? O wretched one! Who that loves sells for a price? The kiss of thy shameless wickedness convicts thy design!

*Troparion 9:*
The disposition of the murderers of God was mingled with the sale of the lover of money: the former indeed armed themselves unto murder, while the latter rushed after the silver pieces; for having then by hanging predetermined the repentance of his evil deeds, he was deprived of life!

*Glory:*
Undivided in Essence, unconfused in Persons, I theologize Thee, the one Triune Godhead, as consubstantial and co-enthroned: I cry aloud unto Thee the great hymn, sung with thrice-holy praise in the highest!

*Both now. Theotokion:*
A conception beyond speech, a birth-giving beyond nature is Thine, O Birthgiver of God! For both were from the Spirit and not of seed, concealed from the laws of nature, as incorrupt and beyond the nature of every childbearing: for God was the One born of thee, O Virgin!

*And again the same Heirmos, both choirs together.*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^269]
Eating as with Thy disciples, Thou didst mystically reveal Thy holy decease, whereby we have been set free, who honor Thy precious Sufferings!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^270]
Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and before Whom Cherubim and Seraphim tremble, let every breath and creation praise, bless, and supreme exalt unto the ages!

*Troparion 1:*
Having finished the legal commandment, O Thou Who didst write the tablets of the Law on Sinai, Thou didst eat the ancient and shadowy Passover, and there was accomplished the mystical and living-sacrifice Passover.

*Troparion 2:*
Leading them into mystery, O Christ, Thou didst show forth together unto all the Apostles at supper the wisdom hidden from the ages, which the God-bearing Apostles handed down unto the Churches.

*Troparion 3:*
"One of you will deceitfully betray Me, having sold Me to the Hebrews on this night!" Behold, Christ crying out troubled the companions; then one looked upon another in perplexity and wavered.

*Troparion 4:*
Having humbled Thyself for our sake, O Thou Who art rich, rising from supper and taking a towel, Thou didst gird Thyself therewith, and bowing Thy neck, Thou didst wash the feet of the disciples, and of the betrayer.

*Troparion 5:*
At the height of Thine understanding, beyond mind and ineffable, O Jesus, who will not marvel! How Thou didst stand before clay...

...How Thou didst stand before clay, O Creator of all things, washing their feet and wiping them with a towel!

*Troparion 6:*
The disciple whom the Lord loved, leaning on His breast, said unto Him: "Who is he that betrays Thee?" And Christ unto him: "He that hath now dipped his hand into the dish."

*Troparion 7:*
The disciple, having received the bread, being an unmindful servant of the bread, meditating on the sale, runs unto the Judeans, saying unto the transgressors of the Law: "What will ye give me, and I will deliver Him unto you?"

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord:*
I venerate one God in Essence, three Hypostases distinct, other yet not diverse: for the Godhead is one, the dominion in three, as Father, Son, and Spirit!

*Both now. Theotokion:*
Deliver us, O Jesus our Savior, from the deceit of the evil one and from calamity: receive the Theotokos praying unceasingly; for She is Thy Mother, and She is able to entreat Thee!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, chanting and supremely exalting Him unto the ages. And again the Heirmos: Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify... and a prostration.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^271]
The ineffable birth-giving of a seedless conception, the uncorrupted childbearing of a mother who knew not man: for the birth of God renews nature! Therefore all generations right-believingly magnify thee as the Bride of God.

*Troparion 1:*
The great mystery of Thine Incarnation, when Thou wast reclining at supper with Thy disciples, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst declare in a figure: "Eat the Bread of Life with faith, this is My Body; drink also the Blood poured out from the divine-sacrifice slaughter!"

*Troparion 2:*
The Upper Room was shown to be a heavenly veil, where Christ accomplished the Passover: the bloodless supper and the rational service; and the table of the perfected Mysteries there became an intelligible Altar.

*Troparion 3:*
Christ is the great and eternal Passover, eaten as bread, yet slaughtered as a sheep: for He was offered up as a sacrifice for our sake; of His Body piously, and of His Blood we all mystically partake!

*Troparion 4:*
Blessing the Bread, O Thou Who art the heavenly Bread, giving thanks unto the Father Who begat Thee, taking the Cup also Thou didst give it unto the disciples, crying aloud: "Take, eat; this is My Body, and the Blood of incorruptible life!"

*Troparion 5:*
"Amen," the true vine, Christ, said unto the Apostles: "I will not drink of the fruit of this vine, until I drink it new in the glory of My Father, with you, My heirs!"

*Troparion 6:*
