Dost thou sell the Lord for thirty pieces of silver, and dost thou not consider, O lawless Judas, the mystery of the supper or the precious washing of the feet? O how having slipped by a fall thou hast come unto the end of the world, embracing strangulation!

*Troparion 7:*
Thou didst stretch forth the hands wherewith thou didst receive the bread of incorruption, to receive silver pieces; bringing thy lips unto a deceitful kiss, wherewith thou didst receive the Body and Blood of Christ: but woe unto thee, even as Christ said!

*Troparion 8:*
Christ hath feasted the world, He Who is the heavenly and divine Bread! Come therefore, O lovers of Christ, with lips of clay and pure hearts let us faithfully receive the immolated Passover, which is consecrated among us!

*Glory:*
Let us glorify the Father, let us supremely exalt the Son, let us faithfully worship the Divine Spirit...

...the undivided Trinity, a Unity in Essence: as Light and Lights, Life and Lives, quickening and enlightening the ends of the earth!

*Both now. Theotokion:*
The heavenly Bridal Chamber and ever-virgin Bride, thou alone hast appeared, having carried God and given birth without change unto Him Who was incarnate of thee! Therefore all generations right-believingly magnify thee as the Mother unwedded of God.

*And again the Heirmos: The ineffable birth-giving of a seedless conception...*
*And the rest of Small Compline according to custom. And the Dismissal. And three prostrations.*
*Be it known that from this day forward we do not chant the Midnight Office in church, but in cells, until Saint Thomas Sunday.*[^272]

## Great and Holy Thursday: Matins[^273]

*At Matins, the strike-plate striker (Parakklisiarch) strikes the semantron at the 7th hour of the night. And when we have assembled in church, and the Priest makes the Beginning: "Blessed is our God": We begin the customary service of Matins. And we chant the Psalms. We chant the Litanies. Then "Alleluia" in Tone 8.*

*Troparion, Tone 8 (thrice):*[^274]
When the glorious disciples were illumined at the washing of the feet at supper, then Judas the ungodly, diseased with the love of money, was darkened, and unto the lawless judges he delivers Thee, the righteous Judge! Behold, O lover of possessions, him that for their sake acquired hanging; flee from the unsated soul that dared such things against the Teacher: O Lord, Who art good towards all, glory to Thee!

*Then Psalm 50. We do not say: "Save, O God, Thy people."*
*Canon. The Composition of the Lord Cosmas. Tone 6. The Heirmos on 6. And the Troparia on 4 or on 6, according to the quantity. The Canon whose acrostic is: (ΤΗΙ МАКРАI ПЕМПТHI МАКРОН ҮМNON ЕΞАДΩ). Which is: On the Long Thursday I chant a long hymn.*[^275]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^276]

The Red Sea was cut with a stroke, and the wave-nourished deep was dried up; the same becoming at once a passage for the unarmed, and a tomb for the fully-armed: and a God-pleasing hymn was sung: "Gloriously hath Christ our God been glorified!"

*Troparion 1 (I):*
The Cause and Giver of life, the infinite Wisdom of God, built Herself a house from a pure and unwedded Mother: for having clothed Himself in a bodily temple, gloriously hath Christ our God been glorified!

*Troparion 2 (M):*
Leading Her companions into mystery, the Wisdom of God prepares a soul-nourishing Table, and truly pours out the Cup of immortality for the faithful: let us draw near piously and cry aloud: "Gloriously hath Christ our God been glorified!"

*Troparion 3 (A):*
Let all us faithful give ear unto the uncreated and essential Wisdom of God calling with a lofty proclamation: for She cries out: "Taste and understand that I am Christ; cry aloud: Gloriously hath Christ our God been glorified!"

*At the end, again both choirs together chant the Heirmos as Katavasia.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Being Lord of all and God the Creator, the Passionless One, having impoverished Himself, united the created unto Himself, and being Himself the Passover for which He was about to die, He offered Himself beforehand, crying: "Eat My Body, and be established in faith!"

*Troparion 1 (R):*
With the redeeming Cup of joy of the whole human race, Thine own Cup, O Good One, Thou didst give Thy disciples to drink, having filled it: for Thou Thyself didst offer Thyself as a holy sacrifice, crying: "Drink My Blood, and be established in faith!"

*Troparion 2 (A):*
"The senseless man who is among you, the betrayer," Thou didst foretell unto Thy disciples, O Guileless One, "shall not understand these things, and he, being foolish, will not understand; nevertheless abide ye in Me, and be established in faith!"

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The soldiers standing guard at Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^277]
Thou Who didst make the lakes, the springs, and the seas, instructing us in transcendent humility, didst gird Thyself with a towel and wash the feet of the disciples, humbling Thyself through the abundance of Thy compassion, and raising us up from the abysses of malice, O only Lover of mankind!

*Glory, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃їи вѣ́рѣ):*
Humbling Thyself out of compassion, Thou didst wash the feet of Thy disciples, O Lord, and didst direct them unto the divine course; Peter, who at first refused to be washed, straightway obeyed the divine command, being washed, and diligently entreating Thee to grant us great mercy!

*Both now, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Eating, O Master, with Thy disciples, Thou didst mystically show forth Thy most pure slaughter, whereby we have been delivered from corruption, who honor Thy precious Sufferings!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^278]
Foreseeing the ineffable depth of Thy mystery, O Christ, the Prophet proclaimed: "Thou hast set a firm love of Thy strength, O merciful Father: for Thou hast sent Thine Only-begotten Son, O Good One, as a cleansing into the world!"

*Troparion 1 (E):*
Going forth unto the Passion which pours out compassion for all that are of Adam, O Christ, Thou didst say unto Thy friends: "With desire have I desired to partake of this Passover with you, since the Father hath now sent Me, His Only-begotten, as a cleansing into the world!"

*Troparion 2 (M):*
Partaking of the Cup, Thou didst cry unto the disciples, O Immortal One: "I will not drink henceforth of the fruit of the vine, no longer living with you; for the Father hath now sent Me, His Only-begotten, as a cleansing into the world!"

*Troparion 3 (P):*
"In My Kingdom," Thou didst say unto Thy friends, O Christ, "a new drink beyond speech shall ye drink: for as God with you who are gods...

...with you who are gods will I be," Thou didst say: "for the Father hath now sent Me, His Only-begotten, as a cleansing into the world!"

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^279]
Bound with the bond of love, the Apostles, having offered themselves unto Christ Who rules over all, had their beautiful feet washed, preaching good tidings of peace unto all!

*Troparion 1 (I):*
Holding the uncontainable water and that which is suspended high in the air, bridling the abysses and restraining the seas, the Wisdom of God pours water into the basin, and the Master washes the feet of His servants!

*Troparion 2 (M):*
The Master shows unto the disciples an example of humility: He Who covers the heaven with clouds girds Himself with a towel, and bows His knees to wash the feet of His servants, He in Whose hand is the breath of all existing things!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The lowest abyss of sins hath compassed me about, and no longer enduring the tempest, like Jonah unto the Master I cry aloud: "Lead me up from corruption!"

*Troparion 1 (K):*
"Ye call Me Lord and Teacher, O disciples: for I am the Savior," Thou didst cry aloud; "therefore imitate the example which ye see in Me!"

*Troparion 2 (R):*
"He that hath no defilement needs not to wash his feet; but ye are clean, O disciples, yet not all: for by a single impulse one of you rages!"

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щя):*[^280]
Having received the bread into his hands, the betrayer secretly stretches them forth, and receives the price of Him Who fashioned man with His own hands: and uncorrected remained Judas, a slave and deceiver!

*Ikos:*
Let us all draw near in fear unto the Mystical Table with pure souls, let us receive the Bread abiding with the Master, that we may see how He washes the feet of the disciples, and let us do as we see, submitting one to another and washing one another's feet: for Christ so commanded, having foretold unto His disciples: "Do ye thus"; but Judas, a slave and deceiver, gave no ear!

*Synaxarion, that is, the Assembly, of Great and Holy Thursday, concerning the Washing of the Feet, and the Mystical Supper, and the Prayer, and the Betrayal of Christ: Bless, father, to read.*
*On the Holy Washing of the Feet. Verses:*[^281]
God washes the feet of the disciples at evening:
Whose foot in Eden trod upon the ancient deception.
*On the Mystical Supper. Verses:*
A twofold Supper, for it bears the Passover of the Law:
And the New Passover, the Blood, the Good Body.
*On the Supernatural Prayer. Verses:*
Prayer and dread, drops of sweat flowing:
Christ, Thy face is bathed,
Utterly deceiving the enemy of death.
*On the Betrayal. Verses:*
Why the need of knives? Why pieces of wood for lovers of deceit,
Against Him Who wills to die for the redemption of the world?

*Synaxarion Reading:*[^282]
On Great and Holy Thursday, the divine Fathers, who have ordered all things well, receiving in succession from the holy Apostles and the sacred and divine Gospels, handed down unto us to celebrate four things: the Holy Washing of the Feet, the Mystical Supper, the Transmission plainly of the Dread Mysteries that are for our sake, the Supernatural Prayer, and the Betrayal itself. For the Jewish Passover was to be sacrificed on Friday; and it was fitting that the type should precede the truth, furthermore that Christ should be slaughtered as our Passover. Therefore our Lord Jesus Christ, having anticipated it, as the divine Fathers say, accomplishes it with His disciples on Thursday evening: for that evening and the whole of Friday are reckoned...

...are reckoned as one single day among the Hebrews; for thus do they count, reckoning by a day-and-night. He accomplished this therefore, and then (as some have said) with the disciples according to the Law, of whom one is the divine Chrysostom: first simply standing, and girded, shod with sandals, and leaning on staves, and all other things that the Law commands, lest He should be thought a transgressor of the Law. For Zebedee prepared these things; for he was the one carrying a pitcher of water, as the great Athanasius says, although others have spoken otherwise concerning this.[^283]
Thereafter, showing unto the disciples the more perfect things, He also transmits the Mystery of our Passover in the Upper Room, night having already come: "For when evening was come," it says, "He reclined at table with the twelve." Behold, this was not the legal Passover; for there was a supper, and reclining, and bread, and water, whereas there all things were roasted with fire and unleavened. And before beginning to sup—for thus says the divine Chrysostom—He rises from supper, and lays aside His garments, and pours water into a basin, Himself performing all things with His own hands: both putting Judas to shame, and at the same time reminding the other disciples not to seek preeminence; even as after the Washing He instructs them, saying: "He that desires to be first, let him be the last of all," setting Himself as an example. And it appears that Christ washed Judas before all, who had sat down more promptly and shamelessly; and afterwards He comes unto Peter. But he, being more fervent than the rest, forbids the Teacher, and leaves it again more forcefully. Having therefore washed their feet, and having shown forth an exceeding exaltation through humility, taking His garments again and reclining, He instructs them to love one another and not to seek preeminence.
And as they were eating, He introduces the beginning concerning the Betrayal. When the disciples were troubled at the word, Jesus said unto John alone secretly: "He unto whom I shall give the bread when I have dipped it, he it is that betrays Me." For if Peter had known the word, being more fervent than the others, he would have killed Judas; and again: "He that dips his hand with Me in the dish"; thus both took place.
Then, having waited a little, taking bread, He says: "Take, eat"; and likewise the Cup: "Drink ye all of it," having said: "This is My Blood of the New Covenant; this do ye in remembrance of Me."
*(Behold, He calls the bread His Body, and not azymes [unleavened wafers]. Let those therefore who offer azymes in the sacrifice be put to shame!)*[^284]
And after the bread, Satan entered into Judas: for having tempted him before, he now entered into him completely. And going out, it says, he made an agreement with the chief priests, that he would deliver Him unto them for thirty pieces of silver.
And the disciples after supper went out unto the Mount of Olives, unto a certain place called Gethsemane. After much time Jesus says unto them: "All ye shall be offended because of Me this night." But Peter said: "Even if all, yet will not I deny Thee." And it was late, that is, deep night. But He said: "Before the cock crow twice, thou shalt deny Me thrice." Which...

...Which also came to pass, Peter being seized with boundless fear: God showing forth the infirmity of nature, and at the same time, since He was to commit the inhabited earth unto him, that having understood from his own nature how easily shaken it is, he might be merciful unto sinners. Nevertheless, Peter's threefold denial prefigured the sin of all men against God: the first, the commandment which Adam transgressed; the second, the transgression of the Written Law; and the third, the Incarnation of the Word Himself. Which the Savior afterwards healed again by a threefold repentance, through saying thrice: "Peter, lovest thou Me?"[^285]
After these things He says unto the disciples, showing forth the human nature, that death is fearful unto all: "My soul is sorrowful even unto death"; and having gone forward about a stone's cast, He prayed a third time, saying: "O My Father, if this Cup cannot pass away from Me, except I drink it, Thy will be done! If it be possible, let this Cup pass from Me!" Both as man He said this, and at the same time circumventing the devil with subtlety: that thinking Him to be a mere man, lest by any chance being afraid, he should cut short the mystery because of death on the Cross.[^286]
And returning and finding the disciples submerged in sleep, He turns unto Peter, saying thus: "Could ye not watch with Me one hour?" That is to say: "Thou that saidst thou wouldst strive even unto death, dost thou thus slumber with the rest?"
And having passed over beyond the Brook of Kedron, where was an orchard, He entered there with His disciples; and He had the custom of coming thither often. Therefore Judas also knew the place, who, taking some of the soldiers and the following crowd, came upon Jesus, having given them the sign of a kiss. This he said, because many times when He was held, He passed invisibly from their midst; and here likewise Christ Himself first goes forth unto them, saying: "Whom seek ye?" But they did not yet recognize Him; for it was not a night of stumbling, for there were lanterns, it says, and burning torches; and falling down with fear they drew back. And when they came again, He Himself answered them.
And when Judas had made the agreement, Christ says: "Friend, for what purpose art thou come?" That is: "Unto that for which thou art come, Judas, it is the opportune time." And straightway He says: "Have ye come out as against a robber, with weapons and staves, to take Me?" And they came by night, lest there should be any uproar from the people.
Then the most fervent Peter, drawing a sword—for they were coming from the supper, and were prepared for such things—striking the servant of the High Priest named Malchus, cut off his right ear. But Jesus knew that the High Priests would say that he heard and taught the Law badly. Therefore Christ forbids Peter, that it is not good for a spiritual man of the cloth to make use of a sword; and He heals Malchus's ear.
Having therefore seized Jesus bound, they lead Him unto the courtyard of the High Priest Annas, who was father-in-law to Caiaphas: for there all were assembled who were seeking to murder Christ, the Pharisees and Scribes. And here Peter's denial on account of the maidservant takes place; and as the middle of the night was passing, the cock crew...

...the cock crew the third time, and he, remembering, wept bitterly.
And when morning was already come, from Annas they lead Christ unto the High Priest Caiaphas, where He also received spitting, and false witnesses were summoned; and at daybreak Caiaphas sends Him unto Pilate. And they that brought Him, it says, entered not into the Praetorium, that they might not be defiled, but that they might eat the Passover. It is gathered therefore that the High Priests and Pharisees then acted contrary to the Law, having transferred the Passover. For as the divine Chrysostom says: on that night it was proper for them to eat it, but for the sake of murdering Christ they transferred it; and that it was then necessary for them to eat, Christ showed, having eaten before the night of the supper, then mystically having taught the more perfect things, or as was proper in the legal type, even as it was said to be truly. For John says before the Feast of the Passover, on account of these things having happened on Thursday and its night.[^287]
And we celebrate the commemoration of the dread and ineffable deeds, performing it with fear. By Thine ineffable compassion, O Christ our God, have mercy on us, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^288]
The Children in Babylon feared not the fiery furnace, but cast into the midst of the flame, being refreshed with dew they sang: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!"

*Troparion 1 (N):*
Wagging his head, Judas, foreseeing evil, stirred up a favorable time to deliver the Judge unto condemnation, He Who is Lord of all and the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2 (Y):*
Unto you, His friends, Christ cried: "One of you shall betray Me!" Forgetting gladness, they were seized with grief and fear: "Who is this? Tell us!" they said, "O God of our fathers!"

*Troparion 3 (M):*
"He that with Me dips his hand into the dish with boldness, for him indeed it were good if he had never entered the gate of life!" This one He revealed who was the betrayer: "O God of our fathers!"

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^289]
According to ancestral laws the most blessed youths in Babylon, receiving danger, disregarded the senseless command of the ruler, and gathered together in the unconsuming fire, they cried a worthy hymn unto the Sovereign: "Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supreme exalt Him unto all the ages!"

*Troparion 1 (O):*
The most blessed companions, the Apostles, having endured the word in Zion, followed the Shepherd like lambs, and united, not departing from Christ, nourished by the Divine Word, thankfully cried aloud: "Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supreme exalt Him unto all the ages!"

*Troparion 2 (N):*
Wickedly-named Iscariot, having willingly forgotten the legal friendship, having had his feet washed, prepared them unto betrayal; and eating Thy Bread, the Divine Body, he lifted up his heel against Thee, O Christ, and understood not to cry aloud: "Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supreme exalt Him unto all the ages!"

*Troparion 3 (E):*
The dishonorable one received the Body that looses transgressions and the Divine Blood poured out for the world; but he was not ashamed to drink what he was selling for a price, nor did he shrink from malice, nor understand to cry aloud: "Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and supreme exalt Him unto all the ages!"

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^290]
Come, O faithful, with exalted minds let us partake of the Master's hospitality and the immortal Table in the Upper Room, having learned the lofty word from the Word, Whom we magnify!

*Troparion 1 (A):*
"Go," the Word said unto the disciples, "prepare the Passover in the Upper Room, where the mind is established, mystically accomplished for them, with the unleavened word of truth; and magnify the firm goodness!"

*Troparion 2 (D):*
"The Father begat Me, the Wisdom, the Fashioner before the ages, the beginning of His ways, unto works mysteriously accomplished: for being by nature the uncreated Word, I have made Mine own the voices of the nature which I have now received!"

*Troparion 3 (O):*
"As being man in essence, and not in phantom, so God in the manner of exchange, the nature united unto Me: know Me therefore as the one Christ, from which and in which and which are, saving them that believe!"

*Exapostilarion (thrice):*[^291]
I see Thy bridal chamber adorned, O my Savior, and I have no garment that I may enter therein; enlighten the vesture of my soul, O Giver of light, and save me!

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and we chant Stichera Idiomela, Tone 2:*[^292]
The rest of the Jewish council runs together, that it may deliver the Maker and Creator of all things unto Pilate. O the lawless! O the faithless! For they prepare Him for judgment Who is coming to judge the living and the dead; they make ready for sufferings Him Who heals the passions! O longsuffering Lord, great is Thy mercy: glory to Thee!

*Verse 1: Praise Him for His mighty acts, praise Him according to the multitude of His greatness:*
Judas the lawless, O Lord, who at the supper dipped his hand into the dish with Thee, stretched forth his hands unto the lawless to take silver pieces; and he that calculated the price of the myrrh was not afraid to sell Thee, the Priceless One: he that stretched forth his feet to be washed deceitfully kissed the Master, that he might betray Him to the lawless! Casting himself away from the apostolic choir, and casting down the thirty pieces of silver, he knew not Thy third-day Resurrection, through which have mercy on us!

*Verse 2: Praise Him with the sound of the trumpet, praise Him with psaltery and harp:*
Judas the betrayer, being deceitful, with a deceitful kiss betrayed the Savior, the Lord and Master of all things; he sold Him unto the Judeans as a slave, as a sheep to the slaughter: so followed the Lamb of God, the Son of the Father, the only Abundantly-merciful One!

*Verse 3: Praise Him with timbrel and dance, praise Him with strings and flute:*
Judas was revealed by his deeds as a slave and deceiver, a disciple and plotter, a friend and devil: for he followed the Teacher, and took counsel against Him unto betrayal; he said within himself: "I will deliver Him up, and I will acquire the gathered possessions!" He sought that the myrrh should be sold, and that Jesus should be taken by deceit; he will give a kiss, he will betray Christ: and as a sheep to the slaughter, so followed the Lamb of God, the only Tenderhearted and Lover of mankind!

*Glory, both now, Tone 2:*[^293]
He Whom Isaiah proclaimed as the Lamb comes to His voluntary slaughter, and He gives His back to stripes, His cheeks to buffeting, and He turned not away His face from the shame of spitting; He is condemned to an ignominious death: all these things the Sinless One willingly accepts, that He may grant unto all the resurrection from the dead!

*On Great and Holy Thursday, at Matins:*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela, Tone 8:*[^294]
Today the wicked council gathers together against Christ, and meditates vain things against Him, to deliver up the Innocent One unto death before Pilate. Today Judas binds about himself the halter of hanging with his money, and is deprived of both lives, the earthly and the Divine. Today Caiaphas prophesies against his will, saying: "It is better that one man should die for the people." For He came to suffer for our sins, that He might set us free from the servitude of the enemy, as He is good and the Lover of mankind!

*Verse 1: He that ate My bread hath magnified his deceits against Me:*
Today Judas conceals the face of a lover of the poor, and uncovers the image of avarice:[^295] no longer does he care for the poor, no longer does he sell the sinful woman's myrrh, but the heavenly myrrh, and from it he steals pieces of silver! He runs unto the Judeans, saying unto the lawless: "What will ye give me, and I will deliver Him unto you?" O the avarice of the betrayer! Readily does he make a treacherous bargain; according to the will of the buyers he sells the Unsalable One; he shows no dispute regarding the price, but sells Him as a runaway slave: for it is the custom of thieves to cast away holy things; now the disciple casts holy things unto the dogs! For the madness of avarice made him rage against his own Master: whose temptation let us flee, crying aloud: "O longsuffering Lord, glory to Thee!"

*Verse 2: He went forth and spoke together. Verse:*
Thy character is filled with malice, lawless Judas:[^296] for suffering from the disease of avarice, thou didst acquire hatred of mankind. For if thou didst love wealth, why didst thou come unto Him Who taught poverty? But if thou didst love Him, why didst thou deliver the Priceless One, delivering Him unto murder? Be afraid, O sun! Groan, O earth, and quaking, cry aloud: "O Lord without malice, glory to Thee!"

*Verse 3: They laid down a lawless word against Me. Verse:*
Let none of the faithful be unpartaking of the Master's supper; let no one by any means draw near unto the table with deceit like Judas:[^297] for having taken the morsel, he turned away from the Bread. In appearance indeed he was a disciple, but in reality a murderer; with the Judeans indeed he rejoiced, but with the Apostles he dwelt; hating he kissed, and kissing he betrayed Him Who redeemed us from the curse, God and the Savior of our souls!

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Thy character is filled with malice, lawless Judas... *(as above)*.

*Both now, Tone 5:*[^298]
O Lord, Thou didst teach Thy disciples mysteries, saying: "O friends, see that no fear separate you from Me. For if I suffer, yet it is for the sake of the world; therefore be not offended because of Me: for I came not to be served, but to serve, and to give My life as a ransom for the world. If therefore ye are My friends, imitate Me: he that desires to be first, let him be the last; the Master as the servant. Abide in Me, that ye may bear fruit: for I am the living Vine!"

*At the First Hour:*

*At the First Hour, for the Theotokion we say:* "What shall we call thee, O full of grace..."
*Then the Troparion, Tone 3:*[^299]
Thou Who wast struck on the cheek for the sake of the human race and didst not become angry: deliver our life from corruption, O Lord, and save us!

*Glory, at the end; Both now, Theotokion.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 1:* Let the nations know that Thy name is the Lord.
*Verse:* O God, who is like unto Thee?
*Reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah (Jeremiah 11:18–23; 12:1–15):*
O Lord, make it known to me, and I shall understand: then I saw their endeavors. But I, as a gentle lamb led to the slaughter, did not understand: they devised an evil device against me, saying: "Come, and let us put wood into his bread, and let us blot him out from the land of the living, and let his name be remembered no more!" But, O Lord of hosts, Who judgest righteously, Who triest the reins and the hearts: let me see Thy vengeance upon them, for unto Thee have I revealed my plea. Therefore thus says the Lord against the men of Anathoth, who seek my life, saying: "Thou shalt not prophesy in the name of the Lord; otherwise, thou shalt die by our hands." Therefore thus says the Lord of hosts: "Behold, I will visit them: their young men shall die by the sword, and their sons and their daughters shall perish by famine. And there shall be no remnant of them, for I will bring evils upon the dwellers in Anathoth in the year of their visitation."
Righteous art Thou, O Lord, when I plead with Thee; yet let me speak judgments with Thee: why does the way of the ungodly prosper? All they are secure that deal very treacherously. Thou hast planted them, and they have taken root; they have borne children and brought forth fruit; Thou art near in their mouth, and far from their heart. But Thou, O Lord, knowest me; Thou hast proved my heart before Thee. Gather them together as sheep for slaughter, purify them for the day of their slaughter! How long shall the land mourn, and all the grass of the field wither, for the wickedness of them that dwell therein? The cattle and the birds are perished, because they said: "God shall not see our ways." Thy feet run and are weary; all the beasts of the field shall be gathered together, that they may come to devour them. Many shepherds have destroyed My vineyard, they have defiled My portion, they have given My desirable portion into a trackless wilderness; it is become a destruction of desolation. For thus says the Lord concerning all the evil neighbors that touch Mine inheritance, which I divided unto My people Israel: "Behold, I pluck them up from their land, and I will take out Judah from their midst. And it shall come to pass, after I have plucked them out, I will return and have mercy on them, and I will settle each one in his inheritance, and each one in his land!"

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:* Make vows and pay them unto the Lord our God.
*Verse:* God is known in Judah; His name is great in Israel.

*As for the remaining Three Hours, we chant them according to custom in the Narthex at their proper time. And we do not chant the Beatitudes. "Remit, forgive," "Our Father," and the Kontakion of the day, "Lord, have mercy" forty times, and the Dismissal.*[^300]
*From this day until the Sunday of Antipascha, that is, Thomas Sunday, Lities for the reposed do not take place in the Narthex. Behold.*

*Chapter 16: Typikon Rubric on the Confluence of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos with Great and Holy Thursday:*[^301]
*It is necessary to know that if the Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos falls on Great and Holy Thursday:*
*On Wednesday evening, at Vespers, we do not say "Master, Master," and we make no prostrations. At "Lord, I Call," Stichera Idiomela of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5. Glory of the day, Tone 4: "Today Christ comes..." Both now, of the Feast, the same tone: "He was sent..." Entrance with the Gospel. Prokeimenon and Reading of the day, and of the Feast, 3. "Let my prayer be set forth," and 3 prostrations. Gospel of the day, and the rest of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts. And after "Now the powers," 3 prostrations; and at "Blessed be the name of the Lord," 3 prostrations. At the table we drink wine and eat cooked food without oil.*
*At the 6th hour of the night, they strike the great semantron and all the heavy bells. And having gathered in church, we chant Great Compline. The Priest says "O Master" according to custom. And after "Glory to God in the highest," the Procession into the Narthex, and we chant the Stichera Idiomela of the Feast, Tone 1. Glory, both now, Tone 2: "Gabriel proclaims good tidings..." At the Aposticha, of the Feast, Tone 1, with their verses. Glory, both now, of the Feast, the same tone: "Today is joy..." At the Blessing of Loaves: Troparion of the Feast, twice; and the Reading of the Feast.*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice. Glory, both now: "When the glorious disciples..." Then the Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymn, and Reading of the Feast. The 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon: "Let every breath..." Gospel of the Feast. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8, and of the day with the Heirmos on 8. Katavasia: the Heirmos of the day, which is in the Triode, both choirs together. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the day, and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn, Tone... Both now, of the Feast, and the Reading. After Ode 6: Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos, and we read the Prologue and the Synaxarion. At Ode 9, we do not chant "More honorable": Exapostilarion of the Feast. Glory, of the day; Both now, of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1, on 4. Glory, of the Feast, Tone 2: "That which from the ages..." Both now, of the day, the same tone: "He Whom Isaiah proclaimed as the Lamb..." At the Aposticha, Stichera of the day, with their verses. Glory of the day, Tone 8: "Thy character is filled with malice..." Both now, of the Feast, the same tone: "Let the heavens rejoice..." After "It is good to give thanks," and after "Our Father," Troparion of the day. Glory, both now, of the Feast, and Litany. Then the First Hour: "Come, let us worship..." at which we say the Troparion, and the Prophecy of the day, and the Catechesis of the Feast, and the Dismissal.*
*At the 5th hour of the day, they strike the great semantron, and there is a Litiya and a procession outside the monastery. And having returned, we chant the Hours in the Narthex: the Third, Sixth, and Ninth, at which we say the Troparion of the Feast. Glory, of the day; Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we alternate the Kontakia. At the Ninth Hour, quickly the Beatitudes, which is: "Remember us," and the rest of the Litiya for the day. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the Feast, "Lord, have mercy" 40 times, Prayer "All-Holy Trinity," and Dismissal. "Blessed be the Lord" we do not say.*
*At Vespers, they strike the great bell and all the heavy bells at the 5th hour. And we chant at "Lord, I Call," Stichera Idiomela of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5. Glory of the day, Tone 6: "Offspring of vipers..." Both now, of the Feast, the same tone: "He was sent..." Entrance with the Gospel. "O gladsome Light." Prokeimenon and Readings of the day, and of the Feast, 5. Little Litany. Then the Trisagion. Prokeimenon of the day and of the Feast. Epistle of the day and of the Feast. Gospel of the day and of the Feast. And the rest of the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great. In place of the Cherubic Hymn, we chant the Kontakion, Tone 6: "Of Thy Mystical Supper..." thrice. We chant the same also in place of the Communion Hymn, once; and when Communion takes place; and in place of "Let our mouths be filled..." After the Ambo Prayer, the Footwashing takes place according to their order. Afterwards: "Blessed be the name of the Lord," thrice; then "I will bless the Lord." The Priest gives antidoron and the usual blessing unto the brethren, and the Dismissal takes place.*
*At the table, we do not dispense for fish, but only for oil and wine.*

## Great and Holy Thursday: Vespers and Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great[^302]

*At the 8th hour of the day, they strike the semantron, and having gathered in church, we begin Vespers. After the Psalm: "Bless the Lord, O my soul," the Great Litany. Then, "Lord, I Call," in Tone 2. And we chant 10 Stichera:*
*Stichera Idiomela, Tone 2:*
The rest of the Jewish council runs together, that it may deliver the Maker and Creator of all things unto Pilate. O the lawless! O the faithless! For they prepare Him for judgment Who is coming to judge the living and the dead; they make ready for sufferings Him Who heals the passions!

...Him Who heals the passions! O longsuffering Lord, great is Thy mercy: glory to Thee!

Judas the lawless, O Lord, who at the supper dipped his hand into the dish with Thee, stretched forth his hands unto the lawless to take silver pieces; and he that calculated the price of the myrrh was not afraid to sell Thee, the Priceless One: he that stretched forth his feet to be washed deceitfully kissed the Master, that he might betray Him to the lawless! Casting himself away from the apostolic choir, and casting down the thirty pieces of silver, he knew not Thy third-day Resurrection, through which have mercy on us!

Judas the betrayer, being deceitful, with a deceitful kiss betrayed the Savior, the Lord and Master of all things; he sold Him unto the Judeans as a slave, as a sheep to the slaughter: so followed the Lamb of God, the Son of the Father, the only Abundantly-merciful One! *(Twice)*

Judas was revealed by his deeds as a slave and deceiver, a disciple and plotter, a friend and devil: for he followed the Teacher, and took counsel against Him unto betrayal; he said within himself: "I will deliver Him up, and I will acquire the gathered possessions!" He sought that the myrrh should be sold, and that Jesus should be taken by deceit; he will give a kiss, he will betray Christ: and as a sheep to the slaughter, so followed the Lamb of God, the only Tenderhearted and Lover of mankind!

He Whom Isaiah proclaimed as the Lamb comes to His voluntary slaughter, and He gives His back to stripes, His cheeks to buffeting, and He turned not away His face from the shame of spitting; He is condemned to an ignominious death: all these things the Sinless One willingly accepts, that He may grant unto all the resurrection from the dead!

*Glory, both now, Tone 6:*[^303]
Judas is truly an offspring of vipers, who ate the manna in the wilderness and murmured against the Provider: for while the food was yet in their mouths, the ungrateful ones slandered God; and this ungodly one, carrying the heavenly Bread in his mouth, accomplished the betrayal of the Savior! O insatiable character, and inhuman audacity! He sells Him that nourishes him, and delivers unto death the Master Whom he loved: truly he is the son of those lawless ones, and with them he inherited perdition! But spare our souls, O Lord, from such inhumanity, O Thou Who alone art beyond measure in longsuffering!

*Entrance with the Gospel.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 1:* Deliver me, O Lord, from the evil man, and rescue me from the unjust man.
*Verse:* Who have devised injustice in their heart.
*Reading from Exodus (Exodus 19:10–19):*[^304]
The Lord said unto Moses: "Go down, testify unto the people, and sanctify them today and tomorrow, and let them wash their garments. And let them be ready against the third day: for on the third day the Lord will come down upon Mount Sinai before all the people. And thou shalt set bounds for the people round about, saying: 'Take heed to yourselves, that ye go not up into the mount, nor touch any part of it: whosoever touches the mount shall surely die! No hand shall touch him, for he shall surely be stoned or shot through with an arrow: whether it be man or beast, he shall not live!' When the voices and the trumpets sound, and the cloud departs from the mountain, they shall go up upon the mountain."

And Moses went down from the mountain unto the people, and sanctified them; and they washed their garments. And he said unto the people: "Be ready against the third day: come not near a woman." And it came to pass on the third day at morning, that there were voices and lightnings, and dark clouds upon Mount Sinai; and the sound of the trumpet was exceedingly loud, and all the people that were in the camp trembled. And Moses brought forth the people out of the camp to meet God, and they stood at the foot of Mount Sinai. And Mount Sinai was altogether on a smoke, because God descended upon it in fire; and the smoke thereof ascended as the smoke of a furnace, and all the people were exceedingly amazed. And the sounds of the trumpet sounded louder and louder. Moses spoke, and God answered him by a voice.

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:* Deliver me from mine enemies, O God, and from them that rise up against me.
*Verse:* Rescue me from them that work iniquity.
*Reading from Job (Job 38:1–23; 42:1–5):*[^305]
The Lord said unto Job through the whirlwind and cloud: "Who is this that hides counsel from Me, and keeps words in his heart, and thinks to hide them from Me? Gird up now thy loins like a man; for I will demand of thee, and answer thou Me! Where wast thou when I laid the foundations of the earth? Declare unto Me, if thou hast understanding! Who laid the measures thereof, if thou knowest? Or who stretched the line upon it? Whereupon are the foundations thereof fastened? Or who laid the cornerstone thereof, when the stars were made, and all My angels praised Me with a loud voice? And I shut up the sea with doors, when it burst forth, and issued out of its mother's womb. And I placed a cloud for its garment, and wrapped it in mist. And I set bounds for it, and placed bars and doors, and said unto it: 'Hitherto shalt thou come, but no further; and here shall thy waves be broken!' Or did I order the morning light in thy time, and the daystar saw its appointed station, to take hold of the wings of the earth, to shake the wicked out of it? Or didst thou take clay and fashion a living creature, and place this speaking being upon the earth? And hast thou taken away light from the wicked, and crushed the arm of the proud? Hast thou entered into the springs of the sea, and walked in the footprints of the deep? Are the gates of death opened unto thee through fear, and did the gatekeepers of hades tremble when they saw thee? Hast thou learned the breadth of that which is under heaven? Declare unto Me now, how great it is? And in what land does the light dwell, and what is the place thereof? If thou wouldst lead Me to their bounds, and if thou knowest their paths? Thou knowest, then, that thou wast born at that time, and great is the number of thy years! Hast thou entered into the treasuries of the snow, and hast thou seen the treasuries of the hail, which are reserved for thee against the time of enemies, for the day of battle and war?"
And Job answered the Lord and said: "I know that Thou canst do all things, and that nothing is impossible with Thee. For who is he that hides counsel from Thee? Spares words, and thinks to hide them from Thee? Who...

...Who will declare unto me that which I knew not: great and marvelous things which I knew not? Give ear now unto me, O Lord, that I also may speak; I will ask Thee, and do Thou teach me! I had heard of Thee before by the hearing of the ear; but now mine eye hath seen Thee!"

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isaiah 50:4–11):*[^306]
The Lord gives me the tongue of instruction, to know when it is opportune to speak a word. He placed me early in the morning, He added unto me an ear to hear. And the instruction of the Lord opens mine ears, and I do not resist, nor speak contrary. I gave My back to stripes, and My cheeks to buffeting; I turned not away My face from the shame of spitting. And the Lord, the Lord was My helper; therefore I was not confounded, but I set My face as a hard stone, and I knew that I should not be ashamed. For He that justifies Me draws near; who is he that contends with Me? Let him stand up against Me together! And who is he that judges Me? Let him draw near unto Me! Behold, the Lord, the Lord will help Me: who shall harm Me? Behold, all ye shall wax old as a garment, and the moth shall devour you. Who among you fears the Lord? Let him give ear unto the voice of His servant! Ye that walk in darkness and have no light, trust in the name of the Lord, and stay upon God! Behold, all ye kindle a fire and strengthen the flame: walk in the light of your fire, and in the flame which ye have kindled! This hath happened unto you for My sake: ye shall lie down in sorrow!

*Then the Little Litany. Trisagion. Prokeimenon of the Epistle, Tone 7:* The rulers of the people assembled together against the Lord and against His Christ.
*Verse:* Why did the nations rage, and the peoples meditate on vain things?
*Epistle to the Corinthians (1 Corinthians 11:23–32, pericopa 144):* Brethren, I received from the Lord...
*Alleluia, Tone 6:* Blessed is he that understands concerning the poor and needy.
*Verse:* Mine enemies spoke evil things against me.
*Verse:* He that ate my bread hath magnified his deceits against me.
*Gospel from Matthew (composite Passion pericopa 113/114/117):* The Lord said unto His disciples: "Ye know that after two days is the Passover..." And the rest. Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great.

*In place of the Cherubic Hymn, we chant this Kontakion in Tone 6:*[^307]
Of Thy Mystical Supper, O Son of God, receive me today as a communicant: for I will not speak of the Mystery to Thine enemies, neither will I give Thee a kiss like Judas, but like the thief I confess Thee: "Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom!" *(Thrice)*

*And at the end: Alleluia.*
*The same is also the Communion Hymn. And in place of "Let our mouths be filled...":*
*We chant the same repeatedly in place of the Communion Hymn until all have communicated of the Divine Mysteries.*

## The Order of the Washing of Feet[^308]

*After the conclusion of the Ambo Prayer, if the Footwashing is to take place:*
*The Priest comes forth vested outside the great doors of the church, holding in his hands the ewer. Preceding him go the candlelighters with candlesticks, and the large basin prepared by the Paraecclesiarch, and the twelve appointed brethren who are to have their feet washed by the Superior: among whom it is fitting that the Doorkeeper and the Economos be included.*

*We also go forth, all of us with the Priest, chanting Psalm 50.*[^309]
*We chant, Tone 6, Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Bound with the bond of love, the Apostles, having cast themselves unto Christ Who reigns over all, had their beautiful feet washed, proclaiming peace unto all!

*Troparia:*
Holding the uncontainable water and that which is suspended high in the air, bridling the abysses and restraining the seas, the Wisdom of God pours water into the basin, and the Master washes the feet of His servants!

The Master shows unto the disciples an example of humility: He Who covers the heaven with clouds girds Himself with a towel, and bows His knees to wash the feet of His servants, He in Whose hand is the breath of all existing things!

*Then the Stichera Idiomela of the Footwashing. Tone 1:*
Girded with a towel and having washed the feet of the disciples, O Christ God, wash away from us the defilement of soul, and gird us with the spiritual bond, that we may perform the commandments of Thy goodness!

*Tone 2:*
Desiring to receive a great blessing, O faithful, let us run with reverence unto the honorable Washing, not washing bodily defilement, but mystically purifying our souls. For Christ our Savior, Who looks upon the earth and makes it tremble, bows Himself and touches mortal feet, granting a firm ascent over every hostile power: unto Him thankfully let us cry aloud: "Thou Who hast shown us humility as the preeminent path of exaltation, save us, O Good One, as the Lover of mankind!"

*Tone 3:*
Peter was ashamed to have his feet washed by the most pure hands by which Adam was fashioned; but he gave ear: "If I wash thee not, thou hast no part with Me." Seized with great trembling, he cried unto Thee, O Lord: "Not my feet only shalt Thou wash, but also my hands and my head!" O the great and bountiful gifts! He promises to make His disciples partakers of His goodness, and to have a portion with them in unutterable glory: even as He said regarding the Mystical Cup, that they should drink it new with Him in the Kingdom of heaven: of which make us also worthy, as Thou art merciful and the Lover of mankind!

*Tone 8:*[^310]
Today He Who is unapproachable in essence assumed the work of a servant, girded Himself with a towel, He Who clothes the heaven with clouds; He pours water into the basin, He Who divided the Red Sea, and bowing His knees He began to wash the feet of the disciples, and to wipe them with the towel wherewith He was girded. When therefore He had washed the feet of the disciples, He said unto them: "Ye are clean, but not all of you," signifying him that betrayed Him!

It had been good for thee, Judas, if thou hadst not been conceived in thy mother's womb; it had been good for thee, betrayer, if thou hadst not been born an alien to the Son of God: for thy sake the crown of the disciples of Christ is untwined, and the crucified thief embraces the True Vine; through thee the lawless tear down the eternal rampart and the temple not made with hands. The price of the myrrh for the sinful woman thou didst weep over as costly; how wast thou not afraid to deliver the Blood of the Righteous One unto the lawless? It had been good for thee, betrayer...

...betrayer, if thou hadst not been born an alien to the Son of God!

*Glory, both now, Tone 8:*[^311]
Held fast by the slumber of the devil, Judas slept unto death. It is time to be watchful, it is time to be sober: let the heart groan, let the eyes weep, let the psalm be sung! For great is the strength of the gate: Christ is at the doors, the sacrificed Passover is come; glory to Thee, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Then the Litany by the Deacon:*
**Deacon:** In peace let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** For the peace from above and for the salvation of our souls, let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** For the peace of the whole world, for the good estate of the holy churches of God, and for the union of all, let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** That this Washing may be revealed and sanctified by the power, operation, and descent of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** That it may be for the washing away of the defilement of our transgressions, let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** That we may be delivered from all affliction, wrath, and distress, let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** Help us, save us, have mercy on us, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** Commemorating our all-holy, immaculate, most blessed, and glorious Lady, the Theotokos and ever-virgin Mary, with all the saints, let us commend ourselves and one another, and our whole life unto Christ our God.
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.

*Exclamation:*
**Priest:** For Thou art the purification of our souls, and unto Thee we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

*Then the Priest says the Prayer Aloud:*
**Priest:** O Lord and most good God, unapproachable in Divinity, Who didst assume the form of a servant in the likeness of a minister, and for the representation of saving humility didst wash the feet of Thy disciples with Thy most pure hands and wipe them with a towel: look down now also upon us, Thine unworthy servants, who marvelously imitate such great condescension of Thine; and grant that we may be washed from bodily defilements and spiritual impurity by the touch of this water. Grant us the invisible descent of the All-Holy Spirit; strengthen our souls and bodies against the fierce serpent that lies in wait for our heel, that having been instructed we may serve Thee acceptably, treading upon serpents and scorpions, and over all the power of the enemy!

*Exclamation:*
**Priest:** For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

**Priest:** Peace be unto all.
**Choir:** And to thy spirit.
**Deacon:** Bow your heads unto the Lord.
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.

*The Prayer said Secretly:*
**Priest:** O Lord our God, Who didst show unto us the measure of humility in Thine exalted coming, and by calling the last first through voluntary will didst teach us to serve one another: exalt us through divine humility; bring us unspotted, washed ever with tears, purified by the illumination of Thy teaching goodness, that always falling down before Thee in truth, we may find mercy and compassions at Thy dread judgment seat!

*Exclamation:*
**Priest:** For Thou art a merciful and mankind-loving God, and unto Thee we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

*Then the Senior Deacon says:*
**Deacon:** That we may be counted worthy to hear the holy Gospel, in peace let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
*And the Deacon begins to read the First Gospel of the Footwashing. And the Abbot, standing apart from the brethren, says:*
**Abbot:** Wisdom! Stand upright! Let us hear the Holy Gospel!
**Deacon:** The reading from the Holy Gospel according to John (John 13:1–11, pericopa 41):
At that time, before the feast of the Passover...
*And when the Deacon reads the concluding word, saying:*
**Deacon:** He rose from supper and laid aside His garments...
*The Superior takes off his outer vestments. And when the Deacon says:*
**Deacon:** Having taken...

**Deacon:** ...Having taken a towel, He girded Himself...
*Then the Superior girds himself with a linen apron. And when the Deacon says:*
**Deacon:** After that He poured water into a basin...
*The Superior pours water with his own hands into the vessel and basin. And when the Deacon says:*
**Deacon:** And began to wash the disciples' feet, and to wipe them with the towel wherewith He was girded...
*The Superior begins to wash the feet of the brethren, beginning from the Doorkeeper down to the Ecclesiarch or the Economos, and he washes them, wiping the feet of each one of those being washed, and kissing them. And when he comes unto the Economos, the Deacon says:*
**Deacon:** Then came He to Simon Peter; and Peter said unto Him:
**Peter:** Lord, dost Thou wash my feet?[^312]
*And he performs according to the pattern of the Gospel. The Superior answers:*
**Abbot:** What I do thou knowest not now, but thou shalt understand hereafter.
*And the Senior says unto him:*
**Peter:** Thou shalt never wash my feet!
*And the Superior answers:*
**Abbot:** If I wash thee not, thou hast no part with Me.
*Then the Senior rises up a little, as if struck with fear, and lifting up his hands, points with his right hand toward his feet, saying in a quiet voice, so that all may hear:*
**Peter:** Lord, not my feet only!
*And showing his hands, he says:*
**Peter:** But also my hands!
*And again pointing to his head, bowing slightly, he says:*
**Peter:** And my head!
*And the Superior says:*
**Abbot:** He that is washed needs not save to wash his feet, for he is clean every whit; and ye are clean, but not all.
*And when the Deacon has finished the Gospel, the Senior sits down, and the Superior washes his feet also, as he did beforehand unto the rest. And after the washing of all, the Superior, having taken off the towel, girds himself with his own linen cloth, and puts on his vestments. And the Deacon exclaims:*
**Deacon:** That we may be counted worthy to hear the holy Gospel...
*The Superior himself reads the Second Gospel of the Footwashing, saying:*
**Abbot:** The reading from the Holy Gospel according to John (John 13:12–17, pericopa 42):
At that time, when Jesus had washed the feet of the disciples, He took His garments...
*And after the Gospel the Deacon says:*
**Deacon:** Let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.

*Prayer of the Superior in the Hearing of All:*
**Abbot:** O Lord our God, according to Thy great mercy, Who didst empty Thyself and take the form of a servant, Who at the time of Thy saving, life-creating, and voluntary Passion didst deign to sup with Thy holy disciples and Apostles, and thereafter, having girded Thyself with a towel and washed the feet of Thy holy disciples, didst give them an example of humility and love one toward another, and didst say: "As I have done unto you, so do ye also one to another": do Thou Thyself now also, O Master, coming into the midst of Thine unworthy servants who follow Thine example, wash away every defilement and impurity of our souls, that having washed away the dust of transgressions that has cleaved unto us, and having wiped one another with the towel of love, we may be able to be well-pleasing unto Thee all the days of our life, and find grace before Thee!

*Exclamation:*
**Abbot:** For Thou art good and the Cleanser of all things, O Christ our God, and unto Thee we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

*And after the conclusion of the Prayer, all the brethren are sprinkled from the basin. Together we chant the Troparion from the Fifth Ode of Great Friday, Tone 6:*
Having washed their feet and having been purified by the communion of the Divine Mysteries, Thy servants, O Christ, now went up from Zion unto the great Mount of Olives, singing unto Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Then: "Blessed be the name of the Lord," and "I will bless the Lord at all times." Antidoron is given, and the Dismissal.*
*Then we enter into the refectory, and eat cooked food with oil (if there is blessing), and drink wine, three cups each, according to the Jerusalem Typikon and that of the Studite Monasteries.[^313] But in Muscovite editions without oil, only dry eating, because Alleluia is chanted. Yet let all things be according to the discretion of the Superior.*

*On Great and Holy Thursday, at Compline:*
*Be it known that neither Compline today on Great Thursday nor the Midnight Office tomorrow on Great Friday is chanted in church, but only in the cells.*[^314]

## Great and Holy Thursday: Small Compline

*On the same Great Thursday at Compline, we chant the Three-Ode Canon (Triode), the composition of the Lord Andrew of Crete, Tone 8:*
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^315]
Dispel the darkness of my soul, O Christ God, Giver of light, Who didst drive away the primordial darkness of the abyss, and grant me the light of Thy commandments, that rising early I may glorify Thee!

*Troparia:*
The Supper was adorned, and the Passover was prepared for Thee, as Thou didst say, O Christ: but Judas hastened unto Thy betrayal, and being with Thee, estranged from Thy price, he made agreement through avarice!

Christ voluntarily rises from supper, and is girded with a towel about His loins, and bows His neck, while Peter cries aloud: "Thou shalt never wash my feet, O my Creator: yet wash them!"

Judas the deceitful disciple received the bread in his hands unto betrayal, by which also he accomplished the betrayal against Thee; he stretched forth his feet, which Thou Thyself didst wash and wipe with a towel!

With a deceitful kiss Judas then opened wide his mouth unto Thee, with which he unworthily dipped Thy Body, O Word, crying unto Thee: "Hail, Master!" the kisser and betrayer, servant and deceiver!

Peter, as he beheld the things that were being done, being then seized with fear, was convicted by the question of a wretched maidservant: for he denied Thee, the Lord, not only as Thou didst say, but as Thou, the All-Knowing One, didst know!

The Creator is struck on the cheek, and creation is smitten with His reproach; He is willingly beaten with a reed, and the highest things are bowed down; the Judge is spat upon, and all the foundations of the earth are shaken!

*Glory, Tone 8:*
God is crowned with thorns, He Who adorned all the earth with flowers; He receives stripes, and endures reproach longsufferingly, wearing the purple robe of mockery; and being God, He endures all things, and suffers in His flesh!

*Both now, Tone 8:*
Let John cry aloud, declaring through divine teachings Thine Incarnation: THE WORD BECAME FLESH of the Virgin without change, and remained God by nature as He was, not departing from His Father's bosom!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
He Who covers His upper chambers with waters, Who set the sand as a bound for the sea, and Who upholds all things: Thee the sun sings, Thee the moon glorifies, unto Thee all creation offers a hymn, as the Creator of all unto the ages!

*Troparia:*
O Jesus, Who clothest heaven with clouds, sitting on the throne of glory with the incorrupt Father: Thou, having taken a towel, didst gird Thyself to wash the clay feet, being all fire, O Word, although Thou wast incarnate!

When He had washed all, Jesus reclined and said unto His disciples: "Know ye what I have done unto you? For I have given you all an example of humility, that whosoever desires to be first, let him be the last of all by free choice!"

"Ye are clean indeed, but not all of you," Christ said unto His friends reclining at the supper: for one of you...

*On Great and Holy Thursday at Compline:*[^316]
...unto another, being in doubt, He spoke the word; therefore after these things He openly declared the name of the betrayer.

Even as He said unto them, when the Judge of the ages came unto the disciples on the Mount of Olives, He said then: "Come and let us depart hence; for the betrayer is already at hand; let no one deny Me, for I suffer willingly."

O treacherous kiss! "Hail, Rabbi!" Judas says unto Christ; together with this greeting the betrayer delivers Him unto the slaughter; for Judas gave a sign unto the lawless: "Whomever I kiss, that is He Whom I promised to deliver unto you."

Having been sung of as our God by the lawless people, and not answering back, nor crying out, as the Lamb of God Thou didst endure all things: to be examined, and judged, and beaten, bound and led with weapons and clubs unto Caiaphas.

"Let Him be crucified!" cried the Hebrew people together with the Priests and Scribes against Jesus Christ. O faithless people! What evil did He do Who appeared among you? He Who raised Lazarus from the tomb, and made a way of salvation for mankind!

At Pilate's judgment seat, the lawless people cried aloud, shouting: "Crucify Him, and release unto us Barabbas the murderer who is bound; but having first scourged Christ, take Him away and crucify Him with the evil malefactors!"

*Let us bless the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
O ineffable emptying! O unutterable counsel! For being fire, Thou didst wash the feet of the betrayer, O Savior, and our fire Thou didst not kindle; and giving Bread at the Supper, Thou didst begin the Mystical Service.

*Both now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
O new tidings! God is the Son of a woman who knew not wedlock; the Mother is unwedded, and the Child is God! O fearful hearing! O dread Conception! O incorrupt birthgiving of the Virgin! Truly all things are beyond the mind, and higher than vision!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Blessed is the Lord God of Israel, Who raised up a Horn of salvation for us in the house of David His servant; wherein the Dayspring from on high visited us, and guided our feet into the way of peace.

*Troparia:*
"Are ye sleeping again?" Christ said unto the disciples; "Watch, for the hour is already at hand; arise, let us go, My friends; behold, the disciple and betrayer, having the entire cohort, comes to deliver Me unto the murderers of men."

Thy kiss was deceitful, and bitter was thy greeting: to whom dost thou call out, O deceiver, saying: "Hail, Rabbi!" Christ said unto Judas: "Friend, whereunto art thou come? For if thou art come to kiss, why dost thou bring a knife anointed with honey?"

At Pilate's judgment seat, O blameless Judge, Thou didst come willingly to stand, O Christ, and to deliver us from our debts; therefore, O Good One, Thou didst endure to be wounded in the flesh, that we might receive deliverance.

How does the abyss of the heart, the fire, stand before Pilate, who is but shadow and reed and earth? The fire of Godhead did not consume him, O Christ, but Thou didst wait patiently, bringing deliverance as the Lover of mankind.

"Away with Him! Away with Him! Crucify Him Who is called Christ!" the Jews cried out to Pilate again. But he washed his hands and beat upon the reed, imputing no fault, while granting immortality unto all.

*On Great and Holy Thursday.*
The lawless cried out exceedingly unto Pilate: "Away with Him! Away with Him! Crucify Christ!" seeking to kill Him as one condemned. Is not this He Who raised the dead, cleansed the lepers, healed the woman with the issue of blood, and strengthened the paralytics?[^317]

"What evil has He done, that ye rage so greatly? If ye will, take Him and crucify Him!" Pilate cried again unto the senseless people: "I find no fault in Him!" But they cried out bitterly: "Away with Him! Away with Him! Crucify the Savior of all!"

O lawless Jews! O senseless people! Did ye not remember anything of the miracles of Christ, the multitude of healings? Even seeing Him, did ye not understand His Divine power? Even as your fathers were formerly, so now are ye also without understanding.

*Glory:*
Smitten on the cheek, O my Creator, Thou wast delivered over to be crucified for my sake, that having wrought my salvation in the midst of the earth, Thou mightest pour forth life unto the world, and make immortal by Thy precious Blood those who worship Thee.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Thy Lamb, O Master, standing by the Cross and weeping for Thee, the Creator of all, beheld Thy longsuffering; for Thou wast born willingly in the flesh, and didst endure all the passions therein, that Thou mightest save the world.

## Great and Holy Friday: Matins of the Holy and Saving Passion of Our Lord Jesus Christ[^318]

*On Great and Holy Friday, the semantron is struck or the bell sounded at the 2nd hour of the night (approx. 8:00 PM). And when we are assembled in the temple, the Priest says:* Blessed is our God... *And we recite:* O Heavenly King... *The Trisagion; and after* Our Father...: *Lord, have mercy (12 times). And the rest of the usual beginning of Matins. Then the Six Psalms. Glory, both now: Alleluia (3 times). The Great Litany. And after the exclamation, Alleluia in Tone 8.*

*Troparion, slowly and melodiously (3 times):*[^319]
*Tone 8:*
When the glorious disciples were enlightened at the washing of the supper, then Judas the ungodly, stricken with the disease of avarice, was darkened, and to lawless judges he delivers Thee, the Righteous Judge. Behold, O lover of money, him who for its sake hanged himself! Flee from the insatiable soul that dared such things against the Master! O Lord, Who art good towards all, glory to Thee!

*Then the Ecclesiarch, who is the ruler of the church, distributes candles to the brethren, which they light at each Gospel.[^320] And after the Troparion, the Priest goes into the holy Altar, puts on the epitrachelion and phelonion, and having censed the Holy Table, says:* Again and again... Help us, save us... Calling to remembrance our all-holy, immaculate, most blessed... *Then:* That we may be accounted worthy to give ear to the Holy Gospel... *First Gospel from John, pericopa 43 (John 13:31–18:1):* The Lord said unto His disciples: Now is the Son of Man glorified... *Ending:* ...and His disciples.

*The Service of the Holy Passions.*
*And when the Gospel is finished, we say:* Glory to Thy longsuffering, O Lord!
*The Abbot reads this Gospel, and after him the other Priests.*
*And straightway we chant the Antiphons, repeating all the Troparia.*[^321]

*Antiphon 1, Tone 8:*
The rulers of the people assembled together against the Lord and against His Christ. *(Repeat)*
They laid a lawless word against Me; Lord, O Lord, forsake Me not!
Let us present our senses clean unto Christ, and as His friends let us sacrifice our souls for His sake; and let us not be weighed down with earthly cares as was Judas, but in the chambers of our souls let us cry aloud: Our Father Who art in the heavens, deliver us from the evil one!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, Tone 8:*
As Thou didst give birth knowing not wedlock, and didst remain a Virgin, O unwedded Mother, Mary Theotokos: pray Christ our God that our souls may be saved!

*Antiphon 2, Tone 6:*
Judas ran saying to the lawless scribes: "What will ye give me, and I will deliver Him unto you?" And in the midst of those taking counsel, Thou Thyself didst stand invisibly, being taken into counsel: O Knower of hearts, spare our souls!
Let us serve God in mercy, as did Mary at the supper, and let us not acquire avarice like Judas, that we may ever be with Christ God.
"That Thou mightest show mercy unto Adam, as the Immortal One, Thou didst come unto death, O My Son, and the Jews have no mercy upon all My children: but do Thou, O Word, having arisen, graciously have mercy upon us!"

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Him Whom Thou didst bear ineffably, O Virgin, as the Merciful One, cease not ever entreating, that He may save from afflictions all who flee unto thee!

*Antiphon 3, Tone 2:*
Because of the raising of Lazarus, O Lord, the Hebrew children cried Hosanna unto Thee, O Lover of mankind; but lawless Judas would not understand.
At Thy supper, O Christ God, Thou didst foretell unto Thy disciples: "One of you shall betray Me"; but lawless Judas would not understand.
When John asked: "Lord, who is he that betrays Thee?" Thou didst show him by the bread; but lawless Judas would not understand.
At Thy footwashing, O Christ God, Thou didst tell Thy disciples: "Do as ye see"; but lawless Judas would not understand.
"Watch and pray, that ye enter not into temptation," Thou didst say unto Thy disciples, O Christ our God; but lawless Judas would not understand.
For thirty pieces of silver, O Lord, and with a deceitful kiss, the Jews sought to kill Thee; but lawless Judas would not understand.
Lamenting, O Christ, she who bare Thee foresaw the needles of which Simeon of old had spoken; but the lawless people would not understand Thee.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Save Thy servants from perils, O Virgin Theotokos, for after God we all flee unto thee, as unto an unbreakable rampart and protection!

*The Service of the Holy Passions.*
*Sessional Hymn (we do not sit during these sessional hymns, but sing them standing). Tone 7 (chanted in the midst of the church):*[^322]
Feeding the disciples at the supper, and knowing the conspiracy of betrayal, thereon Thou didst expose Judas; knowing him to be incorrigible, yet wishing to make known unto all that Thou wast delivered up willingly, that Thou mightest snatch the world from the alien: O longsuffering Lord, glory to Thee!
*Glory, both now: the same.*

*Then:* That we may be accounted worthy to give ear to the Holy Gospel... *Second Gospel from John, pericopa 58 (John 18:1–28):* At that time, Jesus went forth with His disciples... *Ending:* ...that they might lead Him to Pilate.

*And after the Gospel, we chant Antiphon 4, Tone 5:*[^323]
Today Judas forsakes the Master and receives the devil; he is blinded by the passion of avarice; darkened, he falls away from the light. For how could he look upon the Light, having sold the Luminary for thirty pieces of silver? But unto us He has dawned Who suffered for the world: unto Whom let us cry aloud: O Lord, Who didst suffer and have compassion on mankind, glory to Thee!
Today Judas is estranged from grace, and becomes alien to the gift; being a disciple, he becomes a betrayer; in a customary kiss he hides deceit, and preferring senseless slavery to avarice above blessed love, he becomes the leader of the lawless council: but having the salvation of Christ, let us glorify Him!

*Tone 1:*
Let us acquire brotherly love as brethren in Christ, and not unmercifulness towards our neighbors, lest we be condemned as the unmerciful servant for the sake of money, and repenting like Judas, derive no profit.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Most glorious things have been spoken of thee everywhere, for in the flesh thou didst bear the Creator of all, O Mary Theotokos, all-praised and unwedded!

*Antiphon 5, Tone 6:*
The disciple bargained the price of the Master, and for thirty pieces of silver he delivered up the Lord; with a deceitful kiss he delivered Him up to lawless men unto death.
Today the Creator of heaven and earth said unto His disciples: "The hour is drawn near, and Judas who betrays Me is at hand; let no one deny Me, seeing Me on the Cross between two thieves; for I suffer as man, and I save as the Lover of mankind those who believe in Me!"
Today the Pure One, seeing the Creator of creation separated from His disciples and held by one of the Jews, cried maternally within herself, knowing what was to come: "What a strange marvel! O Son, how hast Thou despised Me, Who formerly preserved Me pure!"

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
O thou who at the end didst ineffably conceive and bear thy Creator: entreat Him that our souls may be saved!

*Antiphon 6, Tone 7:*
Today Judas watches to betray the Lord, the pre-eternal Savior of the world, Who fed the multitudes from five loaves.
Today the lawless one renounces the Master; being a disciple...

*The Service of the Holy Passions.*
...being a disciple, he betrays the Master; for silver he sells Him Who satisfied man with manna.
Today the Jews nailed to the Cross the Lord Who cleft the sea with a rod and led them into the wilderness; today they pierced with a lance His ribs, Who scourged Egypt with plagues for their sake; and with gall they gave Him to drink Who rained down manna as food for them.
O Lord, coming to Thy voluntary suffering, Thou didst cry unto Thy disciples: "If ye could not watch one hour with Me, how did ye promise to die for My sake? At least behold Judas, how he sleeps not, but hastens to deliver Me to the lawless! Arise, pray, that no one deny Me, seeing Me on the Cross!" O longsuffering Lord, glory to Thee!
O Lord, she who bare Thee, knowing Thy ineffable birthgiving, cried unto Thy crucifiers: "Even if He is incarnate, He is bodiless, yet changeless in what He was: why then are ye come to kill Life? Even if ye lift Him upon the tree, He will give life; and if ye send Him into the tomb, He will raise the dead! Arise, O Longsuffering One, show forth Thy Resurrection! O Lover of mankind, Who didst raise us, glory to Thee!"

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, Tone 7:*
Rejoice, O Theotokos, who didst contain in thy womb Him Whom the heavens cannot contain! Rejoice, O Virgin, proclamation of the Prophets, through whom Emmanuel dawned upon us! Rejoice, O Mother of Christ God!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 7:*[^324]
What manner of deceit made thee, Judas, the betrayer of the Savior? Did He separate thee from the choir of the Apostles? Did He deprive thee of the gift of healings? When He ate with the others at supper, did He cast thee out from the table? When He washed the feet of the others, did He despise thine? O how many good things thou didst forget! And thy ungrateful disposition is indeed exposed, while His immeasurable longsuffering and great mercy are proclaimed!
*Glory, both now: the same.*

*Third Gospel from Matthew, pericopa 104 (Matthew 26:57–75):* At that time, the soldiers having taken Jesus led Him away to Caiaphas the High Priest... *Ending:* ...he wept bitterly.

*Antiphon 7, Tone 8:*[^325]
Enduring the lawless men who seized Thee, thus Thou didst say, O Lord: "If ye strike the Shepherd and scatter the sheep, stripping My disciples, I could bring forth more than twelve legions of Angels; but I have suffered long, that the hidden and secret things which I showed you through My Prophets might be fulfilled!" O Lord, glory to Thee!
Peter, having denied the third time, straightway understood what had been spoken unto him, but he brought unto Thee tears of repentance: "O God, cleanse me and save me!"
Passing beyond the bounds of nature, she understood not the manner of the crucifixion; therefore she brought unto Thee tears of love, saying: "O God, arise now and save me!"

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
As the gate of salvation, and delightful paradise, and cloud of the ever-existing light, let us all hymn the holy Virgin, saying unto her: Rejoice!

*Antiphon 8, Tone 2:*

*The Service of the Holy Passions.*
Say, O ye lawless, what did ye hear from our Savior? Did He not ordain the Law and the teachings of the Prophets? How then do ye plot to deliver up to Pilate Him Who is God the Word and the Redeemer of our souls?[^326]
"Let Him be crucified!" cried they who ever enjoyed Thy gifts, and the murderers of the righteous asked to receive a malefactor instead of the Benefactor. But Thou, O Christ, wast silent, enduring their cruelty, willing to suffer and to save us as the Lover of mankind.
Beholding Thee crucified, O Pure One, she who bare Thee from her all-holy womb and endured pain, asked for the joy of Thy Resurrection, crying unto Thee: "Thou Who hast torn up the handwriting against Adam, willing to be buried, save us as the Lover of mankind!"

*Glory, both now, Tone 2:*
Since we have no boldness because of our many sins, do thou entreat Him Who was born of thee, O Virgin Theotokos; for a Mother's prayer has great power to appease the Master; despise not the supplications of sinners, O all-pure one, for He is merciful and able to save, Who willed to suffer for our sake!

*Antiphon 9, Tone 3:*
They weighed the thirty pieces of silver, the price of Him on Whom a price was set, on Whom the children of Israel set a price. Watch and pray, that ye enter not into temptation: the spirit indeed is willing, but the flesh is weak; therefore watch!
They gave Me gall for My food, and for My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink; but do Thou, O Lord, raise Me up, and I will requite them.
The Pure One was dismayed, seeing Thy voluntary nailing to the Cross, and cried: "O Christ, have mercy, Thou Merciful One, upon Thine own flock!"

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
We who are from the Gentiles hymn thee, O pure Theotokos, for thou didst bear Christ our God, Who through thee freed mankind from the curse!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
O how Judas, who was once Thy disciple, plotted Thy betrayal against Thee! Deceitfully from the supper, a conspirator and unrighteous man, he went and said to the Priests: "What will ye give me, and I will deliver unto you Him Who broke the Law and profaned the Sabbath?" O longsuffering Lord, glory to Thee!
*Glory, both now: the same.*

*Fourth Gospel from John, pericopa 54 (John 18:28–19:16):* At that time, they led Jesus from Caiaphas unto the Praetorium... *Ending:* ...that He might be crucified.

*Antiphon 10, Tone 6:*
He Who clothes Himself with light as with a garment stood naked at the judgment, and received buffeting on the cheek from the hands which He had fashioned; and the lawless people nailed to the Cross the Lord of Glory: then the veil of the temple was torn, the sun was darkened, unable to endure seeing God outraged, before Whom all things tremble: Him let us worship!
The disciple denied Him; the thief cried out: "Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom!"
She who bare Thee without suffering entreats Thee for our sake: Remember, O Lover of mankind, those who hymn Thee in faith!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*

*The Service of the Holy Passions.*
Having received joy from the Angel, and having born thy Creator, O Virgin, save those who magnify thee!

*From the Greek:*[^327]
Pacify the world, O Lord, Who wast well-pleased to bear flesh of the Virgin for the sake of Thy servants, that with one accord we may glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Antiphon 11, Tone 6:*
For the good things which Thou didst do, O Christ, unto the Hebrew race, they condemned Thee to be crucified, giving Thee vinegar and gall to drink; but give them, O Lord, according to their deeds, for they understood not Thy condescension!
The Hebrew race was not content with the betrayal, O Christ, but wagged their heads, offering blasphemy and mockery; but give them, O Lord, according to their deeds, for they understood not Thy dispensation!
Neither did the earth shaking, nor the rocks cleaving convince the Jews, nor the veil of the temple, nor the resurrection of the dead; but give them, O Lord, according to their deeds, for they meditated vain things against Thee!
