Seeing the Giver of life and Master of death being led dead upon the tree, the Mother cried out: "Behold, all creation suffers with Me, O My Son! But shine forth from the dead, O unwaning Light, Jesus, for Thou art the Father's Radiance!"

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
We have known God incarnate of thee, O Virgin Theotokos, only blessed one: therefore unceasingly hymning thee, we magnify thee!

*Antiphon 12, Tone 8:*
Thus says the Lord unto the Jews: "O My people, what have I done unto thee? Or wherein have I grieved thee? I enlightened thy blind; I cleansed thy lepers; I raised up the man who was upon a bed. O My people, wherein have I grieved thee? Or what will ye requite Me? For manna, gall? For water, vinegar? For loving you, ye nail Me to the Cross! Henceforth I will endure no longer: I will call My Gentiles, and they will glorify Me with the Father and the Spirit, and I will bestow upon them eternal life!"
Today the veil of the temple is torn for the conviction of the lawless, and the sun hides its rays, seeing the Master crucified.
O lawgivers of Israel, Jews and Pharisees, the apostolic choir cries unto you: "Behold the temple which ye destroyed! Behold the Lamb Whom ye crucified and delivered to the tomb, but He arose by His own power! Be not deceived, O Jews: for this is He Who saved you in the sea, and fed you in the wilderness: this is Life and Light and Peace for the world!"
Unto the lawless, the murder-bearing guard is equal ruin; but the Virgin and Mother cries unto you: "Behold, God is received in the flesh! Behold, He Who took on earthly forgotten clay receives suffering! By His power He shakes creation, the rocks are cleft, the earth quakes, the robe of the temple is torn, the sun is dismayed at the loss of its light! Be ashamed, O disobedient, and know Him Who nourished you!"

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, Tone 8:*

*The Service of the Holy Passions.*
Rejoice, gate of the King of Glory, through which the Most High alone passed, and left it sealed again, unto the salvation of our souls!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^328]
When Thou didst stand before Caiaphas, O God, and wast delivered unto Pilate the Judge, the heavenly powers were shaken with fear; and when Thou wast lifted up upon the tree between two thieves, Thou, the Sinless One, wast numbered among the lawless, to save mankind: O guileless Lord, glory to Thee!
*Glory, both now: the same.*

*Fifth Gospel from Matthew, pericopa 111 (Matthew 27:3–32):* At that time, Judas who betrayed Him, seeing that He was condemned... *Ending:* ...to bear His Cross.

*Antiphon 13, Tone 6:*[^329]
The Jewish assembly asked of Pilate that Thou be crucified, O Lord; for finding no guilt in Thee, they released the guilty Barabbas, and condemned Thee, the Righteous One, inheriting the sin of ruinous murder: but give them, O Lord, their recompense, for they meditated vain things against Thee!
Him before Whom all things tremble and are dismayed, and Whom every tongue hymns, Christ the Power of God and the Wisdom of God, the Priests struck on the cheek and gave Him gall; and He willed to suffer all things, wishing to save us from our iniquities by His own Blood, as the Lover of mankind.
"Thou Who gavest the Law and the Prophets, how art Thou judged by lawless courts, O Son and unoriginate God, willing to be persecuted? How shall the realms of Hades receive Thee Who holdest all things in Thy hand?" said the Pure One, seeing the Immortal One hanging by His own will.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, Tone 6:*
O Theotokos, who through the Word didst bear beyond word the Creator Himself: entreat Him to save our souls!

*Antiphon 14, Tone 8:*
Thou Who didst receive the thief as a fellow traveler, who had defiled his hands with blood: number us also with him, as Thou art Good and the Lover of mankind!
A small voice the thief uttered upon the cross, yet great faith he found; in one hour he was saved, and being the first to open the doors of paradise, he entered in: O Lord, Who didst receive the thief's repentance, glory to Thee!
No small grief had the Pure One, standing by the Cross, and seeing the daring of the murderous ruin, falling down with great weeping, she cried in terror: "O Lord, Who knowest the weakness of earthly men, glory to Thee!"

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, Tone 8:*
Rejoice, O Virgin who through the Angel didst receive joy for the world! Rejoice, O all-pure who didst bear thy Creator and Lord! Rejoice, who wast accounted worthy to be the Mother of Christ God!

*Antiphon 15, Tone 6:*
Today He Who hung the earth upon the waters is hung upon the tree! He Who is the King of Angels is adorned with a crown of thorns! He Who wraps the heaven in clouds is wrapped in a mock purple robe! He Who freed Adam in the Jordan receives buffeting! The Bridegroom of the Church is transfixed with nails! The Son of the Virgin is pierced with a lance!

*The Service of the Holy Passions.*
We worship Thy Passions, O Christ! We worship Thy Passions, O Christ! We worship Thy Passions, O Christ: show us also Thy glorious Resurrection!
Let us not feast as do the Jews: for Christ our Pascha is sacrificed for our sake; but let us cleanse ourselves from all defilement, and purely pray unto Him: Arise, O Lord, save us as the Lover of mankind!
Thy Cross, O Lord, is life and resurrection unto Thy people, and trusting therein, we hymn Thee, our crucified God: have mercy on us!
Being the Luminary of understanding, Thou didst shine forth among Thy people, O Jesus, Lover of mankind, willing to deify them, wishing to open Eden, which Adam closed of old by transgression.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Seeing Thee hanging, O Christ, she who bare Thee cried out: "What is this strange mystery that I behold, O My Son! How dost Thou die in the flesh on the tree, crucified, O Giver of life!"

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*[^330]
Thou hast redeemed us from the curse of the Law by Thy precious Blood; being nailed to the Cross and pierced with a lance, Thou didst pour forth immortality unto mankind: O our Savior, glory to Thee!

*Sixth Gospel from Mark, pericopa 67 (Mark 15:16–32):* At that time, the soldiers led Jesus away into the court, which is the Praetorium... *Ending:* ...that we may believe Him.

*Then the Beatitudes. We appoint 13 verses in Tone 4:*[^331]
In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest.
*Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*
*Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted.*
*Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.*
Through the tree Adam was banished from paradise; but through the tree of the Cross the thief dwelt in paradise: for the one, having tasted, transgressed the commandment of the Creator; but the other, being crucified with Thee, confessed Thee as God, the Judge, saying: "Remember me, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!"

*Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled.*
The lawless bought the Creator of the Law from the disciple, and as a transgressor of the Law they presented Him before Pilate's judgment seat, crying: "Crucify Him!" Who fed the sons of Israel with manna in the wilderness; but we, imitating the wise thief, cry out in faith: "Remember us also, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!"

*Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.*
The council of the deicides, the lawless Jewish race, raging against Pilate, cried out and said: "Crucify the innocent Christ, and rather release Barabbas!" But we utter unto Him the voice of the wise thief: "Remember us also, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!"

*Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.*
Thy life-bearing ribs, pouring forth a fountain as from Eden, water Thy Church, O Christ, as a spiritual paradise, parting thence into heads, into the four Gospels, watering the world, gladdening creation, and faithfully teaching the Gentiles to worship Thy Kingdom!

*Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called the children of God.*
Thou wast crucified for my sake, that Thou mightest pour forth forgiveness for me; Thou wast pierced in the ribs, that Thou mightest drop drops of life for me; Thou wast fastened with nails...

*The Service of the Holy Passions.*
...Thou wast fastened with nails, that by the depth of Thy Passions I, being assured of the height of Thy power, may cry: "O Giver of life, Christ, glory to Thy Cross, O Savior, and to Thy Passion!"

*Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*
When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, all creation trembled seeing it; the foundations of the earth were shaken with fear of Thy power; the luminaries hid themselves, and the veil of the temple was torn; the mountains quaked, and the rocks were cleft; and the faithful thief cries with us: "Remember us, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!"

*Blessed are ye when they shall revile you and persecute you, and shall say all manner of evil against you falsely for My sake.*
Our handwriting Thou didst tear up upon the Cross, O Lord, and being numbered among the dead, Thou didst bind the tormentor there, delivering all from the bonds of death by Thy Resurrection, wherewith we have been enlightened, O Lord, Lover of mankind, and we cry unto Thee: "Remember us also in Thy Kingdom!"

*Rejoice and be exceedingly glad, for great is your reward in heaven.*
Having ascended the Cross, Thou didst destroy the power of death; and being laid in the tomb, Thou didst raise all the righteous, and wast the Firstborn from the dead, granting eternal life: therefore, being saved by Thy rising, we cry unto Thee, O Redeemer Jesus: "Remember us also in Thy Kingdom!"

*Glory:*
Let us, the faithful, all with one accord pray worthily to glorify the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, the Unity of the Godhead existing in three Persons, remaining unconfused, simple, indivisible, and unapproachable, whereby we are delivered from the fiery torment!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Thy Mother, O Christ, who bare Thee in the flesh without seed, and truly remained an incorrupt Virgin even after birthgiving: her we bring unto Thee in prayer, O most merciful Master, that forgiveness of sins may ever be granted unto those who cry: "Remember us also, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!"

*Prokimenon, Tone 4:*[^332]
They parted My garments among them, and upon My vesture did they cast lots. (Ps. 21:19 [LXX])
*Verse:* O God, My God, give ear unto Me: why hast Thou forsaken Me? (Ps. 21:2 [LXX])

*Seventh Gospel from Matthew, pericopa 113 (Matthew 27:33–54):* At that time, the soldiers having come... *Ending:* ...Truly this was the Son of God.
*Then the reading from the Commentary on Matthew.*
*Then Psalm 50.*
*Then the Eighth Gospel from Luke, pericopa 111 (Luke 23:32–49):* At that time, there were also two other malefactors led with Jesus... *Ending:* ...and the women that followed Him from Galilee, beholding these things.

*Canon Triode. The work of Lord Cosmas.*[^333]
*Heirmos (twice). Troparia to 12. And at the end, the Heirmos chanted together by both choirs. The Acrostic or marginal verse of the Triode is:* PRO SABBATON TE, *which is:* Before the Sabbath.
*Tone 6. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Early in the morning unto Thee do I rise, O Word of God, Who for Thy tender mercy didst give Thyself changelessly unto fallen man, and even unto Thy Passions didst bow Thyself impassibly: grant me peace, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Having washed their feet and been purified by the communion of the divine Mystery, Thy ministers, O Christ, now ascended with Thee from Sion unto the great Mount of Olives, hymning Thee, O Lover of mankind!
"Behold," Thou didst say, "O My friends, be not afraid! For now the hour is at hand that I should be taken and slain by the hands of the lawless..."

...of the lawless; but ye shall be scattered, having forsaken Me, compelled to preach Me in faith, the Lover of mankind!"[^334]

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for our sake; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree and said: "Even though Thou endurest crucifixion, Thou art My Son and My God!"

*Ikos:*
Beholding her own Lamb led to the slaughter, Mary followed with disheveled hair together with the other women, crying aloud thus: "Whither goest Thou, O Child? For what cause dost Thou accomplish this swift course? Is there perchance another wedding in Cana of Galilee, and dost Thou now hasten thither, that Thou mayest make wine for them from water? Shall I go with Thee, O Child, or shall I rather wait for Thee? Give me a word, O Word, pass me not by in silence, Thou Who didst preserve me pure: for Thou art my Son and my God!"

*Synaxarion on the Holy and Great Friday of the Holy Passions of our Lord Jesus Christ. Bless, Father.*[^335]

*Verses on the Crucifixion:*
Thou art the living God, and slain upon the Tree,
O naked Dead One, and Word of the living God!

*Verses on the Thief crucified with Christ:*
The Eden gates, shut fast to men, the good Thief opened,
Saying: "Remember me!"

*On Holy and Great Friday we celebrate the holy, saving, and fearful Passions of our Lord and God and Savior Jesus Christ, which He willingly accepted for our sake: the spittings, the beatings, the buffeting, the reproaches, the mockeries, the purple robe, the reed, the sponge, the vinegar, the nails, and before all these the Cross and death—all of which were accomplished on this Friday. For having been sold by His friend and disciple for thirty pieces of silver, Christ was betrayed: first He is led away to Annas the High Priest,*

*who sends Him to Caiaphas. There He was spat upon and buffeted on the cheek, together mocked and derided, hearing: "Prophesy unto us, O Christ, who is he that struck Thee?" There also false witnesses came forward slandering Him, saying: "He said, Destroy this temple, and in three days I will raise it up," and because He called Himself the Son of God, whereat the High Priest, not enduring the blasphemy, rent his robe. When morning was come, He is led away to Pilate in the Praetorium; and they entered not, it says, that they might not be defiled, but that they might eat the Passover—and by the Passover is meant the whole Feast which then took place as was fitting; for Christ celebrated the legal Passover one day earlier, willing to be slaughtered on the Friday. And Pilate having come out, asks them concerning His accusations to condemn Him; and when he found nothing worthy of condemnation, he sends Him to Caiaphas, and he back again to Pilate, since he was intent upon His murder. But Pilate said: "Take Him yourselves, and crucify Him, and judge Him according to your Law." And they say again: "It is not lawful for us to put any man to death," urging Pilate to crucify Him. And Pilate asks Christ whether He is the King of the Jews; but He confesses not an earthly king, but an eternal One, saying: "My Kingdom is not of this world." Then Pilate, desiring to release Him, first said concerning Him that he found in Him no plausible guilt; next, he proposes to release one prisoner according to custom at each Feast: but Barabbas seems more acceptable to them than Christ. Pilate then, showing favor to the Jews, having first scourged Jesus...*

*...first scourged Jesus, delivers Him to the soldiers. Clothed in a scarlet robe, crowned with a crown of thorns, and a reed placed in His right hand, He is mocked by the soldiers saying: "Hail, King of the Jews!" Yet having thus mocked Him before the Jews, Pilate said again: "I find in Him no fault unto death." But they said: "We have Him under torment because He names Himself the Son of God." While this was being said, Jesus remained silent, but the multitude cried unto Pilate: "Crucify, crucify Him!" For they wished to put Him away by a dishonorable death, so that they might destroy His memory even among the accursed. Pilate, as though putting them to shame, says: "Shall I crucify your King?" But they said they had no King but Caesar; for having uttered blasphemy, achieving nothing, they shift the charge unto Caesar, that they might at least thus accomplish their frenzy, saying: "Every one that makes himself a king speaks against Caesar." While these things were occurring, Pilate's wife sent unto him, having been terrified by fearful visions, saying: "Have thou nothing to do with that Just Man, for I have suffered many things this night because of Him." And he, having washed his hands, denies guilt of that blood; but they cried: "His blood be on us and on our children! If thou let Him go free, thou art not Caesar's friend!" Therefore Pilate, fearing this, although he strictly knew Him to be innocent, delivers Him over to harsh condemnation and releases Barabbas. Judas, seeing this, cast down the pieces of silver, departed, and already gave himself over to hanging, being hanged upon a tree, where his belly swelled greatly and burst asunder. The soldiers, having struck His head with the reed, prepare to carry the Cross for Him. They compel Simon of Cyrene to bear the Cross.*[^336]

*[Woodcut Illustration of the Crucifixion: Christ hanging upon the Cross between the two thieves, with the Mother of God, the beloved disciple John, and Roman soldiers at Golgotha outside Jerusalem.]*[^337]

*And having arrived at the third hour at the Place of the Skull, there they crucify Him, and hanging two other thieves on either side of Him, that He might be numbered with the transgressors. For mockery, the soldiers divide His garments among themselves, and cast lots for His seamless tunic, working every excess of reproach. Nor this alone, but while He was upon the Cross they reviled Him, saying: "Aha, Thou that destroyest the temple and buildest it in three days, save Thyself!" And again: "He saved others; cannot He save Himself?" And again: "If He be the King of Israel, let Him now come down from the Cross, and we will believe in Him!" And indeed, even if they spoke truly, they ought to have approached Him without doubt: for not only is He acknowledged King of Israel, but of all the world. For what meant the darkening of the sun for three hours, and that in the middle of the day, that the Passion might be manifest to all? What meant the earthquake? The rending of the rocks, which convicted the contrary-minded Jews? The resurrection of many...*

*...the resurrection of many bodies, for the assurance of the general resurrection and for the manifestation of the power of the Sufferer? The tearing of the temple veil in two, as though the Temple were grieving for the glorious Sufferer outside, and revealing unseen things unto many?*[^338]

*At the third hour, then, Christ was crucified, as the divine Mark says; and from the sixth hour until the ninth hour there was darkness, when also Longinus the Centurion, seeing marvelous things, and above all the sun, cried aloud: "TRULY THIS WAS THE SON OF GOD!"*

*One of the malefactors reviled Jesus, but the other rebuked him with severe reproach, and confessed Christ as the Son of God; and unto his faith Christ promises a saving reward as a gift, namely, abode with Him in Paradise. When every reproach upon Him was fulfilled, Pilate also wrote a title upon Him, saying: "JESUS OF NAZARETH, THE KING OF THE JEWS"; and though they forbade him, saying not to write thus, but that He said: "I am the King of the Jews," Pilate answered again: "What I have written, I have written." Then, having said: "I thirst," they mix hyssop with vinegar and offer it to Him; and having said: "It is finished," bowing His head, He gave up the spirit.*

*When all had departed, His Mother stood by the Cross, and her sister Mary the wife of Cleopas, whom James bare when Cleopas died childless; and also John, the disciple whom He loved. The unthinking Jews, not bearing to see the bodies upon the Cross, asked Pilate, because it was the great day of the Passover and a Friday, that the legs of the condemned might be broken, so that death might come quickly.*

*And they broke the legs of the others, for they were still alive; but coming to Jesus, when they saw that He was dead already, they broke not His legs; but a certain soldier named Longinus, gratifying the senseless crowd, lifted his spear and pierced Jesus in His right side, and immediately there came out Blood and Water: both as from a man, and yet beyond man; that is, the Blood for the sake of the sacred Communion of the divine mysteries, and the Water for the sake of Baptism. For that two-streamed Fountain truly contains the Mystery that pertains to us. This John saw and bare witness, and his witness is true; for he was present at all these things and saw them, and writes: and if he had spoken falsely, he would not have recorded things that seemed unrighteous unto the dishonor of his Teacher. (They say that he then received the divine fluid, the stream of His most pure Blood, in a certain vessel from the life-giving side.) While the sentence was thus fulfilled, when it was already evening, Joseph came forth, he who was of Arimathea, a disciple who previously hid himself like the rest; and coming to Pilate with boldness, since he was known unto him, he begs the Body of Jesus. And Pilate commands it to be taken. And having taken it down from the Cross, he laid it in honorable fashion. When night was already come, Nicodemus comes, bearing a mixture of myrrh and aloes, about a hundred pounds weight, and having wound it in linen cloths with the shroud, as the manner of the Jews is to bury, they laid Him nearby in Joseph's new tomb hewn out of rock, where...*[^339]

*...where no man had ever yet been laid, lest when Christ is risen, they should ascribe the resurrection to another. The mixture of aloes and myrrh, being adhesive, was a memorial, so that when they should see the linen cloths and the napkin left behind in the tomb, they might not suspect that He had been stolen away; for how could there be leisure for anyone to strip off such things from the blessed Body? All these things having marvelously occurred at that time on the Friday, the God-bearing Fathers commanded us to celebrate the memory of them all with contrition of heart and compunction.*[^340]

*It is to be known, therefore, that on the sixth day of the week, on Friday, the Lord was crucified, because on the sixth day man was created in the beginning; and at the sixth hour of the day He was hung upon the Cross, because at that same hour, as they say, Adam stretched forth his hands and touched the forbidden Tree and died: for it was fitting that at the very hour wherein he was ruined, he should also be renewed. And in the garden, even as Adam in Paradise; the tasting of the bitter drink prefigured the tasting of the fruit; the buffeting showed forth our freedom; the spitting and shameful treatment, the honor bestowed upon us; the crown of thorns, the casting away of the curse that lay upon us; the purple robe, instead of the coats of skins and instead of the royal adornment bestowed upon us; the nails, the absolute immobility of our sin; the Cross, the Tree in Paradise; the pierced side prefigured Adam's rib, out of which came Eve, from whom came the transgression; the spear turns away the flaming sword from me; the water from the side, the image of Baptism; the Blood and the reed, wherewith as King He granted and signed our ancient fatherland with scarlet letters.*

*It is said, moreover, that Adam's skull lay there, where Christ the Head of all was crucified, that it might be baptized by the poured-out Blood of Christ. And it is called the Place of the Skull because in the Flood, when the bones of Adam's head were carried outside the earth, only the skull-bone was borne along as a marvelous wonder to behold, which Solomon, out of honor for the forefather, covered with many stones together with all his host. Therefore that place was called Lithostrotos, which is Stone-Paved. The most eminent of the Saints say from tradition that Adam himself was buried there by an angel. Where the corpse was, therefore, thither came the Eagle, Christ the eternal King, the New Adam, healing by the Tree the old Adam who fell through the tree.*

*By Thine exceeding and unsearchable compassions for our sake, O Christ our God, have mercy on us, now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The godly Children exposed the body of god-opposing malice; but the lawless assembly raging against Christ counsels vain things, plotting to slay Him Who holds life in His palm, Whom all creation blesses, glorifying Him unto the ages![^341]

*Troparia:*
"Cast off sleep from your eyes now, O disciples," Thou didst say, O Christ; "and watch in prayer, that ye enter not into temptation, and especially thou, Simon: for unto the stronger belongs the greater trial. Acknowledge Me, Peter, Whom all creation blesses, glorifying Him unto the ages!"

"A defiled word shall never go forth from my lips unto Thee, O Master! With Thee will I die as one of good understanding, though all should deny Thee!" cried Peter. "Neither flesh nor blood, but Thy Father hath revealed Thee unto me, Whom all creation blesses, glorifying Him unto the ages!"

"Thou hast not searched out all the depth of divine wisdom and understanding, neither hast thou comprehended the abyss of My judgments, O man," the Lord said. "Being flesh, therefore, boast not; for thrice shalt thou deny Me, Whom all creation blesses, glorifying Him unto the ages!"

"Thou refusest now to be subdued, Simon Peter, yet shalt thou quickly do as it was spoken: for a maidservant coming unto thee shall terrify thee," the Lord said; "yet weeping bitterly, thou shalt find Me merciful, Whom all creation blesses, glorifying Him unto the ages!"

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim, who without corruption gavest birth to God the Word, very Theotokos, thee do we magnify![^342]

*Troparia:*
The abominable assembly of dreadful men, the council of malicious god-slayers, stood against Thee, O Christ, and dragged Thee, the Creator of all, as an unrighteous man, Whom we magnify!

Not understanding the Law, the ungodly, meditating in vain upon the prophetic voices, dragged Thee, the Master of all, as a sheep to be slaughtered unjustly, Whom we magnify!

The priests and scribes delivered the Life granted unto the nations to be slain, wounded by self-willed malice against the Giver of life by nature, Whom we magnify!

Like many dogs they compassed Thee, and struck Thy cheek, O King, with buffeting; they questioned Thee and bare false witness against Thee; and having endured all, Thou didst save all!

*Then the Heirmos:* More honorable than the Cherubim...

*Exapostilarion (thrice):*
The Wise Thief didst Thou make worthy of Paradise in a single hour, O Lord: and me also enlighten by the Tree of the Cross, and save me!

*Then the Ninth Gospel from John, pericopa 61 (John 19:25–37):* At that time, there stood by the Cross of Jesus... *Ending:* ...Whom they pierced.[^343]

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and we chant Stichera Idiomela, Tone 3:*[^344]
Two and evil things hath My firstborn son Israel committed: he hath forsaken Me, the Fountain of living water, and hath hewn out for himself a broken cistern; he hath crucified Me upon the Tree, and hath asked for Barabbas and released him! Heaven was astonished at this, and the sun hid its rays; but thou, Israel, wast not ashamed, but didst deliver Me unto death: Holy Father, forgive them, for they know not what they have done!

Every member of Thy holy Flesh endured dishonor for our sake: the thorns, the Head; the face, spitting; the cheeks, buffeting; the mouth, the tasting of vinegar mingled with gall; the ears, the ungodly blasphemies; the back, scourging, and the hand the reed, and the stretching of the whole body upon the Cross; the joints, the nails; and the side, the spear. O Thou Who didst suffer for us and freed us from passions, Who didst condescend unto us...

...in Thy love for mankind and exalted us, O almighty Savior, have mercy on us!

When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, all creation trembled seeing it; the foundations of the earth were shaken with fear of Thy power: for when Thou wast lifted up today, the Hebrew race perished; the veil of the temple was torn in two, and the dead arose from their tombs; the centurion, seeing the wonder, was struck with awe; and Thy Mother standing by cried aloud, lamenting with motherly grief: "How shall I not weep and beat my breast, seeing Thee naked upon the Tree, hanging as one condemned, O Thou Who wast crucified, buried, and risen from the dead: Lord, glory to Thee!"

*Glory, Tone 6:*[^345]
They stripped Me of My garments and clothed Me in a scarlet robe; they placed a crown of thorns upon My head, and into My right hand they gave a reed, that I might shatter them like potter's vessels!

*Both now, Tone 6:*
I gave My back to scourges, and My face I turned not away from spittings; I stood before the tribunal of Pilate, and endured the Cross for the salvation of the world!

*Then the Tenth Gospel from Mark, pericopa 64 (Mark 15:43–47):* At that time, Joseph having come... *Ending:* ...and they beheld where He was laid.

*Then: Glory to God in the highest. We chant the Litany:* Let us complete our morning prayer... *And the rest according to custom.*

*Then the Eleventh Gospel from John, pericopa 62 (John 19:38–42):* At that time, Joseph besought Pilate... *Ending:* ...there they laid Jesus.[^346]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela, both choirs together, Tone 4:*
All creation was changed with fear, beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ: the sun was darkened, and the foundations of the earth were shaken; all things suffered with Thee Who didst create all. O Thou Who didst willingly endure for our sake: Lord, glory to Thee!

*Tone 2. Verse:* They parted My garments among them, and upon My vesture did they cast lots. (Ps. 21:19 [LXX])
O lawless and transgressing people, why do ye meditate vain things? Why have ye condemned the Life of all unto death? A great wonder it is, that the Creator of the world is delivered into the hands of the lawless, and the Lover of mankind is lifted up upon the Tree, that He may free the prisoners in hades who cry: "O longsuffering Lord, glory to Thee!"

*Verse:* They gave Me gall for My food, and for My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink. (Ps. 68:22 [LXX])
Today the blameless Virgin, seeing Thee stretched out upon the Cross, O Word, lamenting with motherly grief, was pierced in her heart bitterly, and groaning in pain from the depth of her soul, disheveling her hair, she wept; therefore, beating her breast, she cried out sorrowfully: "Woe is me, O divine Child! Woe is me, O Light of the world! Why hast Thou set from mine eyes, O Lamb of God?" Therefore the hosts of the Bodiless Powers were seized with trembling, saying: "O incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee!"

*Verse:* God is our King before the ages: He hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth. (Ps. 73:12 [LXX])
Seeing Thee hanging upon the Tree, O Christ, Creator and God of all, she who bare Thee without seed cried bitterly: "O My Son, whither hath the beauty of Thy form departed?...

...Thy form departed? I cannot bear to see Thee unjustly crucified: make haste therefore and arise, that I also may behold Thy third-day Resurrection from the dead!"

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Lord, when Thou didst ascend the Cross, fear and trembling fell upon creation; and Thou didst forbid the earth to swallow up those who crucified Thee, and didst command hades to release its prisoners for the renewal of men, O Judge of the living and the dead! Thou camest to grant life and not death: O longsuffering Lord, glory to Thee!

*Both now, Tone 8:*
Now the pen of the sentence is dipped by unjust judges; Jesus is judged and condemned to crucifixion, and creation suffers seeing the Lord upon the Cross: but O Thou Who in the nature of Thy Flesh didst suffer for my sake, O most good Lord, glory to Thee!

*Then the Twelfth Gospel from Matthew, pericopa 124 (Matthew 27:62–66):* On the next day, which is after the Friday... *Ending:* ...and sealed the sepulchre with the guard.[^347]

*Then: It is good to give thanks unto the Lord. Trisagion. And after Our Father:*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thou hast redeemed us from the curse of the Law by Thy precious Blood; having been nailed to the Cross and pierced with the spear, Thou didst pour forth immortality unto men: O our Savior, glory to Thee!

*Then the customary Litany. And the Dismissal.*

## Great and Holy Friday: Royal Hours

*At the 2nd Hour of the Day (about 8:00 AM), the small semantron is struck, and having gathered in the temple, we chant the First Hour with the customary Psalms: a work of Cyril, Archbishop of Alexandria.*[^348]

*The Priest begins:* Blessed is our God... *And we:* Amen.
*Heavenly King. Trisagion. And after Our Father:* Lord, have mercy *(12).*
*Glory, Both now:* Come, let us worship *(thrice).*

*Then Psalm 5:*[^349]
Give ear to my words, O Lord, understand my cry.
Attend to the voice of my supplication, my King and my God.
For unto Thee will I pray, O Lord; in the morning Thou shalt hear my voice.
In the morning will I stand before Thee, and Thou shalt look upon me.
For Thou art a God that willest not iniquity.
Neither shall the wicked dwell with Thee, nor shall the transgressors abide before Thine eyes.
Thou hast hated all them that work iniquity; Thou shalt destroy all them that speak a lie.
The Lord abhors the man of blood and deceit.
But as for me, in the multitude of Thy mercy I will enter into Thy house; I will worship toward Thy holy temple in Thy fear.
Lead me, O Lord, in Thy righteousness, because of mine enemies; make my way straight before Thee.
For there is no truth in their mouth; their heart is vain.
Their throat is an open sepulchre; with their tongues have they dealt deceitfully: judge them, O God!
Let them fall by their own counsels; cast them out according to the multitude of their ungodliness, for they have provoked Thee, O Lord.

And let all those that trust in Thee rejoice; they shall exult forever, and Thou shalt dwell in them.
And they that love Thy name shall glory in Thee; for Thou wilt bless the righteous man.
O Lord, as with a shield of good will hast Thou crowned us!

*Psalm 2:*
Why did the heathen rage, and the peoples meditate vain things?
The kings of the earth stood up, and the rulers were gathered together against the Lord and against His Christ.
"Let us break their bonds asunder, and cast away their yoke from us!"
He that dwells in heaven shall laugh them to scorn, and the Lord shall deride them.
Then shall He speak unto them in His wrath, and vex them in His sore displeasure:
"Yet have I been set King by Him upon Sion His holy mountain, declaring the decree of the Lord!"
The Lord said unto Me: "Thou art My Son, this day have I begotten Thee.
Ask of Me, and I will give Thee the heathen for Thine inheritance, and the uttermost parts of the earth for Thy possession.
Thou shalt shepherd them with a rod of iron; Thou shalt dash them in pieces like a potter's vessel."
And now, ye kings, understand; be instructed, all ye that judge the earth.
Serve the Lord with fear, and rejoice in Him with trembling.
Accept instruction, lest at any time the Lord be angry, and ye perish from the righteous way, when His wrath is kindled quickly.
Blessed are all they that put their trust in Him!

*Psalm 21 (LXX):*
O God, My God, give ear unto Me: why hast Thou forsaken Me?
Far from My salvation are the words of My transgressions.
O My God, I will cry by day, and Thou wilt not hear; and by night, and it is not unto folly in Me.
But Thou dwellest in the Holy Place, O Praise of Israel!
In Thee our fathers hoped; they hoped, and Thou didst deliver them.
Unto Thee they cried, and were saved; in Thee they hoped, and were not ashamed.
But I am a worm, and no man; a reproach of men, and despised of the people.
All they that saw Me laughed Me to scorn; they spoke with their lips, they shook the head:
"He hoped in the Lord, let Him deliver Him; let Him save Him, seeing He delights in Him!"
For Thou art He that drew Me out of the womb, My hope from My mother's breasts.
Unto Thee was I cast from the womb; from My mother's belly Thou art My God: depart not from Me!
For trouble is near, for there is none to help Me.
Many calves have compassed Me; fat bulls have beset Me round.
They opened their mouth against Me, as a ravening and a roaring lion.
I am poured out like water, and all My bones are scattered.
My heart is become like wax melting in the midst of My belly.
My strength is dried up like a potsherd, and My tongue hath cleaved to My jaws, and Thou hast brought Me into the dust of death.
For many dogs have compassed Me; the assembly of the wicked have enclosed Me.
They pierced My hands and My feet; they numbered all My bones.
They looked and stared upon Me; they parted My garments among them, and upon My vesture did they cast lots.
But Thou, O Lord, remove not Thy help far from Me; attend unto My defense!
Deliver My soul from the sword, and Mine only-begotten from the hand of the dog!
Save Me from the lion's mouth, and My lowliness...

...My lowliness from the horns of the unicorns!
I will declare Thy name unto My brethren; in the midst of the Church will I hymn Thee.
Ye that fear the Lord, praise Him; all ye the seed of Jacob, glorify Him!
Let all the seed of Israel fear Him!
For He hath not despised nor abhorred the prayer of the poor, neither hath He turned away His face from Me; and when I cried unto Him, He heard Me.
From Thee is My praise in the great church; I will pay My vows before them that fear Him.
The poor shall eat and be satisfied, and they that seek after the Lord shall praise Him; their hearts shall live unto ages of ages.
All the ends of the earth shall remember and turn unto the Lord, and all the kindreds of the nations shall worship before Him.
For the kingdom is the Lord's, and He is the Governor of the nations.
All the fat ones of the earth have eaten and worshiped; all they that go down to the earth shall fall down before Him.
And My soul lives unto Him, and My seed shall serve Him.
The generation that comes shall declare unto the Lord, and they shall declare His righteousness unto a people that shall be born, whom the Lord hath made.

*Glory, Both now:* Alleluia *(thrice).* Lord, have mercy *(thrice).*
*Glory:*
*Troparion, Tone 1:*
When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, tyranny was destroyed, and the power of the enemy was trampled underfoot: for neither an angel nor a man, but the Lord Himself saved us: Glory to Thee!
*Both now, Theotokion:*
What shall we call thee, O full of grace? Heaven, for thou hast made the Sun of Righteousness to dawn; Paradise, for thou hast budded forth the Flower of Incorruption; Virgin, for thou hast remained incorrupt; Pure Mother, for thou hast held in thy holy arms a Son Who is the God of all: Him do thou entreat that our souls may be saved!

*Then Stichera, Tone 8:*[^350]
Today the veil of the temple is torn in two for the conviction of the lawless, and the sun hides its rays, beholding the Master crucified!
*And again the other choir with the Verse:*
*Verse:* They parted My garments among them, and upon My vesture did they cast lots. (Ps. 21:19 [LXX])
As a sheep led to the slaughter wast Thou, O Christ the King, and as a blameless lamb nailed to the Cross by lawless men for our sins, O Lover of mankind!
*Verse:* The kings of the earth stood up, and the rulers were gathered together against the Lord and against His Christ. (Ps. 2:2 [LXX])
*And again the same Sticheron.*
*Glory, Tone 8:*
When the transgressors seized Thee, enduring it Thou didst cry, O Lord: "Though ye strike the Shepherd and scatter the twelve sheep, My disciples, yet could I summon more than twelve legions of angels; but I long-suffered, that the secret and hidden things which I revealed unto you by My Prophets might be fulfilled: Lord, glory to Thee!"
*Both now:* The same Sticheron.

*Then the Prokimenon, Tone 4:* His heart gathered iniquity to itself. (Ps. 40:7 [LXX])
*Verse:* Blessed is he that considers the poor and needy. (Ps. 40:2 [LXX])

*Reading from the Prophecy of Zechariah (Zech. 11:10–14; 12:10; 13:7; 14:6–11):*[^351]
Thus says the Lord: "I will take My fair staff, and cast it away, to break My covenant which I made with all the peoples. And it shall be broken in that day; and so the Canaanites, the sheep that are kept for Me, shall know that it is the word of the Lord. And I will say unto them: 'If it be good in your eyes, give Me My price...

...give Me My price, or else forbear.' And they weighed for My price thirty pieces of silver. And the Lord said unto Me: 'Cast them into the furnace, and see if it be proven, in what manner I was proven by them.' And I took the thirty pieces of silver, and cast them into the house of the Lord, into the furnace. And I turned away My second staff, the measure, to break the covenant between Judah and Israel." (Zech. 11:10–14)
"And they shall look upon Him Whom they pierced." (Zech. 12:10)
"And I will say unto Him: 'What are these wounds in the midst of Thy hands?' And He shall say: 'Those wherewith I was wounded in the house of My beloved.' Awake, O sword, against My Shepherd, and against the Man Who is His fellow, says the Lord Almighty: Smite the Shepherd, and the sheep shall be scattered; and I will bring My hand upon the Shepherd." (Zech. 13:7)
"And it shall come to pass in that day, that there shall not be light, but cold and frost. It shall be one day, and that day shall be known unto the Lord, and not day, nor night, and toward evening there shall be light. And in that day living water shall go out from Jerusalem, half of it toward the former sea, and half of it toward the hinder sea; in summer and in spring it shall be so. And the Lord shall be King over all the earth: in that day shall the Lord be one, and His name one, encircling all the earth and the wilderness. And that which is upon the bridles of the horses shall be Holy unto the Lord Almighty; and there shall be no more a Canaanite in the house of the Lord of hosts in that day." (Zech. 14:6–11)

*Then the Epistle to the Galatians, pericopa 215 from the half (Galatians 6:14–18):*[^352]
Brethren: Far be it from me to glory, except in the Cross of our Lord Jesus Christ, through Whom the world is crucified unto me, and I unto the world. For in Christ Jesus neither circumcision avails anything, nor uncircumcision, but a new creation. And as many as walk according to this rule, peace be on them, and mercy, and upon the Israel of God. Henceforth let no man trouble me; for I bear the marks of the Lord Jesus in my body. The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ be with your spirit, brethren. Amen.

*Then the Gospel according to Matthew, pericopa 110 (Matthew 27:1–56):* At that time, when morning was come, all the chief priests took counsel... *Read continuously with pericopas 111, 112, 113. Ending:* ...and the mother of Zebedee's children. *And we read the words concerning the Passions of the Lord.*[^353]

*Then:* Direct my steps according to Thy word. Let my mouth be filled with Thy praise. *Trisagion. And after Our Father:*
*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for our sake; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree and said: "Even though Thou endurest crucifixion, Thou art My Son and My God!"

*Then:* Lord, have mercy *(40).*
*And the Prayer:* Thou Who at all times and at every hour...[^354]
*Lord, have mercy (thrice). Glory, Both now: More honorable than the Cherubim. In the name of the Lord, bless, Father.*
*Then this Prayer:*
O Christ, the True Light, Who enlightenest and sanctifiest every man that comes into the world... *and the rest.*
*And the Dismissal.*

*[Woodcut Headpiece: King of Glory / Man of Sorrows. In a circular central medallion, Christ stands in the tomb before the Cross with the Crown of Thorns, spear, sponge, and scourge, flanked by the Theotokos on the left and Saint John the Theologian on the right; set within rich floral and foliate Baroque scrollwork.]*

## Great and Holy Friday: Third Hour

*At the seventh hour of the day, the small semantron is struck, and having gathered in church, we begin the Third Hour:*[^355]

**Priest:** Blessed is our God, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.

**Reader:** Amen.

Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

O Heavenly King, Comforter, Spirit of truth, Who art everywhere present and fillest all things, Treasury of good gifts and Giver of life: come and abide in us, and cleanse us from every stain, and save our souls, O Good One.

Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us. *(thrice)*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

All-holy Trinity, have mercy on us. Lord, cleanse us from our sins. Master, pardon our iniquities. Holy One, visit and heal our infirmities for Thy name's sake.

Lord, have mercy. *(thrice)*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

Our Father, Who art in the heavens, hallowed be Thy name. Thy kingdom come. Thy will be done, on earth as it is in heaven. Give us this day our daily bread. And forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors. And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from the evil one.

**Priest:** For Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.

**Reader:** Amen.

Lord, have mercy. *(12 times)*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

O come, let us worship and fall down before our King and God.
O come, let us worship and fall down before Christ, our King and God.
O come, let us worship and fall down before Christ Himself, our King and God.

*Psalm 40 (LXX):*
Blessed is he that considers the poor and needy; the Lord shall deliver him in an evil day.
The Lord preserve him, and keep him alive, and make him blessed upon the earth, and deliver him not into the hands of his enemies.
The Lord help the needy man upon his bed of sorrow; Thou hast turned all his bed in his sickness.
I said: Lord, be merciful unto me; heal my soul, for I have sinned against Thee.
Mine enemies speak evil against me: When shall he die, and his name perish?
And if he came to see me, he spoke vanity; his heart gathered iniquity to itself; he went abroad, and spoke of it together.
All mine enemies whispered together against me; against me did they devise evil against me.
An unjust word have they poured out against me: Shall not he that sleeps rise up again?
Yea, even the man of my peace in whom I trusted, who ate my bread, hath lifted up his heel against me.[^356]
But Thou, O Lord, have mercy on me, and raise me up, and I shall requite them.
By this I know that Thou delightest in me, because mine enemy shall not rejoice over me.
And because of my innocence Thou hast helped me, and hast established me before Thy face for ever.
Blessed be the Lord God of Israel from eternity and unto eternity. Amen. Amen.

*Psalm 68 (LXX):*
Save me, O God, for the waters are come in even unto my soul.
I am stuck fast in the mire of the deep, and there is no standing; I am come into the depths of the sea, and a tempest hath overwhelmed me.
I am weary with crying, my throat is become hoarse; mine eyes have failed while I wait for my God.
They that hate me without a cause are multiplied more than the hairs of my head; mine enemies that persecute me wrongfully are grown strong; then I paid them things which I took not away.
O God, Thou knowest my foolishness, and my faults are not hidden from Thee.
Let not them that wait on Thee, O Lord, Lord of powers, be ashamed for my sake; neither let them that seek Thee, O God of Israel, be confounded for my sake.
Because for Thy sake I have borne reproach; shame hath covered my face.
I am become a stranger unto my brethren, and an alien unto my mother's children.
For the zeal of Thine house hath eaten me up; and the reproaches of them that reproached Thee are fallen upon me.[^357]
And I covered my soul with fasting, and that was made a reproach to me.
And I made sackcloth my clothing, and I became a proverb unto them.
They that sit in the gate spoke against me, and they that drank wine sang against me.
But as for me, with my prayer I cry unto Thee, O Lord; it is an acceptable time, O God: in the multitude...

...of Thy mercy give ear unto me, in the truth of Thy salvation.
Deliver me out of the mire, that I sink not; let me be delivered from them that hate me, and out of the deep waters.
Let not the waterflood drown me, neither let the deep swallow me up, and let not the pit shut her mouth upon me.
Give ear unto me, O Lord, for Thy mercy is good; according to the multitude of Thy tender mercies turn unto me.
And turn not away Thy face from Thy servant, for I am in trouble; hear me speedily.
Attend unto my soul, and redeem it; deliver me because of mine enemies.
For Thou knowest my reproach, and my shame, and my confusion: mine adversaries are all before Thee.
Reproach hath broken my heart, and misery; and I looked for one to grieve with me, but there was none; and for comforters, but I found none.
They gave Me gall for My food, and in My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink.[^358]
Let their table become a snare before them, and a recompense, and a stumbling-block.
Let their eyes be darkened, that they see not, and continually bow down their back.
Pour out Thine indignation upon them, and let the wrath of Thine anger overtake them.
Let their habitation be desolate, and let none dwell in their tents.
For they persecuted him whom Thou hast smitten, and they added to the pain of my wounds.
Add iniquity unto their iniquity, and let them not come into Thy righteousness.
Let them be blotted out of the book of the living, and not be written with the righteous.
As for me, I am poor and sorrowful; let Thy salvation, O God, help me.
I will praise the name of my God with a song, and will magnify Him with praise.
And this shall please God better than a young calf that hath horns and hoofs.
Let the poor see this and be glad; seek ye God, and your soul shall live.
For the Lord hath heard the poor, and hath not despised His prisoners.
Let the heaven and the earth praise Him, the sea and everything that moves therein.
For God will save Zion, and the cities of Judah shall be built; and they shall dwell there, and inherit it.
And the seed of Thy servants shall possess it, and they that love Thy name shall dwell therein.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia, glory to Thee, O God. *(thrice)*
Lord, have mercy. *(thrice)*

*Psalm 50 (LXX):*
Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy; and according to the multitude of Thy tender mercies blot out my transgression.
Wash me thoroughly from mine iniquity, and cleanse me from my sin.
For I acknowledge mine iniquity, and my sin is continually before me.
Against Thee only have I sinned, and done evil before Thee, that Thou mightest be justified in Thy words, and overcome when Thou art judged.
For behold, I was conceived in iniquities, and in sins did my mother bear me.
For behold, Thou hast loved truth; the hidden and secret things of Thy wisdom hast Thou revealed unto me.
Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed; Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow.
Thou shalt make me to hear joy and gladness; the bones that are humbled shall rejoice.
Turn away Thy face from my sins, and blot out all mine iniquities.
Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew...

...a right spirit within me.
Cast me not away from Thy presence, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me.
Restore unto me the joy of Thy salvation, and uphold me with Thy governing Spirit.
I will teach transgressors Thy ways, and the ungodly shall turn again unto Thee.
Deliver me from blood-guiltiness, O God, Thou God of my salvation; and my tongue shall sing aloud of Thy righteousness.
O Lord, open Thou my lips, and my mouth shall declare Thy praise.
For if Thou hadst desired sacrifice, I would have given it; with burnt offerings Thou wilt not be pleased.
A sacrifice to God is a broken spirit: a broken and a contrite heart God will not despise.[^359]
Do good, O Lord, in Thy good pleasure unto Zion, and let the walls of Jerusalem be built.
Then shalt Thou be pleased with the sacrifice of righteousness, with oblation and burnt offerings; then shall they offer calves upon Thine altar.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*

*Troparion, Tone 6:*
Lord, the Jews condemned Thee unto death, the Life of all. Those who passed dryshod through the Red Sea by Moses' rod nailed Thee to the Cross; and those who sucked honey from the rock offered Thee gall. But willingly didst Thou endure this, that Thou mightest free us from the slavery of the enemy. O Christ our God, glory to Thee![^360]

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, thou art the true Vine that didst bud forth for us the Fruit of life: we entreat thee, intercede, O Lady, together with the Apostles and all the Saints, that He may have mercy on our souls!

*Then Stichera Idiomela, Tone 8:*
For fear of the Jews, Thy friend and neighbor Peter denied Thee, O Lord; and weeping he cried aloud thus: "Despise not my tears! For I said I would keep the faith, yet I kept it not! Receive our repentance also, and have mercy on us!"[^361]

*And the other choir sings the same Sticheron.*

*Verse:* They gave Me gall for My food, and in My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink. (Ps. 68:22 [LXX])

*Sticheron, Tone 8:*
Before Thy precious Cross, when the soldiers mocked Thee, O Lord, the spiritual hosts were astonished: for Thou didst adorn with flowers the earth which Thou didst frame, and Thou wast crowned with a crown of thorns; Thou Who clothest the heaven with clouds wast clothed in a purple robe of mockery. But by such endurance was Thy compassion made known, O Christ, the Great Mercy: glory to Thee!

*Verse:* They parted My garments among them, and upon My vesture did they cast lots. (Ps. 21:19 [LXX])

*And again the same Sticheron.*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 5:*
As Thou didst hang upon the Cross, O Lord, Thou didst cry aloud: "For what deed do ye wish to crucify Me, O Jews? Is it because I strengthened your paralytics? Is it because I raised your dead as from sleep? The woman with an issue of blood I healed, the Canaanite woman I pitied. For what deed do ye wish to murder Me, O Jews? But ye shall behold Christ, Whom ye now pierce, O lawbreakers!"[^362]

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
The same Sticheron.

*Then the Prokimenon, Tone 4:* For I am ready for scourges, and my sorrow is continually before me. (Ps. 37:18 [LXX])
*Verse:* O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger, neither chasten me in Thy wrath. (Ps. 37:2 [LXX])

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isaiah 50:4–11):*[^363]
The Lord gives me the tongue of instruction, that I should know when to speak a word in season; He gave me a word early, He added unto me an ear to hear. And the instruction of the Lord, the Lord opens mine ears, and I was not disobedient, nor contradicted. I turned not away...

...My back from scourgings, and My cheeks to buffeting, and I hid not My face from the shame of spittings. And the Lord was My helper; therefore I was not confounded, but I set My face as a solid rock, and I know that I shall not be ashamed. For He is near that justifies Me: who will contend with Me? Let him stand up against Me! And who is My judge? Let him draw near unto Me! Behold, the Lord will help Me: who will harm Me? Behold, all ye shall wax old as a garment, and a moth shall devour you. Who among you fears the Lord? Let him give ear to the voice of His Servant. Ye that walk in darkness and have no light, trust in the name of the Lord, and stay upon God. Behold, all ye kindle a fire, and strengthen the flame: walk in the light of your fire, and in the flame which ye have kindled. This hath happened unto you from My hand; in sorrow shall ye lie down.

*Then the Epistle to the Romans, pericopa 88 from the half (Romans 5:6–10):*[^364]
Brethren: For why did Christ, when we were yet without strength, in due time die for the ungodly? For scarcely for a righteous man will one die; yet perhaps for a good man someone would even dare to die. But God commends His love toward us, in that, while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us. Much more then, being now justified by His blood, we shall be saved from wrath through Him. For if, when we were enemies, we were reconciled to God by the death of His Son, much more, having been reconciled, we shall be saved by His life.

*Then the Gospel according to Mark, pericopas 67 and 68 (Mark 15:16–41):*[^365]
At that time, the soldiers led Jesus away into the hall called Praetorium; and they call together the whole band. And they clothed Him with purple, and platted a crown of thorns, and put it about His head, and began to salute Him: "Hail, King of the Jews!" And they smote Him on the head with a reed, and spat upon Him, and bowing their knees worshiped Him. And when they had mocked Him, they took off the purple from Him, and put His own clothes on Him, and led Him out to crucify Him. And they compel one Simon a Cyrenian, who passed by, coming out of the country, the father of Alexander and Rufus, to bear His Cross. And they bring Him unto the place Golgotha, which is, being interpreted: The Place of a Skull. And they gave Him to drink wine mingled with myrrh; but He received it not. And when they had crucified Him, they parted His garments, casting lots upon them, what every man should take. And it was the third hour, and they crucified Him. And the superscription of His accusation was written over: THE KING OF THE JEWS. And with Him they crucify two thieves: the one on His right hand, and the other on His left. And the Scripture was fulfilled, which says: "And He was numbered with the transgressors." And they that passed by railed on Him, wagging their heads, and saying: "Ah, Thou that destroyest the temple, and buildest it in three days, save Thyself, and come down from the Cross!" Likewise also the chief priests mocking said among themselves with the scribes: "He saved others; Himself He cannot save! Let Christ the King of Israel descend now from the Cross, that we may see and believe!" And they that were crucified with Him reviled Him. And when the sixth hour was come, there was darkness over the whole land until the ninth hour. And at the ninth hour Jesus cried with a loud voice, saying: "Eloi, Eloi, lama sabachthani?" which is, being interpreted: "My God, My God, why hast Thou forsaken Me?" And some of them that stood by, when they heard it, said: "Behold, He calls Elijah." And one ran and filled a sponge full of vinegar, and put it on a reed, and gave Him to drink, saying: "Let alone; let us see whether Elijah will come to take Him down." And Jesus cried with a loud voice, and gave up the spirit. And the veil of the temple was torn in two from the top to the bottom. And when the centurion, who stood over against Him, saw that He so cried out, and gave up the spirit, he said: "Truly this Man was the Son of God!" There were also women looking on afar off: among whom was Mary Magdalene, and Mary the mother of James the less and of Joses, and Salome; who also, when He was in Galilee, followed Him, and ministered unto Him; and many other women who came up with Him unto Jerusalem.

*Then:*
Direct my steps according to Thy word, and let no iniquity have dominion over me. Deliver me from the slander of men, and I will keep Thy commandments. Make Thy face to shine upon Thy servant, and teach me Thy statutes. Let my mouth be filled with Thy praise, O Lord, that I may hymn Thy glory and Thy majesty all the day long.

*Trisagion through Our Father.*

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for our sake; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree and said: "Even though Thou endurest crucifixion, Thou art My Son and My God!"

*Then:* Lord, have mercy. *(40 times)*

*And the Prayer of the Hours:*
Thou Who at all times and at every hour, in heaven and on earth, art worshiped and glorified, O Christ God, long-suffering, plenteous in mercy, most compassionate; Who lovest the righteous and hast mercy on sinners; Who callest all to salvation through the promise of good things to come: do Thou Thyself, O Lord, receive also our supplications at this hour, and direct our life in Thy commandments. Sanctify our souls, purify our bodies, set right our thoughts, cleanse our minds, and deliver us from all sorrow, evil, and pain. Compass us about with Thy holy Angels, that, guarded and guided by their host, we may attain to the unity of the faith and to the comprehension of Thine unapproachable glory: for blessed art Thou unto ages of ages. Amen.

Lord, have mercy. *(thrice)*
Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim, who without corruption gavest birth to God the Word, the very Theotokos, thee do we magnify.
In the name of the Lord, bless, Father.

**Priest:** God be merciful unto us and bless us, and cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us.

*And this Prayer of Saint Mardarios:*[^366]
O Master God, Father Almighty, and Lord, Only-begotten Son Jesus Christ, and Holy Spirit: one Godhead, one Power, have mercy on me a sinner; and by the judgments which Thou knowest, save Thine unworthy servant: for blessed art Thou unto ages of ages. Amen.

## Great and Holy Friday: Sixth Hour

**Priest:** Blessed is our God, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.

**Reader:** Amen.

Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.
O Heavenly King... Trisagion through Our Father... Lord, have mercy *(12 times).* Glory, Both now:

O come, let us worship and fall down before our King and God.
O come, let us worship and fall down before Christ, our King and God.
O come, let us worship and fall down before Christ Himself, our King and God.

*Psalm 51 (LXX):*
Why boastest thou in malice, O mighty man?
Iniquity all the day long hath thy tongue devised; as a sharpened razor hast thou wrought deceit.
Thou hast loved evil more than good, unrighteousness rather than to speak righteousness.
Thou hast loved all words of drowning, a deceitful tongue.
Therefore God shall destroy thee, O ungodly man, for ever: He shall pluck thee out and remove thee from thy dwelling-place, and thy root out of the land of the living.
The righteous shall see, and fear, and shall laugh at him, and say:
Behold the man who...

...made not God his help, but trusted in the abundance of his riches, and strengthened himself in his vanity.
But as for me, I am like a fruitful olive tree in the house of the Lord; I have trusted in the mercy of God for ever, and unto ages of ages.
I will give thanks unto Thee for ever, because Thou hast done it; and I will wait on Thy name, for it is good before Thy saints.

*Psalm 108 (LXX):*[^367]
O God, pass not over my praise in silence;
For the mouth of the sinner and the mouth of the deceitful are opened against me: they have spoken against me with a deceitful tongue.
And with words of hatred have they compassed me about, and fought against me without a cause.
In return for my love they falsely accused me, but I gave myself unto prayer.
And they rewarded me evil for good, and hatred for my love.
Set Thou a sinner over him, and let the devil stand at his right hand.
When he is judged, let him go forth condemned, and let his prayer become sin.
Let his days be few, and his bishopric let another take.
Let his children be fatherless, and his wife a widow.
Let his children wander continually, and beg; let them be cast out of their desolate places.
Let the extortioner search out all that he hath, and let strangers plunder his labors.
Let there be none to help him, neither let there be any to have compassion on his fatherless children.
Let his children be cut off; in one generation let his name be blotted out.
Let the iniquity of his fathers be remembered before the Lord, and let not the sin of his mother be blotted out.
Let them be before the Lord continually, and let the memorial of them be destroyed from the earth;
Because he remembered not to show mercy, but persecuted the poor and needy man, and was grieved at heart, to put him to death.
And he loved cursing, and it shall come unto him; and he desired not blessing, and it shall be far from him.
And he put on cursing as a garment, and it entered like water into his bowels, and like oil into his bones.
Let it be unto him as a garment wherewith he is clothed, and as a girdle wherewith he is girded continually.
This is the work of them that falsely accuse me before the Lord, and of them that speak evil against my soul.
But Thou, O Lord, Lord, deal with me for Thy name's sake, for Thy mercy is good.
Deliver me, for I am poor and needy, and my heart is troubled within me.
I am taken away like a shadow that declines; I am shaken off as the locusts.
My knees are weak through fasting, and my flesh is changed for lack of oil.
And I became a reproach unto them; they looked upon me, they shook their heads.
Help me, O Lord my God, and save me according to Thy mercy.
And let them know that this is Thy hand, and that Thou, O Lord, hast done it.
They shall curse, but Thou shalt bless; let them that rise up against me be put to shame, but Thy servant shall rejoice.
Let them that falsely accuse me be clothed with shame, and let them cover themselves with their confusion as with a mantle.
I will greatly praise the Lord with my mouth, and in the midst of many will I praise Him.
For He stood at the right hand of the poor, to save my soul from them that persecute me.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia, glory to Thee, O God. *(thrice)*
Lord, have mercy. *(thrice) Psalm 90 (LXX):*
He that dwells in the help of the Most High shall abide in the shelter of the God of heaven.
He shall say unto the Lord: Thou art my protector and my refuge, my God, and in Him will I trust.
For He shall deliver thee from the snare of the hunters, and from every troubling word.
With His pinions shall He overshadow thee, and under His wings shalt thou trust; His truth shall compass thee with a shield.
Thou shalt not be afraid for the terror of the night, nor for the arrow that flies by day;
Nor for the pestilence that walks in darkness, nor for the mishap and demon of noonday.
A thousand shall fall at thy side, and ten thousand at thy right hand, but unto thee it shall not come nigh.
Only with thine eyes shalt thou behold, and see the reward of sinners.
For Thou, O Lord, art my hope; Thou hast made the Most High thy refuge.
There shall no evil come unto thee, neither shall any scourge draw near unto thy dwelling.
For He shall give His Angels charge over thee, to keep thee in all thy ways.
In their hands they shall bear thee up, lest at any time thou dash thy foot against a stone.
Thou shalt tread upon the asp and the basilisk, and the lion and the dragon shalt thou trample underfoot.
Because he hath set his hope on Me, therefore will I deliver him; I will protect him, because he hath known My name.
He shall cry unto Me, and I will give ear unto him; I am with him in affliction, and I will deliver him and glorify him.
With length of days will I satisfy him, and show him My salvation.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia, glory to Thee, O God. *(thrice)*
Lord, have mercy. *(thrice)*

*Troparion, Tone 2:*
Thou hast wrought salvation in the midst of the earth, O Christ God. Thou didst stretch out Thy most pure hands upon the Cross; Thou hast gathered all the nations crying aloud: O Lord, glory to Thee![^368]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*
The same Troparion.

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*
Seeing that we have no boldness because of our many sins, do thou entreat Him Who was born of thee, O Virgin Theotokos: for the supplication of a Mother avails much unto the favor of the Master. Despise not the supplications of sinners, O all-pure one; for He is merciful and able to save, He Who also deigned to suffer for us!

*Then Stichera Idiomela, Tone 8:*
Thus says the Lord unto the Jews: "My people, what have I done unto thee? Or wherein have I grieved thee? Thy blind have I enlightened, thy lepers have I cleansed, the man lying upon his bed have I restored. My people, what have I done unto thee, and wherewith hast thou repaid Me? Instead of the manna, gall; instead of water, vinegar; instead of loving Me, to the Cross thou hast nailed Me! No longer will I endure you: I will call My Gentiles, and they shall glorify Me with the Father and the Spirit, and I will bestow upon them eternal life!"[^369]

*And the other choir sings the same Sticheron.*

*Verse:* They gave Me gall for My food, and in My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink. (Ps. 68:22 [LXX])

*Sticheron, Tone 8:*
Ye lawgivers of Israel, Jews and Pharisees, the apostolic choir cries aloud unto you: "Behold the Temple which ye destroyed; behold the Lamb Whom ye crucified, and delivered unto the tomb, but by His own power He arose! Deceive not yourselves, O Jews; for this is He that saved in the sea, and fed in the wilderness: this is the Life and the Light and the Peace of the world!"[^370]

*Verse:* Save me, O God, for the waters are come in even unto my soul. (Ps. 68:2 [LXX])

*And again the same Sticheron.*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 5:*
Come, ye Christ-bearing peoples, let us behold what Judas the traitor plotted with the lawless priests against our Savior! Today they rendered liable to death the immortal Word, and delivered Him to Pilate, having crucified Him at the Place of the Skull. And while suffering this, our Savior cried aloud, saying: "Father, forgive them this sin, that the Gentiles may know My resurrection from the dead!"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
The same Sticheron.

*Then the Prokimenon, Tone 4:* O Lord our Lord, how marvelous is Thy name in all the earth! (Ps. 8:2, 10 [LXX])
*Verse:* For Thy magnificence is lifted up above the heavens. (Ps. 8:2 [LXX])

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isaiah 52:13–54:1):*[^371]
Thus says the Lord: "Behold, My Servant shall understand, and shall be exalted and glorified exceedingly. As many shall be astonished at Thee, so shall Thy form be without glory from men, and Thy glory from the sons of men. So shall many nations marvel at Him, and kings shall shut their mouths: for they to whom it was not spoken concerning Him shall see, and they that have not heard shall understand. Lord, who hath believed our report? And to whom hath the arm of the Lord been revealed? We have proclaimed before Him as a child, as a root in a thirsty land: He hath no form nor comeliness; and we saw Him, and He had no form nor beauty. But His form was dishonored and forsaken among all the sons of men, a Man of sorrows, and acquainted with grief; for His face was turned away, He was dishonored and not esteemed. Surely He hath borne our griefs and carried our sorrows; yet we esteemed Him to be in pain, smitten and afflicted. But He was wounded for our transgressions, and bruised for our iniquities: the chastisement of our peace was upon Him, and with His stripes we are healed. All we like sheep have gone astray; every one hath turned to his own way; and the Lord delivered Him for our sins. And He, because He was afflicted, opens not His mouth: He was led as a sheep to the slaughter, and as a lamb before its shearer is dumb, so He opens not His mouth. In His humiliation His judgment was taken away: and who shall declare His generation? For His life is taken away from the earth; for the transgressions of My people was He led to death. And I will give the wicked for His burial, and the rich for His death; because He did no iniquity, neither was guile found in His mouth. And the Lord is pleased to cleanse Him from His stripe: if ye give an offering for sin, your soul shall see a prolonged seed. And the Lord is pleased to take away the travail of His soul, to show Him light, and to form Him with understanding; to justify the Just One Who serves many well, and He shall bear their sins. Therefore shall He inherit many, and shall divide the spoils of the strong; because His soul was delivered unto death, and He was numbered with the transgressors; and He bare the sins of many, and was delivered for their iniquities. Rejoice, thou barren that bearest not; break forth and cry, thou that travailest not with child: for more are the children of the desolate than of her that hath a husband."

*Epistle to the Hebrews, pericopa 306 (Hebrews 2:11–18):*[^372]
Brethren: Both He that sanctifies and they who are sanctified are all of one; for which cause He is not ashamed to call them brethren, saying: "I will declare Thy name unto My brethren, in the midst of the church will I sing praise unto Thee." And again: "I will put My trust...

...in Him." And again: "Behold, I and the children whom God hath given Me." Forasmuch then as the children are partakers of flesh and blood, He also Himself likewise took part of the same; that through death He might destroy him that had the power of death, that is, the devil; and deliver them who through fear of death were all their lifetime subject to bondage. For truly He took not on Him the nature of Angels; but He took on Him the seed of Abraham. Therefore in all things it behooved Him to be made like unto His brethren, that He might be a merciful and faithful High Priest in things pertaining to God, to make reconciliation for the sins of the people. For in that He Himself hath suffered being tempted, He is able to help them that are tempted.

*Then the Gospel according to Luke, pericopa 121 (Luke 23:32–49):*[^373]
At that time, there were also two other malefactors led with Jesus to be put to death. And when they were come to the place which is called Calvary, there they crucified Him, and the malefactors, one on the right hand, and the other on the left. Then said Jesus: "Father, forgive them; for they know not what they do." And they parted His raiment, and cast lots. And the people stood beholding. And the rulers also with them derided Him, saying: "He saved others; let Him save Himself, if He be Christ, the chosen of God!" And the soldiers also mocked Him, coming to Him, and offering Him vinegar, and saying: "If Thou be the King of the Jews, save Thyself!" And a superscription also was written over Him in letters of Greek, and Latin, and Hebrew: THIS IS THE KING OF THE JEWS. And one of the malefactors who were hanged railed on Him, saying: "If Thou be Christ, save Thyself and us!" But the other answering rebuked him, saying: "Dost not thou fear God, seeing thou art in the same condemnation? And we indeed justly; for we receive the due reward of our deeds: but this Man hath done nothing amiss." And he said unto Jesus: "Lord, remember me when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom!" And Jesus said unto him: "Amen I say unto thee, today shalt thou be with Me in Paradise!" And it was about the sixth hour, and there was a darkness over all the earth until the ninth hour. And the sun was darkened, and the veil of the temple was torn in the midst. And when Jesus had cried with a loud voice, He said: "Father, into Thy hands I commend My spirit!" And having said thus, He gave up the spirit. Now when the centurion saw what was done, he glorified God, saying: "Certainly this was a righteous Man!" And all the people that came together to that sight, beholding the things which were done, smote their breasts, and returned. And all His acquaintance, and the women that followed Him from Galilee, stood afar off, beholding these things.

*Then:*
Let Thy tender mercies speedily prevent us, O Lord, for we are greatly impoverished. Help us, O God our Savior, for the glory of Thy name; O Lord, deliver us, and purge away our sins, for Thy name's sake.

*Trisagion through Our Father.*

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for our sake; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree and said: "Even though Thou endurest crucifixion, Thou art My Son and My God!" *(see fol. 102)*

*Lord, have mercy (40 times).*

*Then the Prayer of the Hours:* Thou Who at all times and at every hour...
*Lord, have mercy (thrice). Glory, Both now: More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord, bless, Father.*

**Priest:** Through the prayers of our holy Fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us.

*Then this Prayer:*[^374]
O God and Lord of powers, and Maker of all creation, Who through the compassion of Thine incomprehensible mercy didst send down Thine Only-begotten Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, for the salvation of our race, and by His precious Cross didst tear asunder the handwriting of our sins, and didst triumph over the principalities and powers of darkness: do Thou Thyself, O Master, Lover of mankind, accept also from us sinners these thanksgiving and supplicatory prayers; and deliver us from every destructive and dark transgression, and from all visible and invisible enemies that seek to harm us... [Some do not say this prayer today at all three Hours.]

*And after the Prayer, immediately the Ninth Hour.*

## Great and Holy Friday: Ninth Hour

O come, let us worship and fall down before our King and God.
O come, let us worship and fall down before Christ, our King and God.
O come, let us worship and fall down before Christ Himself, our King and God.

*Psalm 58 (LXX):*
Deliver me from mine enemies, O God, and from them that rise up against me redeem me.
Deliver me from them that work iniquity, and save me from men of blood.
For behold, they have hunted after my soul; the mighty have set upon me: not for mine iniquity, nor for my sin, O Lord.
Without iniquity have I run and directed my steps: arise Thou to meet me, and behold.
And Thou, O Lord God of powers, the God of Israel, give ear to visit all the heathen; be not merciful to any that work iniquity.
They shall return at evening, and hunger like dogs, and go round about the city.
Behold, they shall speak with their mouth, and a sword is in their lips: for who, say they, hath heard?
And Thou, O Lord, shalt laugh at them; Thou shalt bring all the heathen to nought.
My strength, upon Thee will I wait, for Thou, O God, art my defense.
As for my God, His mercy shall prevent me; God shall reveal it unto me among mine enemies.
Slay them not, lest they forget Thy law; scatter them by Thy power, and bring them down, O Lord, my protector.
The sin of their mouth is the word of their lips: and let them be taken in their pride, and for cursing and lying shall they be spoken of in their destruction.
In the wrath of their destruction they shall cease, and shall not be; and they shall know that God rules over Jacob, and unto the ends of the earth.
They shall return at evening, and hunger like dogs, and go round about the city.
They shall be scattered abroad to eat; and if they be not satisfied, they shall murmur.
But as for me, I will sing of Thy power, and will rejoice in the morning at Thy mercy.
For Thou hast been my protector...

...and my refuge in the day of my trouble.
Unto Thee will I sing, O my Helper; for Thou, O God, art my defense, my God, my mercy.

*Psalm 139 (LXX):*
Deliver me, O Lord, from the evil man; rescue me from the unrighteous man;
