Who have devised unrighteousness in their heart; all the day long did they prepare wars.
They have sharpened their tongue like a serpent; the poison of asps is under their lips.
Keep me, O Lord, from the hand of the sinner; rescue me from unrighteous men, who have purposed to trip up my goings.
The proud have hidden a snare for me; and with cords have they spread a net for my feet; they have set stumbling-blocks for me near the path.
I said unto the Lord: Thou art my God; give ear, O Lord, to the voice of my supplication.
O Lord, Lord, the strength of my salvation, Thou hast overshadowed my head in the day of battle.
Deliver me not, O Lord, from my desire unto the sinner: they have plotted against me; forsake me not, lest they be exalted.
As for the head of them that compass me about, the mischief of their own lips shall cover them.
Coals of fire shall fall upon them; Thou shalt cast them down into the fire; in afflictions they shall not stand.
A man full of tongue shall not prosper upon the earth; evils shall hunt the unrighteous man unto destruction.
I know that the Lord will execute judgment for the needy, and justice for the poor.
Surely the righteous shall give thanks unto Thy name, and the upright shall dwell in Thy presence.

*Psalm 85 (LXX):*
Bow down Thine ear, O Lord, and hear me, for I am poor and needy.
Preserve my soul, for I am holy; O Thou my God, save Thy servant that hopes in Thee.
Be merciful unto me, O Lord, for unto Thee will I cry all the day long.
Rejoice the soul of Thy servant, for unto Thee, O Lord, have I lifted up my soul.
For Thou, O Lord, art good and gentle, and plenteous in mercy unto all them that call upon Thee.
Give ear, O Lord, unto my prayer, and attend to the voice of my supplication.
In the day of my trouble I cried unto Thee, for Thou hast heard me.
There is none like unto Thee among the gods, O Lord, neither are there any works like unto Thy works.
All nations whom Thou hast made shall come and worship before Thee, O Lord, and shall glorify Thy name.
For Thou art great, and workest wonders; Thou art God alone.
Guide me, O Lord, in Thy way, and I will walk in Thy truth; let my heart rejoice, that I may fear Thy name.
I will praise Thee, O Lord my God, with all my heart, and I will glorify Thy name for ever.
For great is Thy mercy toward me, and Thou hast delivered my soul from the lowest Hades.
O God, transgressors have risen up against me, and the assembly of the violent have sought after my soul, and have not set Thee before them.
And Thou, O Lord my God, art compassionate and merciful, long-suffering, and plenteous in mercy and true.
Look upon me, and have mercy on me; give Thy strength unto Thy servant, and save the son of Thine handmaid.
Work in me a sign for good, and let them that hate me see it and be put to shame, because Thou, O Lord, hast helped me and comforted me.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia, glory to Thee, O God. *(thrice)*
Lord, have mercy. *(thrice) Then, Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Beholding the Author of life hanging upon the Cross, the Thief said: "If He Who is crucified with us were not God incarnate, the sun would not have hidden its rays, nor would the earth quaking have been shaken! But Thou Who endurest all things: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom!"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*
Thou Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, O Good One, Who by death didst despoil death and didst manifest the Resurrection as God: despise not those whom Thou hast fashioned with Thine own hand; show forth Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; accept the Theotokos that bare Thee, who intercedes for us; and save, O our Savior, a despairing people!

*Then Stichera Idiomela, Tone 7:*
A wonder it was to behold the Creator of heaven and earth hanging upon the Cross, the sun darkened, the day turned into night, and the earth giving up the bodies of the dead from their tombs! With them we worship Thee, our Savior! *(twice)*

*We sing the Verse, Tone 2:*
*Verse:* They parted My garments among them, and upon My vesture did they cast lots. (Ps. 21:19 [LXX])

*Sticheron, Tone 2:*
When the lawless nailed the Lord of glory to the Cross, He cried aloud unto them: "Wherein have I grieved you, or wherein have I provoked you to anger? Before Me, who delivered you from afflictions? And now what do ye repay Me, evil for good? Instead of the pillar of fire, ye have nailed Me to the Cross; instead of the cloud, ye have dug Me a tomb; instead of the manna, ye have brought Me gall; instead of water, ye have given Me vinegar to drink! Henceforth will I call the Gentiles, and they shall glorify Me with the Father and the Holy Spirit!"

*Verse:* They gave Me gall for My food, and in My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink. (Ps. 68:22 [LXX])

*And again the same Sticheron.*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 6:*

*[Woodcut Illustration: The Crucifixion at Golgotha. Christ hangs upon the Cross between the two thieves, beneath the darkened sun and moon; the Mother of God, the holy women, and Saint John stand at the foot of the Cross, alongside Roman soldiers; the skull of Adam is visible beneath the base of the Cross; the walls and towers of Jerusalem appear in the distance.]*

Today He Who hung the earth upon the waters hangs upon the Tree!
He Who is King of Angels is arrayed in a crown of thorns!
He Who wraps the heaven in clouds is wrapped in a purple robe of mockery!
He Who freed Adam in the Jordan receives a buffet!
The Bridegroom of the Church is pierced with nails!
The Son of the Virgin is pierced with a spear!
We worship Thy Passions, O Christ!
We worship Thy Passions, O Christ!
We worship Thy Passions, O Christ!
Show us also Thy glorious Resurrection!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
The same Sticheron.

*Then the Prokimenon, Psalm 13, Tone 4:* The fool hath said in his heart: There is no God. (Ps. 13:1 [LXX])
*Verse:* There is none that does good, no, not one. (Ps. 13:1 [LXX])

*Reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah (Jer. 11:18–23; 12:1–5, 9–11, 14–15):*
O Lord, make known to me, and I shall know; then saw I their devices... *(see fol. 77)[^375] on Great Thursday at the 4th Hour, ending: "and each into their own land."*

*Then immediately the Epistle to the Hebrews, pericopa 324 (Hebrews 10:19–31):*
Brethren...

Brethren, having therefore boldness to enter into the holies by the blood of Jesus, by a new and living way which He has consecrated for us through the veil, that is to say, His flesh; and having a Great Priest over the house of God: let us draw near with a true heart in full assurance of faith, having our hearts sprinkled from an evil conscience, and our bodies washed with pure water. Let us hold fast the confession of our hope without wavering (for He is faithful That promised); and let us consider one another to provoke unto love and good works: not forsaking our own assembly, as the manner of some is; but exhorting one another, and so much the more as ye see the [Day of Judgment] approaching. For if we sin willfully after having received the knowledge of the truth, there remains no more sacrifice for sins, but a certain fearful expectation of judgment, and fiery indignation which shall devour the adversaries. Anyone who rejected Moses' law died without mercy upon the testimony of two or three witnesses: of how much worse punishment, think ye, shall he be deemed worthy, who has trampled underfoot the Son of God, and has counted the blood of the covenant, wherewith he was sanctified, an unholy thing, and has insulted the Spirit of grace? For we know Him Who said: Vengeance is Mine, I will repay, says the Lord. And again: The Lord shall judge His people. It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God.

*Then, the Gospel from John, pericopa 59 [John 18:28–19:37]:*  
*At that time, they led Jesus from Caiaphas into the Praetorium... Ending: They shall look on Him Whom they pierced.*

*Then: Cast us not off unto the end... Trisagion. After Our Father: Kontakion: He Who for our sake was crucified... (see fol. 102).[^376] Then, Lord, have mercy (40 times). Prayer: At all times... Lord, have mercy (thrice). Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. More honorable than the Cherubim... In the Name of the Lord, bless, father. Priest: Through the prayers of our holy fathers...*

*At the Ninth Hour.*

*Then, this Prayer:*  
*O Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who art long-suffering... Ending: now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Then immediately the Beatitudes: In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest: We sing the sixth verse.*  
*The Heavenly Choir... Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: The assembly of holy Angels... Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: I believe in one God... Forgive, remit... We sing: Our Father... Kontakion: He Who for our sake was crucified... Lord, have mercy (40 times). And the Prayer: O All-Holy Trinity, consubstantial Dominion unconstrained... Then: Blessed be the Name of the Lord (thrice). Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Psalm 33: I will bless the Lord at all times... Then: It is truly meet... More honorable than the Cherubim... Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Lord, have mercy (twice). Lord, bless. And the Dismissal.*

*It should also be known that we have received tradition not to celebrate the Presanctified, nor again the full Liturgy, on this holy day of Great Friday. Therefore, we do not even set a refectory table, nor do we eat on this day of the Crucifixion. But if anyone is exceedingly infirm or very aged, and unable to remain fasting, let bread and water be given him after the setting of the sun. For thus have we received from the holy injunctions of the holy Apostles,[^377] not to eat on Great Friday. For the Lord's word also, which He spoke to the Pharisees:*

*to the Pharisees: That when the Bridegroom shall be taken away from them, then shall they fast in those days. Which the most blessed Apostles also observed, as thou shalt find in the Apostolic Traditions, that is, in the Canons, by examining these things attentively.*

*In the Studite Typikon, moreover, it is commanded that on the holy Great Friday of Holy Great Week, foods are given according to the first week of the Fast.[^378]*

*The Typikon of the Holy Mountain, however, judges that on Great Friday one may eat bread and uncooked vegetables. Even as in another determination it testifies consistently, saying: On Great Friday, if it be possible, it is not proper to eat, as the Fathers command. Yet we do not hold this as an absolute prohibition from not eating sacred things, but it was appointed by the Fathers according to each one's strength. And for this reason, if it be possible to keep these two days in fasting, Friday and Saturday, it is good and acceptable. But if not, at least on Saturday. On Great Friday, then, eat bread and uncooked vegetables, and drink water; yet in this also, as each wills and is able. Only let all things be done with the discernment of the Superior, or of the most experienced.*

## Typikon for the Feast of the Annunciation on Great and Holy Friday[^379]

*Chapter 96. It should be known how to proceed if the Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos coincides with Great Friday.*

*On Thursday evening, at "Lord, I have cried": Stichera to 10: of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: of the day, Tone 6: "The gathering of the iniquitous..."; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: of the Feast, the same Tone: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent...". Entrance with the Gospel. "O Gladsome Light". Prokeimenon and Readings of the day, and of the Feast, 3 readings. Little Litany. Exclamation, and Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel of the day. And the rest of the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great, as indicated previously. At the refectory, we partake of oil and wine.*

*At the sixth hour of the night, they strike the great bell and all the heavy bells, and we begin Matins. At "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Feast (twice). Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: "When the glorious disciples...". After this, the Little Litany. And immediately the Priest says: "That we may be accounted worthy to hear the Holy Gospel...", the 1st Gospel. Then the Antiphons and the Gospels of the Holy Passions according to their order. At the conclusion of the Antiphons, the Exclamation: "He Who for our sake was crucified...". Then the appointed Gospel, the 6th. Then the Beatitudes with 6 verses, and the Prokeimenon of the Holy Passions, Tone 4: "They parted My garments among them...". The 7th Gospel, and the Reading of the day. After this, the Polyeleos and the Megalynarion, and the Reading of the Feast. Gradual Hymns: the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon: "Let every breath praise the Lord". The Gospel of the Feast: from Luke, pericopa 4: "Mary arose...". After this, the 8th Passion Gospel is read immediately in course. Then Psalm 50, and the Sticheron of the Feast. Priest, the Prayer: "Save, O God, Thy people..."; and we: "Lord, have mercy" (12 times). The Priest chants the Exclamation: "Through the mercy and compassions...". Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos to 14; Katavasia of the Feast, both choirs together. Where there is the Triodion, with the Heirmos to 8; afterwards the Katavasia is the Heirmos of the Triodion, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn: Reading of the Feast, "Again joy...". After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast and Ikos, and the Reading of the day. At Ode 9, we chant "More honorable than the Cherubim...". Exapostilarion of the Feast. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: of the Feast. Then the 9th Passion Gospel. At the Praises, Stichera Idiomela of the Holy Passions. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 8: "A reed is dipped in vinegar..."; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: of the Feast, the same Tone: "Let the heavens rejoice...". Then the 10th Gospel. After this, the Great Doxology. Then the Priest: "Let us complete our morning prayer...", and the Exclamation. After this, the 11th Gospel. At the Aposticha, Stichera Prosomoia, Tone 4. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: of the day, Tone 6: "They stripped Me of My garments..."; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: of the Feast: "The Angel, seeing...". Then the 12th Gospel. After this: "It is good to give praise...". After Our Father: Troparion, Tone 4: "Today is the fountainhead of our salvation...". Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: of the Feast. Litany, Wisdom, and the Dismissal. And the First Hour we sing as appointed on Great Friday; there is said at it the Troparion of the Feast and of the day, and the Kontakion of the Feast.*

*At the 5th hour of the day, they strike the small semantron, and we make a Litiya outside the monastery. And having returned, we sing the Third Hour, the Sixth, and the Ninth, with their Troparia, and with the Paremias, and with the Epistles, with the Gospels, and with the rest of their service. Likewise the Troparia are sung: that of the day and that of the Feast. The Kontakia of the day: "He Who for our sake was crucified...". At the Ninth Hour, after the Trisagion, the Reading of the day; then: "Cast us not off..."; after Our Father, Kontakion of the day: "He Who for our sake was crucified...", "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), and the Prayer: "O Master, Lord Jesus Christ...". Then the Beatitudes: "In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest...". Rapidly all the chants: "Remember us, O Lord...", "The heavenly choir...", "I believe in one God...", "Forgive, remit...". After Our Father:*

*Kontakion: "He Who for our sake was crucified..."; "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), and the Prayer: "O All-Holy Trinity, consubstantial Dominion unconstrained...". And the Dismissal. "I will bless the Lord at all times..." we do not say, because the Liturgy is celebrated.*

*At the 8th hour of the day, they strike the great bell and all the heavy bells. We begin VESPERS. And the Priest performs the Proskomide. At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera to 10: of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: of the day, Tone 6: "How the iniquitous assembly..."; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: of the Feast, the same Tone: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent...". Entrance with the Gospel. "O Gladsome Light". Prokeimenon and Readings of the day, and of the Feast, 5 readings. "Let my prayer be set forth". Little Litany. Then, Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel: first of the Feast, and then of the day. Communion Hymn of the Feast. The Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated. At the refectory, we eat dry food and drink wine.*

## Typikon for the Feast of the Holy Great-Martyr George[^380]

*It should be known concerning this: If the commemoration of the holy Great-Martyr George coincides with Great Friday. For this begins (when the Paschalion key number is 88) and extends unto the Thursday of the 5th week after Pascha.*

*Chapter 100. If it falls on Great Friday, or on Great Saturday, or on Pascha itself: The service of the Saint is sung, with the other service, on the Monday of Bright Week, together with the daily service, even as it was indicated for the Feast of the Annunciation.*

*Chapter 101. If the commemoration of the holy Great-Martyr George coincides with one of the days of Bright Week: If the Superior wishes to sing Great Vespers, let it be sung as appointed in the Order of the Church Assembly, or in the Chapter on the Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos. On Bright Monday we sing Small Vespers. Then at Great Vespers, Stichera to 10: Resurrectional, 3; and 1 Eastern; and to the Saint, 6. If not, in the evening at "Lord, I have cried": Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and to the Saint, 3. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 6: "A name worthy of thy deeds..."; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: Theotokion, Tone 1. Entrance. "O Gladsome Light". Prokeimenon of the day, and 3 Readings for the Saint. At the Aposticha, a Resurrectional Sticheron, one from the Octoechos; then the Paschal Stichera with their verses. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 4: "O spiritual adamant..."; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen, Tone 5: "This is the day of Resurrection...". "Christ is risen from the dead" (thrice). Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: Troparion of the Saint; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: Theotokion, Tone 1. Priest: Wisdom. And the Dismissal.*

*AT MATINS. After "Christ is risen from the dead" and the four verses: Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: Troparion of the Saint; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: Theotokion, Tone 1. Then the usual Litanies, and after these the Polyeleos. Then the Sessional Hymn to the Saint. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: Theotokion, the Tone, and the Reading of the day. Gradual Hymns: the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon of the Saint: "Let every breath praise the Lord". The Gospel of the Saint. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" (thrice). Psalm 50 is not said. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: "Through the prayers of the Passion-bearer..."; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: "Through the prayers of the Theotokos...". Then the Sticheron of the Saint, Tone 6: "Today the whole universe...". Then the Priest says: "Save, O God, Thy people..."; and we: "Lord, have mercy" (12 times). Exclamation. Canon of Pascha with the Heirmos to 8, and to the Saint to 6; Katavasia: the Heirmos of the day of Resurrection. After Ode 3, Kontakion of Pascha and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn to the Saint. After Ode 6, Kontakion to the Saint and Ikos, and the Reading. At Ode 9, we chant "More honorable than the Cherubim...". Exapostilarion of Pascha. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: to the Saint; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: of Pascha. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and to the Saint, 3. Then the verse: "Let God arise...", and the Paschal Stichera with their verses. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: to the Saint, Tone 2: "Let the right hand be raised..."; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen, Tone 5: "This is the day of Resurrection...". "Christ is risen from the dead" (thrice). Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: Troparion of the Saint; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: Theotokion, of the same Tone. Litanies, and Dismissal. The Litiya is outside the monastery. The Hours we sing of Pascha according to custom.*

*AT THE LITURGY. Antiphons of Pascha. After the Entrance: "Christ is risen...". Then Troparion to the Saint. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: Kontakion of the Saint; Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen: Kontakion of Pascha. Instead of the Trisagion: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ...". Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn: first of the day, and then of the Saint.*

## Great and Holy Friday: Vespers

*AT THE TENTH HOUR OF THE DAY.*

*[Woodcut Illustration: The Deposition from the Cross. Joseph of Arimathea and Nicodemus take down the Body of Christ from the Cross with a linen shroud, while the Mother of God, Saint John, and the holy women embrace and lament over Him.][^381]*

*They strike the great wooden board [Klepalo], and when we are gathered in the church, we begin Vespers. The Priest having begun: Blessed is our God; and we: Amen. O Heavenly King... Trisagion. After Our Father: Lord, have mercy (12 times). Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Come, let us worship (thrice). Then Psalm 103: Bless the Lord, O my soul... Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Alleluia (thrice). We chant the Great Litany. We do not say the Kathisma.*

*On "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and we sing the Stichera Idiomela:[^382]*

*Tone 1:*  
All creation was changed with fear, beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ! The sun was darkened, and the foundations of the earth were shaken; all things suffered with the Creator of all: O Thou Who didst willingly endure for our sake, O Lord, glory to Thee! *(twice)*

*Tone 2:*  
O lawless and transgressive people, why have ye meditated on vain things? Why have ye condemned to death the Life of all? A great marvel it is that the Creator of the world is delivered into the hands of the lawless, and the Lover of mankind is lifted up upon the Tree, that He may deliver the captives in Hades who cry aloud: O Long-suffering Lord, glory to Thee!

*Tone 2:*  
Today the spotless Virgin, beholding Thee, O Word, lifted up upon the Cross, lamenting with a Mother's inward grief, was wounded bitterly in heart, and groaning in agony from the depths of her soul, she tore her outstretched hair; therefore beating her breast, she cried out piteously: "Woe is me, O Divine Child! Woe is me, O Light of the world! Why hast Thou set from mine eyes, O Lamb of God?" Whereat the armies of the bodiless hosts, seized with trembling, cried aloud: "O Incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee!"

*Tone 2:*  
Beholding Thee hanging upon the Tree, O Christ, the Creator and God of all, she who bare Thee without seed cried bitterly: "O my Son, whither has the beauty of Thy countenance departed? I cannot endure to behold Thee unjustly crucified! Hasten therefore, arise, that I also may see Thy Resurrection from the dead on the third day!"

*Tone 6:*  
Today the Master of creation stands before Pilate, and the Creator of all is delivered over to the Cross, led as a sheep to the slaughter by His own will; He is pierced with nails, and wounded in His side, and He Who rained down manna is given gall and vinegar with a sponge; the Redeemer of the world is struck upon the cheek, and the Fashioner of all is mocked by His own servants! O Master of love for mankind! For those who crucified Him He prayed unto His Father, saying: "Father, forgive them this sin, for the lawless know not what injustice they do!"

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 6:*  
Oh, how the lawless assembly condemned the King of creation to death, without shame for His benefactions, which He recalled, confirming them:

saying unto them: "O My people, what have I done unto you? Have I not filled Judea with wonders? Have I not raised the dead with a single word? Have I not healed every sickness and disease? What therefore do ye render unto Me? Why do ye not remember Me? For healings ye have laid wounds upon Me; for life ye put Me to death, crucifying upon the Tree as an evildoer your Benefactor, as a transgressor your Lawgiver, as a condemned criminal the King of all! O Long-suffering Lord, glory to Thee!"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen, the same Tone:*  
A fearful and marvelous mystery is seen accomplished today! He Who is intangible is held fast; He Who looses Adam from the curse is bound; He Who searches hearts and reins is unjustly tried; He Who shut up the abyss is shut up in prison; He stands before Pilate, before Whom the heavenly Hosts stand with trembling; the Creator is struck by the hand of the creature; He Who judges the living and the dead is condemned to the Tree; the Destroyer of Hades is enclosed in a tomb! O Thou Who endurest all things in Thy tender mercy, and hast saved all from the curse: O Gentle Lord, glory to Thee!

*Entrance with the Gospel.[^383]*

*O Gladsome Light.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 21, Tone 4:* They parted My garments among them, and upon My vesture did they cast lots. (Ps. 21:19 [LXX])  
*Verse:* O God, My God, attend unto Me; why hast Thou forsaken Me? (Ps. 21:2 [LXX])

*Reading from Exodus (Exodus 33:11–23):[^384]*  
The Lord spoke unto Moses face to face, as a man would speak unto his own friend; and he returned into the camp. But his servant Joshua, the son of Nun, a young man, departed not out of the tabernacle. And Moses said unto the Lord: "See, Thou sayest unto me: Bring up this people; but Thou hast not shown me whom Thou wilt send with me. Yet Thou hast said unto me: I know thee above all, and thou hast found grace with Me. If therefore I have found grace in Thy sight, manifest Thyself unto me, that I may clearly see Thee, that I may find grace in Thy sight, and that they may know that this nation is Thy people." And the Lord said unto him: "I Myself will go before thee, and I will give thee rest." And Moses said unto Him: "If Thou Thyself go not with us, lead me not up from hence. And how shall it be known truly that I and Thy people have found grace with Thee, except Thou go with us? So shall I and Thy people be glorified above all the nations that are upon the earth." And the Lord said unto Moses: "I will do this word also that thou hast spoken, for thou hast found grace in My sight, and I know thee above all." And Moses said: "Show me Thy glory!" And the Lord said: "I will go before thee with My glory, and I will proclaim by Name the Lord before thee; and I will have mercy on whom I will have mercy, and I will have compassion on whom I will have compassion." And He said: "Thou canst not see the glory of My face; for man shall not see My face and live." And the Lord said: "Behold, there is a place by Me, and thou shalt stand upon the rock; and while My glory passes by, I will put thee in a cleft of the rock, and will cover thee with My hand until I have passed by. And I will take away My hand, and then shalt thou see My back parts; but My face shall not be shown unto thee."

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 34, Tone 4:* Judge Thou, O Lord, them that wrong me; overthrow them that fight against me. (Ps. 34:1 [LXX])  
*Verse:* Take hold of weapon and buckler, and rise up to help me. (Ps. 34:2 [LXX])

*Reading from Job (Job 42:12–17 and the Syrian Epilogue):[^385]*  
The Lord blessed the latter end of Job more than his beginning. For he had fourteen thousand sheep, six thousand camels, a thousand yoke of oxen, and a thousand she-asses feeding in herds. And there were born unto him seven sons and three daughters. And he called the first Day, the second Cassia, and the third Amalthea's Horn [Keren-happuch]. And there were found no women so fair as the daughters of Job in all the world under heaven. And their father Job gave them inheritance among their brethren. And Job lived after this affliction an hundred and seventy years; and all the years that he lived were two hundred and forty-eight. And Job saw his sons, and his sons' sons, even unto the fourth generation. And Job died, an old man and full of days. And it is written that he shall rise again with those whom the Lord raises up. And this is interpreted from the Syrian Book: He dwelt indeed in the land of Ausitis, on the borders of Idumea and Arabia. His name previously was Jobab; and having taken an Arabian wife, he begat a son whose name was Enon. And he himself was the son of his father Zareph, a son of the sons of Esau, and of his mother Bosorra; so that he was the fifth from Abraham.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isaiah 52:13–54:1):*  
*Thus says the Lord: Behold, My Servant shall understand... (see at the 6th Hour, fol. 110 / 123)[^386]; ending: "than she who has a husband."*

*Then immediately the Prokeimenon, Psalm 87, Tone 6:* They laid Me in the lowest pit, in dark places, and in the shadow of death. (Ps. 87:7 [LXX])  
*Verse:* O Lord God of my salvation, I have cried day and night before Thee. (Ps. 87:2 [LXX])

*The Epistle to the Corinthians, pericopa 125 (1 Cor. 1:18–2:2):[^387]*  
*Brethren, the preaching of the cross is to them that perish foolishness...*

*Alleluia, Tone 1:* Save Me, O God, for the waters are come in unto My soul. (Ps. 68:2 [LXX])  
*Verse:* Reproach has my soul expected, and distress. (Ps. 68:21 [LXX])  
*Verse:* Let their eyes be darkened, that they see not. (Ps. 68:24 [LXX])

*The Gospel from Matthew, pericopa 110 [composite Passion Gospel: Matt. 27:1–38; Luke 23:39–43; Matt. 27:39–54; John 19:31–37; Matt. 27:55–61]:[^388]*  
*At that time, all the chief priests and elders of the people took counsel against Jesus to put Him to death...*

*Then the Litany: Let us all say with our whole soul and with our whole mind... Then: Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin... Let us complete our evening prayer... and the rest of the diaconal petitions.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera Prosomoia, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ Дре́ва):[^389]*  
When from the Tree Joseph of Arimathea took Thee down dead, the Life of all, he wrapped Thee, O Christ, with myrrh and a clean Plashchanytsia, and was moved by love to kiss Thy spotless Body with heart and lips; yet held in awe, he cried aloud unto Thee rejoicing: "Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind!"

*Verse:* The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty. (Ps. 92:1 [LXX])  
When in a new tomb Thou wast laid for all, O Redeemer of all, all-mocking Hades, beholding Thee, was dismayed; its bars were broken, its gates were shattered, the tombs were opened, the dead arose! Then Adam, rejoicing in thanksgiving, cried aloud unto Thee: "Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind!"

*Verse:* For He has established the world, which shall not be shaken. (Ps. 92:1 [LXX]) When in the tomb Thou wast enclosed according to the flesh by Thine own will, remaining in the nature of Thy Godhead uncircumscribed and boundless: Thou didst shut up the treasuries of death, and didst despoil all the kingdoms of Hades, O Christ! Then didst Thou account the Sabbath worthy of Divine blessing and glory, and of Thy radiance!

*Verse:* Holiness befits Thine house, O Lord, unto length of days. (Ps. 92:5 [LXX])  
When the hosts of heaven beheld Thee, O Christ, slandered by the lawless as a deceiver, they were struck with awe at Thine ineffable long-suffering, and at the stone of the tomb being sealed by those very hands that pierced Thine undefiled side; yet rejoicing at our salvation, they cried aloud: "Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind!"

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen, Tone 5:[^390]*  
Taking Thee down from the Tree with Nicodemus, Joseph, who saw Thee dead, naked, and unburied, Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, took up a compassionate lamentation, and weeping, said: "Woe is me, O sweetest Jesus! Whom the sun, beholding a little while ago hanging upon the Cross, wrapped itself in darkness, and the earth was shaken with fear, and the veil of the temple was rent in twain! But lo, now I behold Thee, Who for my sake didst willingly accept death! How shall I bury Thee, O my God? Or with what Plashchanytsia shall I wrap Thee? With what hands shall I touch Thine undefiled Body? Or what hymns shall I sing unto Thy departure, O Compassionate One? I magnify Thy Passions, I praise Thy Burial with Thy Resurrection, crying: O Lord, glory to Thee!"

*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace... Trisagion. After Our Father:*

*Troparion, Tone 2:[^391]*  
The noble Joseph, taking down Thy spotless Body from the Tree, wrapped it in a clean Plashchanytsia and sweet spices, and laying it in a new tomb, closed it.

*And the Dismissal.*

## Great and Holy Friday: Small Compline

*Compline in the cells, or according to the Superior in the church, according to custom.*

*[Woodcut Illustration: The Entombment of Christ. The holy Body of Christ is laid into the stone tomb by Joseph of Arimathea and Nicodemus, while the Virgin Mary presses her face to His, surrounded by Saint John and the weeping Myrrh-bearing women.][^393]*

*Concerning this, the rubric is above; see fol. 85.[^392]*

*Then at it we sing also the CANON on the Crucifixion of the Lord, AND ON THE LAMENTATION OF THE THEOTOKOS.*

*Heirmos twice, Troparia to 4; and at the end, the Heirmos by both choirs together.*

*A WORK OF SYMEON LOGOTHETE. Tone 6.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, seeing Pharaoh their pursuer drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory!

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

*Troparion:*  
Beholding her Son and Lord suspended upon the Cross, the pure Virgin was torn with anguish and cried bitterly; groaning with the other women, she said:

*Troparion:*  
"Now I behold Thee, my beloved and dearest Child, hanging upon the Cross, and my heart is bitterly wounded," said the Pure One: "give a word, O Good One, unto Thine handmaid!"

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*

*Troparion:*  
"Willingly, O my Son and Creator, Thou endurest upon the Tree a bitter death," said the Virgin, standing by the Cross with the beloved disciple.

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*  
"Now am I bereft of my expectation, my joy and gladness, my Son and my Lord! Woe is me, my heart is in pain!" cried the Pure One, weeping.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established them upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

*Troparion:*  
"For fear of the Jews Peter hid himself, and all the faithful fled away, forsaking Christ," cried the Virgin, weeping.

*Troparion:*  
"At Thy strange and awesome birth-giving, O my Son, I was magnified above all mothers; but woe is me, now beholding Thee upon the Tree, mine inward parts are inflamed!"

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*

*Troparion:*  
"I would receive upon my hands from the Tree Thine inward parts, on which I held Thee as an Infant," said the Pure One; "yet alas, no one gives Him unto me!"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*  
"Lo, my sweet Light, my Hope and good Life, my God is extinguished upon the Cross! Mine inward parts are inflamed!" cried the Virgin, groaning.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
Christ is my power, my God and Lord, the venerable Church divinely sings, crying out from a pure conscience, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

*Troparion:*  
"O unsetting Sun, eternal God, and Creator of all creatures, O Lord, how dost Thou endure suffering upon the Cross?" cried the Pure One, weeping.

*Troparion:*  
Weeping, the Unwedded One said unto the noble Joseph: "Hasten, Joseph, draw near unto Pilate, and ask to take down from the Tree thy Teacher!"

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*

*Troparion:*  
Seeing the All-Pure One shedding bitter tears, Joseph was troubled, and weeping, drew near unto Pilate, crying aloud with tears: "Give me the Body of my God!"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*  
"Beholding Thee wounded, inglorious, and naked upon the Tree, O my Child, mine inward parts are consumed as I weep," said the Virgin Mother.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
With Thy Divine light, O Good One, illuminate, I pray Thee, the souls of those who rise early unto Thee with love, that they may see Thee, O Word of God, the true God, recalling them from the gloom of sin.

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

*Troparion:*  
Torn with grief and weeping, and marveling together with Nicodemus, Joseph took Him down, and kissing the spotless Body, wept and groaned, and praised Him as God.

*Troparion:*  
Receiving Him with tears, the Mother that knew not wedlock laid Him upon her knees, entreating Him with tears and kissing Him, bitterly lamenting and crying aloud:

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*

*Troparion:*  
"As mine only hope and life, O Master, my Son and my God, in the light of mine eyes had I Thee, Thine handmaid; but now am I bereft of Thee, my sweet and beloved Child!"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*  
"Pains and sorrows and sighings have overtaken me, woe is me!" cried the Pure One, weeping bitterly, "beholding Thee, my beloved Child, naked and solitary, a dead corpse anointed with sweet spices!"

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Bring up my life from corruption, O Greatly Merciful One!

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

*Troparion:*  
"Beholding Thee dead, O Lover of mankind, Who didst revive the dead, torn with bitter sorrow and wounded in mine inward parts, I would fain die with Thee," said the All-Pure One: "for I cannot bear to behold Thee lifeless and dead!"

*Troparion:*  
"Marveling as I behold Thee, O Most Good God and Most Merciful Lord, without glory, without breath, and without form, weeping as I hold Thee, as I never expected, woe is me, to see Thee, my Son and God!"

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*

*Troparion:*  
"Wilt Thou not speak a word unto Thine handmaid, O Word of God? Wilt Thou have no compassion, O Master, on her who bare Thee?" said the Pure One, lamenting and weeping, kissing the Body of her Lord.

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*  
"I ponder, O Master, that no more shall I hear Thy sweet voice, nor see the beauty of Thy face, as formerly did Thine handmaid; for Thou hast set from mine eyes, O my Son!"

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*  
Come, all ye faithful, let us praise Him Who for our sake was crucified; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree, and said: "Though Thou endurest crucifixion, Thou art my Son and my God!"

*Ikos:*  
Beholding her own Lamb led to the slaughter, Mary followed with hair disheveled, together with the other women, crying aloud thus: "Whither goest Thou, O Child? For what cause dost Thou complete this swift course? Is there another marriage again in Cana of Galilee, and dost Thou now hasten thither to make wine for them from water? Shall I go with Thee, O Child, or shall I rather wait for Thee? Give me a word, O WORD, pass me not by in silence, Thou Who didst keep me pure! For Thou art my Son and my God!"

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Angel made the furnace pour down dew for the Holy Children, and the command of God consumed the Chaldeans, compelling the tyrant to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

*Troparion:*  
"Where, my Son and God, is the ancient Annunciation which Gabriel spoke unto me, calling Thee King, Son, and God of the Most High? But now I behold Thee, my sweet Light, a naked, wounded corpse!"

*Troparion:*  
"O Deliverer from afflictions, now receive me with Thyself, my Son and God, that I also, O Master, may descend into Hades with Thee! Leave me not alone, for I can no longer bear to live without seeing Thee, my sweet Light!"

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*

*Troparion:*  
With the other Myrrh-bearing women, the Spotless One, weeping bitterly as she saw Christ being carried, said: "Woe is me, what do I behold? Whither goest Thou now, my Son, leaving me alone?"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*  
Fainting and weeping, the Spotless One said unto the Myrrh-bearing women: "Lament with me and weep bitterly with me, for lo, my sweet Light and your Teacher is delivered unto the Tomb!"

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
From the flame didst Thou pour down dew for the Holy Youths, and didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous man with water: for Thou dost accomplish all things, O Christ, whatsoever Thou willest; Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

*Troparion:*  
Seeing the Virgin weeping, Joseph was wholly torn with grief, and cried out bitterly: "How shall I, Thy servant, now bury Thee, O my God? In what winding-sheets [Plashchanytsias] shall I wrap Thy Body?"

*Troparion:*  
"Beyond understanding is the strange vision of Thee, the Lord Who sustains all creation! Therefore Joseph, holding Thee dead in his hands, with Nicodemus carries Thee and buries Thee."

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*

*Troparion:*  
"A strange and marvelous mystery do I behold!" cried the Virgin unto her Son and Lord: "How art Thou laid in a poor tomb, Who by Thy command dost raise the dead from the graves?"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*  
"Neither from Thy tomb will I depart, my Child, nor will I cease shedding tears, shedding tears will Thine handmaid not cease, until I also descend into Hades; for I cannot bear separation from Thee, my Son!"

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O All-Pure One, was the Incarnate Word manifested unto us; Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

*Troparion:*  
"Joy shall never touch me henceforth," said the Spotless One, weeping: "my Light and my joy has descended into the Tomb! But I will not leave Him alone; here will I die and be buried with Him!"

*Troparion:*  
"Heal now the wound of my soul, O my Child," the All-Pure One cried out weeping; "arise, and assuage my pain and sorrow! For Thou canst do whatsoever Thou willest, though Thou wast buried willingly!"

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*

*Troparion:*  
"Oh, how has the abyss of compassions been hidden from thee?" the Lord said secretly unto His Mother: "desiring to save My creature, I willed to die; but I will also arise, and will magnify thee, as God of heaven and earth!"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*  
"I will hymn Thy tender mercy, O Lover of mankind, and worship the richness of Thy compassion, O Master! Desiring to save Thine own creation, Thou hast endured death," said the All-Pure One; "but by Thy Resurrection, O Savior, have mercy upon us all!"

*Then, instead of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos: "It is not possible for men to see God...".*

*Trisagion. After Our Father: Kontakion: "He Who for our sake was crucified...". Lord, have mercy (40 times). Prayer: "At all times and at every hour...". Lord, have mercy (thrice). Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. More honorable than the Cherubim... In the Name of the Lord, bless, father. Priest: Through the prayers of our holy fathers... Then the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great: Spotless, undefiled, incorrupt, unpolluted, pure Virgin...[^394] And the Dismissal.*

*And the Midnight Office we sing in the cells.*

## Great and Holy Saturday: Matins[^395]

*AT THE SEVENTH HOUR OF THE NIGHT.*

*[Woodcut Illustration: The Resurrection and Harrowing of Hades. Christ rises victoriously over the tomb and broken gates of death, holding the banner of the Cross, while the Roman soldiers fall prostrate in terror.][^396]*

*The Paraecclesiarch strikes the heavy wooden board, and the great bell, and the iron semantron. And when we are gathered in the church, we sing MATINS. The Priest having begun: Blessed is our God; and we: Trisagion. After Our Father, the Priest: For Thine is the kingdom; and after the Amen: Lord, have mercy (12 times). And immediately: Glory to the Holy, Consubstantial, and Life-creating Trinity... And the Six Psalms are sung, beginning with: Glory to God in the highest... And after the Six Psalms, the Great Litany. At "God is the Lord":*

*Troparion, Tone 2:[^397]*  
The noble Joseph, taking down Thy spotless Body from the Tree, wrapped it in a clean Plashchanytsia and sweet spices, and laying it in a new tomb, closed it. *(twice)*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 2:*  
When Thou didst descend unto death, O Life immortal, then didst Thou slay Hades with the brilliance of Thy Godhead; and when Thou didst also raise the dead from the nethermost parts, all the Hosts of heaven cried aloud: "O Giver of life, Christ our God, glory to Thee!"

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen, Tone 2:*  
Standing by the tomb, the Angel cried aloud unto the Myrrh-bearing women: "Myrrh is fitting for the dead, but Christ has shown Himself a stranger to corruption!"

*The Paraecclesiarch distributes candles to the brethren. And we sing "Blessed are the undefiled" [Kathisma 17], in Tone 5, with Refrains, in Three Staseis.[^398] At the First Stasis, the Superior censes; and at the second and third, the Priests according to rank.[^399] Then first the Superior sings this Refrain in the midst of the church, having censed in the form of a cross:*

*Refrain:* We magnify Thee, O Christ the Giver of life, Who for our sake wast buried yesterday, Who didst endure [the Passion], and Who gloriously quickenest the dead.[^400]

*First Stasis*

*Verse 1 (Ps. 118:1):* Blessed are the undefiled in the way, who walk in the law of the Lord.  
*Troparion:* In a tomb wast Thou laid, O Christ, Who art the Life; and the angelic hosts were filled with awe, glorifying Thy condescension.[^401]

*Verse 2 (Ps. 118:2):* Blessed are they that search His testimonies; they will seek Him with their whole heart.  
*Troparion:* O Life, how diest Thou? How dwellest Thou also in a tomb, yet destroyest the kingdom of death, and raisest up the dead from Hades?

*Verse 3 (Ps. 118:3):* For they that work iniquity have not walked in His ways.  
*Troparion:* We magnify Thee, O Jesus the King, and we honor Thy burial and Thy sufferings, whereby Thou hast saved us from corruption.

*Verse 4 (Ps. 118:4):* Thou hast commanded Thy commandments to be kept diligently.  
*Troparion:* O Jesus, King of all, Who didst appoint the measures of the earth, Thou dwellest now in a narrow tomb, raising up the dead from the graves.

*Verse 5 (Ps. 118:5):* O that my ways were directed to keep Thine ordinances!  
*Troparion:* O my Jesus Christ, King of all creation, what didst Thou seek when Thou camest unto those in Hades? Was it to loose mortal mankind?

*Verse 6 (Ps. 118:6):* Then shall I not be ashamed, when I have respect unto all Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* The Master of all is seen dead, and is laid in a new tomb, Who emptied the graves of the dead.

*Verse 7 (Ps. 118:7):* I will give thanks unto Thee with uprightness of heart, when I have learned the judgments of Thy righteousness.  
*Troparion:* In a tomb wast Thou laid, O Christ, Who art the Life; and by Thy death Thou hast destroyed death, and poured forth life unto the world.

*Verse 8 (Ps. 118:8):* I will keep Thine ordinances; O forsake me not utterly.  
*Troparion:* With malefactors wast Thou numbered as a malefactor, O Christ, justifying us all from the malice of the ancient supplanter.

*Verse 9 (Ps. 118:9):* Wherewith shall a young man direct his way? By keeping Thy words.  
*Troparion:* He that is fairer in beauty than all the sons of men appears as a corpse without form, He that adorned the nature of all things.

*Verse 10 (Ps. 118:10):* With my whole heart have I sought Thee; cast me not away from Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* How shall Hades endure Thy coming, O Savior, and not rather suffer anguish, being darkened and blinded by the lightning-flashes of the rays of Thy light?

*Verse 11 (Ps. 118:11):* In my heart have I hidden Thy words, that I might not sin against Thee.  
*Troparion:* O my Sweet Jesus and Saving Light, how hast Thou hidden Thyself in a dark tomb? O Thine ineffable and unutterable endurance!

*Verse 12 (Ps. 118:12):* Blessed art Thou, O Lord; teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* Both the intelligent nature and the bodiless host are perplexed, O Christ, at the mystery of Thine ineffable and unsearchable burial.

*Verse 13 (Ps. 118:13):* With my lips have I declared all the judgments of Thy mouth.  
*Troparion:* O strange marvels! O new wonders! The Giver of my breath is carried breathless, buried by the hands of Joseph.

*Verse 14 (Ps. 118:14):* In the way of Thy testimonies have I found delight, as in all manner of riches.  
*Troparion:* Thou hast descended into the tomb, yet wast Thou in no wise separated from the Father's bosom, O Christ; strange and marvelous is this together.

*Verse 15 (Ps. 118:15):* I will meditate on Thy commandments, and consider Thy ways.

*Troparion:* O true King of heaven and earth, though Thou wast enclosed in a most narrow tomb, Thou wast recognized by all creation on every side, O Jesus.

*Verse 16 (Ps. 118:16):* I will meditate on Thine ordinances; I will not forget Thy words.  
*Troparion:* When Thou wast laid in the tomb, O Christ Creator, the nether regions were shaken, and the foundations and graves of the earth were opened wide.

*Verse 17 (Ps. 118:17):* Reward Thy servant: give me life, and I will keep Thy words.  
*Troparion:* He Who holds the earth in the hollow of His hand is now held in the earth, slain in the flesh, delivering the dead from the holding of Hades.[^402]

*Verse 18 (Ps. 118:18):* Unveil mine eyes, and I will consider the marvelous things of Thy law.  
*Troparion:* Thou hast ascended from corruption, O my Life, my Savior, when Thou didst die, and didst go unto the dead, and shatter the bars of Hades.

*Verse 19 (Ps. 118:19):* I am a sojourner on the earth; hide not Thy commandments from me.  
*Troparion:* Like a lantern of light, the flesh of God is now hidden under the earth as beneath a bushel, driving away the darkness that is in Hades.

*Verse 20 (Ps. 118:20):* My soul hath longed to desire Thy judgments at all times.  
*Troparion:* The multitude of intelligent hosts converges with Joseph and Nicodemus, to bury Thee, Whom nothing can contain, in a narrow tomb.

*Verse 21 (Ps. 118:21):* Thou hast rebuked the proud; cursed are they that decline from Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* Slain willingly, and laid beneath the earth, O my Jesus, Thou hast quickened me who was dead through bitter transgression.

*Verse 22 (Ps. 118:22):* Remove from me reproach and contempt; for I have sought Thy testimonies.  
*Troparion:* All creation was changed at Thy Passion, for all things suffered with Thee, O Word, Who holdest all the souls of all.

*Verse 23 (Ps. 118:23):* For princes sat and slandered me; but Thy servant meditated on Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* Hades the all-devourer, having received in its belly the Rock of Life, vomited forth the dead whom it had swallowed up from the ages.

*Verse 24 (Ps. 118:24):* For Thy testimonies are my meditation, and Thine ordinances are my counselors.  
*Troparion:* In a new tomb wast Thou laid, O Christ, and Thou hast renewed human nature, rising in divine majesty from the dead.

*Verse 25 (Ps. 118:25):* My soul hath cleaved unto the earth; quicken me according to Thy word.  
*Troparion:* Thou camest down unto the earth to save Adam, and not finding him on earth, O Master, Thou didst descend seeking him even unto Hades.

*Verse 26 (Ps. 118:26):* I have declared my ways, and Thou heardest me; teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* All the earth was shaken with fear, O Word, and the daystar hid its rays, before Thy greatest Light hidden in the earth.

*Verse 27 (Ps. 118:27):* Make me to understand the way of Thine ordinances, and I will meditate on Thy marvelous works.  
*Troparion:* Truly as a mortal Thou diest willingly, O Savior; yet as God Thou hast raised the dead from the graves and from the abyss of sins.

*Verse 28 (Ps. 118:28):* My soul hath slumbered for heaviness; strengthen me in Thy words.  
*Troparion:* Shedding teardrops of weeping upon Thee, O Savior, Thy Mother cried aloud: "How shall I bury Thee, O Son?"

*Verse 29 (Ps. 118:29):* Remove from me the way of unrighteousness, and by Thy law have mercy upon me.  
*Troparion:* As a grain of wheat, having entered into the bosom of the earth, Thou hast yielded a most fruitful ear, raising up the earthly ones descended from Adam.[^403]

*Verse 30 (Ps. 118:30):* I have chosen the way of truth, and Thy judgments have I not forgotten.  
*Troparion:* Beneath the earth hast Thou hidden Thyself now like the sun, and wast covered by the night of death; but shine forth more radiantly, O Savior!

*Verse 31 (Ps. 118:31):* I have cleaved unto Thy testimonies, O Lord; put me not to shame.  
*Troparion:* Even as the moon eclipses the sun's orb, O Savior, so now has the tomb hidden Thee, Who didst end Thy life in bodily death.

*Verse 32 (Ps. 118:32):* I have run the way of Thy commandments, when Thou didst enlarge my heart.  
*Troparion:* Christ, having tasted of death, freed mortal men from death, and now grants life unto all.

*Verse 33 (Ps. 118:33):* Legislate for me, O Lord, the way of Thine ordinances, and I will seek it always.  
*Troparion:* Adam, slain of old through envy, Thou liftest up unto life by Thy slaying, O Savior, the new Adam revealed in the flesh.

*Verse 34 (Ps. 118:34):* Give me understanding, and I will search Thy law, and will keep it with my whole heart.  
*Troparion:* The intelligent hosts, beholding Thee nailed dead upon the Tree, were filled with awe, covering themselves with their wings, O Savior.

*Verse 35 (Ps. 118:35):* Lead me in the path of Thy commandments, for therein is my desire.  
*Troparion:* Taking Thee down dead from the Tree, O Word, Joseph now lays Thee in a tomb; but arise, saving all as God!

*Verse 36 (Ps. 118:36):* Incline my heart unto Thy testimonies, and not to covetousness.  
*Troparion:* Having been the joy of Angels, O Savior, Thou hast now become the cause of grief unto them, being seen in the flesh as a breathless corpse.

*Verse 37 (Ps. 118:37):* Turn away mine eyes from beholding vanity; quicken Thou me in Thy way.  
*Troparion:* Lifted up upon the Tree, Thou hast co-elevated living mortals; and lying upon the earth, Thou co-raisest those beneath it.

*Verse 38 (Ps. 118:38):* Establish Thy word unto Thy servant in Thy fear.  
*Troparion:* As a lion asleep in the flesh, O Savior, Thou risest as a young lion's whelp, having put off the oldness of the flesh.[^404]

*Verse 39 (Ps. 118:39):* Take away my reproach which I fear, for Thy judgments are good.  
*Troparion:* Pierced in Thy side wast Thou, Who didst take Adam's rib, from which Thou didst fashion Eve, and Thou hast poured forth streams of purification.[^405]

*Verse 40 (Ps. 118:40):* Behold, I have longed after Thy commandments; quicken me in Thy righteousness.  
*Troparion:* In figure of old the Lamb was sacrificed mystically; but Thou, slain manifestly, O Innocent One, hast cleansed all creation, O Savior.

*Verse 41 (Ps. 118:41):* And let Thy mercy come upon me, O Lord, even Thy salvation, according to Thy word.  
*Troparion:* Who can declare this truly new and fearful sight? For the Master of creation today receives the Passion, and dies for our sake.

*Verse 42 (Ps. 118:42):* So shall I answer him that reproaches me, for I have trusted in Thy words.  
*Troparion:* "O Treasury of life, how art Thou seen dead?" cried the Angels in awe: "How is God enclosed in a tomb?"

*Verse 43 (Ps. 118:43):* And take not the word of truth utterly out of my mouth, for I have hoped in Thy judgments.  
*Troparion:* From Thy side pierced with a lance, O Savior, Thou droppest life with life from the Life that saved me, and Thou wilt quicken me with Him.

*Verse 44 (Ps. 118:44):* So shall I keep Thy law continually, unto ages and unto ages of ages.  
*Troparion:* Stretched out upon the Tree, Thou hast gathered mankind; and pierced in the side, Thou pourest forth unto all life-giving remission, O Jesus.

*Verse 45 (Ps. 118:45):* And I walked at large, for I sought Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* The noble Joseph wraps Thee reverently, and buries Thee as dead in a noble manner, and is awestruck at Thy fearful form, O Savior.

*Verse 46 (Ps. 118:46):* I spoke of Thy testimonies also before kings, and was not ashamed.  
*Troparion:* Descending willingly beneath the earth as dead, Thou liftest up from earth unto the heavenly heights those who had fallen thence, O Jesus.

*Verse 47 (Ps. 118:47):* And I meditated on Thy commandments, which I loved exceedingly.  
*Troparion:* Though Thou wast seen as dead, yet living as God, Thou liftest up from earth unto the heavenly heights those who had fallen thence, O Jesus.

*Verse 48 (Ps. 118:48):* And I lifted up my hands unto Thy commandments which I loved, and meditated on Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* Though Thou wast seen as dead, yet living as God, Thou hast quickened dead men, having slain our slayer.

*Troparion:* O what joy! O what manifold sweetness! Wherewith Thou didst fill those in Hades, making light shine forth in the dark realms.

*Verse 49 (Ps. 118:49):* Remember Thy words unto Thy servant, upon which Thou hast caused me to hope.  
*Troparion:* I worship Thy Passion, I hymn Thy Burial, I magnify Thy dominion, O Lover of mankind, whereby I have been freed from corrupting passions.

*Verse 50 (Ps. 118:50):* This hath comforted me in my affliction, for Thy word hath quickened me.  
*Troparion:* Against Thee was the sword unsheathed, O Christ, and the sword of the strong man is blunted, and the sword of Eden is turned aside.[^406]

*Verse 51 (Ps. 118:51):* The proud have transgressed exceedingly; yet have I not declined from Thy law.  
*Troparion:* The Ewe-lamb, beholding her Lamb pierced with the point in the slaughter, wailed, moving the flock also to cry aloud.

*Verse 52 (Ps. 118:52):* I remembered Thy judgments of old, O Lord, and was comforted.  
*Troparion:* Though Thou art hidden in a tomb, or in the caves of Hades, yet Thou hast emptied the tombs, and stripped Hades bare, O Christ!

*Verse 53 (Ps. 118:53):* Grief hath taken hold upon me from the sinners that forsake Thy law.  
*Troparion:* Willingly descending unto the earth, O Savior, Thou hast quickened dead men, and raised them unto the Father's glory.

*Verse 54 (Ps. 118:54):* Thy statutes have been my songs in the place of my pilgrimage.  
*Troparion:* One of the Trinity endured reproachful death in the flesh upon the Tree; the sun is afraid and the earth trembles.

*Verse 55 (Ps. 118:55):* I have remembered Thy Name in the night, O Lord, and have kept Thy law.  
*Troparion:* As from a bitter root, the shoots of the tribe of Judah laid in a tomb Jesus, the Nourisher and Giver of manna.

*Verse 56 (Ps. 118:56):* This I had, because I kept Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* The Judge stands before Pilate as one condemned, and condemned an unjust death by the Wood of the Cross.

*Verse 57 (Ps. 118:57):* Thou art my portion, O Lord; I have said that I would keep Thy law.  
*Troparion:* O impure Israel, O murderous people, what had happened that ye loosed Barabbas and delivered the Savior unto the Cross?

*Verse 58 (Ps. 118:58):* I entreated Thy favor with my whole heart; be merciful unto me according to Thy word.  
*Troparion:* Fashioning Adam from the earth with Thine own hand, for his sake Thou becamest man by nature, and wast crucified of Thine own will.

*Verse 59 (Ps. 118:59):* I considered my ways, and turned my feet unto Thy testimonies.  
*Troparion:* In obedience, O Word, unto Thine own Father, Thou didst descend even unto cruel Hades, and hast raised up the human race.

*Verse 60 (Ps. 118:60):* I made haste, and delayed not to keep Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* "Woe is me, Light of the world! Woe is me, O my Light! O my beloved Jesus!" cried the Virgin, sighing with weeping.

*Verse 61 (Ps. 118:61):* The cords of sinners have entangled me, but I have not forgotten Thy law.  
*Troparion:* O envious, murderous, and destructive people, why are ye not ashamed of the Plashchanytsia and the napkin itself, now that Christ is risen?[^407]

*Verse 62 (Ps. 118:62):* At midnight I arose to give thanks unto Thee, because of the judgments of Thy righteousness.  
*Troparion:* Come now, vile murderous disciple, and show me the manner of thy malice, whereby thou becamest the betrayer of Christ!

*Verse 63 (Ps. 118:63):* I am a companion of all them that fear Thee, and of them that keep Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* Pretending to be a friend of mankind, thou blind, all-destructive, faithless man, didst sell Him for a price!

*Verse 64 (Ps. 118:64):* The earth, O Lord, is full of Thy mercy; teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* What value of the myrrh didst thou grasp, the price of that precious ointment? What worthy return didst thou receive? Insatiability didst thou acquire!

*Verse 65 (Ps. 118:65):* Thou hast dealt bountifully with Thy servant, O Lord, according to Thy word.  
*Troparion:* If thou art a lover of the poor, and grievest over the myrrh *Troparion:* poured forth for the purification of the soul, how sellest thou the Giver of light for gold?

*Verse 66 (Ps. 118:66):* Teach me good judgment and knowledge, for I have believed Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* O Word of God! O my Joy! How shall I endure Thy three-day burial? For now my maternal heart is moved within me!

*Verse 67 (Ps. 118:67):* Before I was afflicted I went astray, therefore have I kept Thy word.  
*Troparion:* "Who will give me water and fountains of tears?" cried the Virgin Bride of God, "that I may weep for my sweet Jesus?"

*Verse 68 (Ps. 118:68):* Thou art good, O Lord, and in Thy goodness teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* O mountains and hills, and multitudes of men, weep, and all lament with me, the Mother of your God!

*Verse 69 (Ps. 118:69):* The proud have forged a lie against me, but I will keep Thy commandments with my whole heart. (Ps. 118:70: Their heart is curdled like milk, but I have meditated on Thy law.)  
*Troparion:* "When shall I behold Thee, O Savior, the Timeless Light, the Joy and Sweetness of my heart?" the Virgin Mother cried aloud.

*Verse 70:*  
*Troparion:* Though as a Cornerstone, O Savior, Thou didst receive hewing, yet Thou hast poured forth living streams, being the Fountain of life.

*Verse 71 (Ps. 118:71):* It is good for me that Thou hast afflicted me, that I might learn Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* As from a single fountain in a double stream pouring forth from Thy side, being watered thereby we obtain immortal life.

*Verse 72 (Ps. 118:72):* The law of Thy mouth is better unto me than thousands of gold and silver.  
*Troparion:* Willingly, O Word, wast Thou seen dead in the tomb, but Thou livest, and even as Thou didst foretell, Thou raisest men up by Thy Resurrection, O my Savior!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:* We hymn Thee, O Word, God of all, with the Father and Thy Holy Spirit, and we glorify Thy divine Burial.

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:* We call thee blessed, O Pure Theotokos, and faithfully we honor the three-day burial of thy Son and our God.[^408]

*And again the First Refrain, both choirs together.*  
*Little Litany, and the Exclamation of the First Stasis:*  
**Priest:** For blessed is Thy Name, and glorified is Thy kingdom, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*And having censed, he begins the Second Stasis.*

*Second Stasis*[^409]

*Which the Left Choir begins, with Troparia according to the model of the first:*

*Tone 5:*  
Right worthy it is to magnify Thee, O Christ the Giver of life, Who hast shattered the gates of Hades, and destroyed the tyranny of the devil.

*And again the Choir of the right side sings the second Troparion without a verse:*

*Troparion:* Right worthy it is to magnify Thee, the Giver of life, Who didst stretch out Thy hands upon the Cross, and didst shatter the enemy's dominion.

*Verse 73 (Ps. 118:73):* Thy hands have made me and fashioned me; give me understanding, and I will search Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* Right worthy it is to magnify the Fashioner of all, for by Thy sufferings we have received immortality, having been delivered from corruption.

*Verse 74 (Ps. 118:74):* They that fear Thee will be glad when they see me, because I have hoped in Thy words.  
*Troparion:* The earth was seized with trembling, and the sun hid itself, O Savior, when Thou, the Never-setting Light, O Christ, didst descend bodily into the tomb.

*Verse 75 (Ps. 118:75):* I know, O Lord, that Thy judgments are right, and that Thou in faithfulness hast afflicted me.  
*Troparion:* Thou hast fallen asleep, O Christ, with a naturally life-giving sleep in the tomb, and from the heavy sleep of sin Thou hast awakened the human race.

*Verse 76 (Ps. 118:76):* Let, I pray Thee, Thy merciful kindness be for my comfort, according to Thy word unto Thy servant.

*Troparion:* "Alone among women, without birth-pangs I bare Thee, O Child; yet now at Thy Passion I bear unfamiliar labors," cried the Pure One.[^410]

*Verse 77 (Ps. 118:77):* Let Thy tender mercies come unto me, that I may live; for Thy law is my delight.  
*Troparion:* High above, O Savior, Thou art indivisibly with the Father, yet below stretched out dead upon the earth, the Seraphim are awestruck beholding Thee.

*Verse 78 (Ps. 118:78):* Let the proud be ashamed, for they dealt perversely with me without a cause; but I will meditate on Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* The veil of the Temple is rent by Thy Crucifixion, the luminaries hide their light, O Word, when Thou, the Sun, hast hidden Thyself beneath the earth.

*Verse 79 (Ps. 118:79):* Let those that fear Thee turn unto me, and those that have known Thy testimonies.  
*Troparion:* He Who from the beginning established the circle of the earth by a single nod, lifeless as an earthly man descended beneath the earth: heaven, be awestruck at the sight!

*Verse 80 (Ps. 118:80):* Let my heart be sound in Thine ordinances, that I be not ashamed.  
*Troparion:* Descended beneath the earth hast Thou, Who didst fashion man with Thine own hand, that Thou mightest raise up from their fall the gatherings of mankind by Thine all-powerful dominion.

*Verse 81 (Ps. 118:81):* My soul faints for Thy salvation, but I hope in Thy word.  
*Troparion:* Come, let us sing a sacred lament unto the dead Christ, even as the Myrrh-bearing women of old, that we also may hear with them: "Rejoice!"

*Verse 82 (Ps. 118:82):* Mine eyes fail for Thy word, saying: When wilt Thou comfort me?  
*Troparion:* Truly Thou art inexhaustible Myrrh, O Word; therefore the Myrrh-bearing women brought sweet myrrh unto Thee, the Living One, as unto one dead.

*Verse 83 (Ps. 118:83):* For I am become like a bottle in the smoke; yet do I not forget Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* By Thy burial, O Christ, Thou shatterest the kingdoms of Hades; by death Thou slayest death, and deliverest earthly mortals from corruption.

*Verse 84 (Ps. 118:84):* How many are the days of Thy servant? When wilt Thou execute judgment on them that persecute me?  
*Troparion:* The Wisdom of God, pouring forth streams of life, descending into the tomb gives life unto those in the inaccessible places of Hades.

*Verse 85 (Ps. 118:85):* The transgressors have told me tales, which are not after Thy law, O Lord.  
*Troparion:* "That I might renew shattered human nature, I was wounded unto death willingly in the flesh; O My Mother, burden not thyself with weeping!"

*Verse 86 (Ps. 118:86):* All Thy commandments are faithful; they persecute me wrongfully; help Thou me.  
*Troparion:* Descended beneath the earth hast Thou, O Daystar of righteousness, and raised up the dead as from sleep, driving away all the darkness in Hades.

*Verse 87 (Ps. 118:87):* They had almost consumed me upon earth, but I forsook not Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* The two-sprouted grain, possessing natural life, is sown in the bosom of the earth with tears today; but having sprouted, it will gladden the world.[^411]

*Verse 88 (Ps. 118:88):* Quicken me after Thy loving-kindness, so shall I keep the testimony of Thy mouth.  
*Troparion:* Adam was afraid when God walked in Paradise, but he rejoices when He descends into Hades, having fallen aforetime, yet now being raised up.

*Verse 89 (Ps. 118:89):* Unto the ages, O Lord, Thy word abides in heaven. (Ps. 118:90: Thy truth is unto generation and generation.)  
*Troparion:* Offering Thee a sacrifice of tears, she who bare Thee, O Christ, as Thou art laid bodily in the tomb, cries aloud: "Arise, O Child, as Thou didst foretell!"

*Verse 90 (Ps. 118:90–91):* Thou hast established the earth, and it abides; by Thine appointment.  
*Troparion:* Reverently hiding Thee in a new tomb, O Joseph, he sings funeral hymns worthy of God, mingled with weeping unto Thee, O Savior.

*Verse 91 (Ps. 118:91):* The day continues; for all things are Thy servants.  
*Troparion:* Beholding Thee nailed to the Cross with nails, O Word, Thy Mother was pierced through soul and heart with the nails and arrows of bitter sorrow.

*Verse 92 (Ps. 118:92):* Unless Thy law had been my delight, I should then have perished in mine affliction.  
*Troparion:* Beholding Thee, the Delight of all, given bitter drink, Thy Mother bitterly bedews her sight with tears.

*Verse 93 (Ps. 118:93):* I will never forget Thine ordinances, for with them Thou hast quickened me.  
*Troparion:* Torn with cruel agony and rent in her maternal heart, O Word, beholding Thine unjust slaughter, the All-Pure One cried out weeping.

*Verse 94 (Ps. 118:94):* I am Thine, save me; for I have sought Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* "How shall I close Thy sweet eyes and Thy lips, O Word? How also shall I bury Thee as dead?" cried Joseph in awe.

*Verse 95 (Ps. 118:95):* The wicked have waited for me to destroy me, but I have considered Thy testimonies.  
*Troparion:* Joseph and Nicodemus sing funeral hymns unto Christ dead now, and with them sing also the Seraphim.

*Verse 96 (Ps. 118:96):* I have seen an end of all perfection, but Thy commandment is exceedingly broad.  
*Troparion:* Thou descendest beneath the earth, O Savior, Sun of righteousness; therefore the Moon who bare Thee wanes with sorrows, bereft of Thy countenance.[^412]

*Verse 97 (Ps. 118:97):* O how love I Thy law, O Lord! It is my meditation all the day.  
*Troparion:* Hades was awestruck, O Savior, beholding Thee, the Giver of life, emptying its wealth and raising up the dead from the ages.

*Verse 98 (Ps. 118:98):* Thou through Thy commandments hast made me wiser than mine enemies, for they are ever with me.  
*Troparion:* The sun shines forth its light after the night, O Word; and Thou, having arisen, dost shine forth after death brightly as from a bridal chamber.

*Verse 99 (Ps. 118:99):* I have more understanding than all my teachers, for Thy testimonies are my meditation.  
*Troparion:* The earth, having received the Creator in its bosom, seized with trembling is shaken, waking the dead by its quaking.

*Verse 100 (Ps. 118:100):* I understand more than the ancients, because I keep Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* Nicodemus and the noble Joseph now anointing Thee with myrrh in a new and fitting manner, cried aloud: "Be awestruck, all the earth!"

*Verse 101 (Ps. 118:101):* I have refrained my feet from every evil way, that I might keep Thy word.  
*Troparion:* Thou didst set, O Light-Maker, and with Thee set the sun's light; and creation is gripped by trembling, proclaiming Thee the Creator of all.

*Verse 102 (Ps. 118:102):* I have not departed from Thy judgments, for Thou hast taught me.  
*Troparion:* A hewn stone covers the Cornerstone; and mortal man covers God now as mortal in a tomb: be awestruck, O earth![^413]

*Verse 103 (Ps. 118:103):* How sweet are Thy words unto my taste! Yea, sweeter than honey to my mouth!  
*Troparion:* "Behold the disciple whom Thou lovedst, and Thy Mother, O Child, and grant a most sweet word!" cried the Pure One weeping.

*Verse 104 (Ps. 118:104):* Through Thy commandments I get understanding, therefore I hate every false way.  
*Troparion:* Being the Giver of life, O Word, buried upon the Cross Thou didst not put the Jews to death, but didst raise up even their dead.

*Verse 105 (Ps. 118:105):* Thy law is a lamp unto my feet, and a light unto my path.  
*Troparion:* Thou hadst neither form nor comeliness of Thy former beauty, O Word, when Thou didst suffer; but rising Thou hast shone forth exceedingly, adorning mankind with divine rays.

*Verse 106 (Ps. 118:106):* I have sworn, and will perform it, that I will keep the judgments of Thy righteousness.  
*Troparion:* Thou hast set in the flesh into the earth, O Never-setting Daystar, and not enduring *Troparion:* and not enduring to behold it, the sun was darkened, while it was yet midday.

*Verse 107 (Ps. 118:107):* I am afflicted very much; quicken me, O Lord, according to Thy word.  
*Troparion:* The sun and moon together darkened, O Savior, resembled wise servants clothed in black apparel.

*Verse 108 (Ps. 118:108):* Accept, I beseech Thee, the freewill offerings of my mouth, O Lord, and teach me Thy judgments.  
*Troparion:* The centurion beheld Thee as God, even though Thou wast slain; "How then, O my God, shall I touch Thee with my hands?" cried Joseph in awe.

*Verse 109 (Ps. 118:109):* My soul is continually in Thy hands, yet do I not forget Thy law.  
*Troparion:* Adam slept, yet death was brought forth from his rib; but Thou now hast fallen asleep, O Word of God, and pourest forth life unto the world from Thy side.

*Verse 110 (Ps. 118:110):* The wicked have laid a snare for me, yet I erred not from Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* Thou hast slept a little, and quickened the dead, and rising, Thou hast raised all who have slept from the ages, O Good One.

*Verse 111 (Ps. 118:111):* Thy testimonies have I taken as an heritage unto ages, for they are the rejoicing of my heart.  
*Troparion:* Taken from the earth, Thou didst pour forth the wine of salvation, O life-bearing Vine, glorifying Thy Passion and the Cross!

*Verse 112 (Ps. 118:112):* I have inclined mine heart to perform Thine ordinances always, even unto the end.  
*Troparion:* How did the intelligent hierarchs of heaven behold Thee, O Savior, naked, blood-stained, and condemned, enduring the insolence of the crucifiers?

*Verse 113 (Ps. 118:113):* I hate vain thoughts, but Thy law do I love.  
*Troparion:* O circumcised, most malicious Hebrew race, thou knewest of the rebuilding of the temple, why then hast thou condemned Christ?

*Verse 114 (Ps. 118:114):* Thou art my hiding place and my shield; I hope in Thy word.  
*Troparion:* In the garment of mockery dost thou clothe the Adorner of all, Who established the heavens and adorned the earth marvelously.

*Verse 115 (Ps. 118:115):* Depart from me, ye evildoers, for I will keep the commandments of my God.  
*Troparion:* As a pelican wounded in Thy side, O Word, Thou hast quickened Thine own dead children, dropping life-giving streams into them.[^414]

*Verse 116 (Ps. 118:116):* Uphold me according unto Thy word, that I may live; and let me not be ashamed of my hope.  
*Troparion:* Joshua stayed the sun of old as he smote the foreigners; but Thou hast hidden Thyself, casting down the ruler of darkness.

*Verse 117 (Ps. 118:117):* Hold Thou me up, and I shall be safe, and I will have respect unto Thine ordinances continually.  
*Troparion:* He came and was crucified, Who hung the earth upon the waters, and breathless lies in it, which the earth cannot endure, but quakes bitterly!

*Verse 118 (Ps. 118:118):* Thou hast trodden down all them that err from Thine ordinances, for their deceit is falsehood.  
*Troparion:* Remaining unparted from the Father's bosom, O Compassionate One, Thou wast pleased to become man, and didst descend into Hades, O Christ.

*Verse 119 (Ps. 118:119):* Thou puttest away all the wicked of the earth like dross, therefore I love Thy testimonies.  
*Troparion:* "Woe is me, O Son!" wailed she that knew not wedlock, saying: "Whom I hoped for as King, Him I now behold condemned upon the Cross!"

*Verse 120 (Ps. 118:120):* Make my flesh tremble for fear of Thee, for I am afraid of Thy judgments.  
*Troparion:* "These things Gabriel announced unto me when he flew down, who said that the Kingdom of my Son Jesus is eternal."

*Verse 121 (Ps. 118:121):* I have done judgment and justice; leave me not to mine oppressors.  
*Troparion:* "Alas, Simeon's prophecy is fulfilled, for Thy sword has pierced my heart, O Emmanuel!"[^415]

*Verse 122 (Ps. 118:122):* Be surety for Thy servant for good; let not the proud oppress me.  
*Troparion:* Be ashamed at least of those raised from the dead, O Jews, whom *Troparion:* whom the Giver of life raised up, Whom ye slew through envy.

*Verse 123 (Ps. 118:123):* Mine eyes fail for Thy salvation, and for the word of Thy righteousness.  
*Troparion:* Awe seized those who beheld Thee, O Invisible Light, my Christ, hidden in a tomb breathless, and the sun darkened its light.

*Verse 124 (Ps. 118:124):* Deal with Thy servant according unto Thy mercy, and teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* Thy most spotless Mother wept bitterly, O Word, when in a tomb she beheld Thee, God without beginning and incorruptible.
