*Verse 125 (Ps. 118:125):* I am Thy servant; give me understanding, that I may know Thy testimonies.  
*Troparion:* Beholding Thy deadness, Thy most spotless Mother cried bitterly unto Thee: "Delay not, O Life, among the dead!"

*Verse 126 (Ps. 118:126):* It is time for the Lord to work, for they have made void Thy law.  
*Troparion:* Cruel Hades trembled when it beheld Thee, O immortal Sun of glory, and yielded up its prisoners in haste.

*Verse 127 (Ps. 118:127):* Therefore I love Thy commandments above gold, yea, above topaz.  
*Troparion:* A great and awesome sight is now beheld: the Author of the life of all undergoes death, desiring to quicken all.

*Verse 128 (Ps. 118:128):* Therefore I esteem all Thy commandments concerning all things to be right, and I hate every false way.  
*Troparion:* Pierced in Thy side and nailed by Thy hands, O Master, healing the wound of the side and the intemperance of the hands of our forebears.

*Verse 129 (Ps. 118:129):* Thy testimonies are marvelous, therefore doth my soul keep them.  
*Troparion:* Of old everyone in the house wept for Rachel's son, and for the Son the choir of disciples with the Mother wailed.

*Verse 130 (Ps. 118:130):* The entrance of Thy words gives light; it gives understanding unto the simple.  
*Troparion:* With their hands they gave blows to Christ upon the cheek, Who with His hand fashioned man and crushed the jaws of the beast.

*Verse 131 (Ps. 118:131):* I opened my mouth, and panted; for I longed for Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* With hymns, O Christ, all we faithful now celebrate Thy Crucifixion and Burial, delivered from death by Thy Burial.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:* O God without beginning, Co-eternal Word, and Holy Spirit, strengthen the sceptre of Orthodox kings against their enemies, as Thou art good.[^416]

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:* O all-pure Virgin who didst bear the Life, appease churchly scandals and grant peace, as thou art good.

*And again the first Troparion, both choirs together.*  
*Then the Little Litany. Exclamation:*  
**Priest:** For holy art Thou, Who restest upon the Cherubic Throne of glory, and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine Unoriginate Father, and with Thine All-Holy, Good, and Life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Third Stasis*[^417]

*And the First Choir begins again, in Tone 3:*  
Every generation, O compassionate Lord, offers a hymn unto Thy burial.

*Then the Choir that began sings the verse:*  
*Verse 132 (Ps. 118:132):* Look Thou upon me, and be merciful unto me, as Thou usest to do unto those that love Thy Name.  
*Troparion:* Taking Thee down from the Tree, he of Arimathea wrapped Thee in a Plashchanytsia and laid Thee in a tomb.

*Then the second choir:*  
*Verse 133 (Ps. 118:133):* Order my steps in Thy word, and let not any iniquity have dominion over me.  
*Troparion:* The Myrrh-bearing women came, O my Christ, bringing myrrh with wise devotion.

*Verse 134 (Ps. 118:134):* Deliver me from the slander of men; so will I keep Thy commandments.

*Troparion:* Come, all creation, let us offer funeral hymns unto the Creator.

*Verse 135 (Ps. 118:135):* Make Thy face to shine upon Thy servant, and teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* As dead, let us with the Myrrh-bearing women wisely anoint the Living One with myrrh.

*Verse 136 (Ps. 118:136):* Rivers of waters run down mine eyes, because they keep not Thy law.  
*Troparion:* O thrice-blessed Joseph, bury the Body of Christ the Giver of life.

*Verse 137 (Ps. 118:137):* Righteous art Thou, O Lord, and upright are Thy judgments.  
*Troparion:* Those whom He nourished with manna lifted up their heel against their Benefactor.

*Verse 138 (Ps. 118:138):* Thy testimonies that Thou hast commanded are righteous and very faithful.  
*Troparion:* Those whom He nourished with manna bring vinegar together with gall unto the Savior.[^418]

*Verse 139 (Ps. 118:139):* My zeal hath consumed me, because mine enemies have forgotten Thy words.  
*Troparion:* O madness and Christ-slaying murder of the prophets!

*Verse 140 (Ps. 118:140):* Thy word is very pure, therefore Thy servant loves it.  
*Troparion:* Like a mad servant, the disciple betrayed the Abyss of Wisdom.

*Verse 141 (Ps. 118:141):* I am small and despised, yet do I not forget Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* Forsaking the Redeemer, cruel Judas remains a captive.

*Verse 142 (Ps. 118:142):* Thy righteousness is an everlasting righteousness, and Thy law is the truth.  
*Troparion:* According to Solomon, the mouth of the transgressing Hebrews is a deep pit.

*Verse 143 (Ps. 118:143):* Trouble and anguish have taken hold on me, yet Thy commandments are my delights.  
*Troparion:* In the crooked paths of the transgressing Hebrews are thorns and caltrops.

*Verse 144 (Ps. 118:144):* The righteousness of Thy testimonies is everlasting; give me understanding, and I shall live.  
*Troparion:* Joseph with Nicodemus buries the Creator as a corpse.

*Verse 145 (Ps. 118:145):* I cried with my whole heart; hear me, O Lord; I will keep Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* O Savior, Giver of life, glory to Thy dominion which destroyed Hades!

*Verse 146 (Ps. 118:146):* I cried unto Thee; save me, and I shall keep Thy testimonies.  
*Troparion:* Beholding Thee lying dead, O Word, the All-Pure One wept in motherly fashion.

*Verse 147 (Ps. 118:147):* I prevented the dawning of the morning, and cried; I hoped in Thy word.  
*Troparion:* "O my sweetest breath, my sweetest Child, whither has Thy beauty set?"

*Verse 148 (Ps. 118:148):* Mine eyes prevent the night watches, that I might meditate in Thy word.  
*Troparion:* Thy Virgin Mother was moved with weeping, O Word, when Thou wast dead.[^419]

*Verse 149 (Ps. 118:149):* Hear my voice according unto Thy loving-kindness, O Lord; quicken me according to Thy judgment.  
*Troparion:* The women came with myrrh to anoint Christ, the Divine Myrrh.

*Verse 150 (Ps. 118:150):* They draw nigh that follow after mischief; they are far from Thy law.  
*Troparion:* By death Thou hast slain death, O my God, by Thy divine power.

*Verse 151 (Ps. 118:151):* Thou art near, O Lord, and all Thy commandments are truth.  
*Troparion:* The deceiver is deceived, and the deceived is delivered by Thy wisdom, O my God.

*Verse 152 (Ps. 118:152):* Concerning Thy testimonies, I have known of old that Thou hast founded them unto ages.  
*Troparion:* The traitor was brought down to the bottom of Hades, into the pit of corruption.

*Verse 153 (Ps. 118:153):* Consider mine affliction, and deliver me, for I do not forget Thy law.  
*Troparion:* Thorns and caltrops are the ways of the thrice-wretched fool Judas.

*Verse 154 (Ps. 118:154):* Plead my cause, and deliver me; quicken me according to Thy word.  
*Troparion:* All Thy crucifiers shall perish together, O Word, Son of God, Almighty Ruler.

*Verse 155 (Ps. 118:155):* Salvation is far from the wicked, for they seek not Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* Into the pit of corruption all the men of blood have perished together.

*Verse 156 (Ps. 118:156):* Great are Thy tender mercies, O Lord; quicken me according to Thy judgments.  
*Troparion:* O Son of God, King of all, my God, my Creator, how hast Thou endured suffering?

*Verse 157 (Ps. 118:157):* Many are they that persecute me and afflict me, yet have I not declined from Thy testimonies.  
*Troparion:* Beholding the Calf suspended upon the Tree, the young Heifer wailed aloud.[^420]

*Verse 158 (Ps. 118:158):* I beheld the transgressors and was grieved, because they kept not Thy words.  
*Troparion:* Joseph with Nicodemus prepares the life-bearing Body for burial.

*Verse 159 (Ps. 118:159):* Consider how I love Thy commandments, O Lord; quicken me according to Thy mercy.  
*Troparion:* The Virgin cried aloud with burning tears, pierced to her very womb.

*Verse 160 (Ps. 118:160):* The beginning of Thy words is truth, and every one of the judgments of Thy righteousness endures unto ages.  
*Troparion:* "O Light of mine eyes, my sweetest Child, how art Thou now covered in a tomb?"

*Verse 161 (Ps. 118:161):* Princes have persecuted me without a cause, but my heart stands in awe of Thy words.  
*Troparion:* "Desiring to free Adam and Eve, Thou sufferest these things, O all-good Mother!"

*Verse 162 (Ps. 118:162):* I rejoice at Thy words, as one that finds great spoil.  
*Troparion:* "I glorify Thy supreme tender compassion, O my Son, for the sake of which Thou sufferest these things!"

*Verse 163 (Ps. 118:163):* I hate and abhor iniquity, but Thy law do I love.  
*Troparion:* Thou wast given vinegar and gall to drink, O Compassionate One, loosing the ancient tasting of the forbidden fruit.

*Verse 164 (Ps. 118:164):* Seven times a day do I praise Thee because of the judgments of Thy righteousness.  
*Troparion:* Nailed upon the Cross was He Who of old covered His people with a pillar of cloud.

*Verse 165 (Ps. 118:165):* Great peace have they that love Thy law, and nothing shall cause them to stumble.  
*Troparion:* The Myrrh-bearing women coming to the tomb, O Savior, brought sweet myrrh unto Thee.

*Verse 166 (Ps. 118:166):* Lord, I have looked for Thy salvation, and loved Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* Arise, O Compassionate One, raising us from the foundations of Hades!

*Verse 167 (Ps. 118:167):* My soul has kept Thy testimonies, and loved them exceedingly.  
*Troparion:* "Arise, O Giver of life!" cried she who bare Thee, pouring forth hot tears.

*Verse 168 (Ps. 118:168):* I have kept Thy commandments and Thy testimonies, for all my ways are before Thee, O Lord.  
*Troparion:* "Hasten to arise, O Word, dispelling the sorrow of her who bare Thee purely!"

*Verse 169 (Ps. 118:169):* Let my cry come near before Thee, O Lord; give me understanding according to Thy word.  
*Troparion:* The heavenly powers were astonished, beholding Thee dead with fearful dread.

*Verse 170 (Ps. 118:170):* Let my supplication come before Thee, O Lord; deliver me according to Thy word.  
*Troparion:* Unto them that honor Thy Passion with love and fear, grant forgiveness of transgressions.

*Verse 171 (Ps. 118:171):* My lips shall pour forth praise, when Thou hast taught me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* O dreadful and strange sight, O Divine Word! How does the earth cover Thee?

*Verse 172 (Ps. 118:172):* My tongue shall speak of Thy words, for all Thy commandments are righteousness.  
*Troparion:* Bearing Thee of old, O Savior, Joseph fled; and now another Joseph buries Thee!

*Verse 173 (Ps. 118:173):* Let Thine hand help me, for I have chosen Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* The All-Pure One weeps and laments for Thee, O Savior and my God, Thy Mother.

*Verse 174 (Ps. 118:174):* I have longed for Thy salvation, O Lord, and Thy law is my meditation.  
*Troparion:* The minds of all are struck with dread at the strange and fearful sight of Thee, the Creator of all!

*Verse 175 (Ps. 118:175):* Let my soul live, and it shall praise Thee; and let Thy judgments help me.  
*Troparion:* The Myrrh-bearing women poured out sweet myrrh upon the Tomb, having arrived exceedingly early.

*Verse 176 (Ps. 118:176):* I have gone astray like a lost sheep; seek Thy servant, for I do not forget Thy commandments.  
*Troparion:* Peace unto the Church and salvation unto Thy people grant Thou by Thy Resurrection!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*

*Troparion:* O Trinity, my God: Father, Son, and Spirit, have mercy upon the world!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:* Vouchsafe unto thy servants, O Virgin, to behold the Resurrection of thy Son!

*Then we sing these Troparia in Tone 5, with the Refrain:*  
*Refrain:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* The assembly of angels was amazed, beholding Thee numbered among the dead, yet destroying the power of death, O Savior, and with Thyself raising up Adam, and freeing all from Hades![^421]  
*Refrain:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* "Why do ye mingle the sweet-smelling myrrh with weeping tears, O maidens and disciples?" the Angel shining at the tomb said unto the Myrrh-bearing women: "Behold the tomb and understand: for the Savior is risen from the grave!"  
*Refrain:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* Very early the Myrrh-bearing women ran unto Thy tomb, O Savior, weeping; but an Angel stood before them and said: "The time for weeping has ceased, weep not; but announce the Resurrection unto the Apostles!"  
*Refrain:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thine ordinances.  
*Troparion:* The Myrrh-bearing women, arriving with myrrh at Thy tomb, O Savior, weeping, heard the Angel speaking unto them: "Why do ye reckon the Living One among the dead? For as God He is risen from the grave!"  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Trinitarian:* Let us worship the Father, and His Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Holy Trinity in one essence, crying with the Seraphim: "Holy, holy, holy art Thou, O Lord!"  
*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:* Bearing the Giver of life, O Virgin, thou hast delivered Adam from sin, and granted joy unto Eve in place of sorrow; and He Who became incarnate of thee, God and Man, has restored unto life them that had fallen from it.  
Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia: glory to Thee, O God! *(thrice)*

*Then the Little Litany. Exclamation:*  
**Priest:** For Thou art the King of peace, O Christ our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine Unoriginate Father, and with Thine All-Holy, Good, and Life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben: "The soldiers guarding" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*[^422]  
Having asked Pilate for the Body, Joseph wraps It in a clean Plashchanytsia, anoints It with sweet spices, and lays It in a new tomb. Therefore, early in the morning, the Myrrh-bearing women cried aloud: "Show unto us, as Thou hast foretold, O Christ, the Resurrection!"  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:* The end.  
*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
The angelic choirs were astonished, beholding Him Who sits in the Father's bosom, how He is laid in a tomb as one dead, the Immortal One! Whom the angelic ranks encircle and glorify with the dead in Hades, as Creator and Lord.

*After this, Reading at the end of the Commentary on the Gospel of Matthew, Section 114: "Now the next day, that followed the day of the preparation..." We do not say "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," but immediately Psalm 50.*[^423]

*Canon of the Day, Tone 6.*[^424]  
*The Heirmos is sung twice, and the Troparia are chanted to 12. At the end, the Heirmos is sung by both choirs together.*  
*These odes of the Canon up to Ode 6 are the composition of Mark the Monk, Bishop of Hydrous (Otranto). The Heirmoi are the composition of a certain woman named Kassia. From Ode 6 unto the end, the composition is by Cosmas.*  
*The Acrostic of the Canon:*  
`ΚΑΙ ΣΗΜΕΡΟΝ ΔΕ ΣΑΒΒΑΤΟΝ ΜΕΛΠΩ ΜΕΓΑ.`  
*That is to say: "And today I sing the Great Sabbath."*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
He Who beneath the wave of the sea hid the pursuer and tyrant of old, was hidden beneath the earth by the children of them that were saved; but let us, like the maidens, sing unto the Lord, for He has been gloriously glorified.[^425]  
*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.  
*Troparion:* O Lord my God, an exodus hymn and a funeral song shall I sing unto Thee, Who by Thy burial hast opened the entrances of life unto me, and Who by death hast destroyed death and slain Hades!

*Troparion:* Considering Thee upon the throne above and in the tomb below, O my Savior, things transcendent and subterranean were shaken at Thy slaying; for beyond understanding wast Thou beheld dead, O Author of life!  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:* That Thou mightest fill all things with Thy glory, Thou didst descend into the nether parts of the earth; for my person which is in Adam was not hidden from Thee, and being buried, Thou dost renew me who was corrupted, O Lover of mankind!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Beholding Thee suspended upon Calvary, Who suspended the whole earth upon the waters without restraint, creation was seized with great awe, crying aloud: "There is none holy save Thee, O Lord!"[^426]  
*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.  
*Troparion:* Thou hast shown forth the types of Thy burial, multiplying visions; and now, O Master, Thou hast divinely revealed Thy hidden mysteries even unto those in Hades: "There is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!"  
*Troparion:* Thou hast stretched out Thy palms upon the Tree, O Savior, and united those who of old were divided; and by the vesture of the Plashchanytsia and the Tomb, O Savior, Thou hast loosed them that were bound: "There is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!"  
*Troparion:* By a tomb and by seals, O Incomprehensible One, Thou wast held by Thine own will; for through Thine actions Thou hast shown forth Thy power unto them that sing with divine operation: "There is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!"

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*[^427]  
The soldiers guarding Thy tomb, O Savior, became as dead men from the lightning radiance of the appearing Angel, who proclaimed the Resurrection unto the women. Thee do we glorify, the Destroyer of corruption; before Thee do we fall down, Who art risen from the tomb, our only God!  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.* The same.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
Foreseeing Thy divine condescension upon the Cross, Habakkuk cried out in astonishment: "Thou hast cut down the dominion of the mighty, O Good One, joining Thyself unto those in Hades, as Almighty!"[^428]  
*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.  
*Troparion:* Today Thou hast sanctified the Seventh Day, which of old Thou didst bless by the resting from works; for Thou dost bring all things forth and renew them, keeping the Sabbath, O my Savior, and restoring all.  
*Troparion:* Thy soul was parted from Thy flesh when the power of the superior nature prevailed in Thee, shattering both bonds of death and Hades, O Word, by Thy dominion.  
*Troparion:* Hades was embittered, O Word, when it met Thee, beholding a deified mortal, wounded with stripes yet all-powerful in action; and by a fearful form it was destroyed.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Isaiah, beholding the unwaning Light of Thy divine manifestation, which mercifully came to pass toward us, O Christ, rising early from the night cried aloud: "The dead shall arise, and they that are in the graves shall be raised up, and all they that are upon the earth shall rejoice!"[^429]  
*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.  
*Troparion:* Thou dost renew the earthly, O Creator, having become dust of the earth; and the Plashchanytsia and the tomb reveal the mystery that is in Thee, O Word: for the noble Counselor shapes the counsel of Him Who begat Thee, magnificently recreating me in Thee.  
*Troparion:* By death Thou transformest mortality, and by burial corruption; for Thou makest incorruptible that which Thou didst assume, divinely working immortality: for Thy flesh did not see corruption, O Master, nor was Thy soul strangely abandoned in Hades.
*Troparion:* Proceeding from an unwedded Mother and pierced in Thy side, O my Creator, from them hast Thou wrought the renewal of Eve, becoming Adam; and Thou hast slept beyond nature a natural sleep, and raised up life from sleep and corruption, as Almighty!

*The Work of Saint Cosmas:*[^430]  
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Jonah was seized, but not held fast in the whale's belly; for bearing the image of Thee, Who didst suffer and wast given over unto burial, he came forth from the monster as from a bridal chamber, and cried unto the watch: "Ye that observe vanities and lies, ye have forsaken your own mercy!"  
*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.  
*Troparion:* Thou wast slain, but not separated, O Word, from the flesh which Thou didst partake of; for even though Thy temple was destroyed at the time of the Passion, yet even so the hypostasis of Thy Divinity and of Thy flesh was one: for in both Thou art one Son, the Word of God, God and Man!  
*Troparion:* Adam's transgression was man-slaying, but not God-slaying; for though Thy bodily nature suffered, yet Thy Divinity remained impassible: and Thou hast transferred Thy corruptible part unto incorruption, and revealed the fountain of incorruptible life through Thy Resurrection!  
*Troparion:* Hades reigns, but does not endure eternally over the human race; for laid in the tomb, O Mighty One, with Thy life-originating palm Thou didst shatter the keys of death, and proclaim unto those who slept there from the ages a true deliverance, becoming the Firstborn of the dead, O Savior!

*Kontakion, Tone 6. Podoben: "He who closed the abyss" (Вкопне́ннаго):*[^431]  
He Who closed the abyss is beheld dead, wrapped in myrrh and a Plashchanytsia; the Immortal One is laid in a tomb as mortal. And the women came to anoint Him with sweet spices, weeping bitterly and crying aloud: "This is the most blessed Sabbath, wherein Christ has fallen asleep, to rise on the third day!"

*Ikos:*  
He Who holds all things was lifted up upon the Cross, and all creation laments, beholding Him hanging naked upon the Tree; the sun hid its beams, and the stars withdrew their light. The earth shook with great fear, the sea fled, and the rocks were split asunder. Many tombs were opened, and the bodies of the saints arose radiant; Hades groans below, the Jews take counsel to slander Christ's Resurrection, and the women cry aloud: "This is the most blessed Sabbath, wherein Christ has fallen asleep, to rise on the third day!"

*Synaxarion on Great and Holy Saturday. Father, bless us to read.*[^432]  
*Verses:*  
In vain dost thou guard the Tomb, O watch;  
For no sepulchre can contain Essential Life!  
On Great and Holy Saturday, we celebrate the divine and bodily Burial of our Lord, God, and Savior Jesus Christ, and His descent into Hades, whereby our race was recalled from corruption and passed over unto eternal life. For Holy Great Lent surpasses all other days, and this Holy and Great Week is greater than Great Lent, and this Great and Holy Saturday is greater than the whole Great Week. It is called the Great Week, not because its days or hours are longer, but because great, supernatural, and extraordinary marvels and works of our Savior were wrought therein, and most especially on this day. For just as in the first creation of the world, God, having accomplished every work, and finally on the sixth day having created man, on the seventh day rested from all His works, and sanctified it, naming it Sabbath, which is interpreted as rest; so also in the accomplishment of the intelligible world, having wrought all things excellently, and on the sixth day having remade fallen man and renewed him by His life-bearing Cross and death, on this present Seventh Day He again rested, accomplishing a complete rest by falling asleep in a life-giving and saving sleep. The Word of God descends with His flesh into the tomb, and He also descends into Hades with His incorruptible and divine soul, which was separated by death from the body, and which He commended into the hands of the Father, unto Whom He also offered His Blood, which was our ransom, without His asking for it. For the Lord's soul was not held captive in Hades as were the souls of the other saints. How could it be? For it was not subject to the ancestral curse as they were; nor did our enemy the devil take it, although he held us, for whose sake we were bought. For how could the robber devil seize Him Who was not only from God, but was God Himself? Yet bodily our Lord Jesus Christ was seen in the tomb with His Divinity, supremely united to the flesh. He was also in Paradise with the Thief, and in Hades, as has been said, with His deified soul, and supernaturally sitting with the Father and the Spirit, as uncircumscribed God. Thus the Divinity suffered nothing in the tomb, even as It suffered nothing upon the Cross; and the Lord's Body indeed underwent corruption, which is the separation of the soul from the body, but dissolution or decay—that is to say, the decomposition of the flesh and the limbs, or complete destruction—It suffered in no wise. But Joseph, having taken down the sacred Body of the Lord, buries It in a new tomb, and in a garden near the Jews rolls a very great stone against the door of the tomb, and departs. And Mary Magdalene and the other Mary were there, sitting opposite the sepulchre.
Now the next day, which is the day after the Preparation, the Chief Priests and Pharisees gathered together unto Pilate, saying: "Sir, we remember that that deceiver said, while he was yet alive, 'After three days I will arise.' It seems good unto us, therefore, that thy authority command the guard to secure the sepulchre." Pilate answered: "If he was a deceiver, why do ye trouble yourselves about his words while he was yet alive, since as a condemned man he is dead? When did he say: 'I will arise'?" They deduced this from the parable of Jonah. "Lest, if the tomb be not secured, he be stolen." O how foolish they were! Whatever they did against themselves, they did unwittingly for us! For Pilate having commanded, they themselves with a military detachment and careful sealing secured the sepulchre, so that the guard and seals being alien, His Resurrection could not be falsely slandered. But Hades meanwhile is overturned and driven to frenzy, having touched a stronger power; and it will soon vomit forth those who were unjustly swallowed, struck by Christ the strongest Cornerstone, whom from the ages it held devoured in its belly.  
By Thine ineffable condescension, O Christ our God, have mercy upon us, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
Ineffable marvel! He Who delivered the holy youths from the furnace of fire is laid in a tomb dead and breathless, for the salvation of us who sing: "O God our Redeemer, blessed art Thou!"[^433]  
*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.  
*Troparion:* Hades was wounded, having received into its heart Him Who was pierced in the side with a spear, and it groans, consumed by divine fire, for the salvation of us who sing: "O God our Redeemer, blessed art Thou!"  
*Troparion:* Rich is the tomb; for having received within itself the Creator as one asleep, it was shown forth as a divine treasury of life, for the salvation of us who sing: "O God our Redeemer, blessed art Thou!"  
*Troparion:* The burial in the tomb according to the law of the departed is accepted by the Life of all, and shows this to be the fountain of the resurrection, for the salvation of us who sing: "O God our Redeemer, blessed art Thou!"  
*Troparion:* One and undivided was the Divinity of Christ with the Father and the Spirit in Hades, in the tomb, and in Eden, for the salvation of us who sing: "O God our Redeemer, blessed art Thou!"

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Be astonished in fear, O heaven, and let the foundations of the earth be shaken; for behold, He Who dwells in the highest is numbered among the dead, and is laid in a narrow tomb: Whom, ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt unto all ages![^434]  
*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.  
*Troparion:* The most pure temple is destroyed, but raises up the fallen tabernacle; for the Second Adam, Who dwells in the highest, descended even unto the subterranean treasures of Hades: Whom, ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt unto all ages!  
*Troparion:* The boldness of the disciples has ceased, but Joseph of Arimathea excels; for beholding God over all dead and naked, he asks for Him and buries Him, crying: "Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt unto all ages!"  
*Troparion:* O new marvels! O goodness! O ineffable forbearance! For voluntarily He Who dwells in the highest is sealed beneath the earth, and God is slandered as a deceiver: Whom, ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt unto all ages!

*We do not sing "More honorable than the Cherubim."*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Weep not for Me, O Mother, beholding in the tomb the Son Whom thou didst conceive in the womb without seed; for I shall arise and be glorified, and as God I shall endlessly exalt in glory with faith and love them that magnify thee![^435]  
*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.  
*Troparion:* "At Thy strange birth I escaped birth-pangs beyond nature, and was blessed, O Unoriginate Son; but now beholding Thee, my God, dead and breathless, I am pierced through bitterly with the sword of sorrow; but arise, that I may be magnified!"  
*Troparion:* "The earth covers Me by Mine own will, but the gatekeepers of Hades tremble, beholding Me clothed in the blood-stained garment of vengeance, O Mother; for having struck down the enemies by the Cross as God, I shall arise again and magnify thee!"  
*Troparion:* "Let creation rejoice, let all the earthly be glad; for the enemy Hades has been despoiled! Let the women meet Me with sweet myrrh, delivering Adam with Eve from corruption, and on the third day I shall arise!"

*In place of the Exaposteilarion: "Holy is the Lord our God" thrice, in Tone 2.*

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and sing these Stichera Idiomela in Tone 2:*[^436]  
Today a tomb holds Him Who holds creation in the palm of His hand; a stone covers Him Who covered the heavens with glory. Life sleeps, and Hades trembles, and Adam is loosed from bonds. Glory to Thy dispensation, whereby having accomplished all things, Thou hast granted us eternal rest, as God, and Thy most holy Resurrection from the dead!

What is this sight beheld? What is this present rest? The King of the ages, having accomplished the dispensation through His Passion, keeps the Sabbath in the tomb, granting us a new Sabbath-rest! Unto Him let us cry aloud: "Arise, O God, judge the earth, for Thou reignest unto ages, having boundless and great mercy!"

Come, let us behold our Life lying in the tomb, that He may give life unto them that lie in the tombs! Come, today, beholding Him Who sprang from Judah sleeping, let us prophetically cry aloud unto Him: "Thou hast couched, Thou hast slept as a lion; who shall rouse Thee up, O King? But arise by Thine own sovereign power, Who didst give Thyself up voluntarily for our sake! O Lord, glory to Thee!"

*Tone 6:*  
Joseph asked for the Body of Jesus, and laid It in his own new tomb; for it was fitting for Him to come forth from the tomb as from a bridal chamber, Who crushed the dominion of death and opened the gates of Paradise unto men! O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 6:*  
Today the great Moses mystically foreshadowed, saying: "And God blessed the seventh day"; for this is the blessed Sabbath! This is the day of rest, whereon the Only-begotten Son of God rested from all His works, keeping the Sabbath in the flesh through the dispensation of death, and returning again unto what He was, through the Resurrection, has granted us eternal life, as the only good One and Lover of mankind!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion, Tone 2:*  
Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos, for through Him Who was incarnate of thee, Hades is taken captive, Adam is recalled, the curse is annulled, and Eve is set free! Death is put to death, and we are quickened! Therefore singing we cry aloud: "Blessed is Christ our God, Who was thus well-pleased!"

*Then the Great Doxology.*  
*The Superior vests in full priestly vestments, and the other priests in phelonia. He enters in procession with the Holy Gospel Book while the Trisagion is being sung. And when it is finished,*  
*We sing the Grave Sticheron in Tone 5:*[^437]  
Come, let us bless Joseph of perpetual memory, who went unto Pilate by night and asked for the Life of all: "Give me this Stranger, Who has nowhere to lay His head! Give me this Stranger, Whom a deceitful disciple betrayed unto death! Give me this Stranger, Whom His Mother, beholding hanging upon the Cross, wailed with weeping and cried out in motherly fashion: 'Woe is me, my Child! Woe is me, my Light, and my beloved womb! For what was foretold by Simeon in the temple is come to pass: a sword has pierced my heart! But turn my weeping into the joy of Thy Resurrection!' We worship Thy Passion, O Christ! We worship Thy Passion, O Christ! We worship Thy Passion, O Christ, and Thy holy Resurrection!"

*And we sing the Priest's Exclamation: "Let us attend! Peace be unto all! Wisdom, let us attend!" And we sing the first Troparion:*  
*The noble Joseph... (see fol. 126).*[^438]  
*And we say the Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 2:*  
Thou Who holdest the ends of the earth, O Christ, wast well-pleased to be held by a tomb, that Thou mightest deliver mankind from the swallowing of Hades, and raising us up, quicken us as immortal God!  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: The end. Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*Then the Prokeimenon in Tone 4:*  
Arise, O Lord, help us, and deliver us for Thy Name's sake.  
*Verse:* O God, with our ears have we heard, and our fathers have declared unto us.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel (Ezekiel 37:1–14):*[^439]  
The hand of the Lord was upon me, and carried me out in the Spirit of the Lord, and set me down in the midst of the valley, and it was full of human bones. And He led me round about them on every side, and behold, there were very many upon the face of the valley, and lo, they were very dry. And He said unto me: "Son of man, shall these bones live?" And I said: "O Lord, Lord, Thou knowest these things." And He said unto me: "Son of man, prophesy upon these bones, and say unto them: 'O ye dry bones, hear the word of the Lord! Thus says the Lord God unto these bones: Behold, I bring upon you the breath of life; and I will put sinews upon you, and bring up flesh upon you, and stretch skin upon you, and put My Spirit in you, and ye shall live, and know that I am the Lord.'" So I prophesied as the Lord commanded me: and as I prophesied, there was a noise, and behold, an earthquake, and bone came together unto its joint. And I looked, and behold, sinews and flesh grew upon them, and skin covered them above, but there was no breath in them. And He said unto me: "Prophesy unto the breath, prophesy, son of man, and say unto the breath: 'Thus says the Lord God: Come from the four winds, O breath, and breathe upon these dead, that they may live!'" And I prophesied as the Lord commanded me, and the breath entered into them, and they lived, and stood up upon their feet, an exceeding great army. And the Lord said unto me, saying: "Son of man, these bones are the whole house of Israel; they say: 'Our bones are dried, our hope is lost, we are cut off.' Therefore prophesy, and say unto them: 'Thus says the Lord God: Behold, I open your graves, and will bring you up out of your graves, O My people, and bring you into the land of Israel. And ye shall know that I am God, when I have opened your graves, and when I have brought you up out of your graves, O My people. And I will put My Spirit in you, and ye shall live, and I will place you in your own land, and ye shall know that I the Lord have spoken it, and performed it,' says the Lord God."

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*  
Arise, O Lord my God, let Thine hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end.
*Verse:* I will praise Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart.

*The Epistle to the Corinthians (1 Cor. 5:6–8; Gal. 3:13–14):*[^440]  
Brethren, a little leaven leavens the whole lump. Purge out therefore the old leaven, that ye may be a new lump, as ye are unleavened. For even Christ our Passover is sacrificed for us: therefore let us keep the feast, not with old leaven, neither with the leaven of malice and wickedness; but with the unleavened bread of sincerity and truth. Christ has redeemed us from the curse of the law, being made a curse for us: for it is written: "Cursed is every one that hangs on a tree"; that the blessing of Abraham might come on the Gentiles through Jesus Christ; that we might receive the promise of the Spirit through faith.

*Alleluia, Tone 5:*  
Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered.  
*Verse:* As smoke vanishes, so let them vanish.

*The Gospel according to Matthew, Section 114 (Matt. 27:62–66): "Now the next day, that followed the day of the preparation..."*

*Then the Litany: "Have mercy upon us, O God, according to Thy great mercy..." And we sing: "Let us complete our morning prayer..."*  
*And the Dismissal.*  
*We also attach the First Hour. And the Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours we chant simply in the Narthex, together with the Beatitudes, at their proper time.*

*Chapter 17.*[^441]  
*It is necessary to know: If the Annunciation of the Most Pure Theotokos happens to fall on Great and Holy Saturday.*  
*And if the Superior wishes to celebrate a Vigil: On the night of the Annunciation on Great Friday Evening, at Little Vespers we sing everything of the Feast, and likewise at the Dismissal of Vespers. And at the beginning of Great Vespers, "Blessed is the man" is not sung. But if there be no Vigil, on Great Friday Evening, at the tenth or eleventh hour of the day, there is a great semantron ringing and all the heavy bells. And Vespers is chanted: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera Idiomela of the Day to 6, and of the Annunciation to 4. Glory of the Day, Tone 6: "How the law-transgressing..."; Both now, of the Feast, the same Tone: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent..." Entrance with the Gospel Book. "O Gentle Light." The Prokeimenon and Old Testament Readings of the Day and of the Feast: 5. Then the Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel. Then "Let us all say." Then "Vouchsafe, O Lord, this evening." And again the Priest: "Let us complete our prayer..." And after the Exclamation, at the Aposticha, Stichera of the Day, Tone 2: "When from the Tree...", with their verses. Glory, of the Feast, Tone 5: "The mystery which from the ages..." Both now, Tone 5: "Thee Who clothest Thyself..." Then "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart." And after the Our Father, Troparion of the Feast. Glory, and both now: "The noble Joseph..." And the Dismissal. And we sing Compline in the Great church.*

*At the second hour of the night, they strike the great bell and all the heavy bells. And we chant Matins according to the custom. At "God is the Lord," Troparia of the Day, Tone 2: "The noble Joseph...", once; "When Thou didst descend...", once. Glory: "The Myrrh-bearing women..."; Both now, of the Feast: "Today is the fountainhead of our salvation..." And immediately we sing the Amomos in Tone 5. The Priest censes at the First Stasis, and likewise at the Second and Third, at the beginnings. At the First Stasis, the Refrain: "We magnify Thee, O Christ the Giver of life..." At the Second: "It is meet..." At the Third: "Every generation..." And at the end, we sing the Resurrectional Troparia: "The assembly of angels...", and the rest. Then the Little Litany and Sessional Hymns. After the second: "The soldiers guarding Thy tomb... wrapped It in a clean Plashchanytsia...", and the Reading of the Day. Then the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn of the Feast, and Reading of the Feast. Hymns of Ascent: the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: "Proclaim the good news day by day..." "Let every breath..." Gospel of the Feast, from Luke, Section 4. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast, Tone 6: "Gabriel proclaims..." Canons: of the Feast with the Heirmos to 8, and of the Day with the Heirmos to 8. Katavasia: Heirmos of the Day, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of the Day, Sessional Hymn of the Day, Glory, and both now, of the Feast, and Reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast, Reading in the Prologue, and Synaxarion. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable than the Cherubim," but the Refrains of the Feast. "Holy is the Lord our God" in Tone 2. Exaposteilarion of the Feast, twice. At the Praises, Stichera Idiomela of the Day, 3, and of the Feast, 3. Then Sticheron Prosomoion of the Feast, Tone 8: "Let the heavens be glad...", with the verse: "Proclaim the good news day by day..." Glory, of the Day, Tone 6: "Joseph asked for the Body..." or "Today the great Moses mystically foreshadowed..." Both now: "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..." Great Doxology. The Priest vests in full priestly robes and makes the Entrance with the Holy Gospel Book, while we sing the Trisagion; and after the Trisagion, the Priest says: "Wisdom! Let us attend!" And we sing the Introit Troparion:*

*Troparion, Tone 2: "The noble Joseph..." Immediately the Priest: "Let us attend! Peace be unto all!" Then the Troparion of the Emperor, Tone 2: "Thou Who holdest the ends of the earth..." Prokeimenon and Old Testament Readings of the Day. Then the Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel of the Day. Litany and Dismissal. First Hour. And the other Hours we chant at their proper time, and at the Hours the Troparion of the Feast and of the Day, alternating the Kontakia; and the Beatitudes swiftly without singing. After the Our Father, Kontakion of the Feast: "Lord, have mercy," and the Dismissal.*

*On Great and Holy Saturday, in the Evening. At the eleventh hour of the day, they ring the great bell and all the heavy bells. And having gathered into the church, the Priest vests in all bright vestments. He begins Vespers, saying: "Come, let us worship," thrice; and after the Psalm, the Litany. At "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3: "Our evening prayers..."; and of Great Saturday, Idiomela, 3, Tone 8; and of the Emperor to 4. Glory, Tone 6: "Today the great Moses..."; Both now, of the Feast, the same Tone: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent..." Entrance with the Gospel Book. "O Gentle Light." We do not say the Prokeimenon, but immediately the Priest: "Wisdom!", and the Readings of the Day according to their order. And after the completion of the Readings, the Little Litany. Exclamation for the Trisagion. And in place of the Trisagion: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ..." Then the Prokeimenon and Epistle of the Day; and of the Feast, Alleluia, of the Day according to custom. Gospel of the Day and of the Feast. And in place of the Cherubic Hymn: "Let all mortal flesh keep silence..." And the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great follows in order. Communion Verse of the Day. At table there is a piece of bread, and we drink wine only, for the glory and intercession of the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Note: And let this also be known: On whichever day of Great Week the Annunciation falls, we celebrate a Vigil with Compline. And we eat cooked food with oil, and drink wine; except on Great Friday, for on that day we allow wine only, for the glory and intercession of the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*If the Commemoration of the Holy Great-Martyr George falls on Great Saturday: Concerning this instruction, see on folio 150.*[^442]

## Great and Holy Saturday, in the Evening, at the Tenth Hour of the Day[^443]

*They strike the great wooden semantron for Vespers.*  
*The Studite Monastery and the Holy Mountain strike at the eleventh hour. Others have established the striking when the sun goes down.*  
*And we gather into the church. And the Priest and Deacon having vested in bright vestments:*  
*[According to other Typika, in crimson vestments.]*  
*And the Deacon says: "Bless, Master!" And the Priest:*  
**Priest:** Blessed is the Kingdom of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.  
*And the appointed Reader having said: "Amen," immediately reads:*  
**Reader:** Come, let us worship, *(thrice).*  
*Psalm 103: "Bless the Lord, O my soul." Alleluia, (thrice).*  
*After this, the Great Litany.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," we sing in Tone 1. And the Priest and Deacon, having departed, perform the Proskomedia.*  
*Then the Deacon censes.*  
*Set 8 verses. Resurrectional Stichera to 4, Tone 1:*  
*(Stichera continue on next leaf).*

Receive our evening prayers, O holy Lord, and grant us remission of sins, for Thou alone art He Who has revealed the Resurrection unto the world.  
Encompass Zion, ye people, and surround her, and give glory within her unto Him Who is risen from the dead; for He is our God, Who has delivered us from our iniquities.  
Come, ye people, let us sing and worship Christ, glorifying His Resurrection from the dead; for He is our God, Who has delivered the world from the deception of the enemy.  
By Thy Passion, O Christ, we have been set free from passions, and by Thy Resurrection we have been delivered from corruption. O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Other Stichera Idiomela of Great Saturday, Tone 8:*[^444]  
Today Hades groans aloud: "It were better for me had I not received the One born of Mary! For coming upon me, He destroyed my power, shattered the brazen gates, and being God, resurrected the souls which I formerly held. Glory, O Lord, to Thy Cross and Thy Resurrection!" *(Twice).*  
Today Hades groans aloud: "My dominion is destroyed! I received a dead man as one of the deceased, yet Him I am completely unable to hold, but rather lose even those over whom I reigned. I possessed the dead from all eternity, but behold, He raises all! Glory, O Lord, to Thy Cross and Thy Resurrection!"  
Today Hades groans aloud: "My power is swallowed up! The Shepherd was crucified, and He has resurrected Adam; I am deprived of those over whom I ruled, and all whom I was able to swallow down, I have cast up! The Crucified One has emptied the graves; the dominion of death has no more strength! Glory, O Lord, to Thy Cross and Thy Resurrection!"

*Glory, Tone 6:*[^445]  
Today the great Moses mystically foreshadowed this day, saying: "And God blessed the seventh day." For this is the blessed Sabbath; this is the day of rest, on which the Only-begotten Son of God rested from all His works, keeping the Sabbath in the flesh according to the dispensation of death; and having returned again unto what He was through the Resurrection, He has granted us eternal life, for He alone is good and loves mankind.

*Both now, Theotokion (Dogmaticon), Tone 1:*[^446]  
Let us hymn the Virgin Mary, the glory of the whole world, who sprang forth from mankind and gave birth unto the Master, the heavenly portal, the song of the bodiless hosts, and the adornment of the faithful. For she appeared as heaven and the temple of the Godhead; she broke down the barrier of enmity, brought in peace, and opened the Kingdom. Therefore, having her as the firm foundation of our faith, we have as our defender the Lord Who was born of her. Be of good courage therefore, be of good courage, O people of God; for He will overcome the enemies, as He is almighty!

*Then the Entrance with the Holy Gospel Book.*  
*O Gentle Light.*  
*The Prokeimenon is not sung; but immediately the Priest or Deacon says:*  
**Deacon:** Wisdom!  
*And the Reader begins the Readings, which are fifteen in order.*[^447]

*The First Reading (Genesis 1:1–13):*[^448]  
*A Reading from Genesis (Chapter 1):*  
In the beginning God made the heaven and the earth.

And the earth was invisible and unformed, and darkness was upon the face of the abyss, and the Spirit of God moved upon the face of the water. And God said: "Let there be light," and there was light. And God saw the light, that it was good; and God divided between the light and between the darkness. And God called the light Day, and the darkness He called Night. And the evening and the morning were the first day. And God said: "Let there be a firmament in the midst of the water, and let it divide between water and water," and it was so. And God made the firmament; and God divided between the water which was under the firmament, and between the water which was above the firmament. And God called the firmament Heaven; and God saw that it was good. And the evening and the morning were the second day. And God said: "Let the water which is under heaven be gathered together into one collection, and let the dry land appear," and it was so. And the water which was under heaven was gathered together into its collections, and the dry land appeared. And God called the dry land Earth, and the collections of the waters He called Seas; and God saw that it was good. And God said: "Let the earth bring forth grass, yielding seed according to its kind and according to its likeness, and the fruit tree bearing fruit, whose seed is in itself according to its likeness upon the earth," and it was so. And the earth brought forth grass, yielding seed according to its kind and according to its likeness, and the fruit tree bearing fruit, whose seed is in itself according to its kind upon the earth; and God saw that it was good. And the evening and the morning were the third day.

*The Second Reading (Isaiah 60:1–16):*[^449]  
*The Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 60):*  
Shine, shine, O Jerusalem, for thy light is come, and the glory of the Lord is risen upon thee! For behold, darkness shall cover the earth, and gross darkness the nations; but upon thee the Lord shall appear, and His glory shall be seen upon thee. And kings shall walk in thy light, and nations in the brightness of thy rising. Lift up thine eyes round about, and see all thy children gathered together: behold, all thy sons are come from afar, and thy daughters shall be borne upon shoulders. Then shalt thou see, and rejoice, and shalt fear, and be amazed in heart; because the wealth of the sea, of the nations, and of peoples shall be transferred unto thee. And herds of camels shall come unto thee, and the camels of Midian and Ephah shall cover thee; all they from Sheba shall come, bringing gold, and they shall bring frankincense and precious stone, and they shall proclaim the good news of the salvation of the Lord. And all the sheep of Kedar shall be gathered unto thee, and the rams of Nebaioth shall come unto thee, and acceptable sacrifices shall be offered upon Mine altar, and My house of prayer shall be glorified. Who are these that fly as clouds, and as doves with their young? Zion is with Me; the islands have waited for Me, and the ships of Tarshish among the first, to bring thy children from afar, and their silver and their gold with them, for the sake of the holy name of the Lord, and because the Holy One of Israel is glorious. And the sons of strangers shall build thy walls, and their kings shall stand before thee; for in My wrath I smote thee, and in My mercy I loved thee. And thy gates shall be open continually, O Jerusalem; day and night they shall not be shut, to bring unto thee the wealth of the nations, and their kings led captive. For the nations and the kings that will not serve thee shall perish, and the nations shall be utterly laid waste. And the glory of Lebanon shall come unto thee, with the cypress, the pine, and the cedar together, to glorify My holy place, and I will glorify the place of My feet. And the sons of them that afflicted thee and provoked thee shall come bending down unto thee, and all they that provoked thee shall bow themselves down at the soles of thy feet; and thou shalt be called the City of the Lord, Zion of the Holy One of Israel. Whereas thou hast been forsaken and hated, so that none helped thee, I will make thee an eternal rejoicing, a joy of generations of generations. Thou shalt also suck the milk of the Gentiles, and shalt eat the wealth of kings; and thou shalt know that I the Lord am thy Savior and thy Deliverer, the Holy God of Israel.

*The Third Reading (Exodus 12:1–11):*[^450]  
*A Reading from Exodus (Chapter 12):*  
The Lord spoke unto Moses and Aaron, saying: "This month shall be unto you the beginning of months; it shall be the first month of the year unto you. Speak unto all the assembly of the children of Israel, saying: In the tenth day of this month let every man take a lamb according to the house of his fatherhood. And if they be few in the house, so that they are not sufficient for a lamb, let them take their neighboring acquaintance according to the number of souls; each shall reckon sufficient for himself for a lamb. A whole lamb, a male of the first year, without blemish, shall it be unto you; ye shall take it from the lambs and from the kids. And it shall be kept by you until the fourteenth day of this month; and the whole multitude of the assembly of the children of Israel shall kill it toward evening. And they shall take of the blood, and strike it on the two side posts and on the lintels in the houses wherein they shall eat it. And they shall eat the flesh in that night, roasted with fire, and with unleavened bread and bitter herbs they shall eat it. Ye shall not eat of it raw, nor boiled in water, but roasted with fire: the head with the feet and the entrails. Ye shall let nothing of it remain until the morning, and a bone of it ye shall not break; and that which remains of it until the morning ye shall burn with fire. And thus shall ye eat it: with your loins girded, your sandals on your feet, and your staves in your hands; and ye shall eat it in haste: it is the Lord's Passover."

*The Fourth Reading (Jonah 1:1–4:11):*[^451]  
*The Prophecy of Jonah (Chapter 1):*  
The word of the Lord came unto Jonah the son of Amittai, saying: "Arise, and go unto Nineveh, that great city, and preach in it, for the cry of its wickedness is come up before Me." And Jonah arose to flee unto Tarshish from the presence of the Lord. And He went down to Joppa, and found a ship going to Tarshish; and He paid the fare thereof, and went into it, to sail with them unto Tarshish from the presence of the Lord. But the Lord raised up a great wind upon the sea, and there was a mighty tempest in the sea, so that the ship was in danger of being broken. Then the mariners were afraid, and cried every man unto his god, and cast forth the wares that were in the ship into the sea, to lighten it. But Jonah was gone down into the sides of the ship, and he lay, and was fast asleep and snoring. So the shipmaster came to him, and said unto him: "Why snorest thou? Arise, call upon thy God, if so be that God will save us, that we perish not." And they said every one to his fellow: "Come, and let us cast lots, that we may know for whose cause this evil is upon us." So they cast lots, and the lot fell upon Jonah. Then said they unto him: "Tell us now, for whose cause is this evil upon us? What is thine occupation? And whence comest thou, and whither goest thou? And of what country and of what people art thou?" And he said unto them: "I am a servant of the Lord, and I worship the Lord God of heaven, Who made the sea and the dry land." Then were the men exceedingly afraid, and said unto him: "Why hast thou done this?" For the men knew that he fled from the presence of the Lord, because he had told them. Then said they unto him: "What shall we do unto thee, that the sea may be calm unto us?" For the sea raged, and the surging waves lifted themselves up yet more. And Jonah said unto them: "Take me up, and cast me forth into the sea; so shall the sea be calm unto you: for I know that for my sake this great tempest is upon you." Nevertheless the men forced themselves to return to the land; but they could not, for the sea raged and lifted itself up yet more against them. Therefore they cried unto the Lord, and said: "By no means, O Lord, let us not perish for this man's life, and lay not upon us righteous blood: for Thou, O Lord, hast done as it pleased Thee!" So they took up Jonah, and cast him forth into the sea; and the sea ceased from her raging. Then the men feared the Lord with a great fear, and offered a sacrifice unto God, and made vows.

*Chapter 2:*  
Now the Lord commanded a great whale to swallow up Jonah. And Jonah was in the belly of the whale three days and three nights. Then Jonah prayed unto the Lord His God out of the belly of the whale, and said: "I cried in my affliction unto the Lord my God, and He gave ear unto me; out of the belly of Hades was my cry, and Thou didst hear my voice. Thou didst cast me into the depths of the heart of the sea, and the floods compassed me about; all Thy billows and Thy waves passed over me. And I said: I am cast out of Thy sight; yet will I add to look again toward Thy holy temple. The water was poured round about me, even unto my soul; the lowest abyss compassed me about; my head was submerged. I went down into the clefts of the mountains; into the earth whose bars are eternal locks; yet let my life come up from corruption, unto Thee, O Lord my God! When my soul failed from me, I remembered the Lord; and let my prayer come unto Thee, into Thy holy temple. They that observe lying vanities forsake their own mercy. But I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise and thanksgiving; as much as I have vowed, I will pay unto Thee for my salvation, unto the Lord." And the whale was commanded, and it cast out Jonah upon the dry land.

*Chapter 3:*  
And the word of the Lord came unto Jonah the second time, saying: "Arise, go unto Nineveh, that great city, and preach and preach in it according to the former preaching which I spoke unto thee." So Jonah arose, and went unto Nineveh, according to the word of the Lord. Now Nineveh was a great city unto God, of three days' journey. And Jonah began to enter into the city a day's journey, and he preached, and said: "Yet three days, and Nineveh shall be overthrown!" And the men of Nineveh believed God, and proclaimed a fast, and put on sackcloth, from the greatest of them even to the least of them. For word came unto the king of Nineveh, and he arose from his throne, and he laid his robe from him, and covered himself with sackcloth, and sat in ashes. And it was proclaimed and published through Nineveh by the decree of the king and his nobles, saying: "Let neither man nor beast, herd nor flock, taste any thing: let them not feed, nor drink water. But let man and beast be covered with sackcloth, and cry mightily unto God; and let them turn every one from his evil way, and from the injustice that is in their hands, saying: Who knows if God will repent and be entreated, and turn away from His fierce anger, that we perish not?" And God saw their works, that they turned from their evil ways; and God repented of the evil that He had said He would do unto them; and He did it not.

*Chapter 4:*  
But it displeased Jonah exceedingly, and he was troubled. And he prayed unto the Lord, and said: "O Lord, were not these my words when I was yet in my country? Therefore I fled beforehand unto Tarshish: for I knew that Thou art a gracious God, and merciful, longsuffering, and of great kindness, and repentest Thee of the evil of men. And now, O Master and Lord, take my life from me; for it is better for me to die than to live!" And the Lord said unto Jonah: "Art thou exceedingly grieved?" And he said: "I am exceedingly grieved, even unto death." So Jonah went out of the city, and sat on the east side of the city, and there made him a booth, and sat under it in the shadow, till he might see what would become of the city. And the Lord God commanded a gourd, and it grew up over Jonah's head, that it might be a shadow over his head, to overshadow him from his evils. So Jonah rejoiced of the gourd with great joy. But God commanded a worm early on the morrow, and it smote the gourd, and it withered away. And it came to pass, when the sun did arise, that God commanded a vehement burning wind; and the sun beat upon the head of Jonah, that he fainted, and repudiated his soul, and said: "It is better for me to die than to live!" And the Lord God said to Jonah: "Art thou exceedingly grieved for the gourd?" And he said: "I am exceedingly grieved, even unto death." Then said the Lord: "Thou hast had pity on the gourd, for the which thou hast not suffered, neither didst thou labor for it, neither didst thou rear it; which sprang up in a night, and perished in a night. And should not I spare Nineveh, that great city, wherein dwell more than sixscore thousand persons that cannot discern between their right hand and their left hand, and also much cattle?"

*The Fifth Reading (Joshua 5:10–15):*[^452]  
*A Reading from Joshua the Son of Nun (Chapter 5):*  
The children of Israel encamped in Gilgal, and kept the Passover on the fourteenth day of the first month at evening, on the west of Jericho, on the other side of the Jordan, in the plain. And they ate of the grain of the earth on the morrow after the Passover, unleavened cakes and new grain on that same day. And the manna ceased on the morrow, after they had eaten of the grain of the earth; neither had the children of Israel manna any more, but they gathered the fruits of the land of the Phoenicians in that year. And it came to pass, when Joshua was in Jericho, that he lifted up his eyes and looked, and behold, there stood a Man over against him with His sword drawn in His hand. And Joshua went unto Him, and said unto Him: "Art Thou for us, or for our adversaries?" And He said: "I am the Archistrategos of the Host of the Lord; now am I come." And Joshua fell on his face to the earth, and worshipped Him, and said: "Master, what commandest Thou unto Thy servant?" And the Archistrategos of the Lord said unto Joshua: "Loose thy sandal from off thy foot; for the place whereon thou standest is holy." And Joshua did so.

*The Sixth Reading (Exodus 13:20–15:19):*[^453]  
*A Reading from Exodus (Chapter 13):*  
The children of Israel departed from Succoth, and encamped in Etham, on the edge of the wilderness. And God led them: by day indeed in a pillar of a cloud, to show them the way; and by night in a pillar of fire. The pillar of the cloud departed not by day, nor the pillar of fire by night, from before all the people.

*Chapter 14:*  
And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying: "Speak unto the children of Israel, that they turn and encamp before the village, between Migdol and the sea, over against Baal-zephon: before it shalt thou encamp by the sea. For Pharaoh will say of the children of Israel: These wander in the land, the wilderness has shut them in. And I will harden Pharaoh's heart, that he shall pursue after them; and I will be glorified in Pharaoh, and in all his host, that all the Egyptians may know that I am the Lord." And they did so. And it was told the king of the Egyptians that the people fled; and the heart of Pharaoh and of his servants was turned against the people, and they said: "Why have we done this, that we have let the children of Israel go from serving us?" And Pharaoh made ready his chariots, and gathered all his people with him. And he took six hundred chosen chariots, and every horse of Egypt, and captains over every one of them. And the Lord hardened the heart of Pharaoh king of Egypt, and he pursued after the children of Israel; and the children of Israel went out with a high hand. But the Egyptians pursued after them, and found them encamping by the sea; and all the horses and chariots of Pharaoh, and his host, and his horsemen, were before the village, over against Baal-zephon. And Pharaoh drew near. And when the children of Israel lifted up their eyes, behold, the Egyptians encamped after them;

and they were sore afraid; and the children of Israel cried out unto the Lord. And they said unto Moses: "Because there were no graves in Egypt, hast thou taken us away to die in the wilderness? Why hast thou dealt thus with us, to carry us forth out of Egypt? Is not this the word that we spoke unto thee, saying: Let us alone, that we may serve the Egyptians? For it had been better for us to serve the Egyptians, than that we should die in this wilderness." And Moses said unto the people: "Fear ye not, stand still, and see the salvation of the Lord, which He will show to you today: for the Egyptians whom ye have seen today, ye shall see them again no more unto eternity. The Lord shall fight for you, and ye shall hold your peace." And the Lord said unto Moses: "Why criest thou unto Me? Speak unto the children of Israel, that they go forward. But lift thou up thy rod, and stretch out thine hand over the sea, and divide it; and let the children of Israel go on dry ground through the midst of the sea. And I, behold, I will harden the hearts of Pharaoh and of his servants, and of all the Egyptians, and they shall enter in after them; and I will get Me honor upon Pharaoh, and upon all his host, upon his chariots, and upon his horsemen. And all the Egyptians shall know that I am the Lord, when I have gotten Me honor upon Pharaoh, upon his chariots, and upon his horsemen." And the Angel of God, which went before the camp of Israel, removed and went behind them; and the pillar of the cloud went from before their face, and stood behind them. And it came between the camp of the Egyptians and the camp of Israel; and it was a cloud and darkness, yet the night passed, so that the one came not near the other all the night. And Moses stretched out his hand over the sea; and the Lord caused the sea to go back by a strong south wind all that night, and made the sea dry land, and the waters were divided. And the children of Israel went into the midst of the sea upon the dry ground; and the waters were a wall unto them on their right hand, and on their left. And the Egyptians pursued, and went in after them to the midst of the sea, even all Pharaoh's horses, his chariots, and his horsemen. And it came to pass, that in the morning watch the Lord looked unto the host of the Egyptians through the pillar of fire and of the cloud, and troubled the host of the Egyptians. And He bound their chariot wheels, and drove them heavily; so that the Egyptians said: "Let us flee from the face of Israel; for the Lord fights for them against the Egyptians." And the Lord said unto Moses: "Stretch out thine hand over the sea, that the water may come again and be gathered together upon the Egyptians, upon their chariots, and upon their horsemen." And Moses stretched forth his hand over the sea, and at daybreak the water returned to its accustomed place; and the Egyptians fled beneath the water; and the Lord overthrew the Egyptians in the midst of the sea. And the waters returned, and covered the chariots, and the horsemen, and all the host of Pharaoh that came into the sea after them; there remained not so much as one of them. But the children of Israel walked upon dry land in the midst of the sea; and the waters were a wall unto them on their right hand, and on their left. Thus the Lord saved Israel that day out of the hand of the Egyptians; and Israel saw the Egyptians dead upon the sea shore. And Israel saw that great hand which the Lord showed upon the Egyptians; and all the people feared the Lord, and believed God, and His servant Moses.

*Then sang Moses and the children of Israel this Song unto the Lord, and they spoke, saying:*  
**Reader:** Let us sing unto the Lord, in Tone 5: For He is gloriously glorified!  
*And the Reader gives the verses alternately to each choir:*[^454]  
**Reader:** The horse and his rider has He thrown into the sea.  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
*And the people of both choirs sing:*  
**Choir:** For He is gloriously glorified!  
**Reader:** The Lord is my strength and protector, and is become my salvation:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** He is my God, and I will glorify Him; my father's God, and I will exalt Him:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** The Lord shatters warfare, the Lord is His name; Pharaoh's chariots and his host has He cast into the sea:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** His chosen captains has He drowned in the Red Sea:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** The depths have covered them; they sank into the bottom as a stone:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Thy right hand, O Lord, is become glorious in power:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Thy right hand, O Lord, has dashed in pieces the enemy; and in the greatness of Thy glory Thou hast overthrown them that rose up against Thee:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Thou sentest forth Thy wrath, which consumed them as stubble; and with the breath of Thy nostrils the water was divided:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** The waters stood upright as a wall, and the depths were congealed in the heart of the sea:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** The enemy said: I will pursue, I will overtake, I will divide the spoil; my soul shall be satisfied; I will draw my sword, my hand shall destroy them:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Thou didst blow with Thy wind, the sea covered them; they sank as lead in the mighty waters:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Who is like unto Thee, O Lord, among the gods? Who is like Thee, glorious in holiness, marvelous in praises, doing wonders?  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Thou stretchedst out Thy right hand, the earth swallowed them; Thou in Thy righteousness hast guided forth this Thy people which Thou hast redeemed:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Thou hast summoned them by Thy strength unto Thy holy habitation; the nations heard, and were angry: pangs have taken hold on the inhabitants of Philistia:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Then the dukes of Edom were amazed; the mighty men of Moab, trembling took hold upon them; all the inhabitants of Canaan melted away:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Fear and dread shall fall upon them; by the greatness of Thine arm let them become as still as a stone:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Till Thy people pass over, O Lord, till this Thy people pass over, which Thou hast purchased:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** Bring them in, and plant them in the mountain of Thine inheritance, in the ready habitation which Thou hast made, O Lord; in the Sanctuary, O Lord, which Thy hands have established:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** The Lord reigns unto ages, and unto ages, and yet more! For the horse of Pharaoh went in with his chariots and with his horsemen into the sea, and the Lord brought again the waters of the sea upon them:  
Let us sing unto the Lord!  
**Reader:** But the children of Israel went on dry land in the midst of the sea:  
Glory: Let us sing unto the Lord!  
Both now: Let us sing unto the Lord!  
*And finally the Reader sings the same:*  
**Reader:** For He is gloriously glorified!

*The Seventh Reading (Zephaniah 3:8–15):*[^455]  
*The Prophecy of Zephaniah (Chapter 3):*  
Thus says the Lord: "Wait upon Me until the day of My resurrection, for a witness; for My judgment is to gather the assemblies of the nations, to assemble the kings, to pour upon them Mine indignation, even all My fierce anger: for all the earth shall be devoured with the fire of My jealousy. For then will I turn to the people a language according to its generation, that they may all call upon the name of the Lord, to serve Him under one yoke. From the ends of the rivers of Ethiopia I will receive My worshippers: the sons of the dispersed shall bring Me offerings. In that day shalt thou not be ashamed for all thy doings, wherein thou hast transgressed against Me: for then I will take away out of the midst of thee the reproaches of thine arrogance, and thou shalt no more be haughty upon My holy mountain. And I will leave in the midst of thee an afflicted and poor people, and the remnant of Israel shall revere the name of the Lord. They shall not do iniquity, nor speak vanities; neither shall a deceitful tongue be found in their mouth: for they shall feed and lie down, and none shall make them afraid. Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; preach aloud, O daughter of Jerusalem; be glad and rejoice with all the heart, O daughter of Jerusalem! The Lord has taken away thy judgments, He has delivered thee from the hand of thine enemies; the King of Israel, even the Lord, is in the midst of thee: thou shalt not see evil any more."

*The Eighth Reading (3 Kings 17:8–24):*[^456]  
*A Reading from the Third Book of Kings (Chapter 17):*  
The word of the Lord came unto Elijah, saying: "Arise, get thee to Zarephath of Sidon, and dwell there: behold, I have commanded a widow woman there to sustain thee." So he arose and went to Zarephath. And when he came to the gate of the city, behold, the widow woman was there gathering sticks; and Elijah called to her, and said unto her: "Fetch me, I pray thee, a little water in a vessel, that I may drink." And as she was going to fetch it, Elijah called after her, and said: "Bring me, I pray thee, a morsel of bread in thine hand, and I will eat." And the woman said: "As the Lord Thy God lives, I have not a cake, but an handful of meal in a barrel, and a little oil in a cruse: and, behold, I am gathering two sticks, that I may go in and dress it for me and my children, that we may eat it, and die." And Elijah said unto her: "Fear not; go in and do as thou hast said: but make me thereof a little cake first, and bring it out unto me, and after make for thee and for Thy children. For thus says the Lord God of Israel: The barrel of meal shall not fail, neither shall the cruse of oil diminish, until the day that the Lord sends rain upon the face of all the earth." And the woman went and did according to the word of Elijah, and gave unto him, and she ate, and he, and her children. And the barrel of meal failed not, neither did the cruse of oil diminish, according to the word of the Lord, which He spoke by the hand of Elijah. And it came to pass after these things, that the son of the woman, the mistress of the house, fell sick; and his sickness was sore, that there was no breath left in him. And the woman said unto Elijah: "What have I to do with thee, O man of God? Art thou come unto me to call my sins to remembrance, and to slay my son?" And Elijah said unto the woman: "Give me thy son." And he took him out of her bosom, and carried him up into an upper room, where he abode himself, and laid him upon his own bed. And Elijah cried unto the Lord, and said: "Alas, O Lord, the witness of the widow with whom I sojourn, hast Thou brought evil upon her, that Thou hast slain her son?" And he breathed upon the child three times, and called upon the Lord, and said: "O Lord my God, I pray Thee, let this child's soul come into him again!" And it was so: and the child cried aloud, and the soul of the child came into him again, and he revived. And Elijah took the child, and brought him down out of the chamber into the house, and delivered him unto his mother: and Elijah said: "See, thy son lives!" And the woman said unto Elijah: "Now by this I know that thou art a man of God, and that the word of the Lord in thy mouth is truth."

*The Ninth Reading (Isaiah 61:10–62:5):*[^457]  
*The Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 61):*  
Let my soul rejoice in the Lord: for He has clothed me with the garment of salvation, and with the robe of gladness has He covered me; as a bridegroom He has put a garland upon me, and as a bride has He adorned me with beauty. And as the earth increases her flower, and as a garden causes her seeds to spring forth; so the Lord God will cause righteousness and joy to spring forth before all nations.  
*(Chapter 62):*  
For Zion's sake will I not hold my peace, and for Jerusalem's sake I will not rest, until my righteousness go forth as light, and my salvation burn as a lamp. And the Gentiles shall see Thy righteousness, and all kings Thy glory; and they shall call thee by Thy new name, which the Lord shall name thee. Thou shalt also be a crown of beauty in the hand of the Lord, and a royal diadem in the hand of Thy God. Thou shalt no more be termed Forsaken; neither shall Thy land any more be termed Desolate: but thou shalt be called My Will, and Thy land the Inhabited Earth; for the Lord delights in thee, and Thy land shall be inhabited. And as a young man lives with a virgin, so shall Thy sons live with thee; and as the bridegroom rejoices over the bride, so shall the Lord rejoice over thee.

*The Tenth Reading (Genesis 22:1–18):*[^458]  
*A Reading from Genesis (Chapter 22):*  
And it came to pass after these things, that God did tempt Abraham, and said unto him: "Abraham, Abraham!" And he said: "Behold, here I am." And He said: "Take now thy beloved son Isaac, whom thou lovest, and get thee into the high land, and offer him there for a whole burnt offering upon one of the mountains which I will tell thee of." And Abraham rose up early in the morning, and saddled his ass, and took two of his young men with him, and Isaac his son, and cleaved the wood for the whole burnt offering, and rose up, and went, and came unto the place of which God had told him, on the third day. And Abraham lifted up his eyes, and saw the place afar off. And Abraham said unto his young men: "Abide ye here with the ass; and I and the lad will go yonder, and worship, and return unto you." And Abraham took the wood of the whole burnt offering, and laid it upon Isaac upon Isaac his son; and he took the fire in his hand, and a knife; and they went both of them together. And Isaac spoke unto Abraham his father, and said: "My father!" And he said: "What is it, my son?" And he said: "Behold the fire and the wood: but where is the lamb for a whole burnt offering?" And Abraham said: "God will provide Himself a lamb for a whole burnt offering, my son." So they went both of them together, and came to the place which God had told him of; and Abraham built an altar there, and laid the wood in order, and bound Isaac his son, and laid him on the altar upon the wood. And Abraham stretched forth his hand, and took the knife to slay his son. And the Angel of the Lord called unto him out of heaven, and said: "Abraham, Abraham!" And he said: "Here am I." And He said: "Lay not thine hand upon the lad, neither do thou any thing unto him: for now I know that thou fearest God, seeing thou hast not withheld thy son, thine only beloved son, for My sake." And Abraham lifted up his eyes, and looked, and behold behind him a ram caught in a thicket by his horns: and Abraham went and took the ram, and offered him up for a whole burnt offering in the stead of his son Isaac. And Abraham called the name of that place The Lord Has Seen: as it is said to this day, In the mount the Lord was seen. And the Angel of the Lord called unto Abraham the second time out of heaven, saying: "By Myself have I sworn, says the Lord, for because thou hast done this thing, and hast not withheld thy son, thine only beloved son, for My sake: that in blessing I will bless thee, and in multiplying I will multiply thy seed as the stars of the heaven, and as the sand which is upon the sea shore; and thy seed shall possess the cities of their enemies. And in thy seed shall all the nations of the earth be blessed; because thou hast obeyed My voice."

*The Eleventh Reading (Isaiah 61:1–10):*[^459]  
*The Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 61):*
